# DECISIONS — CCF SDK v0

**Canonical authority:** `CLAUDE.md` (revision 4). CLAUDE.md wins on any disagreement. Whitepaper citations reference `docs/CCF_Whitepaper_1-2.md` sections, never the PDF (locked D-01).

**Reading the `file.py:NNN` citations in this log (reader note, recorded 2026-08-06; states an existing convention, rules nothing new).** Every entry below is dated, and this log is corrected by *appending* a new dated entry rather than by editing an old one — the D-GATE-15 second amendment, the D-IMPL-86 correction and the three D-GATE-15.1 entries all follow that discipline, and one of them is explicitly marked "preserved **unedited**". A line citation inside a dated entry is therefore **a snapshot of the tree at that entry's date, not a maintained pointer**, and the tree has moved under many of them. `ccf.deployment.binder`'s module docstring already leans on exactly this reading, correctly, for the `load_and_boot` citation in the D-IMPL-86 correction. Measured 2026-08-06 by running the `citations` lint over this file with its exemption bypassed: **63 citations, of which 11 would be flagged** — spread across nine entries, one of them the preserved-unedited one. That measurement is why the lint scopes this file out (`HISTORICAL_RECORDS` in `tools/lints/citations.py`) rather than why the entries were rewritten: rewriting them would falsify the record, and rewriting two of eleven would leave a reader to infer the other nine are current. Cite a symbol — `path/to/file.py::name` — in anything that is maintained.

## Open Decisions (D-IMPL)

**D-IMPL-01 through D-IMPL-29** are scaffolded in CLAUDE.md §12, which remains their canonical home — they are referenced here, not duplicated. Status notes:

- **D-IMPL-01 — Language target.** RESOLVED — Python 3.12+ as the sole v0 target; typed contracts as Pydantic v2 `extra=forbid`; Protocol-based seams; no TypeScript port in v0. Rationale: the whitepaper's mechanism commitments are Python-native idioms (structural-typing Π dispatch, `extra=forbid` bases, kw-only `tenant_id` — INV-CH7-12/13/73), CLAUDE.md §11 already presumes Pydantic-in-Python, and the harness ecosystem (D-IMPL-24 candidates) is Python-first. Build-time single-writer prevention gap compensated by the D-IMPL-41 enforcement stack. 2026-07-25. Approver: Dave.
- **D-IMPL-02 — Graph storage substrate.** RESOLVED — `GraphStore` Protocol + reference Postgres implementation + SQLite-for-dev implementation. Rationale: substrate-neutral invariant semantics (INV-CH7-16/17/21/04) carried by the Protocol (see D-IMPL-34); SQLite-for-dev is load-bearing for the §9 examples discipline. 2026-07-25. Approver: Dave.
- **D-IMPL-03 — Vector substrate.** RESOLVED — `SimilarityIndex` Protocol + reference implementation on sqlite-vec (dev default), Chroma as a second reference impl if budget allows. Rationale: the Similarity determinism contract (INV-CH7-27) requires vendor-neutral corpus/index versioning and version pinning in L; sqlite-vec pairs with the SQLite-for-dev graph store for a zero-infra quickstart. 2026-07-25. Approver: Dave.
- **D-IMPL-04 — Runtime topology for v0.** RESOLVED — (a) single distribution with two packages (`ccf` library + `ccf-runtime`) in the monorepo, installable separately; (b) async-first runtime with sync facades; fabric core (operator/projection path) stays sync per the read-only-sync operator contract (INV-CH7-22). Rationale: the `/src` vs `/runtime` seam becomes a packaging boundary reinforcing the harness-neutrality lint; runtime obligations (MCP both roles, A2A, suspended workflows, monitoring triggers) are inherently concurrent. 2026-07-25. Approver: Dave.
- **D-IMPL-05 — Tenancy model.** RESOLVED — multi-tenant with `tenant_id` first-class throughout; single-tenant is the degenerate case (one fixed tenant id), not a separate mode. Rationale: tenancy is structural in the whitepaper (INV-CH7-16 no-untenanted-path; kw-only `tenant_id` per INV-CH7-73(e); tenant in the product cache key per INV-CH7-52); a mode split would fork every signature. 2026-07-25. Approver: Dave.
- **D-IMPL-06 — Snapshot management for replay.** RESOLVED — logical snapshots by construction: a snapshot is an immutable as-of reference (`graph_snapshot_id` = temporal boundary + tenant + store-state watermark) plus a touched-substate manifest (the entity URIs, relationship ids, and lineage entries the decision actually read, already enumerated by lineage strict equality); never a physical graph copy. The full-fidelity tier (INV-CH7-89) additionally materializes the touched substate as a content-addressed ArtifactStore artifact per D-IMPL-26; at compression it reduces to hash + re-hydration recipe per D-IMPL-44. Similarity-index snapshots: the index version pinned in L is the reference; the SimilarityIndex Protocol (D-IMPL-03) must support version-pinned reads; periodic full-index snapshots are ArtifactStore artifacts on a Control-Plane-configured cadence (values, not SDK policy). 2026-07-25. Approver: Dave.
- **D-IMPL-07 — Embedding model abstraction.** RESOLVED — `EmbedderProtocol` with two reference adapters (one API-based, one local/deterministic for tests); model identity + version exposed as part of the Protocol contract. Rationale: "same embedding model version" is a determinism input (INV-CH7-27), so `(model_id, model_version)` reporting is a Protocol obligation; the local deterministic embedder is replay test-strategy infrastructure. 2026-07-25. Approver: Dave.
- **D-IMPL-08 — Unstructured pipeline (Pattern 5) pluggability.** RESOLVED — each stage as a Protocol with one opinionated reference pipeline wired from the reference implementations. Rationale: the non-negotiable mechanism is the output contract (ingestion-time extraction, typed E/R nodes, passage-level L anchors — INV-CH7-19); stage Protocols express the mode variance of the Alternating Derivation Pipeline (INV-CH8-12) and give the `@algorithmic` lint per-stage enforcement targets. 2026-07-25. Approver: Dave.
- **D-IMPL-09 — LLM access for semantic stages.** RESOLVED — `LLMClientProtocol` with a first-class Anthropic reference adapter carrying the §7.10.3 Citations capability; the Protocol requires model-version reporting and models citation-bearing responses as an optional capability surface. Rationale: replay dimension 3 (Model Version Record) makes version capture a structural obligation of the client contract; one Protocol module gives the `@algorithmic` lint a crisp import target; D-IMPL-46 will own the passage-citation provider Protocol shape specifically. 2026-07-25. Approver: Dave.
- **D-IMPL-10 — Repo shape.** Resolved for v0: **monorepo** at `github.com/drcastillo/ccf_code` per CLAUDE.md §2.2 (logged 2026-07-24). The remaining sub-question — whether adapters split into separate packages or repos post-v0 — is tracked outside Phase D as deferred post-v0 scope and is **non-gating** for Phase F/I. Per CLAUDE.md §2.2 the entry retains its "provisional monorepo" orientation label; that label is a CLAUDE.md-governed orientation, not a Phase D `PROVISIONAL` status token, and does not require confirmation at D-GATE-D.
- **D-IMPL-11 — Agent base shape.** RESOLVED — single concrete `Agent` runtime class (the factory's output type), specialized entirely by its definition artifact; archetypes as Protocol-typed composable behavior bundles attached by declaration; no class hierarchy and no user subclassing — code-level extension happens via Protocol seams (skills, harness adapters, archetype components). Ruled after extended discussion including a worked CreditMonitoringAgent/OverdraftMonitoringAgent scenario. Rationale: the §6.1 archetype/agent separation is only structurally safe if there is nothing to subclass; replay dimension 4 capture is mechanical when everything distinguishing agents is serialized in the artifact (INV-CH7-84); archetypes are functional categories, not a taxonomy (INV-CH8-22, CLAUDE.md §6.3). Composition semantics per D-IMPL-19. 2026-07-25. Approver: Dave.
- **D-IMPL-12 — Skill catalog model.** RESOLVED — dynamic Control Plane skill catalog: skills registered as typed, versioned Control Plane artifacts (per the D-IMPL-28 format) at boot or at runtime through an audited Control Plane action; four skill types (local callable | MCP resource/tool | A2A endpoint | tool) — MCP-Resources-as-skills is one type, not the unified mechanism; catalog entries carry a requirements declaration (policy scope, context-contract access, egress) feeding the D-IMPL-29 compatibility check; grants/revocations are separate versioned artifacts referencing catalog entries by version. 2026-07-25. Approver: Dave.
- **D-IMPL-13 — MCP role allocation.** RESOLVED — MCP allocated to exactly the five CLAUDE.md §7.1 edges: one `ccf` MCP client library serving both L1 ingress (via `MCPSourceAdapter` normalizing into the D-IMPL-33 `SignalEnvelope`) and L4–L5 content acquisition; one `ccf-runtime` MCP egress server serving L6 Context Products as Resources and permitted agent invocations as Tools (L6 + Experience access are one server, two resource families); a separate ArtifactStore MCP server contract (§7.4), never merged with egress. Packaging follows D-IMPL-04; single-writer unaffected (acquisition results reach L3 only via L2). 2026-07-25. Approver: Dave.
- **D-IMPL-14 — A2A wire protocol.** RESOLVED — the Linux Foundation A2A protocol (JSON-RPC over HTTPS with Agent Card discovery and the task/artifact model) as the v0 default `WireProtocol` implementation, carrying the typed Context Product as the artifact payload; mTLS via deployment configuration; a plain typed-HTTP fallback transport ships as the second reference impl. The Context Product Contract remains the stable layer; consumer-side Control Plane verification happens above the transport (INV-CH8-07/08). 2026-07-25. Approver: Dave.
- **D-IMPL-15 — Agent identity and signing.** RESOLVED — `IdentityProvider` Protocol with a two-layer model: workload/transport identity via mTLS (X.509) as deployment configuration, and product-level signing via detached JWS over the canonical serialized Context Product (producer agent identity + version + key id) verified at the consumer's Control Plane during A2A admission. Reference impls: static-keypair JWS signer/verifier (dev) + certificate-backed keys (production posture). SPIFFE/SPIRE is a conforming Protocol implementation, not required. 2026-07-25. Approver: Dave.
- **D-IMPL-16 — Control Plane scope at A2A boundary.** RESOLVED — verify every inbound A2A Context Product: full Control Plane verification (signature, Policy bindings, contract validation, lineage integrity) on every admission, no fast path in v0. Peer trust tiers configure only what is admissible (producers, product classes, policy regimes a peer may assert), never verification skips. Post-v0 performance relief via verification caching memoized on immutable artifact identity, preserving verify-everything semantics. Ruled after a performance/safety discussion — admission is off the hot path, amortized once per product version. 2026-07-25. Approver: Dave.
- **D-IMPL-17 — Orchestration primitive scope.** RESOLVED — minimal in-SDK Workflow primitive plus `OrchestrationProtocol` for external orchestrators (the CLAUDE.md §12 recommended option). The Workflow primitive ships exactly the §8.1 committed properties (skill/agent/HITL-gate nodes, state-machine semantics, suspend/resume on Control Plane state per D-IMPL-18, first-class exception paths, harness-neutral agent nodes, definitions as Control Plane artifacts through the Task Factory); traces land in replay dimensions 4/5 by construction. `OrchestrationProtocol` is the seam to Temporal-class external engines with identical replay obligations. LangGraph two-seam disambiguation per the D-IMPL-24 ruling: the LangGraph *harness adapter* binds only the §7.3 agent-loop seams; LangGraph *as an orchestrator of CCF agents* is a distinct, optional `OrchestrationProtocol` integration — one tool, two seams, never conflated; DESIGN.md documents the boundary explicitly. 2026-07-25. Approver: Dave.
- **D-IMPL-18 — Workflow state persistence.** RESOLVED — Control Plane persistence (the CLAUDE.md §12 recommended option): suspended workflow execution state (current node, variable bindings, pending-gate references) lives at the Control Plane boundary as typed, versioned state records on the Control Plane's native store — mutable until terminal, therefore off the ArtifactStore per the D-IMPL-27 mutability rule. Every state transition appends to the execution trace; the trace is written through to the ArtifactStore as part of the replay record at terminal and at each suspend (long-horizon audit). Persistence substrate is the Control Plane's storage seam — pluggable there, no separate workflow-store Protocol. 2026-07-25. Approver: Dave.
- **D-IMPL-19 — Agent archetype expression.** RESOLVED — composition over mixins: archetypes are Protocol-typed component bundles the factory attaches to the single `Agent` class per the artifact's declared archetype list; multi-archetype agents are ordered composition with explicit conflict rules — identical obligations contributed twice are deduplicated; conflicting configuration defaults resolve by declaration order with a factory warning; hard contradictions are a factory construction error. The composed archetype list is channel-1 identity (axis 1) and lands in replay dimension 4. 2026-07-25. Approver: Dave.
- **D-IMPL-20 — HITL transport.** RESOLVED — `HITLTransportProtocol` (typed queue interface) as the core mechanism: pending HITL events (ApprovalGate, ExceptionEscalation, OverrideBoundary, ReviewQueue items) are typed records — event class, workflow state reference, the Context Product the human must see, deadline/escalation policy reference — enqueued by the HITL primitives; actions (approve/reject/override/annotate) return through the same surface and land in the replay record by construction, no transport path bypassing capture. Two reference transports: (1) MCP server endpoints exposing HITL queues as Resources and actions as Tools on the Experience-Layer surface (primary — serves example 05 and the `ccf hitl` CLI); (2) webhooks (secondary, notify-only — the webhook notifies, the action always flows back through the typed queue, so notification and authority are separated and audit-by-construction cannot be bypassed). No inbox UI per CLAUDE.md §8.2/§13. 2026-07-25. Approver: Dave.
- **D-IMPL-21 — HITL identity binding.** RESOLVED — pluggable via the same `IdentityProvider` Protocol family as D-IMPL-15, with a distinct human-principal binding shape: every HITL action record carries a typed `HumanPrincipalBinding` — approver identity (subject id + identity-provider reference), authentication context (method, assurance level, session reference), action time, and the content hash (per D-IMPL-26) of the Context Product the approver saw. OIDC/SAML enterprise providers are the realistic human-principal path (vs. mTLS+JWS for workloads per D-IMPL-15). Reference impls: authenticated-principal record from the HTTP/MCP auth context (dev) and a JWS-signed binding reusing the D-IMPL-15 signing machinery for cryptographic non-repudiation. Audit-by-construction holds regardless of provider: no HITL action lands without a binding. 2026-07-25. Approver: Dave.
- **D-IMPL-22 — CLI shape.** RESOLVED — noun-verb subcommand structure `ccf <noun> <verb>` covering exactly the §8.3 enumerated surfaces (`agent`, `skill`, `workflow`, `hitl`, `replay`, `artifact`, `product`, `config`, `schema`, `backends` noun set), as a thin client over the HTTP API with a `--local` direct-library mode for the SQLite/file-backed dev quickstart (examples 00–02). Every mutating verb routes through the same factories/Control Plane actions as any other client — `ccf agent declare` takes an artifact file, never inline definition flags (No-Hardcoding §6.4) — so the CLI adds zero privileged paths and keeps one authorization/audit surface. 2026-07-25. Approver: Dave. **Amended 2026-08-04 (plan 15-12):** the noun set gains a ninth entry, `schema`, as the mechanical consequence of D-IMPL-79 — which ruled that `ccf schema export` reaches schema generation through an HTTP route rather than a privileged local path. The enumeration is amended rather than re-ruled: D-IMPL-79 is the decision, and this list is the text `tests/http_api/test_routes.py` reads to hold the mounted prefixes to it. **Amended 2026-08-09 (plan 15.4-09):** the noun set gains a tenth entry, `backends`, on the same D-IMPL-79 mechanics — `ccf backends list` answers "which adapter names does this deployment accept" over a route rather than by calling the registry from a privileged local path. The occasion is D-15: plan 15.4-06 removed the deployment manifest's six-name `backend` `Literal` and with it the `enum` an adopter's editor completed from, and this command is what replaces it, answering what is *installed* rather than what was hard-coded.
- **D-IMPL-23 — HTTP API surface.** RESOLVED — minimal v0 skeleton mirroring the CLI noun set: product retrieval, agent declare/list/show/versions, skill grants/catalog, workflow invocations/status/resume, HITL queues/actions, replay decisions/resolve, artifact get/put, health + OpenAPI. OpenAPI generated from the Pydantic `extra=forbid` contracts the SDK already defines — the API adds serialization, auth hooks, and routing, no new semantics. L1 ingestion is deliberately NOT on the v0 HTTP surface: ingress is CDC/MCP-adapter territory (D-IMPL-13/33), and a generic write endpoint would invite a second writer path. 2026-07-25. Approver: Dave.
- **D-IMPL-24 — Harness adapter targets for v0.** RESOLVED — **Dave's own ruling, differing from the recommendation: ALL THREE harnesses ship reference adapters in v0 — Google ADK + Claude Agent SDK (must-haves) + LangGraph.** Dave's rationale: a number of LangGraph installations (including Homecams) could benefit from CCF. Accepted consequences, recorded per the ruling: (a) a third adapter, a third CI leg, and a third example-03 variant enter v0 scope; (b) the LangGraph-vs-Workflow-primitive overlap is handled in design — the adapter binds the agent-loop seams only; orchestration overlap is D-IMPL-17 territory (§8.1). The `HarnessAdapterProtocol` is designed against all three; zero-harness-imports-in-core is unaffected (adapters live in `/runtime/adapters/<harness>/`). 2026-07-25. Approver: Dave.
- **D-IMPL-25 — HarnessAdapterProtocol seam scope.** RESOLVED — three seams mandatory for every conforming adapter: context injection, replay hooks, and effect capture as governed refusal; skill exposure conditionally mandatory (required iff the bound agent has a non-empty skill-set). A read-only consumer adapter may declare `effects: unsupported`, in which case the absence of any write-back path is a declared capability verified by the conformance suite — never a merely-unimplemented seam. Single-writer holds across the adapter boundary (CLAUDE.md §7.3 seam 3, §10). 2026-07-25. Approver: Dave.
- **D-IMPL-26 — Artifact addressing and versioning.** RESOLVED — hybrid addressing: content-addressed canonical identity plus versioned-URI naming, resolved through the Control Plane. Canonical identity of every artifact is `sha256(canonical_serialization)` — immutability and replay determinism become verifiable properties (a tampered artifact fails its own address; replay resolves exact versions by hash per CLAUDE.md §7.4). Human/system-facing addressing is a versioned URI `ccf-artifact://{tenant}/{artifact_class}/{name}@{artifact_version}` per the D-IMPL-28 two-field version discipline, maintained as an append-only Control Plane mapping (versioned-URI → content hash; a version once bound to a hash is never rebound). `@latest` is permitted only at authoring/Control Plane surfaces — never in a replay record or lineage entry, which store the hash (URI carried as annotation). The reference artifact MCP server (per the D-IMPL-13 separate ArtifactStore contract) exposes artifacts addressable by both forms, with a typed metadata resource per artifact. 2026-07-25. Approver: Dave.
- **D-IMPL-27 — Substrate split.** RESOLVED — split by mutability: immutable, versioned, replay-relevant artifacts route through the ArtifactStore (Decision Replay records with all extensions, materialized graph-substate and similarity-index snapshots per D-IMPL-06, Context Products at rest, Control Plane bundles, all No-Hardcoding artifacts — the full CLAUDE.md §7.4 class list); hot mutable state stays on native Protocols (live Context Graph on GraphStore per D-IMPL-02, live similarity index per D-IMPL-03, operator-plan intermediate caches per D-IMPL-36, suspended workflow execution state per D-IMPL-18 — mutable until terminal, whose terminal trace then lands in the ArtifactStore). The mutability criterion is a single structural rule; single-writer preserved trivially (the store never holds live graph state). 2026-07-25. Approver: Dave.
- **D-IMPL-28 — Definition artifact format.** RESOLVED — YAML definition artifacts validated by Pydantic v2 (`extra=forbid`) schemas with exported JSON Schema for non-Python tooling; Jinja2-templated prompt artifacts referenced by versioned URI (never inline text), with template parameters declared in the referencing definition and validated at construction time; every artifact carries `schema_version` and `artifact_version` with independent per-artifact-class semver lanes. Artifact addressing/storage remains D-IMPL-26. Homecams' YAML+Jinja2 shape was a data point, never a default. 2026-07-25. Approver: Dave.
- **D-IMPL-29 — Skilling governance depth.** RESOLVED — two-tier governance, both mechanism: tier 1, the automatic policy-compatibility check, always on and non-waivable (the Control Plane refuses a bind whose declared requirements exceed the agent's policy regime or context contract); tier 2, policy regimes may declare that grants (all, or matching declared criteria) additionally require an ApprovalGate before taking effect — whether tier 2 is engaged is Control Plane configuration, values not SDK policy. Requirements-declaration schema is the typed block on the D-IMPL-12 catalog entry, validated per D-IMPL-28. Grant, approval, and effective-time all land in the audit record. 2026-07-25. Approver: Dave.
- All D-IMPL-01..29 entries are now RESOLVED (D-IMPL-10 resolved for v0 as monorepo per CLAUDE.md §2.2, with the post-v0 adapter-split remainder tracked as non-gating deferred scope under that entry). The full CLAUDE.md §12 Phase I gating list (01..05, 11, 14, 17, 20, 24, 26, 28) is RESOLVED as of the batch-3 rulings, 2026-07-25.

- **Decision Census note (Phase D accounting).** The Phase D "49 decisions" census counts the marathon decision set D-IMPL-01..50 **excluding** D-IMPL-10 and D-IMPL-51: D-IMPL-10 is a CLAUDE.md §2.2 repo-orientation entry predating and outside the marathon protocol (resolved for v0 as monorepo; post-v0 remainder non-gating), and D-IMPL-51 is Phase R triangulation metadata (a one-time live MongoDB metadata query, RESOLVED at the Phase R gate), not a design decision. Appendix C of `docs/DESIGN.md` cites 50 distinct rulings because it indexes every `D-IMPL-NN` referenced by the design, including D-IMPL-10 (orientation-only citation). The three counts — 49 marathon rulings, 50 cited rulings, 51 total entries — are therefore consistent by construction.
- Phase R inputs recorded against existing numbers: Homecams' sync-hot-path + async-enrichment-worker process split is a data point for **D-IMPL-04(b)**; Homecams' YAML agent/task definitions + Jinja2 prompt templates with CI grep guard are a data point (not a default) for **D-IMPL-28**; Homecams' tenant-scoped repository base is a data point for **D-IMPL-05**; Homecams' LiteLLM provider abstraction is a data point for **D-IMPL-09**; Homecams' Protocol+factory classifier registration is a data point for **D-IMPL-07**; Homecams' embedded-field versioning of replay artifacts is a data point for **D-IMPL-26**. See the `homecams-patterns` appendix in `docs/SPEC_INVENTORY.md` — all are triangulation data points, never defaults (CLAUDE.md §2.1).

New entries below are assigned sequentially from **D-IMPL-30** by the Phase R synthesis task (2026-07-25). Every entry: title, status, date, originating section. Ambiguous-passage entries quote the passage and propose readings with consequences per CLAUDE.md §15. Cross-references from `docs/SPEC_INVENTORY.md` entries point here.

---

**D-IMPL-30 — Context Product Contract element count (eight vs nine).** RESOLVED — reading (a) with the (c) annotation: the contract has nine elements including Assembly Mode; the "eight" references are editorial drift, annotated as such in DESIGN.md. §6.4's explicit "ninth element" language plus the nine-row table is the strongest internal evidence of authorial intent, and Assembly Mode is load-bearing mechanism (the §7.6 three-mode model and per-mode replay footprints of INV-CH7-88 hang off it — a contract without the mode cannot say which replay footprint applies). The eight-count references — including CLAUDE.md §5's — are recorded in DESIGN.md as an annotated discrepancy; Dave's ruling doubles as authorization to treat CLAUDE.md §5's "eight" as tracking the same drift rather than overriding the whitepaper table. The Fig-CH07-13 seven-field typed base (INV-CH7-45) is a different artifact — base contract fields vs. specification elements — and DESIGN.md states the mapping explicitly. 2026-07-25. Approver: Dave. Originally: Open — awaiting Dave. 2026-07-25. Originates: whitepaper §6.3 (INV-CH6-03). Ambiguous passage: §6.3 prose opens "Nine elements jointly specify the contract" and the table lists nine (Assembly Mode included; §6.4 calls it "the §6.3 specification's ninth element"), yet the same §6.3 prose describes "the § 6.1 canonical 8-property table" and CLAUDE.md §5 refers to "the eight-element specification per §6.3." Readings: (a) the contract has nine elements and the "eight" references denote the external comparison test only — consequence: the SDK Contract base carries all nine fields including Assembly Mode; (b) the contract has eight elements and Assembly Mode is a per-product *configuration* property layered on the contract — consequence: Assembly Mode lives in Control Plane product-class registration, not the product schema; (c) revision drift; the count is editorial — consequence: Dave rules which count is canonical and the inventory annotates the discrepancy. The 8-property→9-element mapping is asserted but never tabulated.

**D-IMPL-31 — TTL granularity: per-signal vs per-product.** RESOLVED — reading (a): two granularities with a declared derivation rule. The SDK ships (i) a signal-level validity primitive — each observation carries a validity envelope derived from its type's λ_c (a naming of existing §7.7 machinery, not a new decay system); and (ii) the product-level contract TTL (`expires_at` + `ttl_anchor` per INV-CH7-45), whose derivation is declared per product class in the Control Plane: `expires_at = min(class-configured MAX_TTL ceiling per D-IMPL-39, derivation over constituent signal validities)` with the derivation function (min as the reference default shape; choice per class is configuration) declared under the D-IMPL-38 parameter discipline. Product classes may declare constituent-validity floors whose breach refuses assembly rather than emitting a product fresher-labeled than its stalest load-bearing constituent (the mixing-refusal rule, formally footed). 2026-07-25. Approver: Dave. Originally: Open — awaiting Dave. 2026-07-25. Originates: whitepaper §6.4 Step 4 vs §6.3 (INV-CH6-04). Ambiguous passage: §6.4 Step 4 states "Temporal Validation assigns TTL to each mutation," while §6.3 defines TTL as a per-product contract element, and §6.4's worked thread gives both per-product TTLs (4h/24h) and per-signal TTLs (15 min blotter entry). Readings: (a) TTL exists at two granularities and product TTL derives (e.g., min) from constituent signal validities — consequence: SDK needs a signal-level validity primitive plus a product-level TTL with a defined derivation rule; (b) Step 4's "TTL" loosely means the T-dimension validity envelope and only the contract-level TTL is properly TTL — consequence: SDK type surface must disambiguate half-life-derived validity from contract TTL; (c) product TTL is independently configured per class and Step 4 only validates constituent signals — consequence: no derivation rule, but the asymmetric-freshness mixing-refusal rule still needs formal definition. §7.7 partially informs but does not resolve.

**D-IMPL-32 — Context Trust Triangle scoring mechanics.** RESOLVED — reading (a) with (b) folded in as the reference implementation: a `TrustScorer` Protocol with pluggable per-leg scorers and a conjunctive combinator. Three typed legs, each scored in [0,1] by a `LegScorer` Protocol; termination is conjunctive ("all three legs hold" is the whitepaper's own combinator: each leg ≥ its threshold), with per-leg thresholds as Control Plane values per decision class (D-IMPL-38 discipline, no SDK defaults). The reference `LegScorer` set derives leg strengths from the §7.7 debt components (F, C, S per D-IMPL-47), so v0 ships a working trust surface with zero new measurement machinery; deployments with independent trust signals plug their own scorers. Every computed leg score, threshold version, and termination verdict lands in the replay record. 2026-07-25. Approver: Dave. Originally: Open — awaiting Dave. 2026-07-25. Originates: whitepaper §6.6.3 (INV-CH6-08). Ambiguous passage: "when all three legs hold, the recursion may terminate" — no aggregation function, scale, or threshold mechanics are specified for any leg or the aggregate. Readings: (a) thresholds are Control-Plane-configurable; SDK ships a TrustScore Protocol with pluggable per-leg scorers and a conjunctive combinator — mechanism-not-values; (b) the legs are derived quantities via §7.7 D_c mechanics (the §7.7.8 debt-mapping supports this partially: leg strengths derive from the three debt components) — consequence: no independent trust primitive; (c) the triangle is advisory vocabulary — consequence: no SDK primitive; only replay capture of computed trust inputs. Gates whether v0 ships a trust-scoring surface.

**D-IMPL-33 — Canonical L1 event envelope schema.** RESOLVED — typed `SignalEnvelope` contract: the whitepaper-enumerated core (`source` as structured source identity, `ingestion_timestamp`, `observed_time: optional`, `raw_payload` typed opaque-to-L1) plus `tenant_id` (INV-CH7-16 no-untenanted-path), an SDK-assigned `envelope_id` (lineage anchoring, INV-CH7-14), and an opaque `source_metadata` extension; `extra=forbid` on the envelope. "L1 does not interpret" honored by opaque payload typing; content typing happens at L2. 2026-07-25. Approver: Dave. Originally: Open — awaiting Dave. 2026-07-25. Originates: whitepaper §7.1 (INV-CH7-02). "Whatever raw payload the upstream system emitted" — the envelope fields are enumerated in prose (source, ingestion timestamp, observed-time?, raw payload) but no formal schema is given. The SDK must commit a typed L1 envelope contract. Homecams' normalized enrichable-event contract with per-source adapters is one data point.

**D-IMPL-34 — Graph-storage Protocol requirement surface.** RESOLVED — the Protocol requires the six invariant semantics and leaves all physical layout backend-internal: (1) compound uniqueness on `(tenant_id, entity_uri)` at the persistence tier (INV-CH7-16); (2) idempotent upsert with set-union lineage accumulation (INV-CH7-17); (3) logical supersession with `superseded_by`/`superseded_at` and as-of reads (INV-CH7-21); (4) append-only-forward (INV-CH7-04); (5) per-relationship-type edge lineage (INV-CH7-20); (6) bitemporal read support (INV-CH7-14 T axis). Backend-internal: collection/table decomposition ("~10 collections" is reference grounding, not normative), indexing strategy, partitioning. Protocol exposes separate reader and writer interfaces as the seam for D-IMPL-41. Conformance verified by a Protocol test suite both D-IMPL-02 reference impls must pass. (Ruling note: Dave initially selected the thinner-Protocol alternative in error and immediately corrected to the recommendation; the ruling of record is the six-semantics surface.) 2026-07-25. Approver: Dave. Originally: Open — awaiting Dave. 2026-07-25. Originates: whitepaper §7.3.1 (INV-CH7-15). "The physical representation maps to ~10 collections" is reference-implementation grounding, not a normative count. What the SDK's graph-storage Protocol must require of a backend (compound index, supersession semantics, per-relationship-type lineage, append-only discipline) versus what stays backend-internal is a design decision. Do NOT inherit the Homecams collection layout. Interacts with D-IMPL-02.

**D-IMPL-35 — Entity Resolver contract.** RESOLVED — SDK ships the mechanism: an `EntityResolutionStrategy` Protocol (candidate correlation over declared identity attributes → canonical tenant-scoped `entity-class://` URI per INV-CH7-18, set-union lineage accumulation on merge) plus a reference deterministic attribute-correlation strategy driven by user-declared ontology config. Correlation attribute sets, precedence rules, and probabilistic matching are user configuration/plugins, never SDK values. 2026-07-25. Approver: Dave. Originally: Open — awaiting Dave. 2026-07-25. Originates: whitepaper §7.3.5 (INV-CH7-21). Correlation via "shared attributes (the LEI where one exists, registered address and authorized-signatory metadata where it does not)" is a domain-flavored pattern. The SDK needs a general-purpose resolution Protocol (pluggable strategy vs shipped mechanism); attribute-correlation strategies are user configuration.

**D-IMPL-36 — Operator-plan intermediate scheduling and materialization.** RESOLVED — deterministic intermediate cache with Control-Plane-configured materialization policy: the SDK ships the coherence mechanism; when-to-cache is values. Intermediates are keyed on `(tenant, operator_key, parameter_set_version, input_intermediate_keys, graph_snapshot_id)` — a deterministic key, so a cache hit is by construction the same value replay would produce (INV-CH7-28's contract). Invalidation rides the committed supersession/Policy-event cascade (λ_c collapse and supersession invalidate downstream intermediates predictably; lazy re-derivation, never eager). Whether a class materializes intermediates, retention window, and eviction budget are per-class Control Plane configuration under the D-IMPL-38 discipline. The Planner's intermediate-reuse decision at the composition-preservation checkpoint (Fig-CH07-10) reads this cache. 2026-07-25. Approver: Dave. Originally: Open — awaiting Dave. 2026-07-25. Originates: whitepaper §7.4.2, Fig-CH07-08 (INV-CH7-28). The contract is committed (intermediates persistable, cache-coherent under λ_c invalidation, reusable for replay) but the scheduling/materialization mechanism (when intermediates cache, eviction, reuse policy) is not specified. Homecams' TTL-indexed `projection_cache` separate from graph state is one working resolution — do NOT inherit (CLAUDE.md §2.1). Interacts with D-IMPL-06/D-IMPL-27.

**D-IMPL-37 — Operator registry population surface.** RESOLVED — explicit boot-time `register(operator_key, operator)` API invoked at the composition root, fail-fast duplicate-key error at boot per INV-CH7-29; Control Plane declares which registered operators are permitted/parameterized, but code registers them. No entry-point or import-side-effect registration in v0 (post-v0 convenience possible atop the same API). 2026-07-25. Approver: Dave. Originally: Open — awaiting Dave. 2026-07-25. Originates: whitepaper §7.4.2 (INV-CH7-29). "Import-time / boot-time loaded" with fail-fast duplicates is committed, but how user-supplied operators register against the SDK's registry (entry points, explicit registration API, config-driven) is unspecified.

**D-IMPL-38 — Control Plane parameter schema for operator thresholds.** RESOLVED — typed, versioned `OperatorParameterSet` Control Plane artifact class: each operator declares its parameter schema (names, types, bounds, required/optional — no SDK-side default values for domain thresholds); Control Plane stores per-deployment bundles validated against declared schemas, semver-versioned, in-force version captured in L per invocation. Missing required parameter = boot/validation failure, never a silent default (all whitepaper numerics are illustrative, never SDK defaults). 2026-07-25. Approver: Dave. Originally: Open — awaiting Dave. 2026-07-25. Originates: whitepaper §7.4.3/§7.4.6 and §8.4 (INV-CH7-24, INV-CH8-12). Thresholds (materiality floors, similarity `threshold=0.85`/`0.75`, the §8.4 distance-weighted-deduplication confidence threshold) are stated as Control-Plane-governed contract parameters, but no threshold schema, declaration surface, defaulting rule, or versioning discipline is specified. Interacts with §7.8.3 configuration surface and the No-Hardcoding Rule (CLAUDE.md §6.4).

**D-IMPL-39 — MAX_TTL configuration locus.** RESOLVED — reading (a): MAX_TTL is Control Plane configuration per Context Product class, required (no SDK default), validated at gate 3 (`now < expires_at ≤ now + MAX_TTL`, INV-CH7-48) and re-verified at cache-write time; the in-force MAX_TTL version is part of the policy-bindings replay dimension. A product class without a declared MAX_TTL fails Control Plane validation at registration. 2026-07-25. Approver: Dave. Originally: Open — awaiting Dave. 2026-07-25. Originates: whitepaper §7.6.1/§7.6.4 (INV-CH7-48). Ambiguous passage: "TTL bounds rejects a Contract whose expires_at is in the past or beyond a MAX_TTL envelope" — where MAX_TTL is configured and what governs its value is never specified. Readings: (a) Control Plane configuration per Context Product class (consistent with §7.8 posture) — SDK ships mechanism, users supply values; (b) fabric-wide constant — collides with the mechanism/values boundary; (c) derived from the product's λ_c band — elegant but nowhere stated.

**D-IMPL-40 — CAR threat-level scale and class-specific D_c thresholds.** RESOLVED — reading (a): both the threat-level scale and the class-specific D_c thresholds are Control Plane configuration per decision class; SDK ships only the routing mechanism — the four-state CAR predicate chain verbatim (INV-CH7-65) over a user-declared ordered threat-level enum and per-class thresholds, all versioned Control Plane artifacts captured in replay. SDK defines the shape (ordered scale with designated LOW-or-below band; `D_c` threshold in [0,1] per class) but no values and no scale membership. 2026-07-25. Approver: Dave. Originally: Open — awaiting Dave. 2026-07-25. Originates: whitepaper §7.7.7 (INV-CH7-65). Ambiguous passage: "ELIF threat_level ≤ LOW → Conditional … ELIF D_c above the class-specific threshold → Forced LLM" — the threat-level scale, its source, and how class-specific D_c thresholds are set are unspecified. Readings: (a) both are Control Plane configuration per decision class; SDK ships routing mechanism only; (b) threat level is fabric-computed from signal characteristics — would require a computation the paper never gives.

**D-IMPL-41 — Build-time single-writer enforcement substrate.** RESOLVED — four-layer enforcement stack (assumes the D-IMPL-01 Python ruling): (1) interface split — separate `GraphReader`/`GraphWriter` Protocols per D-IMPL-34, read-path constructors accept only `GraphReader`; (2) DI-at-boot — composition root constructs exactly one `GraphWriter`, injected into L2/Assembly only; (3) static gate — strict mypy in CI rejects non-L2 `GraphWriter` type references, plus an import lint (same machinery as the `@algorithmic` and harness lints) forbidding the writer module's import outside L2 and the composition root; CI failure = build failure, Python's honest equivalent of "prevented by the type system at build time" (INV-CH7-73(a)); (4) runtime backstop — writer handle constructed with a boot-time token only the composition root can mint. Residual honesty note (Python cannot make determined-insider evasion impossible; claim strength is "no unforced path exists and every forced path fails CI or fails at boot") goes to DESIGN.md's Invariant Preservation chapter. 2026-07-25. Approver: Dave. Originally: Open — awaiting Dave. 2026-07-25. Originates: whitepaper §7.8.4 (INV-CH7-73). "Compilation of those services against write-capable interfaces is prevented by the type system at build time" assumes a compiled/typed substrate; how the SDK realizes build-time prevention depends on D-IMPL-01 (language target) — in Python, structural typing + DI approximates but does not equal build-time prevention.

**D-IMPL-42 — Learning-signal taxonomy naming.** RESOLVED — the prose taxonomy is canonical: typed enum `OUTCOME | CONTEXT_VALIDITY | AUDIT_LINEAGE`, with the Fig-CH07-24 handler names documented as dispatch-side aliases (context-validity ↔ Correction handler; audit-lineage ↔ Metadata handler; alias table recorded in DESIGN.md so the figure remains legible against the code). The prose names carry signal semantics; the figure names describe handler roles, and an enum named for handler behavior would couple the taxonomy to one processing topology. Matches the inventory's reading (INV-CH7-76: divergence informational, prose carries the commitment). 2026-07-25. Approver: Dave. Originally: Open — awaiting Dave. 2026-07-25. Originates: whitepaper §7.9.2 vs Fig-CH07-24 (INV-CH7-76). Prose categories (outcome / context-validity / audit-lineage) diverge from figure handler names (Outcome / Correction / Metadata) — an informational naming divergence inside the whitepaper. The SDK's typed learning-signal enum must pick one canonical taxonomy.

**D-IMPL-43 — Closed-loop interpretation step: automated vs human-gated.** RESOLVED — configurable per learning-signal class, defaulting to human-gated: the D-IMPL-29 two-tier shape applied to the closed loop. Every closed-loop Control Plane update (operator parameters, projection weights, policy thresholds, half-life adjustments) flows through an interpretation stage that is a workflow with an ApprovalGate by default; Control Plane policy may configure specific signal classes × parameter classes × bounded deltas as auto-applied (tier 1: always-on validation against declared parameter bounds per D-IMPL-38; tier 2: ApprovalGate unless explicitly waived for that class). Every update — gated or auto — is a versioned Control Plane action in the audit plane; "the loop is in the graph, not the model" holds either way. Default-gated is the fidelity-conservative reading of §7.9.1's "business judgment... regulatory calibration" language. 2026-07-25. Approver: Dave. Originally: Open — awaiting Dave. 2026-07-25. Originates: whitepaper §7.9.1 (INV-CH7-75). "The interpretation step is where business judgment, statistical significance, and regulatory calibration constraints enter the loop" — whether interpretation is automated, human-gated, or configurable is unspecified. Interacts with SDK HITL primitives (CLAUDE.md §8.2) and D-IMPL-29 governance depth.

**D-IMPL-44 — Cold-archive re-hydration contract for compressed replay records.** RESOLVED — typed `ReHydrationRecipe` carried by every compressed record, plus a `ColdSourceArchiveProtocol`: the SDK ships the deterministic re-hydration mechanism; the archive substrate is pluggable. At compression (tier 2 of INV-CH7-89) the graph substate reduces to (a) the deterministic content hash of the materialized substate artifact and (b) a typed recipe — L1 envelope ids/source references, entity URIs, temporal boundary, and the operator-plan + parameter-set + policy-bundle version references needed to re-derive the substate. Re-hydration re-runs L2 assembly against the archived source signals under the pinned Control Plane versions and verifies the result against the stored hash — bit-for-bit or the re-hydration fails loudly, never a silent approximation. `ColdSourceArchiveProtocol` is the seam to WORM/durable source archives (the Evidence Chain terminus, INV-CH7-87); file-backed reference impl for dev. Recipes and hashes are ArtifactStore artifacts addressed per D-IMPL-26. 2026-07-25. Approver: Dave. Originally: Open — awaiting Dave. 2026-07-25. Originates: whitepaper §7.10.1/§7.10.5 (INV-CH7-80/81/89). Replay "at any future time the audit obligation requires" plus per-decision graph substate at millions-of-decisions/day scale: the snapshot substrate, the deterministic-hash-based re-hydration mechanism, and the cold-source-archive contract are unspecified. Constrains and is constrained by D-IMPL-06 (snapshot management), D-IMPL-26 (artifact addressing), D-IMPL-27 (substrate split); this entry owns the re-hydration contract specifically.

**D-IMPL-45 — Model-trace composition seam.** RESOLVED — a `TraceCorrelationHook` on replay dimension 5 (Content Generation Path), attached at the Harness Adapter's replay-hooks seam (CLAUDE.md §7.3 seam 4): the CCF replay record stores a typed external trace reference `{trace_provider, trace_id, trace_url?, capture_time}` per LLM invocation; the SDK ships an `ExternalTraceProviderProtocol` (correlate at call time; resolve at replay time) with a no-op reference impl and one worked integration in examples/docs. CCF owns environment replay; the external tool owns model-internals tracing; the seam is a stored correlation, not an ingestion. 2026-07-25. Approver: Dave. Originally: Open — awaiting Dave. 2026-07-25. Originates: whitepaper §7.10.2 (INV-CH7-86). The whitepaper assumes a mature deployment composes CCF environment replay with an external model-trace tool (LangSmith/Weave) but specifies no interface for the composition. The SDK's replay-hook seam (CLAUDE.md §7.3 seam 4, SDK-defined) is the natural attachment point — a Phase D design question.

**D-IMPL-46 — Passage-citation provider Protocol.** RESOLVED — a `PassageCitationProvider` Protocol separate from `LLMClientProtocol`, defined by its typed output contract: generated claims each carrying zero-or-more passage anchors in the whitepaper's L-anchor forms (`{document, page, paragraph, offset_span}` / `{transcript_id, timestamp, text_span}` per INV-CH7-19), plus a provider-identity+version field feeding replay dimension 5. Two reference impls: the Anthropic Citations adapter (thin bridge over the D-IMPL-09 client's citation capability) and a deterministic post-hoc span-matcher as the vendor-neutral, CI-testable fallback. The ReviewQueue and ContentGenerationAgent archetype consume the Protocol, never a vendor API. 2026-07-25. Approver: Dave. Originally: Open — awaiting Dave. 2026-07-25. Originates: whitepaper §7.10.3 (INV-CH7-87). "Populated by whatever component primitive the deployment has selected" — the Protocol shape for a pluggable passage-citation provider (and its relationship to D-IMPL-09 LLM access) is unspecified.

**D-IMPL-47 — Computable definitions for the F, C, S debt factors.** RESOLVED — **refined recommendation ruled**: a per-factor `DebtFactorScorer` Protocol with SDK reference computations built from the runtime signals §7.7.6 names — mechanism shipped, calibration as values — PLUS Dave's explicit evolvability requirement recorded in the ruling. Reference scorers: **F** = aggregation over constituent signal freshness ratios `R(t)/R₀` from the committed decay form `R(t) = R₀ × (1/2)^((t−t₀)/λ_c)` (reference aggregation: weighted minimum over load-bearing constituents; weights/aggregation choice per product class); **C** = declared-slot coverage — the fraction of the product class's required contract slots populated by admissible observations; **S** = 1 − detected-contradiction rate over the assembled substrate (conflicting values for the same (entity, attribute, as-of) under supersession rules). Each factor ∈ [0,1]; `D_c = 1 − (F·C·S)` verbatim (INV-CH7-63). All weights, thresholds, and aggregation selections are per-class Control Plane parameters under D-IMPL-38. **Evolvability (Dave's refinement):** (1) reference scorers are versioned implementations — `(scorer_id, scorer_version)` is part of identity, and the class→scorer-version binding is updatable via the Control Plane without code changes; (2) any class can override with a custom, more precise scorer through the same Protocol seam as deployments learn more or have unique use cases; (3) replay pins the scorer version in force — decisions made under v1 replay under v1 after upgrade to v2. Factor values + scorer identity/version land in the replay record. 2026-07-25. Approver: Dave. Originally: Open — awaiting Dave. 2026-07-25. Originates: whitepaper §7.7.4/§8.7.1 (INV-CH7-62, INV-CH8-16). The extended form `D_c = 1 − (F · C · S)` is given without specifying how F (freshness), C (completeness), and S (semantic consistency) are individually computed; §7.7.6 names the runtime signals per component but not the factor computations. The SDK must commit a computable definition (or a scoring Protocol) per factor.

**D-IMPL-48 — Supervision Quality: scoring function and SDK-scope disposition.** RESOLVED — out of v0 SDK scope: Supervision Quality ships as a documented reference pattern, not mechanism. §8.8 is a domain application of committed primitives, not a new primitive: the five behavioral axes are domain values, the fusion function is calibration, and the VLM/LLM reasoning stage is ordinary semantic-stage mechanism under the Alternation invariant. Everything a deployment needs exists in the fabric: signals through L1, axis computations as user-registered operators (D-IMPL-37), the composite as a user-defined Context Product class, thresholds via D-IMPL-38, escalation via the HITL primitives. DESIGN.md records the disposition with a short "how you would build it" sketch (candidate future example, not v0 suite); no SDK type is named `SupervisionQuality`. 2026-07-25. Approver: Dave. Originally: Open — awaiting Dave. 2026-07-25. Originates: whitepaper §8.8 (INV-CH8-19). The composite scoring function combining the five behavioral axes with VLM/LLM reasoning outputs is unspecified — axis weights, fusion function, and score semantics are open. Also open: whether Supervision Quality is SDK mechanism at all or a reference pattern users implement — CLAUDE.md §13 does not list it in v0 scope.

**D-IMPL-49 — Projection-plan selection signature.** RESOLVED — refined recommendation ruled (after an agent-swarm context-assembly discussion): canonical deterministic selection signature `(perspective, context_product_class, selection_params)` — perspective is the Π role-conditioning already bound as channel-1 axis 3; `selection_params` is an optional typed block declared per product class in the Control Plane per the D-IMPL-38 parameter discipline. Free intent inference is out of v0 scope; where plan selection varies by intent, intent is a declared enum-valued selection parameter — configuration, not inference. `(perspective, product_class, selection_params, plan_version)` fully determines the selected plan, preserving §7.10 replay determinism. ADDITIONALLY, per the ruling: **DESIGN.md obligation** — swarm-level intent decomposition must be explicitly documented in DESIGN.md as an orchestration-layer concern and a NAMED post-v0 extension candidate: intent compiles down to concrete product requests carrying the signature; coordination stays above Π; replay stays deterministic. The drafting plans must pick this up. 2026-07-25. Approver: Dave. Originally: Open — awaiting Dave. 2026-07-25. Originates: whitepaper §7.5 (silent on selection inputs); Homecams resolution observed (homecams-patterns appendix). Homecams derives a projection plan from the (agent role, decision intent, product contract) triple. Is that triple the SDK's canonical projection-selection signature, or is intent modeling out of scope? Data point only, never a default.

**D-IMPL-50 — Replay fidelity-lifecycle mechanism: SDK-shipped or ArtifactStore-delegated.** RESOLVED — SDK-shipped mechanism, executing above the ArtifactStore Protocol. The three-tier compression, the severity exemption, and the structural `retention-reason` field are committed whitepaper mechanism (INV-CH7-89; Patent Claim 14), so the SDK ships the lifecycle engine: deterministic compression function per record class, the never-compress/never-tombstone severity exemption enforced in mechanism, retention-reason written structurally on every transition — operating on artifacts through the ArtifactStore Protocol so any conforming store gets the lifecycle for free. Values: retention windows per decision class, severity threshold per decision class, tombstone minima — all Control Plane configuration under the D-IMPL-38 discipline. 2026-07-25. Approver: Dave. Originally: Open — awaiting Dave. 2026-07-25. Originates: whitepaper §7.10.5 (three-tier compression is committed mechanism); homecams-patterns appendix (one working lifecycle). Does the SDK ship the fidelity-lifecycle mechanism (deterministic compression, severity exemption, retention-reason field) as core, or leave retention entirely to the ArtifactStore implementation? Interacts with D-IMPL-06/D-IMPL-27; distinct in that it allocates the mechanism, not the substrate.

**D-IMPL-51 — Live MongoDB metadata query for Homecams triangulation.** RESOLVED — Dave approved the live query at the Phase R gate (2026-07-25); executed read-only (metadata listing only, existing Homecams venv pymongo, no install, no credentials recorded) and results recorded in the homecams-patterns appendix (fragments/homecams-patterns.md, "Live MongoDB Metadata Query" section). Originally: 2026-07-25. Originates: CLAUDE.md §2.1 (MongoDB Collections bullet); homecams-patterns appendix. Is a live read-only MongoDB metadata query wanted (requires approving a pymongo install)? The source-code route satisfied the §2.1 triangulation intent with zero installs; a live query would add only collection-level stats/index metadata.

**D-IMPL-52 — Examples-suite domain policy: single neutral domain or horizontal mix.** RESOLVED — horizontal mix of domains, with `examples/00-quickstart` (and the Phase I.1 vertical-slice fixtures that precede it) in a **banking** scenario. This reverses CLAUDE.md §9's "one coherent neutral domain across the suite" discipline, which mandated a single non-banking, non-property-intelligence domain (field-service / logistics) so generality would be demonstrable from day one. Dave's reading: generality is better demonstrated by a suite that visibly spans unrelated domains than by one neutral domain held constant, and banking fixtures put realistic policy/regulatory pressure on the fabric — serving the 04-CONTEXT.md D-02 realism bar ("would this slice catch a broken invariant?") in a way a neutral toy domain does not. The §9 constraint that survives unchanged: no example is a domain pack, and everything domain-specific stays in definition artifacts, never in example code (the No-Hardcoding Rule, §6.4). CLAUDE.md §9 amended in Phase 4 plan 04-01 to state the horizontal-mix policy. 2026-07-25. Approver: Dave. Originates: CLAUDE.md §9 (examples-suite discipline); `.planning/phases/04-phase-i-1-fabric-core/04-CONTEXT.md` D-03.

**D-IMPL-53 — CINV-02 harness-import lint scope at the adapter path.** RESOLVED — a structural module-path carve-out, never an allowlist. The `cinv02-harness` rule denied harness imports across the whole of `LINTED_TREES = ("src", "runtime", "examples")`, which forbade them in precisely the directory CLAUDE.md §7.3 designates for harness bindings (`runtime/adapters/<harness>/`) and 06-CONTEXT.md D-04 confirms as the reference adapters' home — demonstrated empirically with a probe module during Phase I.3 research (finding B1), and a hard blocker on the entire harness wave. The ruling: add `HARNESS_ADAPTER_MODULES: frozenset[str] = frozenset({"ccf_runtime.adapters"})` to `tools/lints/harness_imports.py` and guard the deny branch with `and not matches_module(module_name, HARNESS_ADAPTER_MODULES)`, structurally identical to the `LLM_ADAPTER_MODULES` branch that already carves out the vendor LLM SDK for the `LLMClientProtocol` adapters (D-IMPL-09). Explicitly rejected: an allowlist file, a data-driven exception list, or any per-module opt-out — a carve-out that lives in data rather than in reviewed code voids the invariant it exists to enforce. This is a *narrowing of the lint's scope to match CLAUDE.md §7.3's own wording* ("zero harness imports in **core**"), not an exception to the rule; `LINTED_TREES` is unchanged, so the rest of `/runtime` stays linted. The D-21-compliant claim, and the wording the lint's docstring now carries, is: *"lint-enforced: no module outside `ccf_runtime.adapters.*` may import a harness package."* Enforcing evidence is four negative boundary tests in `tools/lints/tests/test_harness_imports.py` — a harness import inside `ccf_runtime.adapters.*` passes, while imports in `ccf_runtime.a2a`, `ccf_runtime.cdc`, and the shared-prefix lookalike `ccf_runtime.adapters_evil` are each still flagged; `matches_module` is dot-bounded, so the lookalike case is a boundary the constant cannot silently widen. Without those tests the carve-out would be an unguarded prefix match. 2026-07-26. Approver: Dave (recorded as D-24 in `.planning/phases/06-phase-i-3-cross-process-harness-adapters/06-CONTEXT.md`). Originates: CLAUDE.md §7.3 (harness neutrality; adapter placement); 06-CONTEXT.md D-21 (scope invariant claims to their actual enforcement), D-04.

**D-IMPL-54 — the control on artifact-driven code execution via `callable_ref` (IN-06 / D-19).** RESOLVED — a registered-callable table (`CallableRegistry`), replacing dotted-path import resolution outright. The question: `src/ccf/skills/dispatch.py` resolved a skill catalog entry's `LocalCallableBinding.callable_ref` with `importlib.import_module` + `getattr`, executing arbitrary module code at dispatch time. I.2 dispositioned this as an accepted risk (IN-06; T-05-18; D-GATE-I.2) on a precondition about *where the ref came from* — an in-repo Control Plane artifact inside the tenant trust boundary — and recorded, in `docs/PHASE_I2_GATE.md` §5 and in the function's own docstring, that the precondition does not survive I.3: once catalog entries can arrive over MCP artifact stores or A2A federation (CLAUDE.md §7.2/§7.4), `callable_ref` is remote-influenced input and the path is remote code execution. `PHASE_I2_GATE.md` §5 named three candidate controls: (a) an import allowlist, (b) a registered-callable indirection removing free-form module paths, (c) a Control Plane admission check on `callable_ref` provenance. The ruling is (b). Three reasons. First, it makes the threat *unrepresentable* rather than merely refused: `src/ccf/skills/callables.py` and `src/ccf/skills/dispatch.py` contain no `importlib` and no dotted-path resolution at all, so a module path is not a value the surface can express — where (a) and (c) both leave the importer in place and interpose a judgement that can be wrong, incomplete, or bypassed. Second, it is the shape D-IMPL-37 already ruled for operators, so the SDK gains no second registration idiom: `CallableRegistry` is a structural port of `OperatorRegistry` — explicit boot-time `register(key, fn)` at the composition root, no decorators and no import side effects, append-only with `DuplicateCallableKey` fatal at registration rather than last-writer-wins, and `CallableNotRegistered` on an unheld key. Third, it costs the deployment nothing new to configure: an allowlist would be a fresh class of deployment *values* (module patterns) the SDK would then have to version, ship defaults for, and defend — exactly the hardcoding CINV-01 forbids. Explicitly rejected: a denylist of dangerous names. The SDK ships no name-shaped judgement; an entry naming `os.system` is refused because nothing registered that key, and `tests/skills/test_callables.py::test_the_refusal_is_absence_not_a_denylist` pins that the hostile ref and an unremarkable absent key are refused by one mechanism. The registry is injected, never ambient: two composition roots in one process binding the same key to different callables each resolve their own, so no deployment can shadow another's registrations (D-23, pinned by `test_two_composition_roots_binding_one_key_cannot_shadow_each_other`). **`callable_ref`'s field name and `str` type are unchanged** — only the meaning of the string moved, from a dotted module path to a registry key — so no artifact schema version breaks; the banking fixture's entry takes an `artifact_version` bump (1.1.0 → 1.2.0) because a binding value change is a content change (D-05), and its grant is re-pinned accordingly. The D-21-compliant claim: *dispatch resolves callable keys against what this deployment's composition root registered, and imports nothing.* One residual, stated rather than papered over: a callable a composition root does register is code that root chose to offer, and its execution-time resource behavior (blocking, looping) is deployment infrastructure, not an SDK control (CLAUDE.md §13). 2026-07-26. Approver: Dave (recorded as D-19 in `.planning/phases/06-phase-i-3-cross-process-harness-adapters/06-CONTEXT.md`, where it is a blocking gate on the MCP and A2A waves). Originates: `docs/PHASE_I2_GATE.md` §5 (IN-06 / T-05-18 handoff); CLAUDE.md §6.2 (skilling channel), §7.2/§7.4 (the artifact sources that invalidate the I.2 precondition); D-IMPL-37 (the registration shape); 06-CONTEXT.md D-21, D-23.

**D-IMPL-55 — where the harness-attributable replay fields live (D-25).** RESOLVED — a named `HarnessTrace` sub-structure on `ModelVersionRecord`, replay dimension 3. The question arose because D-18 splits the harness-portability equivalence assertion into two halves: one agent definition run under three adapters must agree *exactly* on everything CCF governs (the consumed Context Product, the grant-set hash, the agent definition hash, the policy bundle versions, lineage) while the values only the harness can report — which harness ran the loop, the model version it invoked, its execution-trace reference, its token counts — necessarily differ. Those values need somewhere to live that the governed comparison can exclude by name; a comparison including them would fail always across three different SDKs, and a record omitting them would lose the attribution that makes example 03's proof legible. Three placements were considered. **Extend dimension 3 (`ModelVersionRecord`)** — chosen: harness-attributable values *are* model-attributable values, the dimension already exists for exactly that question, and the extension is a sub-structure so dimension 3's own `assembly_mode` and `model_version` stay governed fields the fabric alone writes (T-06-62: a harness reports its own `model_version` inside `harness_trace` and can never overwrite the fabric's claim about what *it* invoked). **Extend dimension 5 (Content Generation Path)** — rejected: dimension 5 records how the delivered content came to be as an operator plan and step sequence, and D-IMPL-45's `TraceCorrelationHook` attaches to the same replay-hooks seam in I.4, so putting the harness identity there would make one dimension answer two unrelated questions. **A sixth top-level block** — rejected outright: INV-CH7-80 makes the five dimensions inviolate, so a sixth block is a whitepaper-fidelity defect, not a design option. `DecisionReplayRecord`'s five dimension fields are unchanged and `tests/replay/test_harness_trace.py::test_the_record_still_has_exactly_five_dimensions` asserts it against the named set rather than a field count (the record also carries `tenant_id` and `recorded_at` as record-level metadata, so a raw `len(model_fields)` check would pass or fail for the wrong reason). This is the **third use of the established extension pattern** — the same three coordinated edits that extended dimension 4 in I.2 with agent identity/version/skill-set, and that will extend dimension 5 in I.4 with workflow step sequences: a doctrine line in the module docstring recording which phase extends which dimension, a new field defaulted to a *positive sentinel* rather than `None` or absence, and an optional pre-serialized block on `build_replay_record` recorded verbatim. The sentinel is `NO_HARNESS = "no-harness"` in the `MODE_1_MODEL_VERSION` voice, exposed as the whole-block constant `NO_HARNESS_TRACE`, so "no harness participated" is a claim the record asserts — without it, an absent trace and a *dropped* trace would be the same artifact and a lost attribution would read as an honest no-harness run (T-06-63). The block is recorded verbatim from what the adapter reported and is never reconstructed inside the builder, for the same reason `agent_conditioning` is not: a record that re-observed anything would describe recording-time state rather than decision-time state. **It lands in I.3 rather than I.4** because ROADMAP criterion 3 places the replay-hook seam in this phase — deferring would build seam 4 with nowhere to write and leave D-18's equivalence assertion, which is example 03's entire proof, unwritable. Being a `/src` contract change it carries this numbered entry rather than arriving incidentally. Byte-identity is claimed at the scope it can earn (D-21): passing `harness_trace=None` is indistinguishable from omitting the argument, and every byte the builder emitted before this extension is unchanged — asserted in `test_every_pre_change_field_is_byte_for_byte_unchanged` against a literal captured by executing the pre-change `build_replay_record` — with dimension 3 gaining exactly one new key. D-IMPL-45's `TraceCorrelationHook` still attaches at this seam in I.4; I.3 delivers the slot and the adapters that write into it. 2026-07-26. Approver: Dave (recorded as D-25 in `.planning/phases/06-phase-i-3-cross-process-harness-adapters/06-CONTEXT.md`). Originates: CLAUDE.md §7.3 seam 4 (replay hooks); whitepaper §7.10.1 (INV-CH7-80, INV-CH7-83, INV-CH7-88); 06-CONTEXT.md D-18, D-21, D-23; D-IMPL-45.


**D-IMPL-56 — where a skill's tool description and argument schema are declared. RESOLVED (Dave, 2026-07-27): the entry declares both, one shared derivation renders them into all three harnesses, and a fifth `procedure` skill type carries the Anthropic Agent Skills model through seam 1.** `SkillCatalogEntry` declares neither a human-readable description nor an argument schema, yet every one of the three v0 harness adapters needs both: Google ADK's `FunctionTool` and LangGraph's `StructuredTool` each want a description string and a typed argument model, and the Claude Agent SDK adapter wants the same fields to build its tool block. All three adapters surfaced the gap independently, which is the signal that it is not an adapter's problem. Plan 06-12 set the in-phase precedent and 06-13/06-14 followed it: compose the description from fields the entry already carries (name, skill type, binding identifiers) and transcribe `Agent.invoke_skill`'s `Mapping[str, str]` signature into one shared schema constant — deliberately *not* authoring prose in SDK code, which would breach the No-Hardcoding Rule (CLAUDE.md §6.4), and deliberately shared rather than per-adapter, because three adapters inventing three descriptions would break the D-18 portability equivalence 06-15 proves. That precedent is a workaround, not a resolution: what a skill is *for* is the deployment's statement, and the place it belongs is the skill-catalog artifact schema — a Control Plane decision (CLAUDE.md §6.2 channel 2, D-IMPL-12/D-IMPL-28), not an adapter's. **The question for Dave:** does `SkillCatalogEntry` gain optional `description` and `argument_schema` fields (making the tool surface a declared artifact property), or does the adapter-composed derivation become the ruled mechanism (making tool prose structurally SDK-derived, and identical across harnesses because one shared constant supplies it)? Consequence of leaving it open: every future adapter re-derives the same fields, and the first one to derive them differently silently breaks harness equivalence. Surfaced: 2026-07-27, plans 06-12/06-13/06-14, gate-assembled in 06-16. Originates: CLAUDE.md §6.2, §7.3 seam 2; `docs/PHASE_I3_GATE.md` §4.

**Dave's ruling (2026-07-27), four parts.** The question was asked as an either/or — declared artifact property *or* ruled adapter derivation — and the ruling takes the first, then extends past what was asked, because the goal Dave stated is broader than the gap the adapters surfaced: *skills must be transferable across the Claude Agent SDK, ADK and LangGraph, with the Anthropic Agent Skills model importable and CrewAI-style dynamic context binding available in the declaration.*

1. **`SkillCatalogEntry` gains a required `description` and a typed `argument_schema`; the adapter-composed derivation is retired, not kept as a fallback.** `description` is required rather than optional: what a skill is *for* is the deployment's statement, and silence is not the same statement as a declared value — the same D-IMPL-38 discipline `SkillRequirements` already applies to an empty-versus-absent frozenset. Optional-with-fallback was rejected specifically because a retained fallback is a retained divergence risk: described and undescribed skills would behave differently and the three composed strings would still exist to drift. The three `_tool_description` helpers (`runtime/ccf_runtime/adapters/{claude_agent,langgraph,adk}/adapter.py`) and the per-adapter `SKILL_ARGUMENT_SCHEMA` constant are **deleted**, not deprecated. Consequence, accepted: a major bump on the `ccf.skill-catalog-entry` schema lane, and every existing entry and fixture declares a description.

2. **`argument_schema` is a typed constrained subset of JSON Schema, never a raw dict.** A `SkillArgumentSchema` contract: named properties carrying a scalar or enum type plus an optional per-property description, and a `required` list. Raw JSON-Schema passthrough was rejected on the entry's own logic — full JSON Schema cannot be reliably derived into ADK's signature inference or a LangGraph Pydantic model, so passthrough would reintroduce exactly the per-harness divergence this entry exists to prevent, while also weakening the typed-boundary invariant. The subset is deliberately narrower to begin with (no nested objects, no `oneOf`): widening is a schema-lane bump when a real deployment case demands it, not speculative generality shipped ahead of need. Ratifying today's shared `Mapping[str, str]` constant as the ruled mechanism was also rejected: it is trivially safe for D-18 equivalence but permanently prevents a skill from declaring typed arguments, which is the capability the question exists to obtain.

3. **One shared derivation module renders the single declaration into three harness-native tool surfaces, so equivalence is structural rather than three adapters agreeing.** Claude Agent SDK → `@tool(name, description, schema)`; LangGraph → `StructuredTool` with `args_schema` built via `create_model`; ADK → `FunctionTool` over a synthesized annotated signature, since ADK introspects `__name__`/`__doc__`/annotations rather than accepting a declaration. Adapters *call* the derivation and never compose. This is the part that closes advisory A6's stated consequence — "every future adapter re-derives the same fields, and the first one to derive them differently silently breaks harness equivalence" — because after this there is nothing per-adapter left to derive.

4. **A fifth `skill_type`, `procedure`, carries the Anthropic Agent Skills model, bound through seam 1 (context injection) rather than seam 2 (tool exposure).** Its binding is an `InstructionBinding` — the existing axis-6 contract, reused rather than reinvented — plus optional pinned resource artifact URIs. It declares `description` (which is the *trigger* surface in the Anthropic model, the same field part 1 adds) and declares **no** `argument_schema`, because nothing calls it. The reasoning is the load-bearing part of this ruling: Anthropic Skills and CCF skills share a word but are different things — a SKILL.md is instructions loaded into context under progressive disclosure, while the four existing types are callable capability bindings invoked with arguments. ADK and LangGraph have no skills concept at all, so forcing a procedure into a tool shape would require inventing an argument vocabulary for something never invoked, and would leave the instruction body with no governed home on a binding whose meaning is "callable". Injected context, by contrast, requires no harness feature and is therefore portable to *any* harness by construction — which is what makes this the transferability mechanism rather than a convenience. And progressive disclosure — what to load, and when — is a fabric and policy decision that lands in replay, which is precisely CCF's job and not the harness's. Consequence I.4 must plan for: `expose_skills` filters to the callable types, and a `procedure` entry surfacing as a harness tool is a defect, not a degenerate case.

**SKILL.md is an authoring format, not a canonical artifact.** Import is one-way (`ccf skill import <dir>`, D-IMPL-22 surface): frontmatter `name` → `name`, frontmatter `description` → `description`, the body → a registered `ccf.prompt-artifact`, and `references/` → further pinned artifacts, with a `procedure` entry emitted referencing them. Replay resolves *exact* artifact versions (D-IMPL-26) and a SKILL.md in a working tree is not content-addressed, so treating it as canonical would put git state in the replay path. `allowed-tools` is **not** auto-mapped onto `SkillRequirements`: a harness permission token is not a policy-regime identifier, the SDK never interprets a requirement identifier (D-IMPL-29), and inferring one would be the SDK inventing the deployment's policy vocabulary.

**Templating reuses `render_instruction`; no second render mechanism is introduced.** The CrewAI-style dynamic binding Dave asked for already exists and is already hardened — `ccf.agents.prompts` holds the sandboxed environment, the exact-match declared-parameters rule in both directions, `str`-only values, and determinism per artifact version, and it is deliberately the SDK's *only* render entry point because the seam that calls it has three implementations and one of them getting the sandbox wrong would be three chances to get it wrong (T-06-60). A skill-side renderer would reopen that.

**Rejected: a separate manifest artifact** holding `description` and `argument_schema` so that prose edits would not churn skill grants. It does not work: replay must pin exact versions, so the entry would have to pin an exact manifest version and any prose change bumps the entry regardless — the indirection buys nothing on the churn axis and costs an artifact class plus a level of resolution. The churn is also arguably correct rather than a cost: a description change alters what the model is told and is therefore decision-relevant, so it belongs inside the versioned identity replay reproduces.

**The `procedure` token is ruled, not left to planning.** D-IMPL-59's naming escalation set the precedent that a vocabulary choice with this much surface is Dave's rather than a planner's; the token is fixed here so it cannot land silently as something else. If I.4 planning finds a materially better token it escalates back rather than substituting.

**Scope: ruled now, implemented in Phase I.4 — this entry does not block `D-GATE-I.3`.** 06-23's verification sweep re-ran every gate checklist row against the current tree (987 passed / 2 skipped, mypy clean, lints green). A breaking skill-schema change reaching all three adapters, every skill fixture and the grant lane would invalidate that sweep, force the gate package to be rebuilt, and put seven closed gap-closure findings at risk for no gain in correctness. Advisory **A6 therefore remains open in the I.3 disposition table with its consequence intact**, and this ruling is A6's designated closure in I.4. 2026-07-27. Approver: Dave. Originates: CLAUDE.md §6.2 channel 2 (skilling), §6.4 (No-Hardcoding Rule), §7.3 seams 1–2; D-IMPL-12, D-IMPL-26, D-IMPL-28, D-IMPL-29, D-IMPL-38, D-13 (prompt artifacts and the sandboxed render), D-18 (harness equivalence); `docs/PHASE_I3_GATE.md` §4.12.

---

**D-IMPL-57 — what lineage strict equality (INV-CH7-47) means for a federated Context Product. RESOLVED (Dave, 2026-07-27): reading (a) — signature accountability is the federated form of the gate.** The whitepaper makes lineage strict equality one of the three Context Product validation gates: the operators the product *declares* it was built from must equal the operators the engine *resolved*. Inside one fabric that is a real check, because the engine's resolution is an independent witness. Across an A2A trust boundary it is not: the consumer never ran the producer's operator plan, so `ccf.a2a.admission._run_contract_gates` currently passes `engine_resolved=product.lineage.operators_invoked` — the producer's own claim — as the witness. The module docstring states plainly that the gate is "structurally vacuous here" and offers signature accountability as the substitute: the producer signed the lineage it asserted, so a false claim is attributable even though it is not independently verifiable. **That substitution is a narrowing of a whitepaper-committed validation gate, and it was made in implementation rather than by ruling — `docs/DECISIONS.md` records it nowhere.** Three readings, with consequences: **(a) Accept signature accountability as the federated form of the gate.** INV-CH7-47 becomes "verifiable inside a fabric, attributable across one." Cheapest; makes the current code correct as written; but a compromised or buggy peer can assert any operator plan and the consumer admits it. **(b) Require an independent consumer-side witness.** The consumer re-resolves the declared operator plan against its own registry before admission, and refuses a product naming operators it cannot resolve. Restores the gate's teeth; costs a resolution pass at admission and means a consumer cannot admit products built with operators it does not itself have — which may be exactly right, or may be too strict for federation across differently-provisioned tenants. **(c) Refuse to run the gate at all on the A2A path and gate differently.** Admission stops claiming INV-CH7-47 and instead names the checks it actually performs (signature, policy bindings, trust policy, TTL), so no gate is asserted vacuously. Most honest about what holds; requires amending the gate documentation and the ROADMAP criterion so neither claims lineage strict equality across the boundary. Consequence of leaving it open: the phase ships with a whitepaper validation gate silently reinterpreted at the one boundary where it was hardest to hold — the precise failure mode CLAUDE.md §11's "surface gaps; do not paper over them" exists to prevent. Surfaced: 2026-07-27, Phase I.3 gate review (blocking finding B1). Originates: whitepaper §6.3/§7.6 (three validation gates, INV-CH7-47), CLAUDE.md §7.2 (A2A admission), §15.

**Dave's ruling (2026-07-27): reading (a).** INV-CH7-47 is **verifiable inside a fabric, attributable across one**. The consumer does not re-resolve the producer's operator plan; it holds the producer to what it signed. Accepted consequence, stated plainly rather than softened: *a compromised or buggy peer can assert an operator plan it did not run, and the consuming fabric will admit the product.* What the gate guarantees at the boundary is not that the claim is true, but that the claim is **attributable and non-repudiable** — the peer signed it, and cannot later deny it.
