**Soundness precondition, verified before this ruling was recorded (orchestrator, 2026-07-27).** Reading (a) is only sound if the asserted lineage is actually inside the signed material — attributability of a claim carried outside the signature would be nil, and (a) would then be strictly worse than (c). Confirmed: `ccf.a2a.identity` signs `payload = canonical_bytes(product)` (`identity.py:484`), `canonical_bytes` walks every `model_field`, and `lineage` is a field of `ContextProductContract`. Tampering with a federated product's declared lineage therefore breaks signature verification. The substitution rests on a real property, not on a hoped-for one.

**What closing blocking finding B1 requires under this ruling** — the ruling settles the *meaning*, but the phase must stop claiming the unnarrowed gate:
1. `ccf.a2a.admission` stops passing the producer's own `operators_invoked` as an `engine_resolved` witness to a gate named "strict equality". Either the call is replaced by an explicit attributability check, or the gate invocation is renamed/reshaped so nothing asserts a verification that is not performed. A gate that runs vacuously is the defect; a gate that honestly checks attribution is the fix.
2. `docs/PHASE_I3_GATE.md` and ROADMAP criterion 2 state the narrowed reading — "lineage is attributable across the A2A boundary, verifiable within a fabric" — rather than implying strict-equality verification travels.
3. A test pins the load-bearing property: mutating a signed federated product's declared lineage fails verification. That is what makes attributability structural rather than asserted, and it is the assertion this reading stands or falls on.

**D-IMPL-57 (correction, 2026-08-05) — the intra-fabric premise was wrong; the federated ruling is not reopened.** Recorded as an appended, dated note rather than an edit to the entry above, following the D-GATE-15 second-amendment precedent, so the reasoning at each point in time survives. **What is corrected.** The entry above argues that inside one fabric lineage strict equality "is a real check, because the engine's resolution is an independent witness." It is not an independent witness. `ProjectionEngine.execute` builds `operators_invoked` and `steps` in the **same loop** over `operator_plan.operators` (`src/ccf/projection/engine.py:271-288`) and returns `operators_invoked=frozenset(outputs)` (`engine.py:316`), so the engine has **no return path where invoked ≠ planned**; it is a faithful *transcriber* of the plan rather than a witness of it. This holds under every design considered, which is why the correction ships regardless of how D-IMPL-86 was ruled. What Gate 2 verifies inside a fabric is therefore **assembly-path integrity** — that the claim transcribed into the Context Product Contract matches the engine's execution of the plan — and *not* that a producer's declaration of its own requirements was right in the first place. **What is NOT corrected: the ruling.** Reading (a) stands exactly as Dave made it — products admitted through a Peering are attributable; locally assembled products are verifiable. If anything, the corrected premise supports the federated path's omission of gate 2 **more** strongly than the original premise did: the local path's justification is now faithful transcription rather than independent witness, and across a Peering the consumer ran no engine at all, so there is not even a transcription on that side to compare a declaration against — only the declaration itself, which is precisely the self-witnessing the federated sequence exists to avoid. The three B1 closure requirements listed above are unaffected and remain closed as they were, and the `AdmissionRecord`/attributability machinery is untouched. **Why this is recorded rather than argued.** Cursor was right on this point at cross-examination — that the intra-fabric line was an *assumed premise* for the federated ruling rather than something D-IMPL-57 adjudicated — and Fable withdrew the engine-as-witness argument. No dispute remains to record; only a premise to correct. **Where the corrected reading now lives in code:** `src/ccf/products/gates.py` (both sites — the module docstring's gate-2 sequence and `run_federated_gates`), `src/ccf/controlplane/artifacts.py` (`ProductClassDeclaration`), and `src/ccf/projection/plan.py` (`ProjectionPlan`). **Where authorable drift is caught instead:** `ccf.deployment.binder.refuse_operator_set_divergence`, comparing two separately authored Control Plane artifacts between plan construction and `ProjectionEngine.execute`. The audit of record is corrected in step: `docs/SURFACE-AUDIT.md` §3.1 item 1 (the claim) and §4.1 (row 9, the count). Originates: D-IMPL-86 and its correction of 2026-08-05; Phase 15.1 plan 09.

**D-IMPL-58 — where a federated product's merged lineage lives in fabric state. RESOLVED (Dave, 2026-07-27): reading (d) — persisted at the admission boundary as an immutable `AdmissionRecord` artifact.** CLAUDE.md §7.2 commits that inbound `L` "extends, not replaces, the consumer's `L` via set-union". `A2AAdmissionGate.admit` computes that union into `AdmittedProduct.lineage` — and nothing reads it. Verified against the tree by two independent passes: `AssemblyWorker.ingest_admitted` (`src/ccf/assembly/worker.py:391-489`) reads `admitted.product` and never `admitted.lineage`; it stamps the **consumer's** `self._policy_bundle_version` and derives `observed_time` from `product.temporal_boundary`. `LineageEntry` (`src/ccf/contracts/claims.py:70-85`) carries `(source, envelope_id, observed_at, passage_anchor)` — no slot for any of `ProductLineage`'s three axes — and `graph_snapshot_at` is referenced nowhere in `/src` outside `products/contract.py` and `union_lineage`. So the merged lineage is a property of a wrapper that L2 discards, while ROADMAP criterion 2 and `docs/PHASE_I3_GATE.md` row 14 claim lineage continuity as a property of the *fabric*. Four readings: **(a) Extend `LineageEntry` to carry the federated axes.** Makes the claim true where it is asserted; costs a contract change to the fabric's most load-bearing structure, reaching transitively into every claim writer, the graph store's INV-CH7-17 set-union accumulation on upsert, and replay hashing. **(b) Add a parallel federated-lineage structure alongside `LineageEntry`.** Narrower blast radius than (a); introduces a second lineage representation the L-axis must then keep coherent, which is the kind of duplication that decays. **(c) Withdraw the fabric-level claim.** ROADMAP criterion 2 and the gate row stop asserting set-union as a fabric property and state only what holds — the union is computed and audited at admission. Cheapest and honest; delivers no lineage continuity. **(d) Persist the union in the admission record rather than in the graph** — added 2026-07-27 during the Peering design discussion (D-IMPL-59). Admission is already an audited Control Plane action (`AuditAction.a2a_admitted`, `src/ccf/controlplane/audit.py:68`) that already reaches replay; the merged `ProductLineage` is written there, at the boundary where the crossing is already recorded, and `LineageEntry` is untouched. Under (d) the claim becomes true *and* correctly located: lineage continuity is a property of the federation boundary, which is what §7.2 describes, rather than a property of graph state that the L-axis was never shaped to hold. Consequence of leaving it open: blocking finding B2 cannot be closed, and Phase I.3 cannot be honestly presented for `D-GATE-I.3`. Surfaced: 2026-07-27, Phase I.3 gate review (blocking finding B2); reading (d) surfaced 2026-07-27 in the Peering discussion. Originates: CLAUDE.md §7.2 (lineage continuity), whitepaper §7.10 L-axis and §8.3 Pattern 4; ROADMAP Phase 6 criterion 2.

**Dave's ruling (2026-07-27): reading (d), realized as a new immutable `AdmissionRecord` artifact class.** The merged `ProductLineage` is persisted at the boundary crossing rather than in graph state. `LineageEntry` is not extended and `_TemporalClaim` is not touched. Lineage continuity is thereby located where CLAUDE.md §7.2 actually describes it — as a property of admission — instead of being forced into an L-axis structure that was never shaped to hold it.

**Correction recorded, because the ruling was first made on a false characterization (orchestrator, 2026-07-27).** Reading (d) was initially presented as free reuse of existing plumbing: that admission was "already audited and already reaches replay," so the union could simply be written where the admission verdict already lands. Both halves were checked after the ruling and both are false. `AuditLog.append(action, key, version, resolved, occurred_at, actor=None, artifact_class=None)` carries **scalars only** — there is no payload field, and an `AuditEvent` cannot hold a `ProductLineage`. And replay does not read the audit log at all: the sole occurrence of "audit" in `src/ccf/replay/` is a docstring sentence at `serve.py:63`, not a code path. The audit plane answers *was this admitted, by whom, when* — a verdict — not *what was admitted*. Conflating a verdict log with a record store is the same defect class as B1 itself: a structure appearing to carry a guarantee it was never shaped to hold. This paragraph is recorded rather than silently repaired because a decision log that shows only the corrected version teaches nothing about how the error was available in the first place.

**The ruling as re-confirmed on the corrected mechanism (Dave, 2026-07-27).** Admission writes an immutable `AdmissionRecord` artifact through the ArtifactStore, and the **existing** audit append references it through the reference triple the signature already provides — `artifact_class="ccf.admission-record"`, `key`, `version`. The audit log keeps doing what it was designed for (append-only scalar verdicts) and points at the artifact carrying the substance; no existing contract changes. D-IMPL-27's substrate-split-by-mutability assigns immutable artifacts to the ArtifactStore, and D-IMPL-26's addressing applies unchanged, so the artifact is replay-resolvable at an exact version rather than at "latest". Cost stated plainly rather than minimized: this is a **new artifact class**, not free reuse — admission gains an ArtifactStore write, the class needs registration, and replay needs a resolution path to it. That cost was accepted against reading (a), whose blast radius reaches every claim writer, the graph store's INV-CH7-17 set-union accumulation on upsert, and replay hashing.

**What closing blocking finding B2 requires under this ruling:**
1. `A2AAdmissionGate.admit` persists the merged `ProductLineage` as an `AdmissionRecord` artifact; the existing audit append gains the `artifact_class` / `key` / `version` reference rather than a new audit shape.
2. The class is registered as immutable per D-IMPL-27 and addressed per D-IMPL-26; replay resolves it at an exact version.
3. A test observes the set-union **in persisted state**, retrieved from the ArtifactStore — not on the transient `AdmittedProduct` wrapper, which is what made the property vacuous in the first place.
4. `src/ccf/contracts/claims.py` is unchanged, asserted rather than assumed — the whole point of (d) over (a).
5. Single-writer is preserved: the ArtifactStore is a persistence substrate reached through mechanism (CLAUDE.md §7.4), never a second writer to L3, and nothing about this path enters the graph except through L2.
6. ROADMAP criterion 2 and `docs/PHASE_I3_GATE.md` row 14 state where lineage continuity lives — a property of the Peering admission boundary — rather than implying it is a property of graph state.

**Amendment (2026-07-27): the artifact class id is `ccf.admission-record`, not `admission_record`.** This entry originally recorded the bare literal `admission_record`. That string was the orchestrator's own proposal, written into this entry as though it were a ruled value; planning then faithfully declined to "improve on a literal Dave named," and a non-conforming id was one plan-execution away from landing. It conforms to neither half of the established convention — the existing family is dot-namespaced and kebab-cased (`ArtifactClass(class_id="ccf.half-life-table")`, `class_id="ccf.debt-profile"`, `src/ccf/controlplane/artifacts.py:268,270`). Surfaced by the Phase I.3 plan-checker, ruled by Dave the same day. Recorded as an amendment rather than a silent edit for the same reason as the correction paragraph above: **the decision log is load-bearing precisely because downstream work treats it as settled, so an error introduced here propagates with the log's authority behind it.** That is the failure mode this amendment documents, not merely the wrong string.

**Naming surface ruled alongside it (Dave, 2026-07-27).** D-IMPL-59 left the mechanical surface to planning; two items were escalated back rather than landed silently. The Peering seam Protocol is **`PeeringProtocol`**, suffixed — every pluggable-substrate seam whose implementations live in `/runtime` carries the suffix (`ArtifactStoreProtocol`, `HarnessAdapterProtocol`, `EmbedderProtocol`, `SimilarityIndexProtocol`, `LLMClientProtocol`, `SignalSourceProtocol`, `ControlPlaneProtocol`, and `WireProtocol` itself, the name being replaced), while the unsuffixed Protocols in `/src` (`GraphWriter`, `Operator`, `IdentityProvider`, `SigningKeySource`, `ApprovalSink`) are in-fabric roles rather than substrate seams. `Peering` sits in the first family, and a bare noun would also read as a value type beside `PeerRef` and `InboundProduct`.

**D-IMPL-59 — the federation seam is an abstraction named `Peering`; A2A is one Peering transport, not the concept. RESOLVED (Dave, 2026-07-27).** The Phase I.3 gate review surfaced three findings (B1, B2, A2) that were all, on inspection, symptoms of one structural fact: a *category* — governed context exchange between peer fabrics — had been named after its *first instance*, A2A. Every core module that legitimately needed the category had to say "A2A" to reach it, so `ccf.assembly.worker` imports `ccf.a2a.admission`, `ccf.controlplane.audit` declares `a2a_admitted` / `a2a_admission_refused`, and `ccf.skills.dispatch` carries `A2ABinding` / `A2AInvocation` / `A2ASkillRegistry` and an `"a2a"` skill type. The dependency *direction* was already correct — `wire.py` declares the Protocol in `/src` while every concrete transport lives in `/runtime` per D-04, and its own docstring already states the governing commitment ("the Context Product Contract is the stable layer; the wire is replaceable") — so what was wrong was vocabulary, not coupling. **Whitepaper posture supports the abstraction rather than merely permitting it.** §8.3 Pattern 4's commitment is to a *discipline* — trust boundaries "resolved via Policy bindings carried in the exchanged Context Product and verified at the consumer's Control Plane before admission into the consuming fabric's graph" — and it names A2A as the industry-emergent instance carrying that discipline, explicitly declining to claim "production-deployed federation networks that are not yet industry-standard". Naming the seam for the discipline rather than for one protocol is therefore the more faithful reading under CLAUDE.md §1, not a departure from it.

**Dave's ruling (2026-07-27), four parts.**
1. **The seam is named `Peering`.** Chosen over `FederationLink`, `LinkSeam`, `FederatedSeam`, and `Legation`. `*Seam` was rejected on collision grounds: CLAUDE.md already uses "seam" as generic vocabulary for any boundary a contract binds (the harness-adapter seam, the ArtifactStore seam, "the neutral seams are the stable layer", and the §3 seam-diagram mandate), so naming one specific thing `Seam` would nest the word inside itself. `Peering` was chosen because its trust semantics are an exact match rather than a metaphor: BGP peering is two autonomous systems that establish an explicit session, exchange policy-bearing announcements, and accept what the peer asserts while holding it accountable, because independent verification is unavailable — which is precisely D-IMPL-57's ruling that INV-CH7-47 is "verifiable inside a fabric, attributable across one". The name teaches the hardest part of the concept to the audience that has to approve it.
2. **A2A is not withdrawn and no code is deleted.** A2A remains a v0 commitment per CLAUDE.md §7.2 and remains the reference Peering transport; the withdraw-from-v0 option was considered and rejected on the stated ground that A2A is the fastest adoption path in the target institution. §7.2 is reframed, not amended: A2A is first-class as a *transport*, `Peering` is the seam. This ruling is a re-description of existing structure, not a rebuild — the transports, the identity primitives, the admission gate, and the trust policy all stay.
3. **Scope is peer-symmetric only. MCP is explicitly not under `Peering`.** The defining property of a Peering is that the counterparty *has a Control Plane*, which is what makes admission meaningful and what makes lineage **merge** rather than originate. MCP ingress at L1/L4/L5 is asymmetric — nothing to verify against, and it *creates* lineage rather than reconciling it — so folding it under the same word would blur the one property that makes federation hard. MCP remains its own surface per CLAUDE.md §7.1.
4. **A Peering declares trust *inputs*, never the verdict.** This preserves the guarantee `wire.py` already records for D-12: "the admission gate sits **above** every transport, so a second transport cannot arrive with its own private check, and a transport that wanted to reach L2 would have to be handed something it has no way to make." A Peering surfaces peer identity, attestation, and the asserted policy bindings; **core runs the admission gate uniformly over every Peering and constructs the admitted value L2 will accept.** A Peering implementation can no more manufacture admission than a harness adapter can acquire a write handle — the same structural device applied at a second boundary, and the reason single-writer survives federation.

**What remains open under this entry.** The mechanical naming surface (module path, Protocol name, skill-type token, Control Plane artifact key) follows from part 1 but is not itself ruled here; it is planning work against this decision. Whether the negotiated terms between two peers get their own artifact name distinct from the channel — an `Accord` or `Compact` governing a `Peering` — was raised and not ruled. Neither blocks the abstraction.

**Interaction with D-IMPL-58.** Reading (d) exists because of this ruling: once the seam is a first-class concept with its own audited admission record, the merged lineage has a natural home that is not `LineageEntry`. D-IMPL-58 remains open and gates the replan of the Phase I.3 gap-closure wave.

Surfaced and ruled: 2026-07-27, design discussion following the Phase I.3 gate review. Originates: CLAUDE.md §7.2 (A2A federation, WireProtocol abstraction), §7.3 (the adapter-seam device this reuses), §1 (harness neutrality and general-purposeness as two faces of one commitment); whitepaper §8.3 Pattern 4; D-IMPL-14, D-IMPL-15, D-IMPL-16, D-IMPL-12 (skill types), D-12/D-04 (06-CONTEXT.md).

**D-IMPL-60 — OrchestrationProtocol driver targets for v0.** RESOLVED — **Dave's own ruling, raised unprompted (2026-07-29), widening the D-IMPL-17 seam to all three v0 SDKs: Google ADK, the Claude Agent SDK, and LangGraph each get a real `OrchestrationProtocol` driver integration in `/runtime`, each driving a CCF workflow end-to-end through that SDK's own topology surface, all three exercised by one factory-parametrized conformance suite.** This is the orchestrator-side mirror of D-IMPL-24's own logic for adapters: if all three SDKs ship reference *harness adapters* (agent-loop seams, §7.3), all three must also be provable as *orchestrators of CCF agents* (the distinct seam D-IMPL-17's two-seam disambiguation named for LangGraph alone). D-IMPL-17 is **cross-referenced, not edited** — its text stands as approved under D-GATE-D; this entry widens the set of orchestrator integrations from the one D-IMPL-17 named to three, consistent with the D-IMPL-55/57/58/59 discipline of taking a new number rather than amending prior ruled text. Accepted consequences, recorded per the D-IMPL-24 template: (a) **three driver legs**, one per SDK, under `runtime/ccf_runtime/orchestration/<sdk>/` — never under `runtime/ccf_runtime/adapters/<sdk>/`. The separate module tree is the structural enforcement of the two-seam distinction: a lint or import test pins that no orchestration module imports an adapter module and vice versa, making advisory A7's failure mode (promoting the LangGraph adapter's private `_build_graph` topology into the SDK's engine) structurally unrepresentable rather than merely documented. (b) **Three conformance legs** in one factory-parametrized suite, each driving the same CCF workflow end-to-end through the SDK's own topology surface with identical replay obligations (D-IMPL-17's condition on external orchestrators). (c) **The CINV-02 harness-import carve-out is re-examined, with the D-IMPL-53 negative-test discipline applying**: `HARNESS_ADAPTER_MODULES` currently admits `ccf_runtime.adapters` only, so the new `ccf_runtime.orchestration` tree needs its own considered carve-out entry — and a harness import in `ccf_runtime.peering` or `ccf_runtime.cdc` must still fail, or the carve-out is an unguarded prefix match. **Declined explicitly: the "one deep, then two against the suite" staging** (I.3's pattern) — all three are real legs in v0. Build *order within the wave* remains planner-level sequencing per the D-GATE-F precedent (intra-phase staging is sequencing, not a revision of a ruling); that discretion does not reduce the leg count below three. 2026-07-29. Approver: Dave. Originates: CLAUDE.md §8.1 (Orchestration Layer, `OrchestrationProtocol`), §7.3/§13 (harness-internal topology is not SDK scope — basis of the A7 boundary); D-IMPL-17 (cross-referenced), D-IMPL-24 (the all-three precedent and the consequence-enumeration template), D-IMPL-53 (carve-out discipline); `.planning/phases/07-phase-i-4-orchestration-hitl-experience/07-CONTEXT.md` D-05..D-07.

---

**D-IMPL-61 — Semantics of a workflow node with no per-node exception ref.** RESOLVED — **option (a), propagate-to-terminal.** A workflow node that declares no per-node exception ref does not fail validation; instead, absence is a *defined, explicit, audited* transition: when the node's exception path fires and no ref is bound, the workflow transitions to a terminal `failed` state, and that transition is recorded in the workflow trace like any other first-class state transition. This is the D-13 residual (D-13 rules that exception refs are per-node; it did not rule what an *absent* ref means) and it is whitepaper-silent — an SDK-defined artifact-schema surface under CLAUDE.md §8.1, which requires exception paths to be first-class state transitions, not error handling glued on. The ruling matches the positive-sentinel doctrine: the terminal `failed` transition is a positive, replayable event in the trace, never an unrecorded fall-through. It also keeps "let it fail" expressible — an author can legitimately intend a node's failure to terminate the workflow, and the alternative reading (option (b), validation error at artifact load, per the D-16 required-description forcing-function precedent) would force a boilerplate ref onto every node of every workflow and quietly make let-it-fail inexpressible, a policy choice the SDK must not impose (mechanism, not values). Accepted consequence: an author can omit a handler they meant to bind and the workflow still validates — mitigated by the trace making every propagate-to-terminal transition visible and audited, so the omission is observable at first exercise rather than silent. This ruling gates plan 07-04's WorkflowDefinition exception-routing validator semantics. 2026-07-30. Approver: Dave. Originates: CLAUDE.md §8.1 (exception paths as first-class state transitions), §15 (whitepaper-silent → Dave's ruling); D-13 (per-node exception refs — the residual this closes), D-16 (the declined forcing-function precedent); `.planning/phases/07-phase-i-4-orchestration-hitl-experience/07-01-PLAN.md` Task 2.

**D-IMPL-62 — Experience-surface HITL authority composition (HTTP/CLI action door).** RESOLVED — **option (a), submit-only with honest-contract hardening. 2026-07-30. Approver: Dave.** The HTTP/CLI HITL channels keep submit-to-transport semantics — this survives cross-process deployment, and the MCP channel's in-process resolve-wiring is a ruled asymmetry, not a defect. Required hardening, in-phase (Phase I.4): the HTTP response (and the CLI output over it) for HITL action submission must state `submitted` — never imply `resolved` — and must include enough execution/queue state for the caller to see the action's disposition honestly. Auth remains tied to T-07-24's residual-risk entry; no new auth surface is forked from this ruling. Surfacing context, preserved: surfaced by the Phase I.4 gate review (Cursor finding X-1, Codex finding C-2). As shipped, `POST /hitl/actions` (and the `ccf hitl decide` verb over it) enqueues a fully-bound `HitlActionSubmission` on the typed transport and **does not itself drive a primitive's `resolve` or land a dim-2 replay record** — no state transition and no authority effect occur from the submit alone; the composition root must pull submissions and drive the owning primitive, as the MCP HITL server demonstrably does (`tests/crossproc/servers/mcp_hitl_child.py`: gate resolve + replay write). Question for Dave: is the v0 HTTP/CLI action door (a) submit-only by design, with authority reserved to primitive-wired surfaces (the MCP channel being D-IMPL-20's primary) — in which case the route's naming/docs state it and D-IMPL-23's "HITL actions" reads as submissions; or (b) required to compose a primitive `resolve` per event class server-side — which needs a ruling on how one generic endpoint maps onto the four primitives' distinct resolution surfaces? Adjacent divergence folded in here rather than a fifth entry: D-IMPL-23 rules the API "adds … auth hooks"; the shipped v0 surface performs no authentication (T-07-24 accepted residual, posture sentence served and test-asserted), and treats future auth as a new contract. Also records the D-IMPL-20 sentence "serves … the `ccf hitl` CLI" (via MCP) vs the shipped CLI's HTTP-thin-client layering (D-IMPL-22/23) — resolved by the same ruling: the CLI's action door is a submission surface. Originates: CLAUDE.md §8.2/§8.3; D-IMPL-20/22/23; `docs/PHASE_I4_GATE.md` §7. *Verb status (2026-07-31, plan 08-04, D-14 obligation):* the v0 HITL CLI surface ships exactly one decision verb, `submit` — `decide` removed tree-wide per D-15 (see the D-15 Phase 8 context ruling, executed in plan 08-02 with an absence test).

**D-IMPL-63 — D-IMPL-21 reference identity-binding implementations.** RESOLVED — **deferred. 2026-07-30. Approver: Dave.** The two D-IMPL-21 reference identity-binding implementations (auth-context-derived principal; JWS-signed binding reusing D-IMPL-15) move to **explicit follow-up-phase scope** — a named deferral, not an indefinite posture. Caller-supplied principal fields are the recorded v0 posture, consistent with the unauthenticated v0 HTTP surface (T-07-24 accepted residual). Surfacing context, preserved: surfaced by the Phase I.4 gate review (Codex finding C-3). D-IMPL-21 names two reference impls: an authenticated-principal record derived from the HTTP/MCP auth context (dev), and a JWS-signed binding reusing the D-IMPL-15 signing machinery. Neither shipped in I.4: `HumanPrincipalBinding` is required by type everywhere (the type-unrepresentable leg holds), but its fields are **caller-supplied** — the MCP server's verified token identity is not bound to the submission's `subject_id`, and the HTTP surface has no auth context to derive from (see D-IMPL-62). Question for Dave: defer both reference impls to a named phase (with the caller-supplied posture stated as the v0 scope of the D-IMPL-21 claim), or pull one (the MCP-auth-context derivation is the smaller) into a Phase 8 plan? — ruled: defer both, with the caller-supplied posture stated as the v0 scope of the D-IMPL-21 claim. Originates: D-IMPL-21, D-IMPL-15; `docs/PHASE_I4_GATE.md` §7.

**D-IMPL-64 — Webhook notify-only reference transport: v0 disposition.** RESOLVED — **deferral REJECTED; build it this phase. 2026-07-30. Approver: Dave.** The ruled notify-only webhook HITL transport (D-IMPL-20) ships in v0, implemented in Phase I.4. Notify-only means exactly what D-IMPL-20 said: authority never flows through the webhook — it notifies external systems of HITL events, and every human action returns through the typed `HITLTransportProtocol` queue. Implementation discipline matches the other transports: injected dependencies, typed refusals, `mypy --strict`, no inline prompts or domain values. Surfacing context, preserved: ratification ask. D-IMPL-20 rules two reference transports; the notify-only webhook emitter was **deferred from v0** by plan 07-08 (single-owner rationale in its SUMMARY: authority never flowed through the webhook leg, a notify-only emitter is a pure observer implementable by any deployment against `HITLTransportProtocol` with zero SDK change, and shipping one would encode notification-infrastructure choices the SDK has no business making). Both gate reviewers flagged that this narrows a ruled deliverable without a decision-log entry — this entry is that surfacing, per the new-number-not-amend discipline (D-IMPL-20's text stands as approved). Question for Dave: ratify the v0 deferral (naming a receiving phase or "post-v0"), or require the emitter in v0 — ruled: require it in v0, this phase. Originates: D-IMPL-20; `07-08-SUMMARY.md`; `docs/PHASE_I4_GATE.md` §4.2/§7.

**D-IMPL-65 — INV-CH7-68 debt cascade at AgentNode handoffs: v0 disposition.** RESOLVED — **composition-root now, engine commitment next phase. 2026-07-30. Approver: Dave.** The Phase I.4 gate closes on composition-root debt-cascade wiring (DESIGN.md §4 stands as corrected in 07-14). This entry records a **NAMED next-phase commitment, scheduled scope and not an indefinite posture**: the engine will refuse an agent-node handoff with no bound debt assessment. Surfacing context, preserved: surfaced by the Phase I.4 gate review (Cursor finding X-7). DESIGN.md §4 lists per-handoff D_c recomputation with fail-closed routing as a Workflow-primitive invariant; the shipped v0 engine performs no per-handoff debt recomputation — the mechanism exists (`assess_debt` + Control-Plane `DebtProfile`, driving ExceptionEscalation's typed trigger) but composing it at each AgentNode handoff is left to deployment node bindings. DESIGN.md corrected in 07-14 to say so. Question for Dave: accept composition-root responsibility as the v0 posture (DESIGN text stands as corrected), or rule an engine-enforced handoff check into a named phase — ruled: both, in sequence (composition root closes this gate; the engine-enforced refusal is the next phase's committed scope). Originates: whitepaper INV-CH7-68; DESIGN.md §4; `docs/PHASE_I4_GATE.md` §7.

### Pending gate actions (not design decisions)

- ~~BLOCKER (Dave): create the private repo and wire `origin`~~ — RESOLVED 2026-07-25: Dave created `github.com/drcastillo/ccf_code`; `origin` wired via SSH; `main` and `phase-r` pushed.

**D-IMPL-66 — Post-execution review routing: Phase 7.1 gap-closure cycle (supersedes the phase-8 scheduling in D-IMPL-63 and D-IMPL-65, not their substance).** RESOLVED — **2026-07-30. Approver: Dave.** The Phase 7 post-execution cross-AI review (07-REVIEWS.md post-execution section, 4 reviewers, consensus concern #1: unauthenticated HTTP + caller-supplied principals on authority-bearing surfaces) is routed to a **Phase 7.1 gap-closure mini-cycle executed before Phase 8**, so the Phase 8 examples suite demonstrates the hardened surface rather than a surface that changes underneath it. Scope ruled into 7.1: (a) the two D-IMPL-21 reference identity-binding implementations plus a minimal HTTP auth hook (accelerates D-IMPL-63's named deferral — the deferral's substance stands, its receiving phase moves from "follow-up phase" to 7.1); (b) the engine-enforced refusal of agent-node handoffs with no bound debt assessment, with its red test (accelerates D-IMPL-65's named next-phase commitment into 7.1); (c) end-to-end `build_replay_record` drives for the three compositionally-covered HITL primitives (ExceptionEscalation, OverrideBoundary, ReviewQueue); (d) `ccf hitl submit` alias with `decide` deprecated (D-IMPL-62 verb-honesty follow-through); (e) post-merge status addendum to `docs/PHASE_I4_GATE.md` recording D-GATE-I.4 and the post-merge main sweep. Explicitly NOT in 7.1 (stays Phase 8+): google-adk `SequentialAgent` migration, performance baselines, replay-explorer / workflow-graph-export CLI ideas. Per the new-number-not-amend discipline, D-IMPL-63 and D-IMPL-65's texts stand as approved; this entry is the scheduling supersession. Originates: 07-REVIEWS.md post-execution consensus; D-IMPL-62..65; CLAUDE.md §3.1 (mini-cycles review too).


**D-IMPL-67 — 7.1 review findings N1/N2/HIGH: wire the identity mechanisms into the HITL doors before merge.** RESOLVED — **2026-07-30. Approver: Dave.** The 7.1 re-review's top three findings (Codex HIGH; Cursor N1; Cursor+Ollama N2) are ruled fixable-now, in a thin 7.2 wave on `phase-i4.1` before the cycle merges: (1) **HTTP door composition** — when an auth hook is bound to `create_app`, `POST /hitl/actions` derives the submission principal from the verified `request.state.token_grant` via `principal_from_token_grant`; a body principal conflicting with the verified identity is a typed 4xx refusal, not an accepted claim. When no auth hook is bound, behavior is unchanged (T-07-24 posture stands as ruled — D-IMPL-62's auth linkage is not reopened). (2) **MCP door composition** — `submit_hitl_action` binds the submission's `subject_id` to the MCP server's verified token identity instead of accepting a caller-typed value; conflicting claims are typed refusals. (3) **Hardened-surface demonstrability (N2's SDK-side half)** — the composed hardened path is itself test-asserted (auth bound ⇒ principal derived ⇒ audited action carries the verified subject end-to-end), so Phase 8 examples have a proven composition to demonstrate rather than unused helpers; the examples-must-bind-auth obligation itself remains Phase 8 scope. Not reopened: default unauthenticated posture, `AUTH_POSTURE_SENTENCE`, signed-binding optionality (N5), FORCED_LLM pass-through (N3). Originates: 07-REVIEWS.md §7.1 consensus; D-IMPL-21, D-IMPL-62, D-IMPL-66.


**D-15 (Phase 8 context ruling) — `ccf hitl decide` removed; v0 ships one verb: `submit`.** RESOLVED — **2026-07-30. Approver: Dave.** Executes the `decide`-alias removal-or-keep policy carried out of D-GATE-I.4.1: the deprecated `decide` alias (introduced under D-IMPL-66(d)) is removed entirely in Phase 8 per 08-CONTEXT D-15 — `ccf hitl decide` no longer parses; the parser rejects it as an unknown choice, and no deprecation path remains. `ccf hitl submit` is the single HITL action verb, unchanged in behavior (D-IMPL-62 submit-only semantics and the `HitlActionReceipt` contract stand as ruled). Enforced by an absence test in `tests/cli/test_cli.py`. Originates: 08-CONTEXT.md D-15; D-IMPL-62, D-IMPL-66(d).

**D-IMPL-68 — WR-01 per-tenant authorization mapping shape.** RESOLVED — **option (c), scope mapping as a versioned Control Plane policy artifact, enforced at the doors, deny-by-default. 2026-08-01. Approver: Dave.** Surfacing context: code review WR-01 (08-REVIEW.md, carried in `.planning/v0.1-MILESTONE-AUDIT.md`) — the HITL doors authenticate when an auth hook is bound (D-IMPL-67) but do not authorize per tenant; `TokenGrant.scopes` is never enforced, so a verified caller for tenant A can act on tenant B's HITL events. Question: where scope enforcement lives (door vs transport vs policy artifact) and what a scope string means. Ruling: the tenant × action grammar for scope strings is never hardcoded — a versioned Control Plane policy artifact declares the scopes → tenant-pattern × action-set mapping; one shared door-side helper interprets it at the HTTP and MCP doors (where identity is actually verified, per D-IMPL-67); no mapping match ⇒ deny-by-default with a typed 403 reusing the `http_api/errors.py` constants pattern; the authorization outcome, including the policy artifact version that authorized (or refused) the action, lands in the audit record. Consequences accepted: an artifact schema for the scope mapping must be ruled small inside Phase 10's implementation (HARD-04); the transport stays a pure queue with no authorization policy below the trust boundary (the D-IMPL-67 identity/data separation stands); the T-07-24 default unauthenticated posture is untouched — this governs behavior only when auth is bound. Consumed by Phase 10 (HARD-04: deny-by-default, typed 403, authz in the audit record, cross-door × cross-action matrix). Originates: CLAUDE.md §8.2/§8.3, §11 (mechanism, not values); D-IMPL-62/63/67; T-07-24 residual; `docs/09-ruling-batch-minicycle.md` item 1.

**D-IMPL-69 — ApprovalGate.resolve × D-16 signed-binding composition.** RESOLVED — **option (a), primitives verify the HitlPolicy on their resolution surfaces. 2026-08-01. Approver: Dave.** Surfacing context: audit review consensus (v0.1-MILESTONE-AUDIT) — D-16's `signed_binding_required` verification lives only on the transport `submit` path (`src/ccf/hitl/transport.py`: `SignedBindingRequiredRefused` / `SignedBindingInvalidRefused` fire in `InMemoryHitlTransport.submit`), so a composition root calling a primitive's `resolve` directly (the MCP channel's resolve-wiring, a ruled asymmetry under D-IMPL-62) bypasses the declared policy entirely: unsigned resolutions can land in replay dimension 2 under `signed_binding_required: true`. Ruling: the four HITL primitives' resolution surfaces (`ApprovalGate.resolve` and peers) resolve the `HitlPolicy` artifact and refuse an unsigned or invalid binding when the policy demands one, reusing the same typed refusals the transport uses — closing the bypass at the authority-bearing call per the audit-by-construction commitment (§8.2), without the signature churn of the type-unrepresentable resolve-context alternative. Consequences accepted: primitives gain a Control Plane read dependency on the resolve path; verification runs (idempotently) twice on transport-fed flows; JWS verification stays outside any transport lock (WR-02 adjacency — the D-02 rulings-first ordering exists for this file). Consumed by Phase 10 (HARD-04 scope). Originates: CLAUDE.md §8.2 (audit-by-construction); D-16 (Phase 8), D-IMPL-62; `docs/09-ruling-batch-minicycle.md` item 2.

**D-IMPL-70 — Performance governance: re-baseline authority, threshold ownership, opt-in posture.** RESOLVED — **option (a), Dave-gated re-baseline, repo-owned reviewed thresholds, opt-in perf workflow. 2026-08-01. Approver: Dave.** Surfacing context: Phase 11 (PERF-04) commits mechanically to runner-class-keyed baselines, `thresholds.toml`, relative-median gating, and an opt-in `perf.yml`; governance was unruled. Ecosystem evidence: absolute-time gates on shared CI runners are flake-by-construction (CodSpeed: holding 1% false-positive on GitHub-hosted runners forces a ~7% minimum gate); practice is median/min relative gating at 10–15% on shared runners. Ruling: re-baselining is a deliberate, Dave-approved act recorded in the decision/baselines log (the D-GATE discipline applied to perf baselines); `thresholds.toml` changes are reviewed like any ruled artifact; perf gating stays `workflow_dispatch` opt-in, mirroring the D-11 live-legs pattern — always-on CI remains deterministic-only per the D-10/D-11 split. Promotion to always-on is a future D-IMPL if and when the runner situation changes (dedicated hardware or an instrumented-measurement service); ruling it now was declined as committing to hypotheticals. Consequences accepted: perf-regression detection latency is bounded by ritual, not automation. Consumed by Phase 11. Originates: CLAUDE.md §13 (observability infra is the deployment's problem); Phase-8 D-10/D-11; `docs/09-ruling-batch-minicycle.md` item 3.

**D-IMPL-71 — Docs toolchain and publishing posture.** RESOLVED — **option (a), CI-artifact-only; no public publishing. 2026-08-01. Approver: Dave.** Surfacing context: Phase 14 (DOCS-02) commits to mkdocs-material + mkdocstrings; the hosting/publishing decision was explicitly deferred in REQUIREMENTS; the repo is private and stays private (CLAUDE.md §2.2 — the whitepaper is proprietary IP). Verified constraint (checked against GitHub's official documentation 2026-08-01, correcting RESEARCH assumption A2 from assumed to verified, with a sharpening): private Pages visibility ("access control") exists only on GitHub Enterprise Cloud and only for project sites published from a private/internal repository owned by an organization — `drcastillo/ccf_code` is a personal-account private repo on a non-Enterprise plan, so any GitHub Pages site published from it would be publicly readable regardless of the repo's visibility. Ruling: `make docs --strict` builds in CI and the rendered site uploads as an Actions artifact; nothing is hosted. The hosting question remains explicitly open as a future D-IMPL should the repo's visibility posture ever change — mkdocs output is host-agnostic, so a later hosting ruling starts from the same build. The mike-versioning option was declined as paying versioning complexity before any consumer exists, with the gh-pages hazard still loaded. Consumed by Phase 14. Originates: CLAUDE.md §2.2 (private repo, proprietary IP); DOCS-02/DOCS-06; `docs/09-ruling-batch-minicycle.md` item 4.

**D-IMPL-72 — Amendment of Phase-8 D-10 `make ci` composition: `make docs --strict` joins always-on CI.** RESOLVED — **option (a), rule now with deferred effectivity. 2026-08-01. Approver: Dave.** Namespace note: "Phase-8 D-10" is the Phase 8 context ruling, finalized in plan 08-12, that fixed the Makefile line `ci: ci-core examples` as the phase-gate command, command-identical to the always-on Actions pipeline — it is not Phase 9's CONTEXT D-10 (the WR-03 error-hierarchy decision). Per the new-number-not-amend discipline (D-IMPL-66 precedent), this amendment takes a new number; Phase-8 D-10's text stands as approved. Ruling: the always-on CI composition grows the strict docs build — `make ci` becomes `ci-core examples docs-strict` and ci.yml gains the corresponding job — **effective at the Phase 14 plan that creates the docs build target** (DOCS-06's "logged D-10 amendment"). Between now and then nothing changes on disk; the ruling pre-authorizes the wiring so Phase 14 planning proceeds against a ruled CI composition (the D-02 rulings-first posture), and no dead `docs` target lands early. Consequences accepted: a ruled-but-not-yet-wired composition exists until Phase 14, mitigated by DOCS-06 naming the receiving phase explicitly. Consumed by Phase 14. Originates: Phase-8 D-10 (08-12; cross-referenced, not edited); DOCS-06; `docs/09-ruling-batch-minicycle.md` item 5.

**D-IMPL-73 — `create_app` insecure-default warning mechanism and suppression surface.** RESOLVED — **option (c), warning category + log record, suppressed only by an explicit acknowledgment kwarg. 2026-08-01. Approver: Dave.** Surfacing context: audit review consensus — `create_app` (`runtime/ccf_runtime/http_api/app.py`) defaults to the unauthenticated posture (T-07-24 accepted residual; `AUTH_POSTURE_SENTENCE` served and test-asserted), but the posture is visible only to someone reading the API response or docstring; adopters need a loud construction-time signal. Ruling: when `create_app` is called with no auth hook, the SDK emits (1) a `warnings.warn` with a dedicated exported category (`CcfInsecureDefaultWarning`-shaped, a `UserWarning` subclass — treatable as an error via `-W error::…` in CI, testable with `pytest.warns`) and (2) a logging WARNING record at first request, covering the operator loop. The single suppression surface is an explicit acknowledgment kwarg on `create_app` (name per Phase 10's implementation, `allow_unauthenticated=True`-shaped, default `False`) — greppable intent visible in the caller's code and in review. No environment-variable suppression: env vars are invisible in review and encourage fleet-wide silencing. Boundary note: the warning is mechanism (a security-posture signal from the SDK), not a domain value — inline warning text is legitimate under the No-Hardcoding Rule, unlike prompts or policy values. Consequences accepted: two emission points to keep consistent; the examples bind `auth=` (their existing Phase-8 obligation) or acknowledge explicitly so `make examples` stays warning-clean and the D-14 byte-identity manifest holds. Consumed by Phase 10 (HARD-05). Originates: T-07-24; D-IMPL-62/67; CLAUDE.md §6.4/§11; `docs/09-ruling-batch-minicycle.md` item 6.

**D-IMPL-74 — `get_context`/`get_lineage`/`get_replay_record` promoted into the SDK Experience surface, default-ON.** RESOLVED — **promotion, enabled by default; 2026-08-02. Approver: Dave.** Surfacing context: the four-AI cross review of Phase 13.1 (`13.1-REVIEWS.md` consensus) raised the SDK-surface question of whether the three external-consumption tools the example registered deployment-side (`examples/external/00-hello-context/serve.py`) belong in the shipped front door. Ruling: the three tools move from deployment-side composition into `build_experience_server` (`runtime/ccf_runtime/mcp/experience.py`), registered **default-ON** — not opt-in — because external context consumption is the primary adoption path; the front door serves it out of the box. Mechanism discipline preserved: each tool resolves through an injected Protocol seam the deployment supplies (a context provider, a lineage provider, a replay-record reader — the `ExperienceProductResolverProtocol` shape), so the module gains no fabric, artifact, or graph handle and the role-separation/zero-privileged-paths source invariants stand unchanged (T-07-20, T-07-22). Per the CINV-01 design point from the phase RESEARCH (Open Question 3), the three tool description strings are **deployment-supplied builder parameters**, never module-authored prose — the same posture as `server_name`. Companion exposure control: the D-05 server-side tool allowlist lands as a keyword-only builder parameter — a tool absent from the allowlist is simply never registered, so `tools/list` cannot advertise it (registration-time omission; the `invoke_agent` live-mode hazard closes by configuration). `list_agents`/`invoke_agent` remain default-registered for backward compatibility with existing consumers. Consequences accepted: `build_experience_server`'s signature grows six required parameters plus the optional allowlist, and both test-tree callers update in-phase; the example's `serve.py` shrinks to composition (D-15, plan 02). Consumed by Phase 13.2 (EXAM-05). Originates: Dave's 2026-08-02 ruling (13.2-CONTEXT D-13/D-14); CLAUDE.md §7.1/§8.3/§15; D-IMPL-56 (shared derivation precedent); 13.1-REVIEWS.md Cursor HIGH.

**D-IMPL-75 — Phase 11 performance baseline is measured and committed inside Phase 14, unblocking DOCS-06.** RESOLVED — **run and commit a baseline; 2026-08-03. Approver: Dave.** Surfacing context: Phase 14 research (`14-RESEARCH.md`) established that DOCS-06's commitment — PERFORMANCE.md publishes the Phase 11 measured baselines with methodology and environment manifest — had no inputs: `perf/baselines/` contains only `.gitkeep`, `perf/results/` is gitignored, and D-IMPL-70 makes committing a baseline a deliberate Dave-approved act rather than something a planner or executor may do on its own authority. Three readings were surfaced: (a) run and commit a baseline now under a new D-IMPL; (b) ship methodology and environment manifest with an explicit "no baseline committed" statement, deferring numbers; (c) drop DOCS-06 from Phase 14 via a ROADMAP/REQUIREMENTS amendment. Ruling: **option (a)** — Phase 14 carries a measurement plan alongside its documentation plans. The Phase 11 battery runs on a named runner class, the resulting baseline is committed under D-IMPL-70's re-baseline ritual, and PERFORMANCE.md cites real measured numbers rather than a placeholder. The phase goal's "every number is measured" clause is a commitment, and satisfying it with a deferral notice would have been the papering-over CLAUDE.md §11 forbids. Consequences accepted: Phase 14's scope grows a measurement act whose output is Dave-gated — the produced numbers require sign-off before PERFORMANCE.md cites them, so the phase carries a human gate that documentation phases normally do not; and the committed baseline binds future perf-gating runs to that runner class per D-IMPL-70's runner-class-keyed design. Consumed by Phase 14 (DOCS-06). Originates: 14-RESEARCH.md open question 1; 14-CONTEXT.md D-19; D-IMPL-70 (Dave-gated re-baseline authority); CLAUDE.md §11, §15.

**Baseline recorded (D-IMPL-70 ritual step 3/5).** APPROVED — **2026-08-03. Approver: Dave. Approval reference: `Dave, 2026-08-03, phase-14 wave-2 gate`.** Runner class **`local`**; battery run at commit **`8aefb2ad18b17c0596d0124378c0d40548cfc623`**; baseline committed as `perf/baselines/local.json` (22 benchmark medians covering micro, scale, crossproc and replay). Measured wall times: micro 53.2 s, scale 201.4 s, crossproc 2.35 s, replay 65.2 s — total ≈5 min 22 s against the ~10 min D-17/D-25 budget, within a few percent of the 2026-08-02 reference table in `perf/README.md`. Review evidence presented before approval (ritual step 2): zero WARNs, zero gated failures, all 22 benchmarks PASS on the confirming gate pass, replay curve exponent `b = -4.08e-05` inside the documented-linear bound, and `tests/perf_gate` green — `gate.py` still has no write path to `perf/baselines/`. Contention caveat recorded and accepted: three executor agents were active on the 20-core host during the run, but load average stayed 0.86–2.24 and every suite matched the quiet reference, so the numbers were judged uncontended; Dave approved them **as measured**. Consequence accepted: `perf/baselines/local.json` now exists, so perf runs gate against it rather than printing the D-13 report-only notice, and future changes to these numbers require a fresh pass through this same ritual. Published by `public/reference/performance.md`, which includes the baseline file whole rather than transcribing any median. Companion in-phase fix authorized by the same ruling: `perf/gate.py` benchmark-ID drift detection is now scoped to the suite that ran, because the first committed whole-battery baseline made every single-suite run report the other suites' entries as drift.

**D-IMPL-142 — the three public names added inside the 17-11/-12/-13 pause are KEPT; waves 6–7 hold until the `phase-17` CI signal.** RESOLVED. Date: 2026-08-16. Approver: Dave. **The three names, each ruled keep with its stated reason.** (a) `~ccf.products.debt_scorer.DeclaredFactor` — *"mechanism that makes a declared factor auditable in the replay record; correctly introduced."* It exists because `~ccf.halflife.assessment.DebtFactors` records that the SDK ships a reference measurement for **F only**: `C` and `S` are irreducibly the deployment's, and `17-11` rejected each candidate library default in turn (`C = 1` is a number the SDK chose sitting inside a delivered product's content address; `C = 0` fail-closes every deployment; deriving `C` from the λ_c table's breadth couples completeness to how many observation types a deployment happens to calibrate). `DeclaredFactor` lets a deployment **state** an assumption that pins into Decision Replay under its own `scorer_id`/`scorer_version` per `D-IMPL-47`, so an examiner can tell an assertion from a measurement without reading code. (b) `~ccf.products.resolver.build_governed_resolver` — *"the construction helper is the right place for the defaults; better than putting defaults on the class itself."* Two measurements selected the helper over the class and neither is a matter of taste: the default is not a value but a **construction needing four further inputs** (a λ_c table, a debt profile, and a `DebtFactorScorerProtocol` for each of `C` and `S`), so defaulting on the class would have grown `~ccf.products.resolver.GovernedResolver` from thirteen constructor arguments to seventeen — four of them dead whenever an explicit scorer *is* supplied, and **a default that enlarges its own seam is not a default**; and a **shipped assertion already forbade it**, `tests/products/test_resolver_seam.py::test_no_constructor_argument_carries_a_default`, which reads `default` and `default_factory` off every dataclass field and requires both `MISSING`. Weakening a shipped assertion to admit a feature is this phase's signature defect, and doing it inside the plan whose subject is inert defaults would have been the defect wearing the fix. `GovernedResolver`'s claim — *"Every value this composition needs that the SDK cannot know arrives from outside it, and nothing has a default"* — survives verbatim and under test. The cost is real and paid down rather than glossed: there are now **two ways to obtain a resolver**, and a reader who finds only the class will not learn the SDK ships a scorer, so the class's `debt_scorer` attribute doc names the helper and the module docstring carries both measurements plus an instruction that a second default not be added without re-reading them. (c) `~ccf.products.resolver.DEBT_SCORER_INPUTS` — *"naming the required inputs is clearer than leaving them implicit."* **What the approval also ratifies, in Dave's words:** *"all three were claimed in surface-change fragments rather than shipped silently — that discipline is correct."* The fragments are `docs/surface-changes/17-11-reference-debt-scorer.md` and `docs/surface-changes/17-13-resolver-defaults.md`; the surface-manifest single-owner rule (`docs/surface-changes/README.md`) was followed rather than each plan regenerating `docs/surface/v0.2-surface.json`. **The generalization worth carrying, and it is the answer to the question the pause opened:** asked what defaulting can mean when two of three debt factors are irreducibly the deployment's, the resolved position is **the default is the machinery, never the values** — the deployment declares `D_c`'s *inputs* and the SDK supplies the `GraphView` → `DebtFactors` adapter, the decay computation, the row-selection choice and the replay pinning. `17-12`'s `debt:` block therefore survives intact, all four fields, because none of the four is something a default may supply. **The sequencing ruling.** Waves 6 and 7 are **unblocked by the 2026-08-16 spec's closure but HELD** pending a clear `phase-17` CI signal: *"starting the deletion/quarantine waves while we still don't know whether the always-on pipeline agrees with the local gate is the wrong order."* `17-08` and `17-09` are both `autonomous: false`, so they require Dave in the loop by construction. Local gate at the time of ruling: `make ci-core` **MAKE_EXIT=0** — 3751 passed / 0 failed / 118 skipped, 400 lint, 144 tools, measured on the main checkout at `f84d11c` with the exit code captured directly rather than through a pipe. `phase-17` pushed to origin at `5e6f55e` under this ruling so Actions runs against the tree containing all three plans; `main` untouched per CLAUDE.md §2.2. **Governs:** `~ccf.products.debt_scorer`; `~ccf.products.resolver`. **Depends on** `D-IMPL-141` (the data-only binding document these defaults serve), `D-IMPL-47` (the scorer surface a declared factor pins into replay under), `D-IMPL-38` (a default is a value nobody chose). **Related:** `D-IMPL-114` (citation form); CLAUDE.md §6.4, §11.

**D-IMPL-143 — the remote gate never ran the local gate's command; `ci.yml` now CALLS Makefile targets instead of restating them.** RESOLVED. Date: 2026-08-16. Approver: Dave. **The failure, measured.** GitHub Actions run `31967852661` on `phase-17` @ `7822344`: three of four jobs green (docs gate, lint + strict types, examples suite); the `tests (python 3.12)` job red at its first step, *"Quick suite (no external substrates)"*, with `13 errors during collection`, `144 deselected`, and `##[error]Process completed with exit code 2` — meaning that job **executed zero tests**, and the four substrate legs and the coverage step below it had never run at all. The errors were all one shape: `ModuleNotFoundError: No module named 'tests'` and `No module named 'tools'`. **The same failure, same job, same step, was recorded on 2026-08-10** (run `31452318023` @ `a328a1b9`) and carried in `.planning/STATE.md` as NOT YET DIAGNOSED with the note that it *"does NOT reproduce locally"* — a note that was true and misleading, because every local reproduction ran through `make`. **The root cause.** `.github/workflows/ci.yml` invoked pytest as a **bare console script**; every Makefile target invokes it as `$(PYTHON) -m pytest`. The `-m` form puts the working directory on `sys.path`; the console script does not. That interacts with package layout to select which suite dies: pytest walks up from a test file while `__init__.py` exists and inserts the first directory *without* one, so for `tools/lints/tests/` — where `tools/` **is** a package — the walk reaches the **repo root** and `from tools.lints import ...` resolves, keeping the lint job green; while for `tests/deployment/` — where `tests/deployment/` is a package but **`tests/` is not** — the walk stops at `tests/`, that directory is inserted rather than the root, and `from tests.deployment.conftest import ...` cannot resolve. Test modules import their siblings' fixtures and the surface tooling, so thirteen modules failed to collect. **Reproduced locally in 0.21 s** once the invocation rather than the environment was the suspect: `.venv/bin/pytest tests/deployment/test_manifest.py` exits 2 with the identical error, while both `PYTHONPATH=src:runtime .venv/bin/python -m pytest <same file>` and `PYTHONPATH=.:src:runtime .venv/bin/pytest <same file>` pass 39 tests. Six days of investigation had been aimed at service-container reachability and at `pip install -e` resolving different versions, because those are environment hypotheses and the difference was in how the command was spelled. **What made it invisible, and why the fix is structural rather than a flag.** `Makefile` lines 2–3 assert that *"Every target CI runs, a developer runs the same way; the Makefile mirrors `.github/workflows/ci.yml` command-for-command"*, and that invariant was **false for all seven pytest steps in the tests job**. The file even documents this exact hazard for the substrate legs — *"`$(ENV)` puts the working tree's src/ on the path. Without it a bare pytest resolves `ccf` through the editable install's hard-coded mapping to the primary checkout"* — so the knowledge existed in the repository and had simply never been applied to the workflow. **A mirror asserted in a comment is not a mirror.** Resolution: the workflow now **calls** the command rather than restating it. Three targets added — `ci-quick-suite`, `ci-harness-conformance`, `ci-coverage` — plus `ENV_TESTS := PYTHONPATH=.:src:runtime`, which states the repo root **explicitly** so the targets are correct under either invocation rather than depending on a property of the `-m` form, and `NO_SUBSTRATE`, so the marker expression that appears in two steps has one spelling. The four substrate legs were deliberately **not** given new targets: `leg-postgres`, `leg-neo4j`, `leg-mongo` and `leg-fluree` already existed, already used the `-m` form, already set their own `CCF_REQUIRE_*`, and their `?=` URI defaults match the values `ci.yml` places in the job env — where an environment value beats `?=`, so the two agree by construction. Adding parallel `ci-leg-*` targets would have created a second spelling of something that already had one correct spelling, which is the defect being fixed. CI passes `PYTHON=python` on each call because the runner has no `.venv`; a command-line assignment overrides the Makefile's `PYTHON :=` (verified with `make -n`). **What is NOT at risk, stated so it is not re-audited:** the two remaining bare-`pytest` invocations in `ci.yml` both target `tools/`, which is a package, so the basedir walk reaches the repo root and they resolve — this is why the lint and docs jobs were green throughout. **Consequences accepted.** The four substrate legs and the coverage step will execute for the first time on the next run and may surface failures that have been latent since they were written; a red leg after this change is new information, not a regression introduced by it. **Governs:** `.github/workflows/ci.yml` (the `test` job); `Makefile` (`ENV_TESTS`, `NO_SUBSTRATE`, `ci-quick-suite`, `ci-harness-conformance`, `ci-coverage`). **Related:** `D-09`/`D-10` (the single documented command surface this restores); `D-07` (a step made non-blocking to hide a gap is worse than the gap — the same principle applied to a step that silently ran nothing); `DEF-17-08` (`gate-tools` reachable from `ci` but not `ci-core` — the same defect class one layer in, and the fifth of what is now seven instances in Phase 17 of a check that stops checking without going red).

**D-IMPL-144 — citations into `docs/DECISIONS.md` use the stable decision ID; line-number anchors into that file are disallowed.** RESOLVED. Date: 2026-08-16. Approver: Dave. **What was observed.** Five citations in the tree anchored a decision reference by line — `docs/DECISIONS.md:290` for `D-IMPL-87` — and every one of them had been passing **by coincidence**. The decision log carries two entries bearing the `D-IMPL-87` token two lines apart (the 15.2 halt entry and its resolution, deliberately sharing one number so the halt-to-ruling chain reads as one thread), so each insertion at the end of the D-IMPL section slid *one of them* onto line 290: before `D-IMPL-142` the resolution sat there, after `D-IMPL-142` the halt entry did, and after `D-IMPL-143` neither did and three citations went red. The `cite02-anchor` rule was doing exactly what it is written to do — confirm the cited lines still hold a token matching the anchor — and that is not the same claim as *this citation points at the entry its author meant*. **Two of the five were invisible to the gate entirely**: `~ccf.projection.segmentation` and `~ccf.operators.base` carry the reference *unanchored* (no backticked code span immediately adjacent), so only `cite01-resolves` applied, which proves a line exists and nothing about its content — the blind spot `~tools.lints.citations`'s own module docstring names as "the largest by count". **Dave's ruling, in substance:** *"Line-number anchors into DECISIONS.md are not acceptable going forward. Every insertion shifts every subsequent line. The gate can only confirm that some line still contains the token; it cannot confirm it is the intended entry."* **The rule.** (1) A citation of a decision entry names the **stable ID** — `D-IMPL-87`, `D-GATE-15.1` — and the file, without a line number. (2) `file:line` forms into `docs/DECISIONS.md` are **disallowed for new citations**. (3) Existing ones migrate when touched; a bulk migration was explicitly not required to block on. **Why the ID is the durable target, and why this is not a weakening of `D-IMPL-114`.** `D-IMPL-114` prefers forms that do not rot silently, and `~tools.lints.citations`'s docstring records that the `::` symbol form "does not rot silently under a line shift" — but `cite03-node-id` resolves a Python `def`, `class` or module-level binding, so a Markdown target had **no durable form available** and line anchors were the only option on offer. The decision ID *is* that durable target: it is stable across every insertion, it survives the entry being moved or re-sectioned, and it is what a reader greps for. The trade accepted with eyes open: an ID citation is not machine-checked by any of the three rules, so it trades a check that was passing for the wrong reason for no check at all — and that is the better trade, because a gate that greens by coincidence is worse than an absent gate, which is at least honest about what it does not know. **Executed with this entry, because deferring it was self-defeating:** all five occurrences were migrated in the same commit. Adding *this* ruling to the decision log shifts the same line numbers again, so recording the rule while leaving the anchors in place would have broken them a fourth time — the entry forbidding the practice would have been the insertion that proved it. **Governs:** every citation of a decision entry in `src`, `runtime`, `tests`, `tools` and `docs`. **Migrated in this commit:** `~ccf.graph.protocols`, `~ccf.projection.engine`, `~ccf.projection.segmentation`, `~ccf.operators.base`, `tests/projection/test_segmentation.py`. **Related:** `D-IMPL-114` (citation form; this extends it to Markdown decision targets rather than amending it); `D-IMPL-143` (the insertion that exposed this, and the seventh Phase 17 instance of a check that reports green while verifying the wrong thing); `~tools.lints.citations` (the three rules and their documented blind spots).

**D-IMPL-145 — `17-09` runs before `17-08`: the replacement example must exist before the condemned ones are deleted; and the `00`–`08` half of `D-27` stands QUARANTINED.** RESOLVED. Date: 2026-08-16. Approver: Dave. Two rulings from `17-08`'s blocking checkpoint, both taken on measurements the executor produced by deleting, running and restoring rather than by reading. **(1) The sequence is inverted in the plan graph, and the graph loses.** `17-08` deletes `examples/s1-00-companies-over-revenue` and `examples/s1-01-hello-context-product`; `17-09` ships the replacement — `consume.py` beside an opaque `deployment/`, the artifact the phase exists to produce. `17-09`'s frontmatter declares `depends_on: ["17-07", "17-08"]`, so the planned order is **delete, then create**. That order cannot hold: deleting `s1-01` removes the **last shipped `examples/` call site of `~ccf.products.request.refuse_request`**, and `tools/docs_tests/test_resolver_seam_obligations.py` requires both resolver Protocol docstrings to cite an example path that resolves on disk. Measured: `2 failed, 13 passed`, *"cites example paths that are not on disk: ['examples/s1-01-hello-context-product/spine.py']"*. Independently confirmed by the orchestrator — `refuse_request` appears under `examples/` **only** in `s1-00` and `s1-01`, no surviving example calls it, and `examples/hw1-*` is not in the tree. The removal is also larger than `17-08`'s action text describes: **31 failed, 17 errors**, including seven fixture-subject tests in `tests/products/test_gate_two_call_shape.py` and disk-reading fixtures in `tests/products/test_request_contract.py`, `tests/replay/test_engine_rehydration.py` and `tests/examples/test_source_estate.py`, none of them named in the plan. **Ruling: re-order.** `17-09` executes first and `17-08` follows, so the gate never observes a tree with zero cited examples; the `depends_on` fields are corrected so the graph stops asserting an order the tree refuses. Rejected: *delete `s1-00` only* — it leaves `D-27` half-discharged, and `D-27`'s own text forbids exactly that (*"there is no partial state"*); and *rule the gate down to one citation* — weakening a shipped assertion to admit a change is the defect class this phase has produced nine times, and `17-13` refused the identical trade when it put the debt-scorer default in a helper rather than delete `tests/products/test_resolver_seam.py::test_no_constructor_argument_carries_a_default`. **What generalizes, and it is the reason this was invisible:** every frontmatter field involved is internally consistent — `17-08`'s `depends_on` is satisfied, `17-09` declares itself downstream, no field is wrong. The coupling exists only through a **gate**: a docstring citation that must resolve on disk at every instant, including the instant between a deletion and its replacement. **A plan graph tracks what plans say they need, never the invariants they must not break in transit**, so an ordering hazard of this shape is undetectable from frontmatter and is found only by executing. **(2) `00`–`08` and `external/00` stay QUARANTINED**, which is the second disposition `D-27` permits in its own words (*"removed **or quarantined**"*). Re-measured at ruling time, not carried from the plan: `command grep -rn "examples/0\|examples/external" public/ | wc -l` → **111** lines across **15** published pages, **102** of them snippet includes; `command grep -rn "s1-0" public/` → **0**. So removing the `s1` trees costs test modules, make targets and floor constants — all inside this phase — while removing `00`–`08` costs re-cutting ~102 snippet includes across 15 published pages, which is the stopped Phase 16 `DOCS-17` work against a surface Phase 16's remaining 14 plans were going to rewrite. Deleting them now leaves 15 published pages sourcing from nothing, with no replacement written, inside a phase whose ruled scope is *"the client role only"*. The trees therefore remain in place, remain scanned by every existing gate (so no floor moves on their account), and remain carried in `RETIREMENT_SCHEDULE` with named owners; plan `17-01`'s regenerated inventory is their complete coupling record. Rejected: *delete-now* (a documentation rewrite inside a client-surface phase, the largest single item in the phase by a wide margin) and *delete-and-drop-pages* (removes the published how-to and artifact-reference surface with no replacement — a documentation regression, not a retirement). **Two corrections to the checkpoint's own premises, recorded because both were asserted in planning and neither survived measurement:** (a) `s1-*` has zero exposure in `public/` **source**, but the **built site** carries `examples/s1-01-hello-context-product/spine.py` at `site/reference/protocols/runtime-surfaces/index.html` — the mkdocstrings rendering of the resolver docstrings — so published exposure exists downstream of the source scan and **no gate checks it**; (b) a naive `command grep -c "00.*08" docs/DECISIONS.md` guard reads **28** and is green *before any ruling exists*, so it cannot distinguish the ruled state from the unruled one — the tenth Phase 17 instance of a check that reports green while verifying the wrong thing, and the first caught before shipping rather than after. **Governs:** the `17-08`/`17-09` execution order; the disposition of `examples/00`–`08` and `examples/external/00-hello-context`. **Related:** `D-27`, `D-25`, `D-26`, `D-31` (`17-CONTEXT.md`); `D-IMPL-144` (citation form — this entry uses stable IDs throughout); `DEF-17-08` (a gate the working command does not reach).

**D-IMPL-146 — the `17-09` checkpoint rulings; Phase 17 closes on the client example, with `17-08` and `17-10` deferred out.** RESOLVED. Date: 2026-08-16. Approver: Dave. Five rulings from `17-09`'s blocking `human-verify` checkpoint, plus the phase-scope ruling that follows from them. **(1) sqlite, not Postgres.** The first example ships on sqlite. Measured: `~ccf.graph.postgres_write.secret_fields` is empty and `binding.yaml`'s `connection:` carries no `from_env` form, so a Postgres deployment cannot be authored without a DSN literal in the tree. Dave: *"Keeping a DSN literal or forcing Postgres would put credentials or extra machinery into the tree. sqlite is the lowest-friction substrate for 'first answer.' `from_env` support can be added later when a real Postgres deployment is needed; it is not required for this example."* No code change. **(2) and (5) — one cause, one fix.** Five gates failed because a **second** projecting `examples/` deployment now exists registering no `ccf.projection-requirements`, and `D-IMPL-137` pins that population at one *deliberately* so a human rules on each addition. Ruling: **teach the premise detector the post-`~ccf.products.resolver.build_governed_resolver` shape; do NOT raise the ceiling.** The decisive measurement, produced by the executor rather than reasoned: `tests/products/test_gate_two_call_shape.py::test_every_projecting_example_body_names_the_binder` demanded a `refuse_operator_set_divergence` call that property (1) **in the same module** refuses — because `required` now arrives as a **parameter** from the helper, so the binder would compare a value against itself; the real comparison happens at boot. The detector was written when a deployment computed `required` itself. As shipped: `_sdk_supplied_operator_set` is a **second premise-failure route** joined at the demand site, expressed as five *positive* conditions and never as an inference from absence, the load-bearing one being that `binding.yaml`'s single permitted `plan:` reference must name this module and function — the Control Plane stating **on disk** that the SDK reaches it. `BINDING_DOCUMENT_NAME` and `REFERENCE_KEYS` are imported from the SDK rather than respelled. **Both ceilings stay at one**: `MAXIMUM_ONE_DOCUMENT_DEPLOYMENTS` is untouched and the new route gets its own `MAXIMUM_SDK_SUPPLIED_DEPLOYMENTS = 1`, with `test_the_two_exemptions_are_disjoint` proving they bound two populations rather than one of two — without which two ceilings of one would silently admit two claimants. Nothing was narrowed to pass: `test_the_premise_detector_reads_the_shipped_tree_correctly` came out **stronger**, asserting set equality between unregistered roots and the union of the claimant lists so a `D-IMPL-137` row cannot appear quietly. The three retirement-sweep rows were re-derived after proving causality **both ways** — parking the example restores the `inline-tree-witness` row, restoring it dissolves the row. Zero-match behaviour was asked of every touched gate and all refuse: `per_row_ownership_problems([])` refuses rather than satisfying an empty `len(owners) >= 2`; `shape_accounting_problems` over an empty census returns three violations rather than satisfying every absence claim; `_sdk_supplied_operator_set` refuses a factory constructing no plan, because `all(...)` over an empty tuple is `True` and the explicit guard is what stops the exemption reaching every function the binding document names. Sabotage on the new detector: `return False` → `3 failed, 93 passed`; `return True` → `19 failed, 77 passed`; restored → `96 passed`. **(3) The one scheme-naming import is intended.** Dave: *"The scheme belongs in the binding/adapter layer, not in client code. One import that names the scheme inside the official adapter is the correct and expected cost of the design."* No change. **(4) `17-08` is deferred out of Phase 17 entirely** — both `s1` trees stay, no seam docstring is edited, `D-27` is discharged in a later phase alongside the `00`–`08` disposition `D-IMPL-145` quarantined. **(6) Phase 17 closes after `17-09`; `17-10` (the MCP door and adapter) moves out with `17-08`.** Dave's reasoning, recorded because it is a scoping principle and not a one-off: the phase was opened to deliver the `ccf.client` surface and `Result` contract, the in-process adapter, the conformance suite, and a clean first example — *"that set is now complete"*. MCP is a second transport carrying its own open questions (tool shape, entitlement, async bridging, stdio vs HTTP), and *"folding it into the same phase risks the accretion pattern we just spent the phase escaping. One solid transport + the full contract + the example is a coherent, shippable increment. The single decode path and the conformance suite already enforce the 'same shape' guarantee; a second adapter strengthens the proof but is not required to close the phase cleanly."* **THE RESOLVER-SEAM COLLISION, made concrete so a later phase inherits facts rather than a summary.** `tools/docs_tests/test_resolver_seam_obligations.py::test_both_seams_cite_both_call_sites` requires **at least two distinct example paths per seam**, and its docstring closes the cheap escape by design: *"Asserted across the pair rather than per-seam so that a docstring citing the same example twice cannot satisfy the per-seam check. Two refusals, two distinct examples."* Both seams — `~ccf_runtime.mcp.egress.ProductResolverProtocol` and `~ccf_runtime.mcp.experience.ExperienceProductResolverProtocol` — cite exactly `examples/04-cross-process/producer_child.py` and `examples/s1-01-hello-context-product/spine.py`. Measured tree-wide: `refuse_bound_payload`'s **only** `examples/` call site is `04-cross-process/producer_child.py`, which survives inside the quarantined `00`–`08` group; `refuse_request`'s only call sites are `s1-01/spine.py` and `s1-00/produce.py`, **both condemned**. So the blocker is narrower and harder than "the client example is client-only": **`refuse_request` would have no surviving demonstration anywhere in `examples/`**, and the honest remedy is to *write a producer-side example demonstrating request refusal* — not to relax an assertion, cite a test fixture (rejected by the gate's own design), or re-point a docstring at an example that does not demonstrate its claim. A consequence worth carrying: the `00`–`08` deletion, whenever it happens, carries the same hazard on the **other** refusal, because that group holds the sole `refuse_bound_payload` demonstration. **A KNOWN CONSEQUENCE OF `D-32`, recorded rather than fixed.** `D-32` forbids committing data to `examples/`, so `deployment/graph.sqlite3` and `deployment/artifact-store/` are gitignored and nothing in the repository populates one — `scenario.yaml` is a vocabulary mapping, not a seed, and the `example-hw1` target says *"needs a populated deployment; never CI"*. Verified on the main checkout: the headline command against a clean deployment prints `0 of 0 assembled`, `D_c 1.0000 → fail_closed`, and a different content address than a populated run. **The mechanism is honest** — that is `17-11`'s empty-assembly branch refusing to report a confident number about a graph that said nothing, carrying no scorer pin — but a reader following the README sees an empty answer. No test depends on a populated deployment (verified: the two new gates are static, and `464 passed` after deleting both generated paths), so CI is unaffected. Left for the producer example that also closes the `refuse_request` gap. **Governs:** `tests/products/test_gate_two_call_shape.py`; `tools/docs_tests/test_retirement_sweep.py`; `examples/hw1-companies-over-revenue/`; the Phase 17 scope boundary. **Related:** `D-IMPL-137` (the exemption ceiling this preserves), `D-IMPL-141` (the data-only binding document whose shape the detector now recognises), `D-IMPL-145` (the re-order that produced this checkpoint, and the `00`–`08` quarantine), `D-32`, `D-27`.

Scope approved as presented: the Agent Specialization Framework per CLAUDE.md §6 — archetype/agent separation (six archetypes as SDK mechanism in `/src`, zero agents in library code), the Agent & Task Factory as the exclusive construction path, all six channel-1 specialization axes bound in versioned definition artifacts, and channel-2 skilling with catalog registration/discovery, the tier-1 policy-compatibility check, the tier-2 ApprovalSink seam, and runtime grant/revoke — with the skill-set in force at decision time captured into Decision Replay dimension 4 by construction, per-agent (D-07/CR-01).
