Triage disposition: `05-REVIEW.md` raised 0 critical, 5 warning, 6 info — **all 11 closed or deliberately dispositioned before presentation**, across two `--fix` iterations (`e1a3bc4`, `9b93763`, `dad888e`, `50d3a08`, `8d38e6a`; `46bef65`, `278560b`, `e797433`, `6737bac`, `5148f18`, `d450d64`), with `05-REVIEW-FIX.md` and `05-REVIEW-FIX-2.md` recording each. IN-06 (`local_callable` artifact-driven code execution) is dispositioned as an accepted risk carried to the I.3 threat model, not a code change — its T-05-18 trust posture is a precondition on the input that I.3 invalidates once `callable_ref` can arrive via MCP/A2A. **Withdrawn deferral, recorded because it corrects this log's own premises:** WR-05 (grant state volatile while grant artifacts are durable) was initially deferred to I.4 on the stated grounds that closing it would preempt D-IMPL-18 and D-IMPL-27. Both were RESOLVED 2026-07-25; the deferral rested on a false reading of this decision log. D-IMPL-27's mutability split and D-IMPL-18's "the Control Plane's storage seam — pluggable there, no separate workflow-store Protocol" together *dictate* the placement, so the fix was implemented in-phase (`fba5b2a`): grant state moved onto a `ControlPlaneStateStore` Protocol with an `InMemoryStateStore` reference impl (`src/ccf/controlplane/state.py`), injected via `ControlPlaneRegistry.state_store`. Consequence for replay: every ledger over one Control Plane now agrees on the effective set and the per-agent `grant_set_hash`, so a skill-set identity is reproducible on a freshly constructed ledger — which is what CLAUDE.md §6.2 requires of replay and what instance-cached state could not provide. I.4's suspending ApprovalGate inherits this seam rather than a re-opened question. Independent verification of the two changes most deserving scrutiny: the new state seam weakens no named invariant (import surface is `typing` alone — single-writer untouched; CINV-01 holds, records opaque; all eight `_put_state` sites audit-paired), and IN-01's widening of `ALLOWED_CCF_PREFIXES` to admit `ccf.controlplane.resolution` was confirmed closed under the pre-existing allowlist by transitive-closure analysis, so AGNT-01 is intact. Advisories carried forward per §3.1: ADV-01 fixed in-phase (`1bfe7de` — the state seam claimed CINV-03 in its docstring without the `__ccf_mode__` tag, so the lint silently skipped it); ADV-02 (audit pairing is paired-call discipline rather than a structurally unrepresentable violation) and ADV-03 (`test_consumption_discipline` is a direct-import check; `ccf.replay.record` transitively reaches fabric modules for five-dimension typing) are pre-existing, non-regressive, and carried to I.3. **D-GATE-I.3** — Phase I.3 (Cross-Process & Harness Adapters) approved. Date: 2026-07-27. Approver: Dave. Scope approved as presented in `docs/PHASE_I3_GATE.md`: bidirectional MCP over the edges I.3 delivers (L1 client ingress normalizing to the L1 envelope, L4/L5 decision-time content acquisition, L6 server egress exposing Context Products as MCP Resources, and the ArtifactStore MCP role in a module distinct from egress — the Experience-surface edge is a named Phase 7 deferral, not a claim of coverage); Peering federation exchanging typed Context Products across processes behind `PeeringProtocol` over two interchangeable transports (typed-HTTP and the D-IMPL-14 default LF A2A) with detached-JWS identity and Control Plane admission ahead of L2; `HarnessAdapterProtocol` plus all three D-IMPL-24 reference adapters (Claude Agent SDK, LangGraph, Google ADK) binding four seams with CINV-02 green and re-verified by relocation; the single-writer invariant demonstrated at three *separate* boundary observables (Peering, adapter, ArtifactStore) rather than as one generalized claim; and the MCP-backed ArtifactStore reference implementation passing the same factory-parametrized conformance suite as the file-backed dev impl. Verification matrix (06-23 post-closure sweep, re-run per checklist row rather than re-read): **987 passed / 2 skipped** with `CCF_REQUIRE_HARNESSES=1`, 193 conformance, `mypy --strict` clean over 99 source files, `tools.lints --all` exit 0, no deletions against either `pre-peering` or `main...HEAD`, 16 recorded renames. The 2 skips are the tracked CINV-04 Phase I.4 deferrals (HITL action capture, workflow trace capture), each naming its unblocking phase in its own skip reason. **Gap-closure wave (plans 06-18..06-25, six waves, all merged before this gate):** blocking findings B1, B2, B3 and advisories A1, A2, A4, A5 closed; three decisions ruled and realized — D-IMPL-57 (reading (a): signature accountability is the federated form of INV-CH7-47, so the federated sequence checks attribution via `GATE_LINEAGE_ATTRIBUTABILITY` and the vacuous "lineage-strict-equality" comparison and its `engine_resolved` witness are gone), D-IMPL-58 (reading (d): the merged federated lineage persists as an immutable `ccf.admission-record` `AdmissionRecord` at the admission boundary, resolvable at an exact version through the contract-parametrized `resolve_artifact`/`resolve_artifact_at_uri` path), and D-IMPL-59 (the federation seam is the abstraction `Peering` with A2A as one transport — `ccf.a2a` → `ccf.peering`, `WireProtocol` → `PeeringProtocol`, `wire.py` → `protocol.py`, transports under `ccf_runtime.peering`, tests under `tests/peering/`, gate id values `peering-*`, the `a2a` skill-type token retired, plus part 4 pinned as a property: a Peering declares trust inputs and never the verdict). Each closure was proved load-bearing by observed counterfactual rather than by assertion — reverting 06-18's rewire admits the unattributable product (`3 failed, 3 passed`); reverting 06-19's store write fails 5 of 14 tests; neutering 06-20's adversarial `capture_effect` produces `DID NOT RAISE`; neutering `AdmittedProduct`'s token check produces `DID NOT RAISE AdmissionBypassError`. **Advisories carried forward as OPEN with their consequences stated in the gate document, not softened:** **A3** — one of three seam-4 legs remains a fixture echo (the Claude adapter's `execution()` is a hand-built `ResultMessage`); **A6** — `SkillCatalogEntry` declares no tool description or argument schema, now with a designated closure in D-IMPL-56 (RESOLVED 2026-07-27, implemented in I.4); **A7** — carried per the 10-row disposition table in `06-REVIEWS.md`. **Narrowings ruled acceptable rather than treated as defects, and required to be visible in the documents themselves:** loop-level harness portability is *not* claimed — all three shipped adapters' `capture_effect` are delegations to `Agent.emit_effect`, so the honest three-leg emission equality is structurally guaranteed rather than three implementations agreeing; what is demonstrated is seam-level equivalence with a falsifiable failure mode on every compared axis, and execution under a harness reasoning loop is `examples/03-harness-portability`'s Phase 8 obligation per CLAUDE.md §9. ROADMAP criteria 1–3 and `docs/PHASE_I3_GATE.md` were reconciled against the code in 06-22, each narrowing carrying its closure attribution inline. **Planning-quality finding recorded against the planner, not the executors:** five of the six gap-closure plans carried a path, symbol, or criterion defect, clustered into a few repeated wrong beliefs — a nonexistent `tests/products` (real: `tests/gates/test_product_gates.py`), the same impossible placement in `src/ccf/controlplane/artifacts.py` in three separate plans (a real `products.contract → controlplane.registry → controlplane.artifacts` cycle), a nonexistent `BINDING_BY_TYPE` symbol (real: `_BINDING_CONTRACTS`), and two `grep -c … == 0` criteria unsatisfiable against their own mandates. Every one was resolved by preserving the property and recording a deviation rather than bending code to satisfy a counter; the pattern is carried into I.4 planning as a process input. Deliverables: `src/ccf/peering/`, `src/ccf/mcp/`, `src/ccf/harness/`, `runtime/ccf_runtime/{peering,mcp,adapters/{claude_agent,langgraph,adk}}/`, `tests/{peering,crossproc,conformance,invariants,gates}/`, `tools/lints/`, `docs/PHASE_I3_GATE.md` (710 lines), `06-VALIDATION.md` (311 lines), `06-REVIEWS.md` (139 lines, 10-row disposition table), 25/25 plan SUMMARYs. **D-GATE-I** — Phase I (Implementation) approved: the v0 declaration. Date: 2026-07-31. Approver: Dave. This is the Phase I gate closing the whole implementation phase (I.1–I.5) per CLAUDE.md §3.1, presented in `docs/PHASE_I5_GATE.md` and approved as presented. Phase 8 (I.5) scope delivered on `phase-i5`: the complete eight-example v0 adoption suite (`examples/00-quickstart` banking L1→L6 slice through `examples/07-artifact-store-mcp` store-swap pluggability), each a thin asserted driver beside its definition artifacts per the No-Hardcoding Rule, spanning seven distinct domains per D-IMPL-52 (banking, field-service, clinical-trials ops, e-commerce, property-intelligence per D-08 with zero Homecams code, energy/grid ops, logistics), D-09 README/artifacts contract 8/8; example 03's harness-neutrality proof — one agent definition executed unmodified under real Claude Agent SDK, Google ADK, and LangGraph loop machinery with byte-level deterministic equivalence and D-04 structural equivalence on the live legs (Ollama keyless default, Anthropic key-gated), discharging advisory A3 with a genuinely SDK-parsed `ResultMessage` through the Claude adapter's seam 4; the hardening wave discharging the D-GATE-I.4.1 carried obligations (D-15 `ccf hitl decide` removed tree-wide, one verb `submit`; Codex fail-closed `isinstance(grant, TokenGrant)` branch; typed refusal `error_code` constants; D-16 signed-binding requiredness as a Control Plane value enforced at the transport authority path and mapped to shared `ERROR_SIGNED_BINDING_*` constants at both HTTP and MCP doors; R1 was-called derivation assertions; ADK `SequentialAgent` migration at the 2.5.0 pin; examples 05/06 binding `auth=BearerTokenAuth(...)` and demonstrating the hardened HITL surface end-to-end and in replay); CI wiring per D-10/D-11 (always-on `examples` job with `CCF_REQUIRE_HARNESSES=1`, never continue-on-error; opt-in `live-legs.yml`); and v0 packaging per D-17 (top-level README, pyproject `0.1.0`). Verification matrix (`make ci`, 2026-07-31, exit 0, re-run after review fixes): **1339 passed / 0 skipped / 0 failed** under `CCF_REQUIRE_HARNESSES=1`, `mypy --strict` clean over 156 source files, `tools.lints --all` exit 0, all eight examples exit 0. §3.1 Phase I verification list covered row-by-row in the gate §2 (No-Hardcoding lint over `/src`+`/runtime`+`/examples`, harness-import lint, `@algorithmic` lint, composition-preservation over the shipped operator pairs, replay-by-construction incl. both flipped CINV-04 deferrals, full §9 suite incl. example 03's portability assertion) plus the whitepaper-fidelity spot check (gate §3: six public surfaces sampled, every cited section specifies the implemented contract, no fidelity defect). Cross-AI review (Cursor full-tree; Codex inline after its sandbox failure, per the I.4 precedent) triaged in the gate §7: one blocking finding (X-1, composition-coverage wording overclaim) verified and fixed in-phase; advisories fixed (D-22 row scoping, `make ci`-vs-Actions precision) or dispositioned (C-4 rejected with rationale — the README tracks CLAUDE.md §1's own mission wording; X-4 carried); zero unfixed blocking findings, no new D-IMPL required. A post-execution 4-AI review is archived in `08-REVIEWS.md`. Standing residuals carry as recorded (T-07-24 default HTTP posture with the optional auth hook bound in the examples; unaudited state reads; google-adk deprecation watch at the next pin bump). Post-v0 (unchanged): Homecams-on-SDK rebuild, PyPI publishing, perf baselines, replay-explorer/graph-export CLI ideas, mobile/chat/web UX. On approval: `phase-i5` merges to `main --no-ff` referencing this entry, and **`v0.1.0` is tagged on the merge commit on `main`** — the D-17 v0 declaration. Push authorization: granted 2026-07-31 (main, phase-i5, v0.1.0 to origin) so the Actions pipeline produces the EXAM-09 observed evidence.

**D-GATE-15** — Phase 15 (SDK Ergonomics — The Integration Surface) approved. Date: 2026-08-05. Approver: Dave. Scope approved as presented in `docs/SURFACE-AUDIT.md` and `docs/SURFACE-CHANGES.md`: the SDK now absorbs the mechanical marshalling-and-wiring an adopter previously wrote by hand, without hiding one governance decision from adopter code or one dimension from the replay record. Delivered: the `ccf.deployment` package (`load_and_boot`, `build_operator_registry`, the D-IMPL-80 role-carrying manifest); declarative artifact classes reachable through one `parse_artifact` seam, replacing per-example hand marshalling; committed JSON Schemas emitted by discovery rather than by list (`tools/dump_schemas.py`'s `unaddressable_classes()` third bucket, which found five schema-less classes where 15-17 had reported two); CINV-01 widened to the `tests/` tree per D-IMPL-78 with **zero allowlist entries added** — every false positive closed by rule refinement, per Dave's explicit choice; all ten examples rewritten onto the streamlined surface with their artifacts reshaped to one-class-one-document and `$schema` headers tree-wide; and the phase-close instrumentation — a curated `docs/SURFACE-CHANGES.md` assembled from 21 per-plan fragments, a **generated** public-surface diff whose completeness is asserted bidirectionally at fully-qualified-name granularity with change-type agreement, and an anti-gaming clause asserting that `Public surface: false` entries are *absent* from the diff so the classification cannot conceal a real public-API change. **Criterion 1 measurement (D-12: removal and relocation are two figures, never summed):** mechanical wiring 1425 → 134 lines (−1291, 90.6%); assertions/prints relocated 737 → 152 (−585); raw 11988 → 11312. The day-1 file `examples/00-quickstart/run.py` — the file the phase opened against — is 451 → 350 raw with mechanical 143 → 13 and all 40 assertion/print lines relocated; its imports fell 54 → 36, and tree-wide 974 → 820. The 134 residual mechanical lines are the 13-line registration cluster each driver keeps (author-versioned classes holding their D-05 bump check, the MaxTTL/product-class pair that is the D-15A line, and two SDK classes a manifest cannot address) — the abstraction line made visible on the page rather than residue. **Verification matrix (`main`, post-merge, 2026-08-05):** 1999 passed / 2 skipped (from a 1479 phase-start baseline), `mypy --strict` clean over 169 source files, `tools.lints --all` exit 0, all ten examples exit 0, `make docs-strict` exit 0, `make schema-headers` tree-wide exit 0, `tools/dump_surface.py --check` exit 0 — **bytes-equality restored for the first time since 15-01 froze the baseline** — and `tools/assemble_surface_changes.py --check` exit 0. **Review and triage (§3.1):** `/gsd-review --phase "Phase 15" --all` run against the deliverables rather than the plan text, with `claude` excluded for independence — codex not-ready (2 blocking, 2 advisory), cursor ready (0 blocking, 6 advisory), gemini ready (0 blocking, 2 advisory). Both blocking findings closed in-phase before presentation: **B-1**, the G-B guard refused `.keys()` off a named receiver but not off a local alias while its docstring claimed AST matching defeated rename-style bypasses — closed with fixed-point alias tracking over assignments, walrus and annotated assignments, four new probes, and an explicit written statement of what receiver aliasing does *not* buy (a registry arriving via a helper's return value remains out of reach; the claim is now "every spelling the phase found plus the obvious evasions", not "impossible to write"); and **B-2**, the ERGO-16 "every" gap, closed as far as an executor may by accounting for all 152 residual lines by category in `SURFACE-AUDIT.md` §2.3 and carrying the *reading* to this gate rather than resolving it — two reviewers did not raise it, which does not make it wrong, and §3.1 classes an unsurfaced decision as blocking. Three advisories were fixed immediately (the schema-header gate used a `>= 9` floor rather than the exact ten-directory set; the baseline provenance check was described more strongly than it is; "the declared side of Gate 2" was imprecise — `required_operator_keys` is *upstream* of Gate 2's two arguments). Eight advisories carried forward, six to Phase 16. **Three rulings issued at this gate:** **(1) ERGO-16's "every" takes the qualified reading and the requirement is met as written** — "every example *driver*'s assertions and print summaries relocate, except where relocation would move an assertion somewhere nothing runs it or would break a test the plan was forbidden to touch." The 152 residual lines are exactly that principled set: 65 in files that are not example drivers (including the three client legs whose zero CCF imports ERGO-15 and EXAM-04 rest on, asserted byte-unchanged by empty `git diff`), 54 inside example 03's opt-in `_live` leg excluded from `make ci` by D-11 (where 15-15 enforced the protected property in the form that survives — `test_no_assertion_survives_on_the_path_ci_actually_runs` parses `run.py` and refuses any `assert` outside `_live`), 22 pinned by `tests/examples/test_ext00_hardening.py`'s `PASS 1:`–`PASS 8:` subprocess assertions in a module 15-20 was forbidden to modify, 4 `READY <port>` process-protocol handshakes that are neither assertion nor summary, and 6 in `08-e2e-demo` where DEMO-03's byte-compared staged transcript *is* the deliverable. No exception entry is required; `SURFACE-AUDIT.md` §2.3 is the accounting of record. **(2) `docs/SURFACE-CHANGES.md` does not publish in v0.2's site** — it stays in `docs/`, unpublished, and Phase 16 `git mv`s it into `public/` when it rewrites the documentation against the new surface. This follows Phase 14's `public/`-as-published-tree precedent with default-deny-by-existence, and avoids publishing a migration log alongside documentation that still describes the pre-15 surface (carried advisory A-3). **(3) The deferred manifest-content-hash idea becomes D-IMPL-81** for a Phase D mini-cycle rather than being dropped or left in a SUMMARY — see that entry. **Standing residuals recorded rather than softened:** A-5, no automated check exists for a *mis-pointed* `public/` include (`docs-strict` catches only orphaned ones) — closed by practice this phase, carried as a gap to Phase 16, which rewrites the prose anyway; A-6, stale PLAN.md text is carried **deliberately** — a PLAN.md records what was *intended* and a SUMMARY records what was *built*, so rewriting the former to match the latter would destroy the audit trail the discrepancies live in, and the correct fix is the convention that a phase's auditor reads SUMMARYs, which `SURFACE-AUDIT.md` now states; A-4, several `tests/examples/test_*_output.py` checks compare `required_operator_keys` against `operators_invoked` and honestly document that editing the artifact alone moves both ends — Phase 16 must not promote those as independence proofs, the independent statements being the consumer-side ones from 15-18 and the artifact-vs-engine comparison. **The phase's strongest finding, beyond ergonomics:** eight numbered instances of "a check that passes for a reason unrelated to what it claims to verify", ledgered one row per discovery in `SURFACE-AUDIT.md` §4.1. Two are outright correctness defects found independently in the same wave by plans that never communicated — 15-17, where lineage strict equality compared a value against itself both in the original decision *and* in the replay that exists to prove that decision; and 15-18, where the consumer's A2A entitlement assertion compared two reads of the *producer's own file*, in the example demonstrating admission verification. Neither was visible until typed artifacts gave the declared side an authored source, and no line-count measurement would have surfaced either. Recorded discrepancy: the phase handoff said seven and the orchestrator's brief said eight-with-15-20-contributing; the record says eight, with 15-19 numbering its own finding the eighth and 15-20's SUMMARY explicitly *declining* a ninth on the grounds that its two Gate 2 tautologies are a second site of a known finding rather than a new discovery. Deliverables: `src/ccf/deployment/`, `docs/SURFACE-AUDIT.md`, `docs/SURFACE-CHANGES.md`, `docs/surface-changes/` (21 fragments), `docs/surface/` (baseline, live manifest, per-example before/after), `tools/{dump_surface,assemble_surface_changes,measure_examples,dump_schemas,check_schema_headers}.py`, `tests/{test_surface_drift,test_surface_completeness}.py`, the three tree-wide standing gates (`tests/examples/test_artifact_schema_headers.py`, `tests/deployment/test_direct_construction.py`, `tests/products/test_required_operators_guard.py`), all ten rewritten examples with reshaped artifacts, 21/21 plan SUMMARYs. Phase 16 (Documentation Rewrite) may begin.


**D-IMPL-76 — how the operator-parameters artifact supplies `permitted_policy_bundle_versions`.** RESOLVED — **option (b), the adopter passes the pin set. 2026-08-04. Approver: Dave.** Surfacing context: Phase 15 research (`15-RESEARCH.md` Q1) found that `PolicyFilterParameters.permitted_policy_bundle_versions` (`params.py:59-61`) is declared in no artifact anywhere — it is `frozenset({policy_version})` at `examples/00-quickstart/run.py:238-240`, where `policy_version` is the *return value* of `registry.register(...)` (`run.py:205-207`), a content hash minted at registration time. A typed `ccf.operator-parameters.policy` artifact class (ERGO-08) therefore cannot declare it, and the phase must choose where it comes from. Ruling: the D-07 view-aware operator-registry builder takes the pin set — ERGO-10's returned typed record — as an argument, and the adopter passes it. Rejected: (a) the artifact declaring `permitted_policy_bundle_keys` with the builder resolving each key's registered version, which is more ergonomic but moves the builder one step toward resolving admission from the registry; (c) leaving the policy category as a `DeclaredArtifact` while the other three get typed classes, which is the least consistent surface and the hardest to document in Phase 16. Rationale: (b) keeps the pin visible in adopter code exactly where D-06 already puts every other pin, which makes it the consistent answer rather than a special case, at a cost of one adopter line. D-15A analysis (recorded, since the call was close): `PolicyFilter._admits` does compare two sources — `record.policy_bundle_version` stamped at assembly against `params.permitted_policy_bundle_versions` — but that is an *operator predicate* whose whole job is the comparison, not a validation gate existing to catch a producer misdeclaring; it is not the `run_gates` situation, so D-15A would have permitted (a). The ruling is the conservative side of a permitted choice. Consumed by Phase 15 (ERGO-08, ERGO-11). Originates: CLAUDE.md §15; ROADMAP Phase 15 ruling D-15A; `15-RESEARCH.md` §5.5 (G-A), §9.2, Q1.

**D-IMPL-77 — `EntityResolutionSpec` semver placement.** RESOLVED — **add `semver` in place, in its own plan with its own regeneration checkpoint. 2026-08-04. Approver: Dave.** Surfacing context: Phase 15 research (`15-RESEARCH.md` Q2) found `EntityResolutionSpec` (`worker.py:105`) is a `CCFContract` with no `semver` field, while SDK-owned artifact classes take their semver lane from `_VersionedArtifact` (`artifacts.py:134-142`, where `semver` has no default). The missing field is one of exactly three blockers preventing `EntityResolutionSpec` from reaching through the existing `parse_artifact` seam — a `ccf.resolution-spec` class is the phase's highest-duplication target (180 lines across 9 sites, and the spec's attribute names already match its YAML keys exactly, making the hand-marshaller dead weight). Ruling: `EntityResolutionSpec` inherits `_VersionedArtifact` directly. Rejected: a wrapper class holding `semver` plus the spec, because `boot()` would then receive a wrapper where it expects a spec (`boot.py` raises `TypeError: the resolution-spec artifact is not an EntityResolutionSpec`), changing `boot()`'s signature — the one contract D-05 committed to preserving; the wrapper trades a data change for a contract change. Consequences accepted and to be sequenced deliberately: adding the field changes the spec's **content hash**, therefore every `resolution_spec_version` pin, therefore every Decision Replay record carrying one. This is the phase's largest blast radius (`15-RESEARCH.md` §7.4), so ERGO-07 executes early, in its own plan, with its own regeneration checkpoint, so the hash churn is never confused with a rewrite defect. Consumed by Phase 15 (ERGO-07). Originates: CLAUDE.md §15; `15-RESEARCH.md` §2.3, §3.1, §7.4, §9.2, Q2.

**D-IMPL-78 — CINV-01 (No-Hardcoding) lint scope over `tests/`.** RESOLVED — **option (a), add `tests` to `RULE_TREE_SCOPE["no-hardcoding"]`. 2026-08-04. Approver: Dave.** Surfacing context: Phase 15 research (`15-RESEARCH.md` §5.6) found `tools/lints/__main__.py:36-51` scopes CINV-01 to `("src", "runtime", "examples")` — **`tests/` is not a linted tree**. Phase 15's D-09 relocates example CI assertions and print summaries into `tests/`, and those assertions carry domain expectations (e.g. `controlplane.yaml:92-99`'s `expected:` block, read at `run.py:364-372`). Left unaddressed, relocating them would move domain values *out of the No-Hardcoding Rule's reach while every CI gate stayed green* — a real, CI-invisible weakening of a structural invariant, arrived at as a side effect of an ergonomics change. Ruling: widen the lint's tree scope to include `tests`. Rejected: (b) a permanent guard test asserting the relocated modules contain no domain literals, which has a contained blast radius but enforces a convention in one place rather than structurally, so a future test module outside its reach reopens the hole. Standing obligation regardless of mechanism: the relocated test modules read the same `expected:` artifact blocks the examples read today; they do not inline the values. Execution note: assumption A6 of the research (widening the scope will not surface a large volume of pre-existing findings across the current 1,548-test suite) is **cheap to falsify and must be falsified before the change is committed** — the planner runs the widened lint first. If the volume proves unreasonable, that is a new surfacing, not a silent fallback to (b). Consumed by Phase 15 (ERGO-16, and the D-09 relocation across ERGO-13/14/15). Originates: CLAUDE.md §6.4, §11, §15; `15-RESEARCH.md` §5.6, §9.2, §9.3 item 3.

**D-IMPL-79 — how `ccf schema export` (D-04) reaches schema generation without breaking the CLI's no-fabric-handle invariant.** RESOLVED — **add an HTTP API route behind it; the CLI noun stays a `RequestSpec` like every other. 2026-08-04. Approver: Dave.** Surfacing context: Phase 15 pattern mapping (`15-PATTERNS.md`) found that `runtime/ccf_runtime/cli/main.py:1-6` states the CLI's posture explicitly — "every command translates to requests against the HTTP API routes; the CLI holds no fabric handle" — and `main()` dispatches unconditionally through `args.build(args)` into httpx, with all eight existing nouns (agent/skill/config/replay/product/workflow/artifact/hitl) returning `RequestSpec`s. D-04's `ccf schema export` is in-process JSON Schema generation with no route behind it, so as worded it would require `main()`'s first non-`RequestSpec` branch. Per CLAUDE.md §15 this is an SDK-extension ambiguity, surfaced rather than defaulted. Ruling: a new HTTP API route serves schema export, and the CLI noun is wired to it as a `RequestSpec` exactly like the other eight. Rejected: (b) dropping the CLI noun in favour of a `tools/dump_schemas.py` + `make schemas` target mirroring Phase 14's OpenAPI triad — closest to the established analog, but deviates from D-04 as ruled; (c) allowing `main()` its first non-`RequestSpec` branch, which weakens the documented posture to "every command except schema export" and leaves Phase 16 documenting a rule with a carve-out — the first exception is the one that makes the second easy. Consequences accepted: a new public HTTP surface for what is substantially a build-time developer utility, and the fabric must be reachable to export a schema. Note that the D-04 **committed-schemas + regenerator + CI freshness gate** triad is unaffected by this ruling and still follows Phase 14's `tools/dump_openapi.py` / `Makefile:168-169` / bytes-equality-test pattern, which `15-PATTERNS.md` established is fully reusable; only the *interactive* export path moves behind a route. Consumed by Phase 15 (ERGO-12). Originates: CLAUDE.md §15; CONTEXT D-04; `15-PATTERNS.md` finding 2; `runtime/ccf_runtime/cli/main.py:1-6`.

**D-IMPL-80 — whether the deployment manifest registers every artifact it names, and how non-Control-Plane artifacts reach the caller.** RESOLVED — **manifest entries carry an explicit role: `register` (Control Plane) or `load` (parsed, validated, returned, never registered). 2026-08-04. Approver: Dave.** Surfacing context: Phase 15 cross-AI review (`15-REVIEWS.md`, finding C-5, raised by Cursor and corroborated in weaker form by Codex) found that `15-10` Task 2 step 3 calls `register_typed` for *every* manifest entry, with Task 3's fixture including a signal batch. Today's quickstart registers only the resolution spec, policy bundle, half-life table, and debt profile, and then *ingests* envelopes through L1 — signals are data plane, not Control Plane. Registering a signal batch content-hashes raw event data into Control Plane state, which by D-06's own rationale (the artifact set in force *is* Decision Replay dimension 2) would place raw signals in the replay pin set: re-authoring an events file would then change "what was in force" in exactly the way a policy-bundle bump does, and adopters would have to deliberately *exclude* those pins when calling `build_replay_record`. That is a silent expansion of a replay dimension arrived at as a side effect of an ergonomics change, which D-15A routes to a ruling rather than a plan-level pick. Ruling: every manifest entry declares a role. `register` entries go into the Control Plane registry and the returned pin set, exactly as today. `load` entries are parsed and validated through the same `parse_artifact` seam and returned to the caller on the boot result, never registered and never pinned. `BootedFabric` (or its successor type) gains a field carrying the loaded-but-unregistered artifacts — which additionally closes review finding C-8's gap, where the day-1 path after `load_and_boot` had no specified means of obtaining envelopes for ingest. Rejected: (b) registering everything and accepting signals in the pin set, which is the defect as found; (c) letting the SDK infer the side from the artifact class, which makes the rule implicit in SDK code rather than visible in the adopter's manifest and leaves a deployment-authored `DeclaredArtifact` with no obvious side; (d) restricting the manifest to Control Plane artifacts only, which is the sharpest conceptual boundary but forfeits absorbing the data-plane loading the phase exists to absorb. Consequences accepted: one additional concept in the manifest schema, and the role must be declared per entry rather than defaulted — a manifest entry with no role is refused, so the choice is never made silently. Consumed by Phase 15 (ERGO-10, and the ERGO-09 signal-batch loader). Originates: CLAUDE.md §15; ROADMAP Phase 15 ruling D-15A; D-IMPL-06; `15-REVIEWS.md` findings C-5 and C-8.

**D-IMPL-80 (amendment) — `BootedFabric.artifacts` exposes both roles; `role` governs registration and pinning, never visibility.** RESOLVED — **keep the extension. 2026-08-04. Approver: Dave.** During the `--reviews` replan the planner extended the ruling above: `BootedFabric.artifacts` is a `Mapping[str, CCFContract]` carrying *every* parsed artifact keyed by manifest key, not only the `load`-role ones. The ruling's letter is preserved exactly — `register` entries still enter the Control Plane registry and the returned pin set, `load` entries still do neither, and `ResolvedPins` still covers `register` entries only (`version_for` raising for a load-role key is documented as correct). What changed is visibility, which was never the property D-IMPL-80 protects: the governance question was what lands in the registry and therefore in Decision Replay dimension 2, not what the caller can see after boot. Rationale for the extension: it closes cross-AI review finding C-8(b), where the adopter's means of assembling the `parameters` mapping for `build_operator_registry` was unspecified and the likely improvisation — `isinstance` narrowing or class-id string filtering, repeated across nine example files — would have created a *new* duplication class of exactly the kind Phase 15 exists to remove. With both roles visible the pattern is `{key: booted.artifacts[key] for key in (...)}`: the adopter names manifest keys, and `isinstance` narrowing survives in exactly one place, inside SDK code (`15-11`'s builder), where its docstring records that it lives there so it need not live in nine adopter files. Rejected: restricting `artifacts` to `load` entries to match the wording literally, which would have left register-role operator parameters reachable only through the registry and reopened C-8(b). The invariant is stated in the attribute docstring: **`role` governs registration and pinning, never visibility.** Consumed by Phase 15 (ERGO-10, ERGO-11, and the example rewrites). Originates: `15-REVIEWS.md` finding C-8(b); D-IMPL-80 above.

**D-IMPL-81 — whether the deployment-manifest content hash is recorded on the Decision Replay record.** OPEN — **routed to a Phase D mini-cycle. Surfaced and logged at D-GATE-15, 2026-08-05, by Dave's ruling.** Surfacing context: the idea arose during Phase 15 planning and was deliberately left out of that phase's scope, because it is new *fabric* behavior and Phase 15's non-goals exclude changes to what the fabric records. At the phase-close gate it was carried as one of three open questions (`docs/SURFACE-AUDIT.md` §5.4) with three dispositions available — mint a decision-log entry, drop it, or leave it recorded only in a SUMMARY. Dave ruled that it be tracked here rather than left in a plan summary. The question: `load_and_boot` reads a deployment manifest that names every artifact the deployment registers or loads, each entry carrying its D-IMPL-80 `role`. Today the *constituent* artifacts are individually pinned into Decision Replay dimension 2 through `ResolvedPins` — the manifest itself is not. A manifest content hash would tie a replayed decision to the exact manifest document that booted the fabric, not merely to the set of artifact versions that manifest happened to name. Readings to weigh in the mini-cycle: (a) the pinned constituent set is already sufficient, since two manifests naming identical artifact versions produce identical Control Plane state and the marginal value is low; (b) the manifest is itself a Control Plane authoring decision — which artifacts a deployment chose to register versus load is exactly the D-IMPL-80 governance boundary, and a `role` reassignment that moved an artifact out of the pin set would be invisible to replay under reading (a); (c) record it only when the manifest is itself a registered artifact, leaving file-sourced manifests unpinned. Note the interaction with D-IMPL-80: if the manifest hash enters dimension 2, the `role: load` entries it names are described by a pinned document while remaining unpinned themselves, and the mini-cycle must state whether that is coherent or whether the hash covers only the `register` section. Also interacts with D-IMPL-06 (snapshot/pin strategy) and D-IMPL-26 (artifact addressing — a manifest hash is a content-address and the hybrid scheme already ruled there applies). Originates: Phase 15 planning deferral; `docs/SURFACE-AUDIT.md` §5.4 item 2; D-GATE-15 ruling (3). Consumed by: a Phase D mini-cycle per CLAUDE.md §3.1, which runs the same protocol scoped to this decision — review, triage, Dave's approval, `D-GATE` entry.

**D-GATE-15 (amendment) — two HIGH defects found by post-gate cross-AI review, carried as named debt.** Date: 2026-08-05. Approver: Dave. The gate above stands; this amendment records that a second, post-approval review round found two blocking defects the pre-gate review did not, and names where they close. Commissioned because Dave was not comfortable with the shipped state after approving. Four independent systems (gemini, codex, cursor, ollama; `claude` excluded for independence) reviewed the **shipped code and the plans** — not the plans alone — against the question "is this a sound design approach, and should this phase have shipped at all?". **All four answered sound, and all four answered yes** (gemini risk LOW; codex and cursor MEDIUM). Both defects below were then verified empirically by the orchestrator against the source rather than accepted on a reviewer's report. Full round in `.planning/phases/15-sdk-ergonomics-the-integration-surface/15-REVIEWS-round3.md`. **CR-1 — author-versioned artifact classes can bypass the D-05 hash-verified bump check through the manifest (HIGH, blocking).** Raised independently by codex and cursor; gemini raised the same split as its only HIGH. Codex stated the falsifiable condition: "If adopters can put workflow/task/agent definitions in the manifest with `role: register` and thereby use `register_typed`, the design is unsound." Verified: `SDK_ARTIFACT_ROLES` fixes exactly one class, `ccf.signal-batch`, so `refuse_role_the_sdk_fixes` accepts `role: register` for `ccf.workflow-definition`, `ccf.agent-definition` and `ccf.task-definition`, each of which then routes through `register_typed`, which does not run the bump check. **This means the 15-19 split ruling recorded in D-GATE-15 above is a convention the shipped examples follow, not an enforced invariant** — `test_the_author_versioned_definitions_kept_their_bump_check` proves the examples do it right, and nothing proves the manifest refuses the wrong thing. All three substantive reviewers independently proposed the same remedy and two called the split *backwards* (the more governance-sensitive the artifact, the less declarative the phase made it): a per-entry registration mode in the manifest (`registration: typed | versioned`), with `load_and_boot` dispatching to `register_versioned` for author-versioned classes and **refusing** any manifest that registers them through `register_typed` — one mental model, everything declarative, governance enforced rather than conventional. **CR-2 — Gate 2 (lineage strict equality, INV-CH7-47) is self-satisfying in every example (HIGH, blocking), and is a NINTH instance of this phase's own signature finding.** Raised by cursor alone. Verified across nine files: every example constructs `ProductLineage(operators_invoked=execution.operators_invoked, ...)` and then calls `run_gates(candidate, engine_resolved=execution.operators_invoked)` — the same value on both sides of the gate (`00-quickstart`, `01-agent-basics`, `02-skilling`, `03-harness-portability`, `04-cross-process/producer_child`, `06-decision-replay`, `07-artifact-store-mcp`, `08-e2e-demo`, `external/00-hello-context/serve`). The phase closed the *upstream* `required_operators()` collapse and numbered around this one; `test_gate_independence.py` already admits that callers outside `ccf.deployment` may pass the same value twice. Cursor's framing, recorded because it is the correct criticism: the phase "celebrated closing the upstream collapse while leaving the gate itself self-satisfied in the adoption contract." **Consequence for the record above: the audit's claim of eight numbered instances is an undercount, and `docs/SURFACE-AUDIT.md` §4.1's ledger is incomplete as written.** **Disposition (Dave, 2026-08-05): both close in a new Phase 15.1 gap-closure cycle, and D-GATE-15 is amended rather than revoked.** The approval was earned on the evidence available at the gate — cursor's own closing note: "Dave already approved. This review is whether that approval was earned. It was — with the HIGH items above still unpaid debt, not closed by the gate." Recording them here rather than only in the review file means a reader of this log alone learns that the gate carries known debt. **Also carried to 15.1 as advisories from this round, not blocking:** the new AST-based gates are tripwires against known patterns rather than walls, and must not be described as structural enforcement on par with single-writer dependency injection (codex and cursor, independently); `BootedFabric.artifacts` exposing both roles blurs "parsed" against "in force"; and D-IMPL-81 being open means the manifest — the document that assigns the roles — is itself unpinned, so this phase's "no replay dimension hidden" claim is incomplete until that mini-cycle rules.

**D-IMPL-82 — whether `ControlPlaneProtocol` gains `register_versioned`.** RESOLVED — **widen the Protocol. Dave, 2026-08-05, Phase 15.1 planning.** `load_and_boot(registry: ControlPlaneProtocol)` (`manifest.py:180`) must dispatch author-versioned classes to `register_versioned` to close CR-1, but the Protocol declares only `register`, `register_typed`, `resolve`, `resolve_latest` and `audit_events` (`registry.py:167-187`). Three readings were offered: widen the Protocol; add a narrow `VersionedRegistrationProtocol` that `load_and_boot` requires only when a manifest declares `versioned`; or narrow `load_and_boot` to the concrete `ControlPlaneRegistry`. Dave ruled the first. Rationale on the record: the D-05 bump check is a governance obligation, and the Protocol's own docstring already argues that obligations sit on the surface "precisely so a remote plane cannot conform while quietly dropping them" — a remote Control Plane that cannot run the bump check should not be able to conform. Consequence accepted: this is a public Protocol change and a remote-plane (FABR-07) implementation obligation. Rejected: the narrow second Protocol, which preserves the same posture at the cost of a two-Protocol mental model in the phase whose subject is one mental model; and narrowing to the concrete class, which breaks the Protocol-over-inheritance posture CLAUDE.md §11 mandates and forecloses remote control planes for manifest boot. Originates: CR-1, D-GATE-15 amendment.

**D-IMPL-83 — where the version/hash bump evidence lands.** RESOLVED — **a plain field on `ArtifactPin`; the audit log stays primary. Dave, 2026-08-05, Phase 15.1 planning.** Codex asked for bump evidence "in the returned `ResolvedPins` or adjacent audit structure". `ResolvedPins` is under an explicit no-growth constraint (`pins.py:9-29`) with `tests/deployment/test_manifest.py:314` pinning its public method set to exactly one. Reading adopted: that constraint forbids *methods that select*, not *fields that record* — `class_id` is already a recorded fact of the same kind — so the registration mode is recorded as a field on `ArtifactPin` and no method is added to `ResolvedPins`. `register_versioned` already audits every outcome with `action="register_versioned"` and `resolved=True|False` (`registry.py:487-494`), which is how `test_the_author_versioned_definitions_kept_their_bump_check` reads it today; that audit record remains the primary evidence and the pin field is the convenience. Consequence accepted: the field is additive to a `CCFContract`, so the pin set's content hash changes and `test_two_loads_of_one_manifest_produce_an_identical_pin_set` (`test_manifest.py:86`) must be re-read — it still passes, both loads changing identically. Rejected: audit-log-only, which leaves the evidence unreachable by inspecting the returned pins; and a `ResolvedPins` accessor, which contradicts the no-growth constraint directly. Originates: CR-1, D-GATE-15 amendment.

**D-IMPL-84 — how wide the refusal is that closes the manifest bypass.** RESOLVED — **refuse envelope resolution for any `ccf.`-prefixed class id. Dave, 2026-08-05, Phase 15.1 planning.** Phase 15.1 research falsified the reviewed *description* of CR-1 by execution rather than by reading: the four author-versioned classes are absent from `SDK_ARTIFACT_CLASSES`, so a manifest naming a real `workflow-definition.yaml` resolves the open `DeclaredArtifact` envelope and raises `ArtifactLoadError` with 13 validation errors — loud, not silent. What *is* reachable is an **envelope-shaped class-id squat**: a valid `DeclaredArtifact` claiming `class_id: ccf.workflow-definition` boots, pins, binds the key to the SDK class id, and enters replay dimension 2 with arbitrary content and no bump check, after which a later correct `register_versioned` on the same key is admitted. Verified reachable for `ccf.hitl-policy`, `ccf.skill-grant` and a wholly invented `ccf.made-up-class` — the `ccf.` namespace is not reserved. Dave ruled the namespace-wide refusal over a four-id list, because a four-id list closes roughly one third of the hole and leaves the identical shape reachable elsewhere; example 05's manifest already documents the `ccf.hitl-policy` case as a hazard (`manifest.yaml:40-45`). **Recorded explicitly because it is a design statement and not merely a bug fix: this reserves the `ccf.` prefix.** `resolve_artifact_class` will refuse an envelope resolution for any `ccf.`-prefixed id the SDK does not own. Consequence for the record: the CR-1 text in the D-GATE-15 amendment above describes the defect as the reviewers described it, and that description is inaccurate as to mechanism — the defect is real and the ruling stands, but the reachable form is the squat, not `role: register` on a well-formed author-versioned document. Originates: CR-1, D-GATE-15 amendment; Phase 15.1 research.

**D-IMPL-85 — whether the manifest `registration` field is required or defaulted.** RESOLVED — **required, no default. Dave, 2026-08-05, Phase 15.1 planning.** `role` is required-with-no-default and `ManifestEntry`'s docstring gives the reason (`artifacts.py:596-601`): "Either default would have the SDK deciding a replay-dimension-2 boundary on the adopter's behalf." That argument transfers to `registration` verbatim, and Phase 15.1's success criterion 2 — the artifact author does not have to know which of two registration paths their class belongs to — is only true if the manifest states the mode explicitly rather than inheriting a silent default. Cost accepted and measured: 163 manifest entries across 11 files plus `tests/deployment/conftest.py`'s fixture tuples, one mechanical wave. Noted in support: there are no external adopters (private repo, pre-1.0; D-14 additivity governed *removals*), so the cost is entirely internal. Rejected: optional-defaulting-to-`typed`, which has the SDK choosing the governance path by silence for every entry that omits it — the convention-mistaken-for-enforcement failure this phase exists to close. Originates: CR-1, D-GATE-15 amendment.

**D-IMPL-86 — what makes Gate 2 (lineage strict equality, INV-CH7-47) a check that can fail.** RESOLVED — **design E2: two authored artifacts bound at boot. Dave, 2026-08-05, Phase 15.1 planning.** This entry supersedes the CR-2 remedy recorded in the D-GATE-15 amendment above, which does not work. **The finding that reopened it**, verified independently by three readers at file:line: the remedy the cross-AI round prescribed — lineage from the authored declaration, `engine_resolved` from `execution`, plus a mutation test — is *circular*. `declared.required_operator_keys` feeds `plan.required` (`examples/00-quickstart/run.py:211` and eight siblings) → `plan.required_operators()` → `build_operator_plan` (`planner.py:131`, where `resolve(k).key == k` because `register()` self-keys at `registry.py:106,116-122`) → `outputs[operator.key]` (`engine.py:280`) → `operators_invoked=frozenset(outputs)` (`engine.py:316`). So `execution.operators_invoked ≡ frozenset(declared.required_operator_keys)` — an identity through five pure transforms with no branch. Both sides of the "fixed" gate would still trace to one root artifact; nothing a deployment could author would make them diverge. **Established and not disputed by any reviewer: the engine is a faithful transcriber of the plan, not an independent witness.** `operators_invoked` and `steps` are built in the same loop over `operator_plan.operators` (`engine.py:271-288`), so the engine has no return path where invoked ≠ planned; this holds under every candidate design, which means D-IMPL-57's intra-fabric "the engine's resolution is an independent witness" premise and the shipped rationale at `gates.py:244-250` are wrong as written **regardless of this ruling**, and are corrected in Phase 15.1. **Process:** ruled after a three-reviewer round (Anthropic Fable, Codex, Cursor) and a head-to-head cross-examination. Fable and Codex recommended A (projection authors its own operator set in deployment code); Cursor recommended D (keep one authored root; rewrite the claim to assembly-path integrity). On cross-examination Fable withdrew four of its five supporting arguments — the engine-as-witness claim; the whitepaper §7.5/L2279 argument (the text is *silent* on the provenance of the operator set and affirmatively blesses data-holder plans, so it does not support A); D-IMPL-57 as precedent (Cursor was right that the intra-fabric line was an assumed premise for the federated ruling, not adjudicated); and the anti-DRY-back AST guard, on the decisive ground that **under A the compliant state (an author typed the keys) and the violating state (an author transcribed them from the artifact) are textually identical, so DRY-back is undetectable in principle**. Fable also established by exhaustive search that `plan.required` has exactly four possible sources — the product-class artifact, projection-code literals, the operator registry (banned by G-B), or AST-extraction from `invoke()` — and that the fourth is impossible on verified fact, because projections consume operators through the flowed `view` chain rather than by subscripting `operator_outputs`, so the dependency set is not structurally recoverable from the body and no runtime "record what `invoke()` read" witness can exist either. **Both reviewers converged that the whitepaper under-determines the question** (WP L2417's "forgery, omission, or accidental drift all fail at the gate" favours an authorable declared side; L2401's "during composition" supports the narrower record-and-verify reading), making this a CLAUDE.md §15 ambiguity for Dave rather than a fidelity derivation. **Dave's ruling and rationale: drift must be authorable, and E2 now rather than D now and E later** — because D requires shipping an honesty sentence and rewriting D-IMPL-57's rationale to "assembly-path integrity", which E would then require rewriting again, and Phase 16 documents this surface; that is Phase 14's G-01 defect repeated. **Design E2 as ruled:** (1) the product-class artifact remains the Control Plane claim and feeds `lineage.operators_invoked` only — never `execution`; (2) the projection's required operator set becomes **its own Control Plane artifact**, distinct from the product-class artifact, which the projection reads; (3) a **new boot-time binder refuses when `product_class.required_operator_keys != frozenset(plan.required_operators())`** — this is where authorable drift fails; (4) Gate 2 remains claim-versus-`execution`, i.e. assembly-path integrity, and is **documented as such**; (5) a tripwire bans filling both gate arguments from `execution`. The projection-requirements artifact was chosen over projection-code literals specifically so the contested §6.4 classification never has to be ruled — under E2 there are no operator-key code constants, so the No-Hardcoding Rule is satisfied by construction rather than by argument. It also answers Cursor's original objection to a second artifact ("two artifacts kept in sync by hand with no mechanism binding them"): the boot binder is that mechanism. **Consequences accepted:** this revises the gated 15-09 decision and therefore runs as a Phase D mini-cycle under CLAUDE.md §3.1 with this entry as the ruling of record; it expands Phase 15.1 past the "two HIGH items only" scope ruling Dave made earlier the same day; and the residual is named rather than softened — a deployment that reads one artifact into the other still collapses the binder, so dual authorship remains convention-assisted at the authoring boundary even though the *check* is structural. Rejected: A (dual authorship in code, unpoliceable and §6.4-contested), B without a binder (Cursor: "ceremony without a meaningful authority boundary"), and D (honest, cheaper, and Fable would not have dissented from it — but it makes Gate 2 permanently unfailable by an author and forces a two-pass documentation rewrite). Full reviewer record: `.planning/phases/15.1-ergonomics-gap-closure/`. Originates: CR-2, D-GATE-15 amendment.

**D-GATE-15 (second amendment) — CR-2's recorded remedy superseded; Phase 15.1 scope widened.** Date: 2026-08-05. Approver: Dave. The first amendment above recorded CR-2's remedy as the cross-AI round prescribed it. **That remedy is now known not to work** — see D-IMPL-86 for the verified circularity — and is superseded by design E2. Three further corrections to the first amendment's text, recorded here rather than edited into it so the reasoning at each point in time survives: (1) CR-1's *mechanism* is the `ccf.`-prefixed envelope squat, not `role: register` on a well-formed author-versioned document, which fails loudly — see D-IMPL-84; (2) the first amendment says the nine examples pass "the same value" on both sides of Gate 2, and verification shows they pass **the same object**; (3) the first amendment's advisory that the new AST gates are tripwires rather than walls is *strengthened* by D-IMPL-86's finding that no AST gate can distinguish an authored literal from a transcribed one — the tripwire framing is now a structural limit, not a caution. **Scope consequence:** Phase 15.1 was scoped by Dave earlier the same day to "the two HIGH items only", explicitly excluding D-IMPL-81 and a per-gate independence matrix. E2 keeps both of those exclusions but adds the projection-requirements artifact class and the boot binder, which are new mechanism rather than defect closure. That widening is deliberate and is this amendment's operative decision.

**D-IMPL-86 (correction, 2026-08-05) — the binder is not boot-time; element (3)'s placement wording was wrong.** Recorded as an appended correction rather than an edit to the entry above, following the D-GATE-15 second-amendment precedent, so the reasoning at each point in time survives. D-IMPL-86 element (3) specifies "a **new boot-time binder** refuses when `product_class.required_operator_keys != frozenset(plan.required_operators())`". **That placement is impossible and the wording is corrected.** Verified independently by two readers against source: `load_and_boot(manifest_path, *, registry, database_path, postgres_dsn, tenant_id) -> BootedFabric` (`src/ccf/deployment/manifest.py:177`) takes no `plan` parameter and has no `ProjectionPlan` in scope at any of its preflights (`_resolve_tenant`, `_preflight_backend`, the `refuse_role_the_sdk_fixes` loop) — a plan is adopter code constructed *after* boot returns, from the artifacts boot resolved. A comparison naming `plan.required_operators()` therefore cannot run at boot. **Corrected wording: the binder is a refusal placed where a plan exists — between plan construction and `ProjectionEngine.execute` — not inside `load_and_boot`.** Phase 15.1 plan 04 Task 1 selects and records the exact placement with a `file:line` citation before any code is written. **E2's essential property is unaffected and was checked explicitly:** with the plan built from the projection-requirements artifact and the binder comparing that against the product-class declaration, a one-key edit to *either* authored document produces a refusal, so drift remains reachable by editing an artifact rather than only by writing a bug — which is the whole content of Dave's ruling. **A new residual the placement creates, named here rather than left to a docstring:** because the binder is invoked by adopter code rather than by `load_and_boot`, nothing outside the SDK's own `examples/` tree holds a deployment to calling it at all; the AST coverage check that enforces the call is scoped to `examples/`. This is a second convention-not-enforcement residual of the same family as the one D-IMPL-86 already names, it is carried to Phase 16 rather than closed in 15.1, and it is surfaced at Phase 15.1's gate for Dave to rule on rather than accepted silently. ROADMAP Phase 15.1 success criterion 4 is amended to match. Originates: Phase 15.1 planning, plan-checker blocking findings 1 and 2.

**D-GATE-15.1 — WITHDRAWN 2026-08-06. This approval does not stand.** Logged 2026-08-05; withdrawn 2026-08-06 by Dave when the post-gate code review returned three verified Critical findings that the gate presentation did not contain (`.planning/phases/15.1-ergonomics-gap-closure/15.1-REVIEW.md`; reasons in **D-GATE-15.1 (withdrawal, 2026-08-06)** below). The text following is preserved **unedited** as the record of what was presented and approved at the time — per this log's appended-correction precedent (the D-GATE-15 second amendment; the D-IMPL-86 correction) — but none of its dispositions hold: not the R-1…R-5 dispositions, not R-5's ratification, and not the ERGO-18/ERGO-19 completion. **ERGO-18 and ERGO-19 are returned to `Pending`.** Phase 16 does not begin on this entry. **Original entry, as logged 2026-08-05, verbatim:** Phase 15.1 (Ergonomics Gap Closure — Governance Enforced, Not Conventional) approved. Date: 2026-08-05. Approver: Dave. **This ran as a Phase D mini-cycle under CLAUDE.md §3.1**, because design E2 revises the gated 15-09 decision; **D-IMPL-86 and its correction of 2026-08-05 are the ruling of record**, and this entry closes the mini-cycle rather than reopening it. **The form of the approval, recorded precisely because the phase is about unsupported claims.** The gate presentation put **five** items to Dave — R-1 … R-5, enumerated in `.planning/phases/15.1-ergonomics-gap-closure/15.1-REVIEWS.md` §5 — and stated that a reply of "approved" would log this entry, mark ERGO-18/19 Complete, and unblock Phase 16. Dave's verbatim reply was **"Approved"**: one approval of the phase as presented, **not five separate written rulings on the five items individually**. Their dispositions below are therefore stated in exactly the terms the presentation asked for and no stronger — three of them are open residuals *accepted at approval*, not residuals closed by it. **Scope approved as presented** in `docs/SURFACE-AUDIT.md` §6, `15.1-REVIEWS.md`, and the ten per-plan SUMMARYs: the two HIGH defects the post-gate cross-AI review found against Phase 15 are closed, plus the mechanism D-IMPL-86 ruled in to close the second properly. **Delivered — CR-1 (ERGO-18):** `registration: typed | versioned` is a required, no-default field on every manifest entry (D-IMPL-85, `ManifestEntry.registration` at `src/ccf/controlplane/artifacts.py:759`, **163 entries across 11 documents** migrated by parsing rather than by memory); `load_and_boot` dispatches on it (`src/ccf/deployment/manifest.py:335`) with an `isinstance` narrowing that turns a returned `RegistrationRefusal` into a loud raise and **no fallback to `register_typed`**; `ControlPlaneProtocol` declares `register_versioned` so a conforming plane cannot drop the D-05 bump check (D-IMPL-82); the registration mode is recorded on `ArtifactPin` with the audit log still primary and no new selector — `ResolvedPins` still exposes exactly one public method (D-IMPL-83); and `refuse_registration_the_sdk_fixes` plus `resolve_artifact_class` refuse envelope resolution for **any** `ccf.`-prefixed class id the SDK does not own, which reserves the namespace as a prefix check rather than a four-id list (D-IMPL-84). The reachable hole research proved — the envelope-shaped class-id squat, **not** the `role: register` path the reviewers described — is closed, with `tests/deployment/test_manifest.py::test_an_author_versioned_class_declared_typed_registration_is_refused`, `::test_an_envelope_squat_on_an_sdk_class_id_is_refused`, `::test_the_ccf_namespace_reservation_is_wide_not_a_four_id_list` and `::test_a_manifest_entry_naming_no_registration_is_refused`, every one **watched to fail** with the source reverted and the failure *reasons* recorded — three raised `DID NOT RAISE ValueError`, i.e. the squat booted, registered and pinned, which is the executed reproduction of CR-1. **Delivered — CR-2 (ERGO-19), design E2:** the product-class artifact remains the Control Plane claim and fills `lineage.operators_invoked` only; the projection's required operator set is its own `ccf.projection-requirements` artifact (**nine** documents, one per projecting example, each `$schema`-headed and named by a manifest entry); `refuse_operator_set_divergence(*, declared, plan) -> None` (`src/ccf/deployment/binder.py`) compares the two as sets and refuses without repairing, called **between plan construction and `ProjectionEngine.execute`** — not inside `load_and_boot`, which has no `ProjectionPlan` in scope — per the D-IMPL-86 correction and the matching ROADMAP criterion-4 amendment; Gate 2 stays claim-versus-execution and is documented as **assembly-path integrity**; and `tests/products/test_gate_two_call_shape.py` is a tripwire refusing any call site that fills both gate arguments from `execution`, with a companion check holding all eleven projecting bodies under `examples/` to naming the binder. All nine CR-2 sites are rewritten; the refusal was watched to fire at every one from a one-key edit to *either* authored document. **Documentation corrections (Criterion 5):** every site claiming the engine is an independent witness is corrected — `gates.py` at both sites, the `ProductClassDeclaration` docstring, `projection/plan.py`, `SURFACE-AUDIT.md` §3.1 item 1, two adopter-facing `public/` pages, eight `product-class.yaml` comment blocks, the generated `docs/schemas/ccf.product-class.json`, and D-IMPL-57's intra-fabric premise (appended dated note; `git diff --numstat` shows 2 insertions and **0 deletions**, so the ruling is not reopened). §4.1's ledger now records nine instances with the earlier count dispute preserved; the AST gates are restated as tripwires and the import lints stay described as walls, `git diff --exit-code` against the phase base for `tools/lints/` exiting 0. **Verification matrix — observed by plan 15.1-10 against the merged tree, re-run after the review's three blocking fixes** (both runs green, identical numbers; a gate not run is a gate this entry could not report): `python -m tools.lints --all` **exit 0**; `make typecheck` **exit 0**, no issues in **170** source files; `make test` **2059 passed / 2 skipped**, 319.77s; `make examples` **exit 0**, all ten invoked and passed; `make docs-strict` **exit 0**; `make ci` **exit 0**; `make schemas` wrote 24 schemas and `git diff --exit-code docs/schemas/` **exit 0**; `make schema-headers` **exit 0** over 10 artifact directories; `tools/dump_surface.py --check` **exit 0** (bytes-equality on the live manifest, regenerated once by 15.1-10 under the single-owner rule); `tools/assemble_surface_changes.py --check` **exit 0**; `git diff --exit-code docs/surface/v0.2-surface-baseline.json` **exit 0** — the digest-pinned frozen baseline was not touched. Excluded by design and deliberately not "fixed": example 03's `--mode live` leg, `ext-00`'s live legs, example 08's `--live`, and the perf battery (D-10/D-11/D-IMPL-70). **Review and triage (§3.1) — and the round did not fully happen, which is recorded rather than smoothed.** `/gsd-review --phase "Phase 15.1" --all` was run against the deliverables rather than the plan text, with `claude` excluded for independence per the D-GATE-15 posture. Of three external reviewers, **one produced a usable review**: **cursor** (substantive, source-cited, 2 blocking + 6 advisory); **codex**, whose bubblewrap sandbox could not create a user namespace (`bwrap: loopback: Failed RTM_NEWADDR`) so it read nothing and correctly refused a verdict — separately, `gpt-5.1-codex-max`, the model CLAUDE.md §3.1 names, returns **404 Model not found** for `CODEX_API_KEY` though authentication itself succeeded, and the `--dangerously-bypass-approvals-and-sandbox` retry was **denied by the environment's permission classifier and not worked around**; and **gemini**, whose report is **rejected in full as fabricated** — every file-reading tool was denied and it produced a confident `file:line`-shaped review anyway, marking all four invariants "Verified" while citing `RESERVED_CCF_CLASSES` and `ArtifactManifest`, **neither of which exists in the tree** (the mechanism is a prefix check, not a list — the entire substance of D-IMPL-84; the class is `DeploymentManifest`). **Four blocking findings, three closed inside the phase before presentation and one escalated:** F-1/F-2/F-3 are one defect — four *further* sites asserted the claim Criterion 5 requires corrected, because plan 15.1-09 swept for the phrase "independent witness" and these use a second vocabulary, "two independent sources". One false property, two spellings, and a search keyed to one found one. The worst was not prose: `tests/products/test_required_operators_guard.py`'s **failure message** — what an author reads at the moment the guard fires — instructed them to declare the plan's keys from the `ccf.product-class` artifact, which after E2 is precisely the collapse the binder exists to prevent; cursor raised it as advisory and it was **reclassified blocking** and closed, with the reason recorded. All four sites corrected to the assembly-path reading with **no assertion, refusal condition, scan constant or lint rule touched**. **Criterion 5 was therefore not actually met until 15.1-10's own Task 2**, which is stated in `SURFACE-AUDIT.md` §6.1 row 5 and §6.6 rather than smoothed over. Advisories carried: F-6 (preflight registration refusals raise without an audit event), F-7 (example 05 registers an author-versioned class at the *registry* seam, which the SDK does not police by design), F-8 (namespace reservation proportionate — non-defect), F-9, F-10. **Phase D mini-cycle invariant check**, each re-verified independently rather than accepted on report: audit-by-construction holds for state-changing actions on the registration path (`registry.py:471-479`, `:518-525`, `:379`) with the preflight asymmetry recorded as F-6; single-writer holds **by construction** — `binder.py` imports exactly two names, `ProductClassDeclaration` and `ProjectionPlan`, so it has no graph handle, no writer and no registry, and `ccf.projection-requirements` enters through `load_and_boot`'s Control Plane path like every other artifact; No-Hardcoding holds by construction and was E2's explicit design reason — under E2 there are no operator-key code constants, so CLAUDE.md §6.4 is satisfied structurally rather than by argument; and the three validation gates' sequence is **unchanged** (`run_gates`: empty-operator → lineage strict equality at `gates.py:228` → TTL at `:239-243`; federated keeps empty → attributability → TTL). **Three of this phase's own acceptance criteria were found wrong as written, and are reported corrected rather than satisfied** — `SURFACE-AUDIT.md` §6.3, because a criterion met by changing the code to suit a bad check is the defect this phase was convened to close. (1) `grep -rn "operators_invoked=execution.operators_invoked" examples/` is asked to be empty and **must not be**: every survivor is a result-carrier dataclass field handing the *engine's* resolution to the paired test module, which is what turns `assert result.product.lineage.operators_invoked == result.operators_invoked` from a tautology into a two-source comparison; zeroing it would undo the phase. **And the predicted count was itself wrong — the observed count is 5, not the 8 that three prior documents state** (15.1-07's SUMMARY, 15.1-08's SUMMARY, and the phase-close brief), because examples 00/01/02 route the same value through a named local (`engine_transcription`, added by 15.1-05) so the literal string is absent while the property is identical. The 8 was correct before wave 5 and propagated three documents deep **without being re-measured against the tree it described**; the correction is recorded here, in `SURFACE-AUDIT.md` §6.3 and in `deferred-items.md`, and the five surviving sites are `examples/03-harness-portability/run.py:480`, `examples/04-cross-process/producer_child.py:438`, `examples/06-decision-replay/run.py:1109`, `examples/07-artifact-store-mcp/run.py:494`, `examples/08-e2e-demo/run.py:1102`. A phase about unsupported claims does not close with an unverified number in its own record. (2) `grep -c "independent witness" src/ccf/products/gates.py == 0` cannot be met honestly — it is 3, all negations or a quoted-then-answered superseded claim, and `binder.py` greps to 0 for the *identical negated sentence* only because the phrase wraps across a line break. (3) `docs/SURFACE-CHANGES.md` is not ordered by plan across the file — the assembler groups by change type first, so plan order applies within groups (all six groups verified monotonic under the `(major, minor, plan)` key), and only four 15.1 fragments exist because plans 05–09 added no public Python name. **Dispositions of the five items put to Dave, in the terms the approval covers them and no stronger:** **R-5 — the Criterion-6 gate edit made by plan 15.1-02** (narrowing assertion 3 of `tests/test_surface_completeness.py` to admit `change_type == "reshaped" and name in difference["added"]`) is **RATIFIED**: it was presented explicitly as an executor-made gate edit needing human ratification, and the approval covers it. The re-read that supports it was independently verified — `ManifestEntry`/`ArtifactPin` sit permanently in the frozen baseline's `added` partition so `reshaped` was genuinely unrepresentable; assertion 2 still requires every `added` name to be claimed by some public entry carrying `added`; the exemption changes what the gate **reads**, not what it **permits**; and 15.1-03 did not rely on the narrowing, so the reconciliation carries exactly one plan's weight. **R-1 — whether a one-working-reviewer round suffices for this gate — is ACCEPTED AS SUFFICIENT FOR THIS GATE**, with the circumstance recorded honestly beside it rather than absorbed: of three reviewers, codex could not run and gemini's report was rejected as fabricated (both detailed above), so CR-2 — the defect that convened this entire phase — was found by cursor alone, and the round this phase closes had four reviewers. The thinness is a recorded fact of this gate, not a resolved one. **R-2 (the binder's *placement* residual), R-3 (the binder's *authorship* residual) and R-4 (the HTTP `/schema` route's contract) are ACCEPTED AS RECORDED RESIDUALS AND CARRIED FORWARD — they are NOT closed, resolved, or accepted-as-fine by this phase.** R-2 is cursor's confirmed **blocking** finding that the binder is a governance mechanism nobody outside the SDK's own tree is held to — the same failure mode as CR-1 at a different seam — stated in four parts: the binder sits between plan construction and `ProjectionEngine.execute` because `load_and_boot` has no `ProjectionPlan` in scope (`src/ccf/deployment/manifest.py:177`); it returns `None`, so nothing observable at runtime proves it was called and a deployment that omits it produces byte-identical output; nothing outside `examples/` holds a deployment to calling it at all; and the AST coverage check that enforces the call (`tests/products/test_gate_two_call_shape.py`) is scoped to `examples/` only. Closing it is new fabric behavior that moves the D-15A abstraction line, which is Dave's ruling to make and was not made here. R-3 is structurally unclosable by a gate — a deployment that reads one artifact into the other collapses the binder, and no AST gate can distinguish an authored literal from a transcribed one (D-IMPL-86's own finding) — so dual authorship remains convention-assisted at the authoring boundary even though the check itself is structural. R-4 is the route, the `ccf schema export` CLI verb and `tools/dump_schemas.py` all enumerating `SDK_ARTIFACT_CLASSES` and answering **404** for the six author-versioned classes (`ccf.agent-definition`, `ccf.prompt-artifact`, `ccf.task-definition`, `ccf.workflow-definition`, `ccf.skill-catalog-entry`, `ccf.skill-grant`) whose schemas *are* committed in `docs/schemas/` and which a manifest may now name for versioned registration; this phase sharpened the question and did not answer it. All three carry to Phase 16 and are recorded in `.planning/phases/15.1-ergonomics-gap-closure/deferred-items.md` and `SURFACE-AUDIT.md` §6.4. **Further standing residuals, recorded rather than softened:** **D-IMPL-81** (pinning the deployment manifest itself) stays open debt against Phase 16 or later, unaffected by the E2 widening; `BootedFabric.artifacts` role blurring (`role: load` entries reachable on `artifacts` and absent from the replay pin set, because role governs registration and pinning and never visibility — D-IMPL-80 amendment) needs both halves said together in the reference pages; F-6's unaudited preflight refusals; and F-7's manifest-seam-versus-registry-seam asymmetry. **Two documentation facts this phase surfaced for later readers:** correcting a contract-class docstring silently changes a *shipped* JSON Schema, because `make schemas` embeds the docstring verbatim as `description` — found by a red `tests/cli/test_schema_export.py`, not by inspection; and correcting a claim means sweeping for the **claim** in every phrasing the tree has used for it, a token search being no substitute (the two known phrasings are "independent witness" and "two independent sources"). **Deliverables:** `src/ccf/deployment/binder.py` and `classes.py`'s registration refusal and `ccf.`-namespace reservation, `ManifestEntry.registration` + `ArtifactPin.registration` + `load_and_boot` dispatch, the `ccf.projection-requirements` artifact class and its nine example documents, `tests/deployment/test_binder.py`, `tests/products/test_gate_two_call_shape.py`, `tests/examples/test_gate_two_divergence.py`, the nine rewritten Gate 2 call sites, `docs/SURFACE-AUDIT.md` §6 (nine criterion rows with named evidence, the observed verification matrix, the three wrong criteria, and the five carried items), `docs/SURFACE-CHANGES.md` with four new fragments, `docs/surface/v0.2-surface.json` regenerated once, `.planning/phases/15.1-ergonomics-gap-closure/15.1-REVIEWS.md`, `deferred-items.md`, and 10/10 plan SUMMARYs. ERGO-18 and ERGO-19 are marked Complete on this approval, their clauses re-verified clause-by-clause against the tree first. **Phase 16 (Documentation Rewrite) may begin** — and must describe Gate 2 as assembly-path integrity, must not inherit any of the three wrong criteria above as a check, and inherits R-2, R-3 and R-4 open.

**D-GATE-15.1 (withdrawal, 2026-08-06) — the 2026-08-05 approval is superseded; Phase 15.1 returns to open.** Date: 2026-08-06. Decided by: Dave. Recorded as an appended entry rather than a deletion, following the D-GATE-15 second-amendment and D-IMPL-86-correction precedent, so that both the approval and its withdrawal survive in the log. **What happened, in order.** The gate was presented and approved on 2026-08-05. The `/gsd:execute-phase` workflow runs its `code_review_gate` step *after* the phase gate, so `gsd-code-reviewer` ran against the phase's 45 changed Python files only once the approval was already logged. It returned **3 Critical, 7 Warning, 5 Info** (`15.1-REVIEW.md`, `status: issues_found`), each Critical verified by execution rather than by inspection. Dave was shown the findings and elected to treat the approval as superseded rather than carry them forward, because the strongest finding is this phase's own defect class appearing inside the phase that was convened to eliminate it. **The three Critical findings.** **CR-01 — arbitrary-path read at the manifest seam.** `ManifestEntry._path_is_a_name_not_a_pattern` (`src/ccf/controlplane/artifacts.py:761-771`) rejects only the glob characters `*`, `?`, `[`; it applies no traversal or absolute-path test, so `(root / entry.path)` accepts `../` escapes and absolute paths, contradicting the docstring sixty lines above it. Executed against a real fixture deployment, a file outside the deployment root was read, parsed and returned to the caller; on `role: register` that content is hashed into Control Plane state and into replay dimension 2. The defect predates this phase, but D-IMPL-84 is what newly makes a manifest an attack surface, so it is in scope here. **CR-02 — the `register_typed` walkaround is three sites, not the one recorded as F-7.** `examples/05-workflow-hitl/run.py:411`, `examples/06-decision-replay/run.py:661` and `examples/08-e2e-demo/run.py:959` each register `ccf.prompt-artifact` — author-versioned, and a member of `SDK_VERSIONED_ARTIFACT_CLASSES` — through `register_typed`, skipping the D-05 bump check. Example 05's own comment eight lines above the call states that the author-versioned classes stay on `register_versioned`; example 08's comment explains why that matters and then does the opposite. Worse, `tests/examples/test_workflow_hitl_output.py::test_the_author_versioned_definitions_kept_their_bump_check` says in its docstring that **four** keys must appear under `register_versioned` and not under `register_typed`, and asserts over a set of **three** — `prompt_artifact_key` is absent from it. **A test that claims four and checks three, while the fourth takes the path the test says it must not, is precisely the defect ERGO-18 and ERGO-19 were minted to close.** **CR-03 — the binder tripwire admits the collapse it exists to prevent.** `_names_the_binder` (`tests/products/test_gate_two_call_shape.py:582`) tests only that `refuse_operator_set_divergence` is *named* in the scope. A plan constructed with `required=declared.required_operator_keys` therefore passes the tripwire while making the binder's comparison unconditionally true — CR-2's original shape, reintroduced at the seam built to close it. The test's docstring honestly disclaims that it cannot assert the call was *reached*; it does not disclaim that it cannot assert the two sides are *independent*. This is **not** covered by residual R-3: R-3 is transcription between two authored YAML documents and is genuinely AST-invisible, whereas this is a readable identifier path that `_path` and `_shares_a_source` in the same module already resolve. **Warnings of the same class, carried into the reopened phase.** WR-01 — `_aliases` does not terminate on a cyclic assignment chain (`a = b; b = c; c = a`), reproduced at `exit=124` under `timeout 15`; the scan runs over all of `src/`, `runtime/` and `examples/`, so a future edit of that shape hangs `pytest` with no output. WR-02 — `_gate_collapses` is red on correct code, because `_path` collapses `obj.method()` to its receiver. WR-03 — two example tests claim a discriminating power their assertions do not have. **What this withdrawal does and does not disturb.** It withdraws the approval and returns ERGO-18/ERGO-19 to `Pending`.
