It does not disturb **D-IMPL-82 … D-IMPL-86**, the D-IMPL-86 correction, or the D-GATE-15 second amendment: those are design rulings Dave made during planning, and none of the Critical findings bears on the rulings themselves — the findings are about the fidelity of this phase's *implementation and its checks* to those rulings. The substantive record inside the withdrawn entry — the verification matrix, the three acceptance criteria found wrong as written, the 8-versus-5 measurement correction, and the reviewer-round circumstances — remains accurate and is not restated here. **Process finding, recorded because it caused this.** Running the code-review gate after the phase gate means a human can be asked to approve a phase before the review of that phase exists. That ordering is the workflow's, not a judgment made here, and it is what put an approval on the record that had to be withdrawn a day later. A gate presentation should carry its code review.

**D-GATE-15.1 (re-approved, 2026-08-06) — Phase 15.1 approved on the round-five verdict; supersedes the withdrawal of 2026-08-06 and the approval that withdrawal superseded.** Phase 15.1 (Ergonomics Gap Closure — Governance Enforced, Not Conventional) approved. Date: **2026-08-06**. Approver: **Dave**. Recorded as a third appended entry rather than as an edit to either of the two above, following the same precedent they follow (the D-GATE-15 second amendment; the D-IMPL-86 correction): the original approval, its withdrawal, and this re-approval all survive in the log, and a reader who consults only one of them is reading a third of the record. **The two entries above stand unedited.** This entry is the one in force; where its dispositions differ from the withdrawn entry's, this one governs, and where the withdrawn entry's *substantive record* is not disturbed (the three acceptance criteria found wrong as written, the 8-versus-5 measurement correction, the reviewer-round circumstances of the pre-withdrawal cross-AI round, the D-IMPL-82…86 rulings) it remains accurate and is not restated at length here. **The form of the approval, recorded precisely because this phase is about unsupported claims.** Dave was presented with the round-five verdict — **0 Critical, 2 Warning, 4 Info** (`.planning/phases/15.1-ergonomics-gap-closure/15.1-REVIEW-FIXES-4.md`) — and with four options. He chose **"Approve now — WR-01/WR-02 as residuals."** That choice, and nothing wider, is what this entry records: the phase is approved as of 2026-08-06; **ERGO-18 and ERGO-19 are marked Complete**; **R5-WR-01 and R5-WR-02 are accepted as recorded OPEN residuals — accepted at approval, not closed by it**; and **Phase 16 is unblocked**. Dave issued **no separate written rulings on the other residuals**; they carry forward in exactly the state the withdrawn entry and the review documents already record them in, which is open. Nothing in this entry should be read as closing a residual the approval did not close. **Why there was a withdrawal to supersede.** The `/gsd:execute-phase` workflow runs its `code_review_gate` step *after* the phase gate, so `gsd-code-reviewer` first ran against the phase's 45 changed Python files only once the 2026-08-05 approval was already logged. It returned **3 Critical, 7 Warning, 5 Info**, each Critical verified by execution rather than by inspection, and Dave withdrew rather than carry them forward — because the strongest of them was this phase's own defect class appearing inside the phase convened to eliminate it (a test whose docstring claimed four keys while its assertion covered three, with the fourth taking the path the test said it must not). The full withdrawal reasoning is the entry immediately above and is not restated. **The five review rounds, with their Critical counts and what each actually found — 3 → 1 → 1 → 1 → 0.** **Round 1 (`15.1-REVIEW.md`, 45 files, 3 Critical / 7 Warning / 5 Info):** CR-01, arbitrary-path read at the manifest seam — `_path_is_a_name_not_a_pattern` rejected only glob characters and applied no traversal or absolute-path test, so a file outside the deployment root was read, parsed and returned, and on `role: register` hashed into Control Plane state and replay dimension 2; CR-02, the `register_typed` walkaround was **three** example sites rather than the one recorded as advisory F-7, each registering author-versioned `ccf.prompt-artifact` past the D-05 bump check, with the guarding test claiming four keys and asserting over three; CR-03, the binder tripwire admitted the collapse it exists to prevent, because `_names_the_binder` tested only that the binder was *named* in scope, so a plan built with `required=declared.required_operator_keys` passed while making the binder's comparison unconditionally true. Warnings included a non-terminating `_aliases` on a cyclic assignment chain (reproduced at `exit=124` under `timeout 15` — a future edit of that shape would hang `pytest` with no output) and `_gate_collapses` red on correct code. **Round 2 (`15.1-REVIEW-FIXES.md`, 1 Critical / 6 Warning / 5 Info):** CR-01 confirmed closed against ten constructed escape shapes with a positive control, CR-02 confirmed closed at the code level with the test *strengthened* three keys to four — but **CR-F1**, the new CR-03 argument check was silently vacuous for any plan class not named `*Plan`. Six Warnings including WR-F2 (the alias and plan-source maps were not line-aware) and WR-F6 (the CR-02 documentation sweep was incomplete and the edited files pointed at unedited ones saying the opposite); five Infos, three of which (IN-F1, IN-F2, IN-F4) are `src/` boundary-validation items deliberately deferred and still open. **Round 3 (`15.1-REVIEW-FIXES-2.md`, 1 Critical / 3 Warning / 3 Info):** the plan recognition was now genuinely structural and survived four evasion shapes — but **`_bindings` read three binding forms out of eight**, so a plan local rebound by a tuple unpack, a `for` target or a `with … as` silently kept its stale binding, putting WR-F2 back on both sides. Also: a module-docstring measurement that did not reproduce (branch 1 matched 11 calls, not 35), an `any()` assertion licensing unreadable siblings, and the finding that `deferred-items.md`'s enumeration — the deliverable offered in place of finishing the sweep — was **itself a subset**. **Round 4 (`15.1-REVIEW-FIXES-3.md`, 1 Critical / 3 Warning / 2 Info):** the eleven-spelling enumeration was real and correct — but `_binding_before` was still strictly-below and **line**-granular, so **nine of the eleven certified spellings were admitted when the compound statement's suite was written on its own header line**, which is ordinary Python. Round 1's CR-01 inversion, one layer down. Its Warnings produced two of the residuals this entry carries: **R4-WR-03**, the loop-carried rebinding that lexical ordering cannot see, and a comprehension `until` that was a line range rather than a lexical scope. **Round 5 (`15.1-REVIEW-FIXES-4.md`, 0 Critical / 2 Warning / 4 Info):** the `(line, column)` position model closes all nine one-line and semicolon spellings, resolves a compound statement's *header* read to the previous binding, and pays none of the cost round 4 priced into its own proposed remedy. Every row of the three-way table, every cell of the nine-spelling table and the comprehension table reproduced under execution against **both** predecessors loaded side by side in one process; nineteen further probes hunting a tenth rebinding spelling all rejected; no `_opaque` token collision could be constructed; the floors and scanned-tree constants were untouched, 19 → 24 test functions with **none removed**, all six removed assertions replaced in place by the same or a stronger one, zero new suppressions, and **no `src/` change at all**. **The remediation plans that closed them:** 15.1-11 (manifest path containment, CR-01), 15.1-12 (CR-02 and the check that did not look), 15.1-13 (the binder tripwire made a check, CR-03), 15.1-14 (the binder check made structural, CR-F1), 15.1-15 (the two unguarded CR-02 sites and the half-finished sweep, WR-F5/WR-F6), 15.1-16 (every binding form, and the per-call property), 15.1-17 (positions, not lines). **Delivered — CR-1 (ERGO-18), re-verified clause by clause against the tree as it stands on 2026-08-06 rather than against the 2026-08-05 verification, whose line numbers the CR-01 containment fix has since moved by roughly a hundred lines:** `RegistrationMode = Literal["typed", "versioned"]` (`src/ccf/controlplane/artifacts.py:681`) and `ManifestEntry.registration: RegistrationMode` (`:833`) — a bare annotation, therefore required with no default (D-IMPL-85); **172 manifest entries across 11 example documents, every one carrying `registration` (172/172, counted by parsing the documents, not by grep and not by memory)** — which reconciles exactly with the withdrawn entry's 163 measured at plan 15.1-02, plus the nine `ccf.projection-requirements` entries that plans 15.1-04…07 added afterwards; `load_and_boot` dispatches at `src/ccf/deployment/manifest.py:436` with an `isinstance(outcome, RegistrationRefusal)` narrowing that turns a returned refusal into a loud raise, and an in-code comment stating there is **deliberately no fallback** to `register_typed`; `refuse_registration_the_sdk_fixes` (`src/ccf/deployment/classes.py:243`) runs in preflight at `manifest.py:407` against **every** entry before any document is opened; the `ccf.` reservation is a **prefix** test (`_CCF_NAMESPACE = "ccf."` at `classes.py:124`, applied at `:330`), not an id list (D-IMPL-84); `register_versioned` is declared on the Control Plane surface so a conforming plane cannot drop the D-05 bump check (D-IMPL-82); and the registration mode is a recorded fact on `ArtifactPin` (`src/ccf/deployment/pins.py:100`) that adds **no selector** — `ResolvedPins` still exposes exactly one public method, `version_for` at `:126` (D-IMPL-83). The refusal tests were re-run at this gate: `pytest tests/deployment/test_manifest.py -k "registration or squat or namespace_reservation"` → **6 passed, 31 deselected**, and they assert that the refusal *fires*, not that the examples happen to avoid the pattern. **And the CR-01 hole the post-gate review found in this same surface is closed:** `_leaves_its_own_directory` now refuses absolute, rooted, drive-qualified and `..`-bearing paths lexically, and `manifest.py:189-191` answers again against a **resolved** root (`resolved.is_relative_to(anchor)`), so a symlink out is refused where no string inspection could see it — a two-site rule the round-2 review confirmed neither half subsumes, driven against ten escape shapes plus a positive control that a symlink resolving back *inside* is admitted. **Delivered — CR-2 (ERGO-19), design E2, likewise re-verified:** the product-class artifact remains the Control Plane claim and fills `lineage.operators_invoked` only; the projection's required operator set is its own `ccf.projection-requirements` artifact — **nine** documents (00, 01, 02, 03, 04, 06, 07, 08, `external/00`), each `$schema`-headed and named by a manifest entry; `refuse_operator_set_divergence(*, declared, plan) -> None` (`src/ccf/deployment/binder.py:121-123`) compares the two as sets and refuses without repairing, called **between plan construction and `ProjectionEngine.execute`** — re-verified at `examples/00-quickstart/run.py`, plan at `:231-234`, binder at `:235`, `.execute(` at `:238` — and not inside `load_and_boot`, which has no `ProjectionPlan` in scope, per the D-IMPL-86 correction; Gate 2 is documented as **assembly-path integrity**, with a two-vocabulary sweep of `src/`, `runtime/`, `examples/`, `tests/` and `public/` returning **20** occurrences of "independent witness" or "two independent sources" and **every one of them a negation or a quoted-then-answered superseded claim**; and the tripwires now hold what round 1 found they did not — `tests/products/test_gate_two_call_shape.py` reads the binder's *arguments* as well as its name, recognizes plan constructions structurally rather than by a `*Plan` suffix, enumerates every binding form Python has and records the ones it cannot read through as **unreadable** rather than skipping them, and orders bindings and reads by source **position** `(line, column)` rather than by line. Re-run at this gate: `tests/deployment/test_binder.py` **5 passed**, `tests/products/test_gate_two_call_shape.py` **62 passed**, `tests/examples/test_gate_two_divergence.py` **2 passed**. **Verification matrix — observed at this gate, 2026-08-06, by the executor writing this entry, against `main` at `3a59098` plus this entry's own documentation changes. Every command below was run and its exit status read; a gate not run is a gate this entry cannot report:** `python -m tools.lints --all` **exit 0**; `make typecheck` **exit 0** — `no issues found in 170 source files`; `make test` **2105 passed / 2 skipped**, 105 warnings (the withdrawn entry's 2059 predates seven remediation plans and is superseded, not contradicted); `make examples` **exit 0** — all ten invoked and passed (00–08 plus `external/00`, `make` stopping on first failure); `make docs-strict` **exit 0** — pre-build scan, `mkdocs build --strict` (built in 2.00s), site scan; **`make ci` exit 0 — run twice, before and after this entry's own documentation changes, both green with identical pass and skip counts** (318.12s and 320.36s), because a matrix reported for a tree is a matrix that has to have been run on that tree; `make schemas` wrote **24** schemas and `git diff --exit-code docs/schemas/` **exit 0**; `make schema-headers` **exit 0** over **10** artifact directories; `tools/dump_surface.py --check` **exit 0** (plain bytes-equality on the live manifest); `tools/assemble_surface_changes.py --check` **exit 0**; `git diff --exit-code docs/surface/v0.2-surface-baseline.json` **exit 0** — the digest-pinned frozen baseline was not touched. Excluded by design and deliberately not "fixed": example 03's `--mode live` leg, `ext-00`'s live legs, example 08's `--live`, and the perf battery (D-10/D-11/D-IMPL-70). **Every open residual, named as OPEN. None of these is closed by this approval, and the list is longer than the withdrawn entry's because four review rounds added to it.** **(1) R5-WR-01 — `_Binding.until` covers the comprehension's outermost iterable, which Python evaluates in the *enclosing* scope. OPEN, accepted at approval.** `until` is `_after(node)` over the whole comprehension node and `_at(node)` is its `[`, so the outermost iterable sits inside the recorded span and `_binding_before` attributes its reads to the comprehension's own target. The docstring at `tests/products/test_gate_two_call_shape.py:823-824` claims containment in that span "is exactly Python's scope rule" — **it is not**, and the same over-claim is in `:1042-1052`'s "in force for reads *lexically inside* the comprehension … and for no other read". The divergence was **measured bidirectionally against `6cf00d3` with renamed-target controls**, by driving the shipped helpers rather than by argument: **(a)** a genuine Gate-2 collapse — `[x for ops in run_gates(candidate, engine_resolved=ops)]`, both sides reading the same prior `ops`, which is CR-2's shape exactly — now **ADMITs** where `6cf00d3` **REJECTed** (control with the target renamed: REJECT on all three versions); **(b)** correct code — `[row for plan in wrap(ProjectionEngine(reader=r).execute(plan=plan))]`, where the engine runs the **outer** plan the binder was handed — now **REJECTs** where `6cf00d3` **ADMITted** (control: ADMIT on all three; the multi-line spelling measures the same, so it is not a one-liner artifact). It is therefore a regression against `6cf00d3` in both directions. **The reviewer classified it Warning rather than Critical, and the reasoning is recorded here because it is why the phase closes with it open:** WR-01's trigger requires a comprehension whose target *shadows the very name read in its own outermost iterable*, with the gate or engine call inside that iterable, which is **strictly less reachable** than R4-WR-03 — the loop-carried rebinding this phase already leaves open with Dave's knowledge — and, in the reviewer's words, *"A finding cannot be Critical while a more reachable sibling is an accepted open item; calling it one would be inconsistent, not rigorous."* The classification was reasoned to on that ground and explicitly not to make the phase closable. Two candidate fixes are described in the round-five review and **neither was measured or certified**, which is why neither was applied at the gate: given this phase's five-round history of proposed remedies that recreate the defect one layer down, applying an unmeasured remedy would have been the same mistake a sixth time. What is owed regardless: the sentence at `:823-824` cannot stay as written, and the remedy belongs to whoever can measure it against the outermost-iterable **walrus** case, which the reviewer explicitly did not. No body in the tree uses either shape — 0 problems on both tripwires under `aedf81b` and HEAD alike — so `make ci` being green contradicts none of this; a gap cannot fail itself. **(2) R5-WR-02 — a measurement cell in the record that did not measure. The error is CORRECTED; the residual is the claim-discipline lesson, and that stays OPEN.** `15.1-17-SUMMARY.md:229`'s before-column read **ADMIT** where the unmodified `aedf81b` measures **REJECT** — via property (2), for the wrong reason (`handed` resolved to `None`, so the execution read as unbound). The verdict did not change between predecessor and HEAD; the *diagnosis* did, moving from "the engine runs a plan no binder was handed" to "the binder's two sides are one document". Rows 1 and 3 of that table reproduce exactly. **Corrected in place with a marked `CORRECTION` block at this gate (2026-08-06), and `:432`'s success-criterion row — "Every number in this SUMMARY is one I ran" — re-scoped in place rather than left standing.** What remains open is the discipline finding behind it: one figure in a round whose entire §6 exists to correct records that asserted more than the code did was reasoned to rather than run, and nothing structural prevents the next one. **(3) R-2 — the binder's *placement* residual. OPEN, unchanged, and re-affirmed by this approval rather than resolved by it.** The binder sits in adopter code between plan construction and `ProjectionEngine.execute`; it returns `None`, so nothing observable at runtime proves it was called and a deployment that omits it produces byte-identical output; nothing outside the SDK's own `examples/` tree holds a deployment to calling it; and the AST coverage check that enforces the call is scoped to `examples/` only. It is CR-1's failure mode — governance by convention outside the tree the SDK controls — at a different seam, and closing it is new fabric behavior that moves the D-15A abstraction line, which is Dave's ruling to make and was not made here. **(4) R-3 — the binder's *authorship* residual. OPEN, and structurally unclosable by the mechanism family in use** (no AST gate can distinguish an authored literal from a transcribed one — D-IMPL-86's own finding), so dual authorship remains convention-assisted at the authoring boundary. Distinct from R-2 and never to be merged with it; the round-five review re-verified that nothing in the new docstrings implies it is closed. **(5) R-4 — the HTTP `/schema` route's contract. OPEN.** The route, the `ccf schema export` CLI verb and `tools/dump_schemas.py` all enumerate `SDK_ARTIFACT_CLASSES` and answer **404** for the six author-versioned classes whose schemas *are* committed in `docs/schemas/` and which a manifest may now name for versioned registration. This phase sharpened the question and did not answer it. **(6) R4-WR-03 — a loop-carried rebinding is out of the Gate-2 tripwires' reach. OPEN by decision.** Both tripwires resolve a name to the binding textually last before the position that reads it, which is right for straight-line code and wrong for a loop; measured at **0 problems** on a body where, from the second iteration on, the engine runs a plan built from the binder's own `declared=`. Closing it needs control-flow rather than lexical ordering — a fixed point over a loop body, a different analysis from the single-pass AST walk this module is, and one whose termination argument would have to be rebuilt from scratch (two predecessors of `_resolve_at` failed exactly there, one by hanging `pytest` with no output). Plan 15.1-17 instead named it in **both** limits lists and asserted it *as a limit* in `test_a_loop_carried_rebinding_is_out_of_reach_and_says_so`, which **fails** if a later change closes it — the round-five review simulated exactly that and watched the test fail with the message it claims to carry. Note that R5-WR-01's Warning classification **depends on this item being open**; closing one without revisiting the other would leave the record inconsistent. **(7) The augmented-assignment mutable-set opacity. OPEN by decision** — `ops |= x` records an unreadable binding, so a body that mutates the lineage's own set in place and hands it to the gate is ADMITTED; no AST walk can tell the mutable case from the rebinding one, and recording the pre-`|=` path instead would emit a message that is untrue. Stated in the module's limits list. **(8) The attribute/subscript-target limit. OPEN** — such writes are unfollowed, never misfollowed, which is the conservative direction and is stated; but round five measured that the same "likewise" framing extended to `global`/`nonlocal` is **inaccurate when the redirected assignment lives in a nested body**, where the enclosing body's stale binding stays in force and answers affirmatively (measured ADMIT on both `aedf81b` and HEAD, with `global` identical), and the same reservation applies to `from m import *` at module level. A wording debt, not a mechanism debt. **(9) IN-F1, IN-F2 and IN-F4 — three `src/` boundary-validation items from round two, deliberately deferred and still OPEN.** IN-F1: `path: ""` and `path: "."` pass both containment checks and fail at the read with `IsADirectoryError`, which is **not among `load_and_boot`'s documented `Raises:`** — CLAUDE.md §11 puts validation at the boundary, and the fix is two lines. IN-F2: a hardlink inside the deployment to a file outside it is admitted, because `Path.resolve()` cannot see hardlinks; the practical gain to an attacker is narrow (write access inside the tree is also enough to copy the file), but `_document_inside_the_deployment`'s docstring reads as though the case were covered and it is not. IN-F4: the platform-invariance rule refuses the legal POSIX filename `a:b.yaml`, an accepted consequence whose cost is stated nowhere. All three were left because every round after round two was scoped to the tripwire modules with **no `src/` change at all** — a property those reviews verified and relied on. (Round two's IN-F3, the preflight/read TOCTOU, is recorded but not carried: the reviewer could not construct a case that does not already presuppose write access inside the deployment tree.) **(10) The stale-claim sweep is still incomplete for `examples/01`–`04` and `docs/surface-changes/`. OPEN.** Two superseded claims (D-IMPL-85's "a manifest entry naming an author-versioned class would silently drop the governance" and D-IMPL-84's "naming them here would resolve them to the open envelope") survive at enumerated sites in examples 01–04 and in the per-plan surface records. The enumeration has been corrected three times — it was a subset each time — and now carries a measured file and line per site, a **seventh phrasing** the six-phrasing input list did not contain, and a "checked and left, because they are TRUE" set that a mechanical sweep must not edit. **(11) Standing items unchanged by this phase:** D-IMPL-81 (pinning the deployment manifest itself); `BootedFabric.artifacts` role blurring; F-6's unaudited preflight registration refusals; and F-7's manifest-seam-versus-registry-seam asymmetry — the *examples* half of F-7 is closed (all three sites moved to `register_versioned`, and the guarding test widened from three keys to four), but whether the SDK should police the registry seam at all is still owed Dave's word. **All eleven are enumerated with their source review document in `.planning/phases/15.1-ergonomics-gap-closure/deferred-items.md`, which is the single file Phase 16 should read for what it inherits** — `docs/SURFACE-AUDIT.md` §6.4's five carried items are the 2026-08-05 set and are no longer the whole of it, which §6 now says in a dated note. **Also carried forward from the withdrawn entry and not disturbed:** the three of this phase's own acceptance criteria found **wrong as written** (Phase 16 must not inherit any of the three as a check), and the 8-versus-5 measurement correction. **The process finding, recorded prominently because it is the reason this entry exists and because Phase 16 must not repeat it.** The `/gsd:execute-phase` workflow runs its `code_review_gate` step **after** the phase gate. That ordering means a human can be — and here was — asked to approve a phase **before any code review of that phase existed**, and it is what put an approval on the record that had to be withdrawn a day later and re-earned across five review rounds and seven remediation plans. The ordering is the workflow's, not a judgment made in this phase. **A gate presentation must carry its code review**: for Phase 16 and after, the code review of a phase is a *precondition* of presenting that phase's gate to Dave, in the same way CLAUDE.md §3.1 already makes `/gsd-review` a precondition — the two are complementary, and this phase demonstrated that satisfying one does not substitute for the other. **What this entry does not disturb:** **D-IMPL-82 … D-IMPL-86** and the D-IMPL-86 correction, the D-GATE-15 second amendment, and R-5's ratification of the Criterion-6 gate edit made by plan 15.1-02 — the withdrawal explicitly did not disturb the design rulings, and this re-approval reinstates R-5's ratification on the same reasoning the withdrawn entry recorded, the narrowing having been independently re-verified there and untouched by any of the five review rounds. **R-1** — whether the pre-withdrawal one-working-reviewer cross-AI round sufficed — is superseded in substance rather than answered: five further deep review rounds have since been run against this phase, each executing the shipped code rather than reading it, and the thinness that R-1 recorded is no longer the condition under which the phase closes. **Phase 16 (Documentation Rewrite) may begin.** It must describe Gate 2 as assembly-path integrity; it must not inherit any of the three wrong criteria as a check; it must not claim the binder makes dual authorship structural; it inherits all eleven residuals above open; and it must carry its code review into its own gate presentation.

**D-IMPL-87 — Phase 15.2 halted at the 15.2-01 pre-execution gate: the prefix-rule amendment to D-01 is REJECTED; multi-segment pushdown is to be designed before any query-semantics prose is written.** Date: 2026-08-07. Decided by: Dave. **Origin.** Phase 15.2 plan 15.2-01, Task 0 — a blocking `checkpoint:decision` placed by planning because the plan set as drawn implements a *first-maximal-pushable-prefix* rule, which amends locked D-01's "one substrate query per pushable segment, with data-dependent later-segment seeds." Per CLAUDE.md §15, an amended reading of a locked decision is surfaced, never silently picked. **The verified finding presented at the checkpoint** (source-checked by the executor in-session): `CATEGORY_ORDER` at `src/ccf/operators/base.py:67` is `("policy", "similarity", "temporal", "traversal")` — Similarity sits between Policy and the two other pushable categories — and multi-segment push with survivors-as-seeds is **unsound under the locked D-02 query vocabulary**: a second pushed query can resurrect entities an in-process operator already excluded, an information-disclosure defect (RESEARCH Pitfall 2). Making multi-segment push sound requires growing the frozen query vocabulary — the checkpoint's candidate mechanism is an allowed-node-set, whose known weakness is that the set can be as large as the surviving view. **Options put to Dave:** (A) `amend-prefix-rule` — planner-recommended; push only the first maximal pushable prefix, everything after the first non-pushable operator runs in-process; leak-free, plan-static (D-04 holds), no vocabulary growth; cost is a named performance cliff for Similarity-bearing plans (prefix collapses to Policy alone). (B) `extend-vocabulary-now` — keep D-01 literal by adding the allowed-node-set now; cost is permanent vocabulary growth across every adapter and a full redraw of plans 15.2-01..12. **Dave's ruling and rationale, verbatim directive:** "If we don't fix this now we're just incurring tech debt. I need you to focus on minimizing tech debt and doing things the right way. This product has not been rolled out so let's make sure that we're providing the right solution rather than creating something that we're going to have to change anyway." On the explicit follow-up choice between shipping the prefix rule now versus halting to design multi-segment pushdown properly, Dave selected **halt-and-design-multi-segment**. **What this entry rules:** (1) Phase 15.2 execution is halted; no plan work is committed (15.2-01 stopped at Task 0 with nothing written; the 15.2-02 and 15.2-04b executors were stopped mid-first-read with single-file uncommitted edits, discarded with their worktrees; the only commit under the phase banner is the pattern-map/state commit `a5fb5e7`). (2) A **Phase D mini-cycle under CLAUDE.md §3.1** is convened to design multi-segment pushdown semantics — the allowed-node-set or a better mechanism — including its soundness argument against the resurrection defect, its scaling story against the shipping-whole-views objection, its replay-pin shape, and its adapter translation burden. (3) The mini-cycle's design ruling gets its own dated entry when Dave approves it; plans 15.2-01..12 are then redrawn against the ruled design via `/gsd:plan-phase 15.2`. **What this entry does not do:** it does not amend D-01 — D-01 stands as locked, and whether the ruled design preserves its letter (per-segment queries with seeds) or amends it is precisely the mini-cycle's question; it does not select the allowed-node-set mechanism — improvising that in-flight is what the checkpoint existed to prevent; and it does not disturb D-05's ordering commitment (semantics prose before fallback before adapters), which the redrawn plans inherit. Originates: Phase 15.2 planning, RESEARCH Open Question 1 / Pitfall 2, 15.2-01 Task 0.

**D-IMPL-87 (resolution, 2026-08-07) — multi-segment pushdown designed: the survivor set travels in the query; D-01 stands amended, not replaced.** Date: 2026-08-07. Decided by: Dave, in the Phase D mini-cycle the halt entry above convened, four decisions each presented with explicit options and consequences. **(1) Mechanism — in-query frozen field.** `GraphQuery` gains `allowed_entities: frozenset | None`. The query remains a pure value — `content_hash(q)` stays the per-round-trip replay pin unchanged, canonical ordering/serialization stay in the interface — and adapters translate the set natively (SQL `IN`/temp table, Cypher `UNWIND`, Mongo `$in`, fallback set-intersection); the wire realization is adapter-internal. Rejected: substrate session state (a handle is not replayable bytes — breaks query-as-value; adds state lifecycle to five adapters; buys nothing for memory/ProductSource), and per-adapter side channels (moves serialization out of the interface, violating locked roadmap constraint 3). **(2) Semantics — the uniform rule.** Every pushed segment after an in-process segment carries the survivor set, and every operator family in that segment computes within it: traversal may not cross outside the set, filters select within it; seeds keep exactly one meaning, walk starting points. This closes RESEARCH Pitfall 2's entity-resurrection defect — seeds say where walks start, not which nodes they may cross — and makes `apply(full_view) == pushed(intent)` provable per-plan on Similarity-bearing plans. Rejected: a traversal-only rule (seeds would mean two things by operator family; a future expanding operator would silently inherit the unsound default). `allowed_entities` is `None` on first segments and throughout fully-pushable plans, so the common no-Similarity case is untouched. **(3) Scaling — cap mechanism now, Control Plane value.** The engine ships a survivor-set size-cap mechanism; the threshold value lives in Control Plane configuration with **default `None` (no cap)** — mechanism-not-values per CLAUDE.md §11. Above cap, the segment runs in-process, which is always sound and free (the surviving view is already in memory after the in-process segment). The split thus depends on plan + data + Control Plane config, never the substrate — cross-substrate replay portability holds and the config pins into replay dimension 2. Rejected: always-push-no-cap (adding a cap later is a mid-flight mechanism change — the afterthought class this mini-cycle exists to avoid), and an SDK-default numeric cap (a threshold constant in `/src` is a domain value; No-Hardcoding). **(4) Replay pin — hash tuple in the record, bytes in the ArtifactStore.** The Decision Replay record carries an ordered tuple of per-round-trip `content_hash` values as a sentinel-defaulted field (the Pitfall-4/workflow-trace precedent); full canonical query bytes land in the ArtifactStore keyed by those hashes (§7.4 — immutable, content-addressed, exact-version resolution). Replay recomputes each query from the pinned snapshot + plan and compares hashes; an auditor resolves the literal question from the store without re-running. Rejected: hashes-only (an auditor mid-dispute cannot quote the question — weakens audit-by-construction for no saving once the store exists) and full-bytes-in-record (relocates the scaling objection into every decision record). **(5) Vocabulary versioning — prose lane only.** `docs/GRAPH_QUERY_SEMANTICS.md` carries a semver lane from day one (v1 includes `allowed_entities`); the `GraphQuery` contract has no version field — it evolves by additive sentinel-defaulted fields under frozen/`extra=forbid`, with the differential suite as the compatibility check. **Disposition:** D-01's letter — one substrate query per pushable segment with data-dependent later segments — **survives, made sound**; the prefix rule's performance cliff never ships. This entry is the mini-cycle's ruling of record per the halt entry's element (3); recorded as a D-IMPL-87 resolution rather than a new number so the halt-to-ruling chain reads as one thread (the D-IMPL-86-correction precedent). Full option/consequence record: `.planning/phases/15.2-substrate-adapters-and-query-pushdown/15.2-DISCUSSION-LOG.md`. **Consequences:** the 13 plans drawn against pre-amendment D-01 are void; `/gsd:plan-phase 15.2` redraws them against the amended CONTEXT.md; per CLAUDE.md §3.1 the mini-cycle closes with review and Dave's gate, and the 15.2-12 gate package re-ratifies this entry with full evidence. D-05's ordering (prose → fallback → adapters) is inherited unchanged — the semantics prose is where the uniform rule and the induced-subgraph detail (relationships require both endpoints in-set) get their normative sentences, and the differential corpus MUST include multi-segment (Similarity-bearing) plans.

**D-IMPL-88 — Fluree posture: CCF stays substrate-neutral; Fluree is an adapter and a source, never a foundation. Phase 15.3 (tier-1 Fluree adapter) inserted; the bank's KG approached as an external source.** Date: 2026-08-07. Decided by: Dave. **Origin.** A large financial institution CCF is courting develops its knowledge graph on Fluree. Dave had earlier considered building CCF as a layer on top of Fluree to leverage its KG capabilities and MCP surface, and decided against it; when the adapter request surfaced he asked for a recommendation on the posture — tight integration versus external KG. Feasibility research: `.planning/research/FLUREE-ADAPTER.md` (2026-08-07, committed `de08fe7`). **The ruling.** (1) **CCF is not built on Fluree.** Substrate neutrality at the storage layer mirrors harness neutrality at the loop layer (CLAUDE.md §1, §7.3): coupling the core to one vendor's engine would foreclose every adopter who runs a different one, and the capability overlap is a trap, not a shortcut — the research's load-bearing negative finding is that Fluree's native time travel is transaction-time and MUST NOT implement CCF's as-of (INV-CH7-14 filters on observed/superseded claim data; the two diverge on late arrivals and supersession), the same delegation hazard applying in kind to Fluree's policy engine versus the Control Plane and its ledger provenance versus the L axis. (2) **The bank's Fluree KG is an external *source*, not CCF's graph.** Their KG is a system of record; CCF's `G(E,R,T,P,Π,L)` is decision-ready context assembled *from* systems of record. Near-term path: L1 MCP-client ingress (§7.1) consuming Fluree's MCP server — existing mechanism, configuration only, no new SDK code. Longer-term: a tier-3 queryable external source, which remains **deferred** per the Phase 15.2 insertion ruling (Dave, 2026-08-07) — this entry does not open tier 3. (3) **A tier-1 Fluree substrate adapter is ruled in as Phase 15.3** (INSERTED, after 15.2's gate) — the consolidation option for deployments that want CCF's Context Graph co-located on Fluree they already operate. Research basis: Fluree v4 line (Rust rewrite, GA 2026-04, BSL 1.1 → Apache 2.0; the retrieved Additional Use Grant restricts only database-service hosting, so CI containers and wire-only adapters are clean; AGPL applies only to the legacy v2 ledger line); v4 speaks openCypher over Bolt, making the 15.2-12 Neo4j plan the direct translation template; no new Python dependency (httpx wire client — `fluree-py` is not on PyPI, an unregistered squattable name flagged as a supply-chain hazard; `aioflureedb` targets the dead v2 API); lightest of the container CI legs. (4) **The one-sentence 15.2-02 hedge is applied now** (the only 15.2 artifact whose shape could foreclose an HTTP substrate): the `_GraphTxn` write-seam Protocol must be implementable by a remote substrate whose commit is a single batched transaction (no held session), with per-operation rowcounts resolvable before commit — sound under INV-CH7-10 single-writer (read-before-buffer cannot be invalidated by a concurrent writer). Applied as a must_haves truth, a docstring obligation, and an acceptance criterion on plan 15.2-02 Task 1; the 15.2 plan set is otherwise untouched and remains as verified. **Preconditions before 15.3 is planned** (recorded, not defaulted, per CLAUDE.md §15): (a) confirm the institution runs the v4 line — a v2-AGPL shop changes the deliverable entirely; Dave to confirm with the bank; (b) the write-transaction posture (buffered batch recommended; Bolt explicit transactions rejected — Raft/peer deployments refuse BEGIN, a landmine for the exact enterprise clusters motivating the adapter; per-op autocommit rejected — abandons write atomicity) is 15.3 discuss-phase's to ratify. **What this entry does not do:** it does not amend Phase 15.2's scope (14 plans stand as checker-verified); it does not open tier 3; and it does not commit the bank engagement to the adapter — the source path (MCP ingress) proceeds independently and first. Originates: author conversation (Dave, 2026-08-07), `.planning/research/FLUREE-ADAPTER.md` Q1–Q8.

**D-IMPL-89 — Replay artifact resolution is byte-fidelity: `resolve_artifact` verifies the stored content address against the raw fetched bytes, before model validation — never by re-hashing a re-validated model.** Date: 2026-08-07. Decided by: Dave. **Origin.** Plan 15.2-08's mandatory A5 verification (Task 1, pre-committed STOP clause) found the assumption false: `src/ccf/replay/engine.py::resolve_artifact` parsed the fetched payload with the *current* record class, re-hashed the rebuilt model, and raised `ReplayDivergence` on mismatch. Because canonicalization serializes sentinel-defaulted fields added by later contract versions, an honest, untampered record written before any record-contract extension re-hashes to a different address and trips the tamper check. Empirical repro: the pre-I.3 `PRE_CHANGE_CANONICAL` record literal (tests/replay/test_harness_trace.py) — put-time address `26cdaded112c219b…`, re-derived `ad21e6abc5633f99…`. The same latent break applies to every prior record extension (I.2 agent fields, I.3 `harness_trace`, I.4 `hitl_actions`/`workflow_step_sequence`); it never surfaced because no fixture crosses a model-version boundary. **The ruling (option a of three presented).** `resolve_artifact` verifies `sha256(raw fetched bytes) == content_address` *before* validation; the re-validated-model re-hash is removed as the tamper check. Tamper detection (T-04-17) keeps identical cryptographic strength: the put-time address *is* the sha256 of the stored canonical bytes (`file_store.py`), and the ArtifactStore Protocol's `get` contract already promises the exact stored bytes. The check becomes model-class-independent, which makes the sentinel-default extension strategy (D-IMPL-87 lineage) actually deliver its compatibility promise — retroactively for the four prior extensions and for 15.2-08's dimension-5/dimension-2 fields. A regression test must resolve a genuinely pre-extension record (the existing `PRE_CHANGE_CANONICAL` literal qualifies). Rejected: (b) accepting a migration boundary — permanently exempts every record-touching SDK upgrade from INV-CH7-79/86 forensic reconstruction; (c) schema-versioned multi-class resolution — out of proportion for v0. **Scope.** `src/ccf/replay/engine.py` is added to plan 15.2-08's `files_modified`; the plan otherwise proceeds as drawn. Originates: 15.2-08 executor checkpoint (2026-08-07), D-IMPL-26 territory (artifact addressing), T-04-17.

**D-IMPL-90 — RAISED (as OPEN): claim-JSON timestamp rendering diverges from the canonical rendering, so the pushed SQL validity predicate and the fallback disagree on an offset-authored claim (`GQ-31` byte-identity has a reachable counterexample).** Date raised: 2026-08-08. Raised by: plan 15.2-14, the phase-close audit, on a BLOCKING finding from the §3.1 cross-AI self-review (Codex). Status: **RESOLVED the same day — see the `D-IMPL-90 (resolution, 2026-08-08)` entry below, which rules option (a). The text below is preserved exactly as raised, per the append-only discipline of the D-IMPL-86-correction and D-IMPL-87-resolution precedents: what was known at the moment of escalation is part of the record.** **Origin.** Plan 15.2-09 recorded the corner as DI-15.2-03 and routed it to the phase-close audit. The audit measured it rather than restating it, and the measurement changes the analysis materially. **The measured facts** (probe against the tree, 2026-08-08, one `EntityClaim` authored with `valid_from = 2026-03-01T08:00:00+05:00`): (1) the contract tier accepts the offset-aware datetime and stores it verbatim on the model; (2) `claim.model_dump(mode="json")` — which is exactly what `src/ccf/graph/_writer.py` persists into `claim_json` — renders `'2026-03-01T08:00:00+05:00'`, keeping the offset; (3) `canonical_bytes(claim)` renders `'2026-03-01T03:00:00.000000Z'`, normalizing to UTC. **The two serializers disagree about the same claim**, and the pushed SQL validity predicate compares canonical UTC *text*, so both SQL backends render the non-`Z` form as NULL (`sqlite.py::_utc_fixed_width`, `postgres.py::_utc_fixed_width`) and exclude a record the fallback — comparing datetime *values*, which are offset-aware and compare correctly — includes. Same question, same data, different bytes by path: a reachable counterexample to `GQ-31`, the differential byte-identity clause that is this phase's central conformance claim. **What the measurement corrects.** DI-15.2-03 and the review both assumed a contract-tier UTC normalization would move claim content hashes and therefore constitute a replay-compatibility break. **It would not.** Claim identity is `content_hash(_entity_content_key(claim))` (`src/ccf/graph/_writer.py`), which passes the `datetime` value through `canonical_bytes` — already UTC-normalized per fact (3). The identity lane and the payload lane are independent, and only the payload lane keeps the offset. Answer bytes are likewise unaffected for `Z`-form claims, because every answer is re-canonicalized on the way out. **Options, with consequences.** (a) **Normalize the persisted payload** — have the writer render `claim_json` through the canonical serializer instead of `model_dump(mode="json")`. Closes the divergence for every adapter at once, at the source, and per the measurement moves no content hash and no answer bytes. Cost: it changes what is persisted, so rows already written with the offset form keep it and stay mis-compared — a data-migration question, which at pre-release v0 may be moot but is Dave's to declare moot. Touches `src/ccf/graph/_writer.py`, a file this plan does not own. (b) **Align the adapters** — teach both SQL dialects to order offset-bearing RFC3339 correctly (SQLite `strftime` with a timezone modifier, Postgres `::timestamptz`). Leaves persistence untouched and fixes existing rows too. Cost: real two-dialect SQL rework with a new test surface, at a phase gate, on a predicate that is currently fail-closed — the highest-risk option and the reason the executor did not take it unilaterally. (c) **Amend the prose** — define the `GQ-07`/`GQ-09` comparisons over canonical UTC text and declare an offset-authored claim datetime out of vocabulary. Cheapest, and explicitly **not recommended**: it would have the normative document bless a known fallback/adapter disagreement rather than resolve it, which is the papering-over CLAUDE.md §11 forbids. (d) **Accept and document** — carry the corner with a corpus case that exercises it and a stated alignment rule. Honest, but deliberately softens `GQ-31`. **Risk posture while open.** The divergence direction is **exclusion**, never inclusion: a non-`Z`-rendered claim is dropped from a pushed `require_valid_at_boundary` answer where the fallback would have kept it. No principal sees anything they should not, and the T-15.2-03/T-15.2-26 information-disclosure posture is strictly preserved. The corner also requires an offset-authored claim datetime, and nothing in the SDK, the examples, or the fixtures produces one — every in-repo path renders `Z`, which is why the differential corpus never caught it. **Why this is an entry and not a fix.** CLAUDE.md §3.1: a blocking finding that cannot be fixed without a decision Dave has not made is a `D-IMPL-NN` entry and a stop. Every option above is either a persisted-format change, a substantive adapter rework outside this plan's scope, or a normative-prose concession — none of which is an executor's call at a close-out gate. Originates: plan 15.2-09 (DI-15.2-03), plan 15.2-14 §3.1 self-review, `GQ-09`/`GQ-31`, threats T-15.2-03/T-15.2-26.

**D-IMPL-90 (resolution, 2026-08-08) — OPTION (a): the writer renders `claim_json` through the canonical serializer. One instant, one rendering, everywhere in the fabric.** Date: 2026-08-08. Decided by: Dave, on the escalation entry above, at the D-GATE-15.2 checkpoint. **The ruling.** `ccf.graph._writer` persists `claim_json` as `canonical_bytes(...)` of the claim's non-lineage fields (the new `_claim_payload` helper) instead of `json.dumps(claim.model_dump(mode="json", exclude={"lineage"}), sort_keys=True)`. The divergence is closed **at its source, for every adapter at once**, rather than by teaching two SQL dialects to order offset-bearing RFC3339 (option (b)), by having the normative prose bless a known disagreement (option (c) — explicitly not recommended and not taken), or by documenting the corner and softening `GQ-31` (option (d)). **The warrant is the measurement, not the argument.** The escalation's own probe established that the identity lane and the payload lane are independent: claim identity is `content_hash(_entity_content_key(claim))`, which passes the `datetime` **value** through `canonical_bytes` — already UTC-normalized — so an offset-authored claim and a `Z`-authored claim of the same instant always shared one content hash, and answers are re-canonicalized on the way out of `ccf.graph.reader` regardless of what was on disk. The fix was then verified against that prediction as a **single-variable A/B on one tree** — the same fixed content written into a fresh fabric twice, once with the legacy renderer monkeypatched back in, across **three backends × two authoring forms × two renderers (12 arms)**, 2026-08-08. The aggregate claim-identity digest over every persisted `claim_id` is `f7f27f52063700d5…` in **all twelve arms** — memory, sqlite and postgres, offset-authored and UTC-authored, legacy renderer and canonical alike: the identity lane does not move, at all, under any combination. The aggregate answer-bytes digest over a fixed three-boundary × three-question battery is `79ede0b085eaa82c…` under **both** renderers for UTC-authored content on all three backends: no answer bytes move for content already in the fabric. The single thing that moves is the defect: for **offset-authored** content the legacy renderer answers digest `9e794faade451d52…` with an **empty** `require_valid_at_boundary` answer on sqlite *and* postgres, and the canonical renderer restores it to `79ede0b085eaa82c…` — byte-identical to the UTC-authored answer, which is the strongest available statement of the ruling: **the answer no longer depends on how the source spelled the instant.** (The memory backend was correct under both renderers, as predicted — it compares datetime values in-process and never reads the payload as text.) No content hash moved. No answer bytes moved. What moved is the bytes at rest, which is exactly and only what option (a) proposed to move. **The migration assumption, stated rather than assumed.** Rows already persisted in the offset form keep it and would stay mis-compared; option (a) is therefore sound only if such rows are regenerable. Dave declared them so — **the project is pre-release v0 with no shipped release, so any store holding the offset form is dev-store data**. The executor verified nothing in the tree contradicts that declaration before proceeding, as instructed: there is **no migration machinery of any kind** (the SQL backends create their schema with `CREATE TABLE IF NOT EXISTS` and there is no `ALTER TABLE`, no `schema_version`, and no `migrat*` path anywhere under `src/ccf/graph/`); there is **no publishing path** (no PyPI/twine step in any of the three GitHub workflows, and the repository is private per CLAUDE.md §2.2 — the `v0.1.0` tag is the internal `D-GATE-I` phase marker of 2026-07-31, not a released artifact); and **no fixture pins the offset form on disk** (a tree-wide sweep for non-`Z` offsets in `src/`, `tests/` and `examples/` returns only in-memory parsing helpers and HITL `+00:00` action timestamps — nothing that persists a claim payload). The assumption is auditable, and a future deployment that has shipped rows before adopting this change needs a re-write of `claim_json`, not a code change. **What shipped.** (1) `src/ccf/graph/_writer.py::_claim_payload`, used by both `upsert_entity` and `upsert_relationship`, carrying the reasoning above in its docstring. (2) Durable cross-backend regression coverage, because a decision entry is not a test: the differential corpus (`tests/conformance/query_corpus.py`) gains an `offset_authored_validity_cases` shape — the chain's validity window re-expressed in a drawn non-UTC offset, over open, closing-after-boundary and closing-before-boundary windows, asked through the validity family alone and crossed with the policy, traverse and `allowed_entities` families — so `GQ-31` byte-identity is now asserted over an offset-authored claim on **every** registered backend and on the tier-2 mounted source; and `tests/conformance/test_substrate_differential.py::test_offset_authored_validity_answers_identically_on_each_reader` is the named per-param regression, carrying a non-empty control (the chain must actually be answered, so the test cannot pass by both paths excluding everything) and an identity twin (re-admitting the same claims authored in UTC returns `claim_created` false — the direct in-test evidence that the identity lane was already normalized). Both were confirmed to **fail** under the legacy renderer on sqlite and postgres and to pass under the canonical one, so the coverage is proven to catch the defect rather than assumed to. **Postgres needed nothing beyond the writer.** Re-verification appeared at first to show the fix holding on sqlite, memory and mongo but *not* on postgres, which would have meant option (a) could not reach every adapter without the dialect work option (b) was rejected for. It was a measurement artifact, and it was diagnosed by probe rather than patched around: a by-hand `pytest -m postgres` run inside the execution worktree resolved `import ccf` through the editable install's `sys.meta_path` finder, whose `MAPPING` is pinned to the **main checkout** — so the leg was exercising unmodified code, and `_claim_payload` was not even present on the imported module. The probe dumped the persisted payload directly out of the postgres table: `"valid_from": "2026-03-01T00:00:00-08:00"` with spaced JSON separators — the legacy rendering verbatim. With the tree's own source on the path the same probe shows `"2026-03-01T08:00:00.000000Z"`, the width-normalizing CASE returning a value instead of NULL, and all three chain entities surviving the pushed predicate. `make ci` was never affected (the Makefile has always exported `PYTHONPATH=src:runtime`), which is why it stayed green throughout while a hand-run leg did not. The postgres adapter was correct as shipped; no line of it changed for this ruling. **Scope held.** `ccf.graph.product_source`'s synthetic rows still render `model_dump(mode="json")` and are deliberately untouched: that payload is round-tripped in-process through `json.loads` + `model_validate` and is never compared as text by any predicate, so it carries no divergence — verified rather than presumed. The Neo4j and Mongo adapters were already immune: both re-canonicalize the validity window at write time into `valid_from_cmp`/`valid_to_cmp` projections. `docs/GRAPH_QUERY_SEMANTICS.md` is **unamended** — the prose was right and the adapter was wrong, which is the whole reason option (c) was rejected. The `_utc_fixed_width` fail-closed CASE in both SQL dialects stays as defence in depth. Originates: the escalation entry above; `GQ-09`/`GQ-31`; DI-15.2-03; threats T-15.2-03/T-15.2-26.

**D-IMPL-87 (pointer correction, 2026-08-08) — the resolution entry's "the 15.2-12 gate package" means what is now plan 15.2-14.** Date: 2026-08-08. Decided by: Dave, at the D-GATE-15.2 checkpoint. Recorded as an appended correction rather than an edit to the entry above, following the D-IMPL-86-correction precedent, so the reasoning at each point in time survives. The `D-IMPL-87 (resolution, 2026-08-07)` entry closes with the commitment that "**the 15.2-12 gate package re-ratifies this entry with full evidence**". That sentence was written before `/gsd:plan-phase 15.2` redrew the phase against the amended CONTEXT.md, and the redraw produced a **fourteen**-plan set whose phase-close plan is **15.2-14**; plan 15.2-12 became the Neo4j backend plan. The commitment is unchanged in substance and is discharged by the 15.2-14 gate package, whose re-ratification register carries the six evidence lines (mechanism, semantics, soundness, scaling, replay, versioning) the resolution names. Nothing about the ruling moves; only the plan number the sentence points at. Recorded so the halt → ruling → re-ratification chain reads cleanly end to end without a reader having to reconstruct the redraw. Originates: plan 15.2-14 §3.1 close review, checkpoint question Q5.

**D-GATE-15.2 (checkpoint dispositions, 2026-08-08) — Dave's rulings on the three non-gating questions the gate package put to him. The gate itself is NOT yet given.** Date: 2026-08-08. Decided by: Dave, on the `15.2-14-SUMMARY.md` checkpoint questions Q2, Q3 and Q4. Recorded now, separately from the gate, because these three are settled while the phase approval is still pending — a reader must be able to see which is which, and this entry deliberately does **not** close the phase. **Q2 — `MAX_DEPTH_CEILING = 100` is RATIFIED as-is.** Two of the three §3.1 reviewers read the named constant in `src/ccf/graph/backends/neo4j.py` as a CLAUDE.md §11 "mechanism, not values" violation; the executor triaged it advisory and put it to Dave explicitly, as plan 15.2-14 required. Dave rules it **mechanism**: it is an injection-safety ceiling on a literal-inlining site — the one place a caller-supplied depth is rendered into generated Cypher text rather than bound as a parameter — and it bounds *the generated query string*, not the domain. It changes **which path answers, never the answer**: a plan whose depth exceeds it declines to translate and the D-04 fallback answers, byte-identically, which is exactly the `GQ-32`/`GQ-34` decline contract every adapter already honours. A domain value would change results; this changes only whether the work happens in Cypher or in process. It stays where it is, at the value it has, under the name it has. **Q3 — SUBQ-01, SUBQ-04 and SUBQ-08 stay Complete.** One reviewer argued SUBQ-01 could not be Complete while `D-IMPL-90` was open. Dave rules with the executor's reading: the prose decides `GQ-09` unambiguously, so the vocabulary requirement was met; what `D-IMPL-90` described was an *adapter* failing to implement a decided clause, which is SUBQ-04/SUBQ-08 territory — and with the ruling above that defect is now closed with cross-backend regression coverage, so the question is moot in the direction that keeps the rows Complete. No requirement row moves. **Q4 — the perf run is ACCEPTED as evidence; the re-baseline decision stays OPEN and separate.** The six gated FAILs recorded in `15.2-14-SUMMARY.md` §5 are accepted as an honest measurement of the phase's interim read path, not as a correctness finding: the phase's correctness evidence is the byte-identity and invariant work, and none of it depends on a median. Whether to re-baseline is the **`D-IMPL-75`-gated** decision and it is **not** taken here — `perf/baselines/` and `perf/thresholds.toml` remain byte-untouched by this phase (`git diff --exit-code` exit 0), and PERF-04's re-baseline ritual remains the documented response whenever Dave chooses to invoke it. Keeping (a) correctness approval and (b) re-baseline approval as two decisions is the whole point of `T-15.2-19`; this entry keeps them two. **What is still open.** `D-GATE-15.2` itself — the phase approval, the merge to `main`, and the D-IMPL-87 re-ratification sentence — awaits Dave's review of the gate package. Nothing in this entry grants it. Originates: plan 15.2-14 checkpoint questions Q2/Q3/Q4; CLAUDE.md §3.1, §11; `D-IMPL-75`; threats T-15.2-18/T-15.2-19.

**D-GATE-15.2 — Phase 15.2 (Substrate Adapters & Query Pushdown) APPROVED.** Date: 2026-08-08. Approved by: Dave. **Approval form.** Approved with one condition, which was satisfied before this entry was written: fix `DI-15.2-04` (the container-leg harness hazard) rather than carry it. Commit `0a47496` adds `make legs` / `leg-postgres` / `leg-neo4j` / `leg-mongo`; `DI-15.2-04` is closed. **What the phase delivered.** Five substrates — sqlite, postgres, memory, Neo4j, MongoDB — answer the same `GraphQuery` byte-identically to an independently audited fallback, each with native pushdown proven by its own `translated_answers` telemetry rather than by byte-identity alone (byte-identity is satisfied by a never-translating adapter under D-04/GQ-34, so it is a conformance bar, not a pushdown bar). The query semantics are fixed in normative prose (`docs/GRAPH_QUERY_SEMANTICS.md` 1.0.1, 35 `GQ-NN` clause anchors, its own semver lane); the write seam is typed and substrate-agnostic (`_GraphTxn`, nine operations, first exercised by a non-SQL backend); operators declare pushability via `read_intent()` and the engine owns segmentation under the D-IMPL-87 uniform rule; replay pins the ordered query-hash tuple in dimension 5 with the literal query bytes in the ArtifactStore and the D-10 survivor cap in dimension 2; product classes enforce accepted attestation modes; and admitted A2A products mount as tier-2 read sources. **Gate evidence, re-verified on `main` at `597171b` after merge** (not inherited from worktree runs — the `DI-15.2-04` artifact is exactly why): `make ci` exit 0, lint 0 findings, `mypy --strict` clean across 176 files, 2270 passed / 44 skipped, all ten examples OK, docs-strict green; container legs each run fail-closed under `CCF_REQUIRE_*` — postgres 25 passed against PostgreSQL 16.14, neo4j 20 passed against Neo4j 5.26.29, mongo 22 passed against MongoDB 7.0.39; surface `--check` and `make schemas` both bytes-equal; `perf/baselines/` and `perf/thresholds.toml` byte-untouched across the entire phase (`266e72b..HEAD`). **Two decisions escalated mid-phase and ruled, both pre-existing defects rather than phase regressions:** `D-IMPL-89` (replay's tamper check re-hashed re-validated models, so records written before any of four prior contract extensions would fail verification — resolved as byte-fidelity resolution) and `D-IMPL-90` (the persisted payload kept authored UTC offsets while everything else normalized, giving `GQ-31` a reachable counterexample — resolved as payload normalization at the source, option (a), proven across 12 measurement arms to move no content hash and no answer bytes). **§3.1 self-review:** three independent reviewers, none approved as submitted; three blocking findings fixed in-phase, one escalated (`D-IMPL-90`) and resolved, one refuted on the record as referencing code that does not exist. **Checkpoint dispositions ruled at this gate** are recorded in the `D-GATE-15.2 (checkpoint dispositions)` entry: `MAX_DEPTH_CEILING = 100` ratified as an injection-safety ceiling (changes which path answers, never the answer); SUBQ-01/04/08 stay Complete; perf accepted as evidence with the D-IMPL-75 re-baseline decision left open and separate. **Carried forward, named:** `GQ-01`'s wording routed to the `GQ-35` lane; `_writer.py` at ~2300 lines as a consequence of the CINV-02 one-module rule now that five backends live behind the seam — a `ccf.graph._writer` package would preserve confinement while improving structure. **Next:** Phase 15.3 (tier-1 Fluree adapter, inserted by D-IMPL-88), whose two preconditions remain open and are not defaulted — confirming the institution runs the Fluree v4 line, and ratifying the write-transaction posture at discuss-phase. **APPENDED CORRECTION (2026-08-08, Dave's ruling; original text above preserved per the D-IMPL-86/-87 amendment discipline).** The sentence above reading "each with native pushdown proven by its own `translated_answers` telemetry" overclaims by one adapter. **Four** substrates — sqlite, postgres, Neo4j, MongoDB — translate `GraphQuery` natively and each carries its own discriminating pushdown telemetry. The **memory** backend does not and never will: `MemoryGraphReader` has no `_translate_query`, no `translated_answers`/`fallback_answers` counters, and does not subclass `BaseGraphReader` — it is the deliberate zero-engine floor (D-06) that answers everything through `ccf.graph.fallback`, which is precisely what makes it the adversarial floor for the seam and the differential control for the Neo4j pushdown test. The substance of the gate is unaffected: the discriminating-telemetry claim was independently falsification-tested at phase verification (every `_translate_query` monkeypatched to return `None`; all pushdown assertions failed while every differential suite stayed green — `15.2-VERIFICATION.md`), and byte-identity to the audited fallback holds for all five. Raised as WARNING-1 by the independent phase verifier, which correctly declined to edit an approved gate entry silently.

**D-IMPL-91 — Phase 15.3 ships a Fluree **v4-only** adapter. No v2 support, now or later; D-IMPL-88's precondition (a) is dissolved rather than answered.** Date: 2026-08-08. Decided by: Dave. **Origin.** D-IMPL-88 (2026-08-07) inserted Phase 15.3 and recorded two preconditions before planning, the first being "confirm the institution runs the v4 line — a v2-AGPL shop changes the deliverable entirely; Dave to confirm with the bank." Asked to route Phase 15.3, Dave ruled directly on scope: build only for v4. **The ruling and what it changes.** The adapter targets the Fluree **v4** line exclusively — the Rust rewrite (GA 2026-04, BSL 1.1 → Apache 2.0, openCypher over Bolt). The legacy **v2** ledger line is out of scope permanently, not deferred: no v2 adapter, no dual-line abstraction, no version-negotiation seam. This **dissolves precondition (a)** instead of satisfying it — what the institution currently runs stops being a planning gate and becomes an engagement question, because the deliverable no longer varies with the answer. A v2 shop is told to upgrade or to use the *source* path (L1 MCP-client ingress, §7.1), which D-IMPL-88 already established as available now, configuration-only, and independent of the adapter. **Why this is the right shape and not merely the cheap one.** (1) It removes the one branch that could have forced version-shaped conditionals into a substrate adapter, which is exactly the vendor coupling CLAUDE.md §1 forbids at the storage layer — a v4/v2 abstraction inside `ccf.graph.backends.fluree` would be a second seam serving one vendor's release history. (2) The three technical warrants in D-IMPL-88's research all point at v4 and none survive on v2: v4 speaks openCypher over Bolt, making the 15.2-12 Neo4j plan the direct translation template; the licence analysis that clears CI containers and wire-only adapters is BSL-1.1-on-v4 (AGPL applies only to the v2 line); and the only sane Python client story is a plain `httpx` wire client against v4 (`aioflureedb` targets the dead v2 API, and `fluree-py` is an unregistered, squattable PyPI name flagged as a supply-chain hazard). Supporting v2 would mean an AGPL-adjacent leg and a dependency the research explicitly refused. (3) It keeps the adapter honest as a *demonstration* of the 15.2 no-refactor claim: one substrate, one translation, going green against unchanged conformance and differential suites. **What this entry does not do.** It does not open tier 3 (queryable external source — still deferred). It does not commit the bank engagement to the adapter; the MCP-ingress source path proceeds independently and first. It does not resolve D-IMPL-88's **precondition (b)** — the write-transaction posture (buffered batch recommended; Bolt explicit transactions rejected because Raft/peer deployments refuse `BEGIN`; per-op autocommit rejected as abandoning write atomicity) remains `/gsd:discuss-phase 15.3`'s to ratify, and it is the only precondition still standing. Originates: author conversation (Dave, 2026-08-08); D-IMPL-88 precondition (a); `.planning/research/FLUREE-ADAPTER.md` Q2/Q5/Q7.

**D-IMPL-92 — The permitted-change surface for adding a storage substrate: there is no registration seam, the enumeration *is* the mechanism, and the no-refactor claim is stated in two layers (normative rule + mechanically-enforced proxy).** Date: 2026-08-08. Decided by: Dave, on blocking finding BF-1 raised by the Phase 15.3 research. **Origin.** Phase 15.3's CONTEXT.md D-10 committed the phase to a script that "enumerates the permitted change surface and **fails on anything outside it**", and D-11 stated the hard line as "nothing under `src/ccf` changes except the adapter module and the `_writer.py` additions." The 15.3 research measured that claim against the MongoDB landing — the most recent substrate added post-design — and found it false as literally worded. `git show --stat` over `dfb2f80`, `e854946`, `761fba1`: alongside the three permitted paths (`backends/mongo.py`, `_writer.py`, the `artifacts.py` `Literal`), the landing necessarily touched `src/ccf/boot.py` (+63/−13), `src/ccf/deployment/manifest.py` (+20), `tests/conftest.py` (+40), `pyproject.toml` (+23/−1), the generated `docs/schemas/ccf.deployment-manifest.json` (+5), and the surface-changes fragments (+185/+181). **The structural finding, which is the durable part of this entry.** `src/ccf/boot.py:118-218` (`_open_backend`) selects the substrate by explicit argument and refuses to infer (`selected != 1` → `ValueError`); `src/ccf/deployment/manifest.py:215-263` (`_preflight_backend`) maps `manifest.backend` → required connection argument. **There is no registration seam. The enumeration in those two files *is* the mechanism.** A backend absent from both cannot be booted and cannot be declared in a manifest. Phase 15.3's own CONTEXT.md `<code_context>` said as much ("Boot exclusivity wiring — single-writer enforcement at boot, same shape as the other four backends"), contradicting its own D-10 list in the same document. Recorded here rather than only in the phase record so that substrate #7 does not rediscover it. **The ruling — two layers, because a rule that cannot be checked is the narration D-10 exists to prevent.** *Layer 1, normative (what the claim asserts).* D-11's hard line is relaxed for the minimum registration surface the existing boot and deployment mechanism requires. Permitted for any new backend, each scoped to the named block and nothing else: `src/ccf/boot.py` — only the explicit backend-selection / exclusivity block; `src/ccf/deployment/manifest.py` — only the backend → required-connection-argument mapping; `src/ccf/controlplane/artifacts.py` — only the `backend` `Literal` member (already permitted by D-10; named here so the allowlist is complete); `tests/conftest.py` — only the fixtures needed to exercise the new backend; `pyproject.toml` and the generated `docs/schemas/ccf.deployment-manifest.json` — only the entries required to declare, mark and install the backend. All other paths under `src/ccf` remain restricted; any further expansion requires a new `D-IMPL`. Documentation (`docs/SURFACE-CHANGES.md` and its per-plan fragment), backend-specific unit tests, CI workflows and the `Makefile` were never inside D-11's `src/ccf`-scoped hard line and are free. *Layer 2, mechanical (what a phase's gate script actually runs).* Region scoping is the right rule and is not greppable, so the script enforces a deliberately conservative proxy, and both layers are recorded together so a reader can see which is the claim and which is its approximation: (1) a **path allowlist** — any changed path outside it fails; (2) **no existing backend name is removed** from any enumeration; (3) **per-file added-line budgets** taken from the mongo precedent (`boot.py` ≤ 70, `manifest.py` ≤ 25, `artifacts.py` ≤ 10, `conftest.py` ≤ 45) — a change beyond budget in a registration file is the refactor the claim denies; (4) an **AST assertion** that no existing class or function body in `src/ccf/graph/_writer.py` is modified, only new ones added. The budgets are an admitted proxy for "extension, not modification"; they are to be tightened to a direct AST check (enumeration members added, nothing else touched) if any future phase finds them gameable. **Why the relaxed claim is stronger, not weaker.** It reads, truthfully: *adding a real substrate touches one new module, one writer class, and a bounded one-line extension of four existing enumerations — no mechanism anywhere in the SDK changed.* The original wording asserted something **no** substrate could satisfy, which would have forced either a phase that cannot close or an executor quietly widening the list — the "narrated, not checked" outcome D-10 exists to prevent. A claim that survives contact with the tree is worth more than a stricter one that cannot. **What is NOT reopened.** D-10's requirement that the surface be script-checked and committed with the gate package. D-11's hard line on *mechanism* change anywhere else under `src/ccf`. D-11's softer line on tests, carried forward verbatim: the conformance-suite `pytest.param` additions are expected, and a corpus addition is **permitted but must be recorded in the gate package** with why it was needed and whether it exposes a gap in the 15.2 corpus rather than a substrate quirk — *"the SDK bent"* is a failure, *"the corpus grew"* may be a finding, and collapsing the two would pressure an executor away from adding a fixture that ought to be added. The `docs/GRAPH_QUERY_SEMANTICS.md` freeze (amendments go in the `GQ-35` lane, line-number-neutral). **Noted future candidate, well-evidenced by this entry and explicitly NOT commissioned by it.** Build an actual backend registration seam, so a substrate registers itself rather than being enumerated in four places. This entry is the evidence both that the seam does not exist and that its absence is paid for once per substrate. It is a candidate phase, not a task inside Phase 15.3: constructing it *during* the phase whose whole claim is "no refactor was needed to accept a new substrate" would be precisely the refactor the claim denies. **Scope.** Standing. Applies to every storage substrate added hereafter, not only Fluree. Phase 15.3's CONTEXT.md D-10/D-11 carry a pointer to this entry rather than a copy, so the ruling has one home. Originates: `.planning/phases/15.3-fluree-adapter-tier-1-substrate-inserted/15.3-RESEARCH.md` §"Blocking Findings" BF-1; measured against commits `dfb2f80`/`e854946`/`761fba1`; `src/ccf/boot.py:118-218`; `src/ccf/deployment/manifest.py:215-263`; Phase 15.3 CONTEXT.md D-10/D-11; CLAUDE.md §3.1, §15.

**D-IMPL-92 (correction, 2026-08-08) — the budget baselines were read off the wrong column; the numbers stand, their derivation and one budget do not.** Date: 2026-08-08. Recorded as an appended, dated correction rather than an edit to the entry above, following the D-IMPL-86/-87 correction discipline, so the reasoning at each point in time survives. Raised by the Phase 15.3 pattern-mapping pass and independently re-measured before recording. **The error.** BF-1, and D-IMPL-92 quoting it, read `git show --stat`'s right-hand number as the added-line count. That column is **insertions + deletions**. Re-measured with `git diff --numstat` across `dfb2f80`/`e854946`/`761fba1`, the mongo landing's real per-file counts are: `src/ccf/boot.py` **+52 / −11** (entry said +63/−13), `src/ccf/deployment/manifest.py` **+15 / −5** (said +20), `src/ccf/controlplane/artifacts.py` **+5 / −2** (said +7), `tests/conftest.py` **+40 / −0** (said +40 — correct, because that file had no deletions, which is exactly why it was the one that happened to agree). The structural finding of BF-1 is untouched: those files were still all modified, there is still no registration seam, and the enumeration is still the mechanism. Only the magnitudes move. **One budget changes.** `tests/conftest.py` was set at **≤ 45** against a true baseline of 40 — five lines of headroom on a structurally identical addition whose research sketch already runs one comment line longer than mongo's. A budget that tight measures noise rather than refactoring: a single added docstring line trips it, and a gate that fires spuriously trains an executor to game the number instead of respecting the rule. **`tests/conftest.py` is raised to ≤ 50** (25% headroom over the measured baseline), which still fails any real restructuring by an order of magnitude. The other three budgets are **unchanged** — `boot.py` ≤ 70 (baseline 52), `manifest.py` ≤ 25 (baseline 15), `artifacts.py` ≤ 10 (baseline 5) — but the gate script MUST print the true baseline beside each budget, so a future reader can see the headroom rather than inferring it. **A second correction, cosmetic but load-bearing for `read_first` fields.** `_open_backend`'s real span is **`src/ccf/boot.py:119-221`**, not the `118-218` cited in BF-1 and in the entry above. `_preflight_backend` at `manifest.py:215-263` was not re-verified in this pass and should be treated as approximate until it is. **A gate-scope clarification the pattern pass surfaced.** `tests/conformance/test_substrate_differential.py:109-116` carries a `#:` comment block above `BACKEND_PARAMS` that **names the container backends explicitly** ("postgres, neo4j and mongo") along with their plan numbers and env vars. Adding fluree therefore cannot be "exactly one added line, zero deleted" in that file unless the comment is left stating something false. The gate permits, per conformance suite: one added `pytest.param` line **plus** edits confined to the `#:` comment block immediately above the param tuple, with the comment change recorded in the gate package. Leaving a comment that undercounts the backends would be a documentation defect purchased to make a line-count look tidier, which inverts what the gate is for. **Also confirmed by the same pass, and worth recording because it bounds the Tier-B allowlist:** `runtime/` contains **no** backend enumeration — the CLI does not know about substrates — so the five Tier-B paths named in the entry above are the complete registration surface, not a sample of it. **What this correction does not touch.** The two-layer structure, the normative rule, checks (1), (2) and (4), the standing scope, the carried-forward D-11 test line, or the noted future candidate. Originates: `15.3-PATTERNS.md` §1 (budget evidence), re-measured against `git diff --numstat`; `src/ccf/boot.py:119-221`; `tests/conformance/test_substrate_differential.py:109-116`.

**D-IMPL-92 (second correction, 2026-08-08) — the gate found the proxy under-enumerating the rule twice, in the test tree. The proxy is extended; the normative rule is untouched.** Date: 2026-08-08. Decided by: Dave, on two `D-IMPL` questions raised by plan 15.3-05's executor when the first run of `make gate-15.3` exited 1. Recorded as an appended, dated correction rather than an edit to either entry above, following the same D-IMPL-86/-87 discipline, so the reasoning at each point in time survives. **What happened, and why it is worth an entry rather than a line in a SUMMARY.** `tools/gates/gate_15_3.py` was written to implement layer 2 exactly as worded, run against the phase's real diff, and **exited 1 on two changes**. The executor declined to widen the allowlist — which the plan text, the phase's threat register (T-15.3-16) and D-10's whole purpose all forbid — and surfaced both as questions instead. Both turned out to be changes the **normative rule already permits**, or that adding a substrate **forces**; in each case the defect was in layer 2, which enumerated one instance of a class layer 1 states in general terms. **This is the same finding as BF-1, one level up.** BF-1 established that there is no registration seam and the enumeration *is* the mechanism.
