What this correction adds: **the phenomenon recurs in the test tree, and the gate's own path allowlist is itself an enumeration — which under-enumerated.** A reader comparing layer 1 against layer 2 finds gaps that reading either layer alone would miss; that is the argument for recording both together, and it is now demonstrated rather than asserted. **Q1 — backend-specific unit tests are free as a CLASS, not as one instance.** Adding a required `fluree_url` keyword argument to `_preflight_backend` forced three identical `fluree_url=None,` lines into `tests/graph/test_mongo_backend.py` (+3/−0), which calls it at three sites and would otherwise not run. Layer 1 already frees this verbatim — "Documentation …, **backend-specific unit tests**, CI workflows and the `Makefile` were never inside D-11's `src/ccf`-scoped hard line and are free" — but layer 2's Tier-C allowlist named only the *new* substrate's module. **Ruling: the allowlist names the class** (`tests/graph/test_*_backend.py`), not the offending file. Adding `test_mongo_backend.py` as a bare special case was explicitly refused: a special case would repeat the very under-enumeration being fixed, and substrate #7 would hit it again against a different neighbour. **Q2 — a conformance suite may carry ONE forced enumeration extension beside its param line.** `tests/conformance/test_graphstore.py` came to +2/−1: the expected `pytest.param("fluree", marks=pytest.mark.fluree)` line, plus its fixture's set-membership guard (`if request.param in {"memory", "postgres", "neo4j", "mongo", "fluree"}:`). Without the new name in that set the added param raises `NotImplementedError`, so the param addition is **not self-contained**. That file has **no `#:` comment block**, so the first correction's comment-block permission — which exists for exactly this reason in the *other* suite — cannot reach it. **Ruling: per conformance suite, one added `pytest.param` line PLUS a one-line extension of a backend-name enumeration (set/tuple/list literal) that is forced by that param addition, with the change recorded in the gate package.** The permission is bounded mechanically, not by good intentions: the added and deleted lines must be **identical once every string literal is removed**, the added line's literals must be the deleted line's **plus exactly one**, and that one must be the **new backend's name derived from the `backend` `Literal`** rather than hard-coded. Arbitrary edits to those files still fail. **Both companion permissions are recorded, not merely granted.** The `#:` comment change (first correction) and the enumeration extension (this one) are each printed verbatim by the gate and asserted present in the gate package. An unrecorded companion is a quiet exemption, which is the outcome permissions are granted in order to avoid. **What is NOT reopened, and this is the load-bearing half.** The **normative rule is untouched** — layer 1 gains nothing and loses nothing. The claim stays exactly as strong: *adding a real substrate touches one new module, one writer class, and a bounded one-line extension of existing enumerations — no mechanism anywhere in the SDK changed.* Layer 2's four checks, the budgets and their baselines, the `_writer.py` AST assertion, the corpus rule, the freeze lines and the standing scope are all unchanged. **Only the approximation moved, and only toward the rule.** That direction is the whole constraint: a proxy may be corrected *upward* to match the rule it approximates, never *downward* to match a diff it happens to be measuring. **Two implementation choices accepted with the ruling**, because the plan's recipe was defective in the workflow it actually runs in: (a) the gate's base ref is a **pinned literal SHA**, not `git merge-base main HEAD` — each wave is merged to `main` as it lands, so by the last wave the live merge-base resolves to the phase's own tip and a recomputed base measures an **empty diff**, i.e. a gate that passes everything; the pinned SHA is guarded by an assertion that the measured surface contains the adapter module; (b) the generated-schema check compares the `backend` enum directly against the `Literal` rather than re-running `make schemas`, because a gate whose first check is "the working tree must be clean" must not itself dirty the tree, and byte-level regeneration drift is already covered by the always-on `tests/cli/test_schema_export.py::test_each_committed_schema_is_its_class_byte_for_byte`. **Scope.** Standing, like the entry it corrects. Applies to every storage substrate added hereafter. Substrate #7 inherits both extensions and, more importantly, inherits the reason they exist. Originates: plan 15.3-05 execution, first `make gate-15.3` run against the phase diff at base `96f54c5`; `tools/gates/gate_15_3.py`; `tests/graph/test_mongo_backend.py`; `tests/conformance/test_graphstore.py`; D-IMPL-92 and its 2026-08-08 first correction.

**D-IMPL-92 (third correction, 2026-08-08) — the decision log joins Tier C, bounded append-only; and the defect recurred *recursively*, on the record of its own fix.** Date: 2026-08-08. Decided by: Dave, on a third `D-IMPL` question raised by plan 15.3-05's executor. Appended and dated, never an edit to the three entries above, per the same discipline this correction goes on to make mechanical. **What happened.** Recording the second correction in this file — which CLAUDE.md §11 makes **mandatory** for any `D-IMPL` ruling — put `docs/DECISIONS.md` into the measured change surface, where layer 2's Tier C had named `docs/SURFACE-CHANGES.md` and `docs/surface-changes/*` and omitted the decision log. `make gate-15.3` went red again, on exactly one path: `docs/DECISIONS.md (+2/−0)`. **The executor again declined to edit the allowlist and surfaced it instead**, which is the correct behaviour and is worth recording as such: making that edit silently, while implementing the ruling about not making such edits silently, would have hollowed out the entire exercise. **The recursion is the durable lesson, more than the path is.** This is the *third* instance of one defect — layer 2 enumerating instances of a class layer 1 states in general terms — and the third arrived **recursively: the act of recording the correction for the second instance created the third.** Substrate #7 should expect the shape rather than the specific paths: any enumeration written by hand against a rule stated as a category will under-enumerate, and the enumeration in a *gate* is no more exempt from that than the enumeration in `boot.py` that BF-1 found. The gate's own allowlist is itself a registration enumeration; it has now under-enumerated three times, and each time the fix was to name the class. **The ruling.** `docs/DECISIONS.md` joins **Tier C**, bounded **append-only**. Three grounds, in order of weight: (1) the test layer 1 actually applies is *"was never inside D-11's `src/ccf`-scoped hard line"*, and this path is not under `src/ccf` at all — it is free on precisely the same footing as the `Makefile` and the CI workflows, both of which layer 1 names explicitly; (2) CLAUDE.md §11 makes logging a `D-IMPL` ruling here **mandatory**, so a gate that fails because the project's own decision-logging rule was followed is measuring the wrong thing; (3) D-IMPL-92 is **standing**, so every future substrate that surfaces a `D-IMPL` hits this identically — name the class, not the instance, exactly as Q1 was ruled. **Why append-only and not a bare path allow.** The D-IMPL-86/-87/-92 discipline is that a correction is *appended and dated, never edited into the entry it corrects, so the reasoning at each point in time survives*. Bounding the allowlist entry to **zero deleted lines** encodes that discipline **mechanically**: added lines pass, any deletion is a `gate153-append-only` finding. The entry therefore stays a real check — one that would still fire if someone rewrote decision history — rather than becoming a hole in the surface. A documented discipline that nothing enforces is the same class of thing as an unfalsifiable architectural claim, which is what this whole gate exists to convert. Verified by negative test: a deletion in `docs/DECISIONS.md`, committed, is a finding. **Explicitly considered and REJECTED: broadening Tier C to `docs/**`.** It is tidier and it is wrong — it would free `docs/GRAPH_QUERY_SEMANTICS.md`, whose freeze this same ruling makes a hard line (amendments go in the `GQ-35` lane, line-number-neutral) and which the gate asserts byte-untouched across the phase. Real coverage traded for a shorter allowlist. Recorded here so a future reader does not "simplify" the entry into the hole it was designed to avoid. **What this correction does not touch.** The normative rule, layer 2's four checks, the budgets and their baselines, the `_writer.py` AST assertion, the conformance-suite rules including both companion permissions, the corpus rule, the freeze lines, the standing scope, or the noted-but-uncommissioned registration seam. As with the second correction, **only the approximation moved, and only toward the rule.** **Scope.** Standing. Originates: plan 15.3-05 execution, the `make gate-15.3` run following the second correction's own commit; `tools/gates/gate_15_3.py` (`Allowed.append_only`, `RULE_APPEND_ONLY`); CLAUDE.md §11; D-IMPL-92 and its 2026-08-08 first and second corrections.

**D-IMPL-92 (fourth correction, 2026-08-08) — the gate's own rule surface is pinned to this log, and the `_writer.py` AST check reaches module level. The last narrated-not-checked part of the phase was the checker.** Date: 2026-08-08. Decided by: Dave, on code-review findings WR-09 and WR-10 against `tools/gates/gate_15_3.py`, ruled fix-in-phase. Appended and dated, never an edit to the four entries above, per the discipline the third correction made mechanical. **What is different about this one.** The first three corrections were found by *running* the gate — it went red, an executor declined to widen the rules, and Dave ruled. This one was found by *reviewing* the gate, and it is the same defect one level further in: **the part of the artifact that was still narrated rather than checked was the artifact's own rule surface.** **WR-10 — the gate could not police its own allowlist or budgets.** `tools/gates/*` is Tier C, free, with the reason "this gate itself. A gate that could not add itself could not exist". The consequence is that `ALLOWLIST`, `BUDGETS`, `FROZEN_PATHS`, `SINGLE_OWNER_DENY` and `WRITER_EXPECTED_ADDITIONS` were all editable *inside the measured surface with no check at all* — a wave that widened the allowlist or raised a budget to make its own diff pass was mechanically indistinguishable from the gate's own authorship. `main()`'s failure message forbids precisely that in prose ("Fix the DIFF — never the allowlist, the budgets, or the rules") and nothing enforced it. The pressure was real rather than hypothetical: the allowlist was widened three times during this phase, correctly each time, and nothing in the gate distinguished a correct widening from an expedient one. **The ruling: hash the rule surface, pin the digest here.** `rule_surface()` normalizes the gate's rule DATA into canonical, sorted, order-independent lines; `rule_surface_digest()` hashes them; `check_rule_surface` (`gate153-rule-surface`) requires the digest to appear verbatim in `docs/DECISIONS.md`. The choice of this file is the load-bearing part: the third correction already bounds it **append-only**, so recording a rule change cannot be done by editing the record of the previous one. The current digest, which the gate asserts is present in this file, is **`sha256:0320e23fd7978653f3ce01831221677af6b65895c6ed88f895c83659a754f513`** — 44 normalized rule lines. **What the pin buys, stated without overclaiming.** It makes a rule change **visible and audited; it does not make one impossible.** A rule change plus an appended, dated entry carrying the new digest passes the gate, and that is the design rather than a hole in it — D-IMPL-92's entire method is that the proxy may be corrected *upward, toward the rule*, when it under-enumerates, and three such corrections have already had to happen. A pin that forbade correction would have forbidden all three. What it removes is the **silent** change. **What the digest covers, and what it does not — recorded here because a check whose coverage is unstated is the thing this entry is about.** Covered: every `ALLOWLIST` entry's pattern, tier and `append_only` flag; every `BUDGETS` limit **and** its stated mongo baseline (the baseline is a factual claim about the precedent, and the first correction exists because it was wrong); `SINGLE_OWNER_DENY` paths; `FROZEN_PATHS`; `ENUMERATION_FILES`; `WRITER_EXPECTED_ADDITIONS`; `CONFORMANCE_SUITES` anchors and `CONFORMANCE_PARAM_RE`; the pinned `PHASE_BASE`; and the named path constants the checks resolve against. **Not** covered, deliberately: (a) **prose** — `Allowed.reason`, `Budget.note`, the deny messages — because a digest that fires when someone improves an explanation trains a reader to route around it, which is worse than no digest; (b) **declaration order** — the lines are sorted before hashing, so a reorder does not fire; `allowed_for` returns the first match, so order *would* be semantic if two patterns overlapped on a path with different tiers, none currently do, and the pin does not check that they do not; (c) comments and formatting; (d) **the check bodies** — this pins rule data, not the code interpreting it, and WR-09 is the standing example of why code review is still needed. **A residual, recorded rather than implied.** `--base` still accepts an arbitrary ref. `PHASE_BASE` is inside the digest so the DEFAULT is pinned, and `check_base_resolution` requires ancestry plus the adapter module in the measured surface, but an override remains an override. **WR-09 — the `_writer.py` AST check did not reach module level.** `top_level_defs()` collected only `ClassDef`/`FunctionDef`/`AsyncFunctionDef`, so every module-level statement in that file — `__all__`, module constants, the per-backend query-string constants — was invisible to layer-2 check (4). `src/ccf/graph/_writer.py` is Tier A with **no line budget** (it is absent from `BUDGETS`), so rewriting an existing module-level constant there passed every check the gate ran, while the gate's headline claim was "no mechanism anywhere in the SDK changed". **Ruling: HEAD's module body must be an ordered SUPERSEQUENCE of the base's** — existing module-level statements may be added to, never modified or removed; new ones are free, because this is Tier A and a new substrate's constants have nowhere else to go under CINV-02. A failed match does not consume the walk cursor, so one modified constant reports as one finding rather than cascading into every statement below it. **A third bounded companion permission, measured before it was granted.** A strict supersequence fires on this phase's own diff. Measured at base `96f54c5`: the module carried **33** module-level statements and HEAD carries **51**, with **exactly one** base statement unmatched — `__all__`, which gained `FlureeGraphWriter` and is therefore a *modified* `Assign` rather than an addition. The permission granted is as tight as the two conformance companions it mirrors: a flat literal collection of string constants, bound to the same name, gaining **exactly one** member, and that member must be a **new top-level definition in this same module whose name begins with a backend name this phase added to the `backend` `Literal`** — derived from the mechanism, never hard-coded, which resolves to `{FlureeGraphWriter}` here. More than one companion is a finding. It is **recorded** in the gate package like the other two, not merely permitted. This is a permission for one name; it is **not** a blanket exemption for module-level assignments, and the entry says so because that is the loosening a future reader would reach for. **No pytest coverage was added for the gate, deliberately.** `tests/gates/*` is not in the allowlist and `testpaths = ["tests"]`, so a unit test for this gate could only be added by widening the gate's own allowlist — the exact move this correction exists to make visible. The substitute is the discipline already in force: negative tests are performed as **throwaway commits** (the diff is three-dot and does not see the working tree) and the result is recorded. Verified this way before recording: a raised budget, committed, makes the gate exit 1 on `gate153-rule-surface`; reverted, it exits 0. **The durable lesson, and it is the fourth instance of one shape.** The first three corrections found hand-written enumerations under-enumerating a rule stated as a category — in `boot.py`, in the test tree, and recursively in the gate's own allowlist. This one finds the same thing about the **checker**: the gate's rule surface was the last narrated-not-checked part of a phase built to replace narration with checks, and being Tier C was the reason nobody had looked. Substrate #7 should assume the same of its own gate before a reviewer does. **What this correction does not touch.** The normative rule (layer 1 gains nothing and loses nothing), the four original layer-2 checks, the budgets and their baselines, the conformance-suite rules including both existing companion permissions, the corpus rule, the freeze lines, or the noted-but-uncommissioned registration seam. As with the second and third corrections, **only the approximation moved, and only toward the rule.** **Scope.** Standing, like the entry it corrects. Substrate #7 inherits the pin, the module-level check, the companion bound, and the reason each exists. Originates: Phase 15.3 code review WR-09 and WR-10; `tools/gates/gate_15_3.py` (`rule_surface`, `rule_surface_digest`, `check_rule_surface`, `module_level_statements`, `check_writer_module_level`, `bounded_name_addition`, `RULE_RULE_SURFACE`); `src/ccf/graph/_writer.py`; D-IMPL-92 and its 2026-08-08 first, second and third corrections.

**D-IMPL-93 — The substrate-evolution standard: the substrate may grow, but never per-adapter and never toward a named adapter.** Date: 2026-08-09. Decided by: Dave, clarifying the criterion Phase 15.3's gate had been approximating. **Why this is recorded.** Phase 15.3 closed against `D-IMPL-92`, whose mechanical proxy bounds the *magnitude* of substrate change when an adapter is added — a path allowlist plus per-file added-line budgets. Four dated corrections in a single phase, and both remaining budgets arriving at their limit (`manifest.py` 24 of 25, `artifacts.py` 9 of 10), made it clear the proxy was measuring the wrong quantity. Magnitude was never the concern. **The standard, in three tests.** A change under `src/ccf` is judged by: **(1) Direction** — does the substrate carry a unique dependency on a specific adapter? A violation. **(2) Recurrence** — does adding an adapter force a substrate change *again*? A violation; "we do not want to change the substrate each time we add an adapter." **(3) Generality** — does the change add capability that makes *all* future adapters cheaper, carrying no adapter-specific knowledge? **Welcome, and explicitly authorized**: "it's OK for the substrate to evolve and add capabilities I did not realize we needed", and "our substrate is evolving and we need to be OK with enhancing it as we learn more through this process." **What the tree measured against it, 2026-08-09 at `773ed92`.** Tests (1) and (2) both **fail today**, and the `D-IMPL-92` gate is structurally blind to both because it bounds magnitude rather than direction or recurrence. Measured: `src/ccf/boot.py:55,58,59,60,61` and `src/ccf/graph/_writer.py:96,133,142,152,156,157` **import** `ccf.graph.backends.*` — the substrate depends on the adapters, not the reverse, and `_writer.py` cannot be imported without importing all six backend modules. `_open_backend` takes two-to-three keyword arguments per substrate (`postgres_dsn`, `postgres_schema`, `neo4j_uri`, `neo4j_partition`, `mongo_uri`, `mongo_partition`, `fluree_url`, `fluree_partition`, `fluree_bearer_token`), mirrored by `_preflight_backend`, with a hand-maintained `Literal` in `controlplane/artifacts.py:975` naming all six. Per-substrate cost is ~96 added lines across three core files. **The reframe this forces on `D-IMPL-92`.** Its four corrections are a *symptom*, not a documentation defect. The enumeration kept proving incomplete because there is no registration seam, so "add a backend" is really "edit the substrate in N places"; the corrections were the project getting better at *describing* per-adapter substrate churn rather than eliminating it. `D-IMPL-92`'s structural finding — "there is no registration seam; the enumeration *is* the mechanism" — was correct and is now the thing to fix rather than the thing to document. **What is NOT relaxed.** `D-11`'s hard line on *mechanism* change elsewhere under `src/ccf`; the `docs/GRAPH_QUERY_SEMANTICS.md` freeze; the corpus rule ("the SDK bent" fails, "the corpus grew" is recordable); and `D-IMPL-92`'s prohibition on raising a budget to make a gate pass — under this standard the remedy for an exhausted budget is the seam, never a larger number. **Consequence.** Phase 15.4 is commissioned to build the registration seam (see ROADMAP). Its success criterion is structural rather than numeric: no import of `ccf.graph.backends.*` from outside `backends/`, and no substrate name in core outside the registry — both lint-checkable in the same machinery as the existing harness-import lint, replacing the line budgets `D-IMPL-92` admits are a proxy. **Scope.** Standing. Governs every substrate, adapter and pluggable-Protocol addition hereafter, not only storage backends. Originates: Dave, 2026-08-09, in response to the Phase 15.3 gate presentation; measured against `main` at `773ed92`; `D-IMPL-92` and its four corrections; `src/ccf/boot.py`; `src/ccf/graph/_writer.py`; `src/ccf/controlplane/artifacts.py`; `src/ccf/deployment/manifest.py`.

**D-GATE-15.3** — Phase 15.3 (Fluree Adapter — Tier-1 Substrate, INSERTED) approved. Date: 2026-08-09. Approver: Dave. **Delivered.** A Fluree v4 tier-1 storage adapter behind the unchanged Phase 15.2 seams: `FlureeGraphStore` over `httpx` with **zero new packages** (`httpx==0.28.1` was already a core pin, so no extra, no lazy import, no mypy override, no lint suppression — every absence deliberate SUBQ-14 evidence); `_FlureeTxn`/`FlureeGraphWriter` satisfying the `_GraphTxn` write seam as one buffered ground JSON-LD POST per transaction; stage-1 openCypher read pushdown and stage-2 traversal; and `tools/gates/gate_15_3.py`, which reduces the phase's own claim to a script that can fail. **Verification matrix (`main`, post-merge, at `773ed92`):** `make gate-15.3` exit 0; `make lint` exit 0; `mypy --strict` clean over 177 source files; `make ci-core` 2298 passed / 81 skipped; all four substrate legs green — postgres 25, neo4j 20, mongo 22, fluree 37; phase verification 6/6 must-haves. `tests/conformance/query_corpus.py` **byte-untouched** for the whole phase, and `docs/GRAPH_QUERY_SEMANTICS.md`, `perf/baselines/` and `perf/thresholds.toml` likewise. **The goal's own wording is corrected here, and the ROADMAP text is deliberately left intact** so the falsified prediction stays visible rather than being edited into retroactive correctness. Two clauses do not survive measurement. **(a) "with zero changes to the SDK core"** — `src/ccf` is `+2542/−17`. The target was measured as unreachable by this phase's *own research* (BF-1) **before implementation began**, against the MongoDB landing rather than Fluree, and `15.3-CONTEXT.md` contradicted itself in the same document (its `<code_context>` described the `boot.py` change its `D-11` forbade). `D-IMPL-92` replaced it. The true claim as of this gate is narrower and truer than either: *adding a substrate today requires bounded, adapter-named changes in three core files, because no registration seam exists — a cost `D-IMPL-92` documents and `D-IMPL-93` now commissions paid down.* All 17 deletions are docstring sentences enumerating one more backend. **(b) "suite diff: one fixture parameter"** — falsified. Actual: `test_graphstore.py` `+2/−1` (the param **plus a forced line of executable code** without which the param raises `NotImplementedError`), `test_substrate_differential.py` `+9/−8` (the param plus an eight-line comment rewrite that would otherwise state something false). Both companions were ruled permissible by corrections written **during this phase, in response to this diff**, with the executor declining to widen the allowlist and escalating instead. A falsified prediction caught by the mechanism built to catch it is worth more than a prediction that happened to hold, and is recorded as such. **The phase's strongest output is not the adapter.** It is that `make gate-15.3` was **watched to fail eight times on eight distinct violations before being believed once**, and that **three of those failures changed `D-IMPL-92` rather than the diff** — each the identical defect, a rule stated as a category enforced by a hand-written enumeration born incomplete: first in the test tree (layer 2 never examined it), then in the gate's **own** path allowlist, then recursively in the act of recording that fix. Each time the fix was *name the class, not the instance*. The fourth correction finally pinned the gate's rule surface by digest to the append-only decision log — using a bound the third correction had just created — and the pin was independently reproduced firing on a silently-raised budget while every other check, including the budget check itself, passed. **Two falsification results, both independently reproduced at the gate.** With `FlureeGraphReader._translate_query` neutered to return `None`, **all seven differential tests stay GREEN** while both telemetry tests go **RED** — `GQ-34` makes the degraded posture byte-identical *by design*, so byte-identity to the fallback is a conformance bar and provably **not** a pushdown bar. Any adapter claiming pushdown on corpus-green alone claims something its evidence cannot distinguish from an absent translation. **Probes before code, and they earned it.** F-A6 established that `c[$key]` dynamic property access parses, returns HTTP 200, and evaluates to **null** — porting `_ENTITY_POLICY` verbatim would have shipped a stage-1 policy family that silently excludes every claim. F-A3(e) returned `[{"props": [null, null]}]`, confirming Fluree relationships over plain RDF triples carry no properties, which is why the Neo4j walk was replaced by the interposed `CcfEdge` node (BF-1) rather than copied. Both were caught before a line of adapter code existed. **Code review: 1 Critical, 10 Warning, 3 Info; eight closed in-phase.** **CR-01** was real and is the phase's most instructive defect: `policy_key` gated two of its three components, because the docstring asserted *"The class name is safe by construction"* one line above an argument that was otherwise valid — so a claim the audited fallback **excluded** was returned by the pushed path (T-15.2-03 over-disclosure). Reproduced end-to-end before acceptance and re-verified fixed after merge. Four mechanisms were structurally blind to it, including the unit test, which repeated the guard's own two-of-three gap because it was written from the same false premise; the regression test that now protects it needs **no server and runs in ~20ms**. Also closed: WR-02/WR-03 (a credentialed Fluree URL **connected anonymously** — `httpx` derives no header from `base_url` userinfo, and `bearer_token` was reachable from no supported path), WR-07, WR-08, WR-09, WR-10, IN-01. **Six remain open as recorded debt:** WR-01, WR-04, WR-05, WR-06, IN-02, IN-03. **Rulings issued during the phase:** F-A4(a) `approved` (the ground `delete`+`insert` body holds, so `D-02`'s letter is unchanged); Q1/Q2 extend the proxy to match the normative rule, never loosen the rule; Q3 `docs/DECISIONS.md` joins Tier C bounded **append-only**, with `docs/**` explicitly rejected because it would free the frozen `GRAPH_QUERY_SEMANTICS.md`; keep the weekly `fluree-drift` cadence; fix CR-01, WR-02/03, WR-07, WR-08, WR-09/10, IN-01 in-phase. **Residual, non-blocking:** `15.3-HUMAN-UAT.md` carries two GitHub-Actions observations (the fluree leg green on `main`; the drift job's first firing) that cannot be established from the working tree. **Substrate #7 warning, carried forward:** `manifest.py` is at 24 of 25 and `artifacts.py` at 9 of 10 — **tied**, not tiered — so a seventh substrate breaches both on arrival, and `D-IMPL-92` forbids raising either. `D-IMPL-93` commissions the remedy. Deliverables: `src/ccf/graph/backends/fluree.py`, the `_FlureeTxn`/`FlureeGraphWriter` additions in `src/ccf/graph/_writer.py` (887 added, **0 deleted**), `tools/gates/gate_15_3.py`, the fourth always-on CI leg and the `fluree-drift` job, `15.3-GATE-PACKAGE.md`, `15.3-REVIEW.md`, `15.3-REVIEW-FIXES-SUMMARY.md`, `15.3-VERIFICATION.md`, 5/5 plan SUMMARYs. Phase 15.4 (Backend Registration Seam) is commissioned by `D-IMPL-93` and precedes Phase 16.

**D-GATE-15.4** — Phase 15.4 (Backend Registration Seam — The Substrate Stops Naming Its Adapters, INSERTED) approved. Date: 2026-08-09. Approver: Dave. **Delivered.** The registration seam `D-IMPL-93` commissioned, in twelve plans across nine waves. `ccf.graph.registry` — one registration record, two discovery sources (the adapter package on disk for built-ins, the `ccf.graph_backends` entry-point group for third parties), one resolution path, fail-closed refusals for unknown, duplicate and reserved names; discovery imports nothing and `resolve()` imports exactly the one adapter a deployment named. Six per-backend write modules at `src/ccf/graph/backends/<name>_write.py`, leaving `src/ccf/graph/_writer.py` at 935 lines from 3,215 and importing **no** backend — importing it now pulls zero substrate modules into `sys.modules`, which is the `D-IMPL-93` test-(1) dependency arrow turned around. Six typed connection contracts, one per adapter, each declaring its own requirements; the hand-maintained substrate-to-required-argument table is gone and both of its refusals are now the contract's own — a required field, and the inherited `extra="forbid"`. `boot()` from sixteen keyword arguments to eight, none naming a database, with the eleven-row connection-key mapping in the `Migration` field of `ccf.boot.boot` in `docs/SURFACE-CHANGES.md` (the `D-15B` condition on the break). Credentials by named environment-variable reference, resolved at boot, refused when unset, never echoed. Decision Replay dimension 2 pins the adapter — name, version, and the *names* of the credential references in force; per correction `C-02` this **creates** the first adapter pin rather than widening one. Two structural lint rules, `cinv02-backend-import` and `cinv04-substrate-name`/`-prose`, registered in `make lint` and the always-on CI lint job. `ccf backends list` over `GET /backends`, answering what the removed schema enum never could — what is installed *here*, third-party adapters included. And `tests/fixtures/ccf-demo-adapter`, a second installable distribution that registers through the entry-point group, boots a working fabric, honours single-writer across the boundary, and passes both conformance suites as an ordinary unmarked param. **Verification matrix (`main`, post-merge, post-review-fix).** `make ci` exit 0 — `make ci-core` 2450 passed / 81 skipped against the 2298/81 phase base, delta accounted for plan by plan; `make examples` all ten; `make docs-strict` exit 0; `make lint` exit 0 with both new rules registered; `mypy --strict` clean over 186 files; `pytest tools/lints/tests -q` **141 passed** — a separate command, because `testpaths = ["tests"]` keeps the checker suite out of `ci-core` and CI runs it as its own step. Substrate legs: postgres 25, neo4j 20, mongo 22 (port 27019 on this host, via `CCF_TEST_MONGO_URI`; the Makefile default hangs rather than refusing), fluree 37 — *not* the 38 three separate plans predicted, a stale expectation `15.4-04` measured and corrected at the phase base. `tests/conformance/query_corpus.py` and `docs/GRAPH_QUERY_SEMANTICS.md` byte-untouched for the whole phase. **The headline claim, and the bound that makes it honest.** `git diff --quiet f9857d94..9f80986^ -- src/ccf` exits **0**: a seventh graph adapter arrived from outside the repository, passed the conformance suites the six built-ins pass, and cost **zero lines of substrate**. The same form over the whole phase exits **1**, which is what makes the exit-0 evidence rather than a command that cannot fail — `src/ccf` changed a great deal *building* the seam and not at all once the adapter arrived. The window closes at `9f80986^` rather than `HEAD` because seven code-review fix commits landed afterwards, five touching `src/ccf`; the gate package prints the unbounded exit-1 and its cause rather than editing them out, and none of those five files changed because an adapter arrived. That re-anchoring was **found by the phase verifier**, not by the phase: the gate package as written printed a command that returned red on a green phase. **Post-execution code review: 3 BLOCKER, 11 WARNING, 2 Info — all three blockers fixed inside the phase**, per §3.1, before this gate was requested. **CR-01** is the phase's most instructive defect and sits precisely on the seam the phase widened: `registration.make_writer(store, token)` sat between the mint guard above it and the `shutdown()` guard below it, protected by neither, so a third-party `make_writer` that raised leaked the store connection **and** left the graph in `_LIVE_WRITERS` — unbootable for the process lifetime. Reproduced with a probe adapter before acceptance, mutation-checked after. **CR-02**: `imported_module_names` discards an `ImportFrom`'s alias names, so `from ccf.graph import _writer` reported only `ccf.graph` and **both new rules were blind to that import form** — the phase's central guarantee partially hollow. The invariant test that mirrors the lint carried the *same* blind spot in its own regex, so it structurally could not have caught what the lint was missing; both are fixed, including `from ccf.graph import reader, _writer`, the same import wearing a comma. **CR-03**: duplicate discovery compared distribution *name* only, so the ordinary failed-uninstall state (two dist-info directories, one name, different versions, different modules) was accepted and one silently chosen — and the winner's version then landed in replay dimension 2 as fact. **One finding was checked and REJECTED: WR-05** ("neither new checker has a test of any kind") is false — 41 test functions exist in `tools/lints/tests/`, created in plan 15.4-01. Its grep searched `tests/` and `examples/`. The underlying condition is real and is now recorded in the gate package: `ci-core` never reaches the checker suite, so a local sweep alone supports exactly that false inference. **Four rulings taken at this gate.** **(1) The `tools/gates/gate_15_3.py` deletion is accepted.** The ROADMAP says twice that `D-IMPL-92`'s gate is "superseded by the structural check, **not deleted**", and plan 15.4-07 deleted the script and its whole package. The reading ruled here is that "gate" means the **criterion**, not the artifact: the criterion is superseded by `cinv02-backend-import` and `cinv04-substrate-name`/`-prose`, which measure direction and recurrence rather than magnitude, and the four obligations `D-IMPL-93` lists as NOT relaxed are carried in the Makefile in prose. The script could not have been retained meaningfully — `PHASE_BASE = 96f54c55` and `ADAPTER_MODULE = src/ccf/graph/backends/fluree.py` pinned it to one commit range and one adapter by construction, seven of its rules diff against that base, and its own docstring says twice that it proves a fact about ONE diff and expects supersession. Run once before removal it crashed with `KeyError: 'enum'` (the manifest enum it read no longer exists), and even repaired it would assert that `_writer.py` was never split — which `D-01` deliberately did. **Recorded explicitly because it is not routine cleanup: `tools/gates/gate_15_3.py` is a Phase 15.3 deliverable named in `D-GATE-15.3` and approved there, removed by a later phase. It is recoverable at `d364b65^`** (`git show d364b65^:tools/gates/gate_15_3.py`), and that SHA is recorded here so the executable record of Phase 15.3's claim — the artifact `D-GATE-15.3` calls that phase's strongest output, watched to fail eight times on eight distinct violations before being believed once — is retrievable rather than merely gone. **(2) The HTTP error code is hyphenated, at this gate, while it is still free.** `backend_registry_conflict` became `backend-registry-conflict`, matching all seven values in the API's shared vocabulary. Plan 15.4-09 named the literal with underscores and the executor followed plan over house style, correctly; `errors.py` records that renaming a code is a breaking change to every consumer, so the asymmetry — free before release, permanently expensive after — is why this was ruled rather than booked. One thing changed beyond the literal: the route's test asserted the constant against itself, which agrees with whatever the constant says, so it was green throughout the inconsistency and would have been green through the next silent rename; the literal is now asserted alongside the symbol. **(3) The `CINV-04` split is decided now and executed later, deliberately.** `CINV-04` currently names two unrelated invariants — the established replay-by-construction one, and the no-substrate-name rule this phase minted. `CINV-05` is free and the split is approved. The **rename is deferred to a single coordinated pass** across code *and* record, because the identifier appears in gate tables already signed and in all twelve of this phase's plans and summaries: renaming the code alone would leave a document whose rule ids disagree with the history behind it. Tracked as `DI-15.4-03`. **(4) The private-surface gap stays open as a design item, not a defect.** Six names an out-of-tree adapter must reach for are not exported — `materialize_view`, `_ordered_view`, `_entity_records`, `_relationship_records`, `_LineageRowsFor` (`ccf.graph.reader`) and `_GraphTxn` (`ccf.graph._writer`) — and none is avoidable today: `_ordered_view` cannot be replaced without forking the ordering convention it holds, the two record builders have no public counterpart so `entity_history`/`relationship_history` are unimplementable from public names, and the public `BaseGraphReader` is SQL-shaped and unusable by a dict store. **`D-11`'s claim holds exactly as written — no line of `src/ccf` changed — *and* the surface an adapter reaches for is not the public surface. Both are true, and the second bounds how much the first proves.** This is a design question deserving its own cycle rather than a fix wedged into a gate; the published adapter-authoring guide states the gap plainly rather than implying the public surface suffices. **`D-13` deviation, confirmed at this gate.** Neither new rule reads `tools/lints/allowlist.yaml`, deviating deliberately from `D-IMPL-93`'s own wording that the allowlist governs exceptions; carve-outs are reviewed named constants instead, and `test_neither_new_rule_can_reach_the_allowlist` makes that structural rather than a one-time grep. **No carve-out proved necessary.** Two independent pre-registration dry runs (plans 15.4-06 and 15.4-07) each returned zero findings over the tree, which is also what closes the enforcement window the phase opened by writing the rules in wave 1 and registering them in wave 6: at the phase base the run found 53 findings, every one inside the two files plan 15.4-06 owned and **none in any other core module** — the negative result the per-file checks of waves 2–5 structurally could not establish. **Residual debt, recorded not fixed:** the `ccf` distribution name is taken on PyPI by an unrelated project at 7.0.12, so a third-party adapter's natural `dependencies = ["ccf"]` resolves to someone else's package (upstream of anything an adapter author can do); `ENTITY_ADMIT_PREFIX`/`RELATIONSHIP_ADMIT_PREFIX` remain duplicated across `backends/fluree.py` and `fluree_write.py` (`D-14` ruled on the policy key only); `WR-04`/`WR-06`/`IN-02`/`IN-03` from Phase 15.3 stay debt per `D-14`; and `15.3-HUMAN-UAT.md`'s two GitHub-Actions observations remain non-blocking. **Two planning-template defects worth fixing before the next phase, both repeated rather than isolated:** five consecutive plans listed a surface-change *fragment* in `files_modified` but not the assembled `docs/SURFACE-CHANGES.md` it regenerates, and four plans hit the same self-referential trap where a docstring, comment or Makefile note explaining why a token is absent fails the grep asserting that absence — resolved the same way each time, describe the token rather than spell it. Deliverables: `src/ccf/graph/registry.py`, the six `backends/<name>_write.py` modules, the reshaped `src/ccf/boot.py`, `src/ccf/deployment/manifest.py` and `src/ccf/controlplane/artifacts.py`, the adapter pin in `src/ccf/replay/record.py`, `tools/lints/backend_imports.py` and `tools/lints/substrate_names.py`, `runtime/ccf_runtime/{http_api/routes,cli/commands}/backends.py`, `tests/fixtures/ccf-demo-adapter/`, `public/how-to/write-a-graph-backend-adapter.md`, `docs/15.4-GATE-PACKAGE.md`, `15.4-REVIEW.md`, `15.4-REVIEW-FIXES-SUMMARY.md`, `15.4-VERIFICATION.md`, `deferred-items.md`, 12/12 plan SUMMARYs. Phase 16 (Documentation Rewrite) follows, and documents the backend surface this phase reshaped.

**D-GATE-15.4 (addendum, 2026-08-09) — the close-time cross-AI panel, and two structural invariants it found unenforced.** Recorded because the entry above describes a state that has since changed, and because `docs/DECISIONS.md` is the canonical phase record: a reader of it alone would not otherwise learn that the panel ran, that it found more than the single-agent review did, or that what it found was not a Phase 15.4 defect. **What happened.** `D-GATE-15.4` was approved on a self-review that consisted of the single-agent `gsd-code-reviewer` pass (`15.4-REVIEW.md`, 3 blockers, all fixed in-phase). CLAUDE.md §3.1 step 1 required `/gsd-review --phase 15.4 --all` — the cross-AI panel — and the single-agent review was substituted for it. Dave directed the panel be run afterwards and that all defects close before Phase 16. **What the panel found.** Four reviewers (Gemini, Codex, Cursor, Ollama; Claude excluded for independence) over ~50k tokens of *shipped* code, given the three already-fixed blockers with an instruction not to re-derive them but to search their neighbourhoods: **six further findings, every one verified against the tree before being acted on, and one rejected on verification** — recorded at `15.4-CLOSE-REVIEWS.md`. **The serious one is not a Phase 15.4 defect and predates it.** The harness-neutrality lint did not see `from google import adk`: `HARNESS_DENY` holds dotted entries and the deny loop still called `imported_module_names`, which reports only `google` for that spelling, while line 227 of the same file already used the CR-02-corrected `reached_module_names`. Phase 15.4 fixed that exact defect one file over and stopped twenty-seven lines short. Reproduced by execution, not argued. **And it was worse than the panel reported.** The panel named one blind checker and guessed at two more; all three were blind, and the two it could not prove were blind in every direction. The sharpest instance is the algorithmic seam rather than the harness one: `from ccf import llm` inside a module tagged `__ccf_mode__ = "algorithmic"` produced no finding, so **CCF's own LLM seam could enter the algorithmic path unremarked** (CLAUDE.md §11's Algorithmic ⇄ Semantic Alternation) — by a spelling a CCF developer is *more* likely to write than the covered one. Two structural invariants with CI enforcement that an idiomatic import form walked past. **The diagnosis, which is the part that generalizes: only *dotted* deny entries were ever blind.** `langgraph`, `claude_agent_sdk`, `anthropic` and `openai` have no parent package to be named by, so every spelling a test author would naturally write already worked for them. The blind spot existed only for `google.adk`, `ccf.llm` and `ccf_runtime.adapters`. That asymmetry is why a 141-test checker suite stayed green over two invariants — the tests were written against the entries that could not fail. The replacement suite derives its cases from the deny constants rather than from a spelling list, so a new harness arrives covered in every form; it is now 400 tests. **All six findings closed** (`15.4-CLOSE-FIXES-A-SUMMARY.md` for F0/F3/F6, `-B-` for F1/F4/F5), each with a regression test that fails without its fix, F0/F1/F6 mutation-checked. F6 shipped *both* candidate fixes rather than choosing: the public `GraphWriter` protocol gains `close()` (the declaration — without it an adapter author reading `protocols.py` cannot learn the requirement) and `adopt_writer` refuses a non-`BaseGraphWriter` (the enforcement), by `isinstance` rather than `hasattr`, because a `close` that does not release the mint is the same defect with one more step in it. F1's fix was mutation-checked in both directions: reinstating the echo fails with a DSN visible in the refusal, and *over*-redacting fails three tests including two pre-existing, so neither half passes for the wrong reason. **One finding carried, deliberately: F2** — `substrate_vocabulary` derives from in-tree adapters only, so `cinv04-substrate-name` enforces "no *built-in* substrate name in core". Codex's framing is recorded verbatim because it is the accurate one: this makes the third-party zero-cost claim *"partly true by construction of the measurement."* It is not a defect but a bound on what the measurement proves, and the obvious remedy — deriving the vocabulary from installed entry points — would make a structural invariant environment-dependent, green or red by virtualenv. Carried to a design cycle with `DI-15.4-05`. **Sweep after the fixes:** `make ci` exit 0, **2457 passed / 81 skipped** (from 2450 at the gate), checker suite **400 passed** (from 141), both invariant holes re-verified by execution on merged `main`. **Nothing here reopens this gate or falsifies the phase's headline claim** — the out-of-tree adapter did arrive at zero substrate cost. What the panel established is narrower and worth stating precisely: *the enforcement was weaker than the achievement.* **Process consequence, applied.** CLAUDE.md §3.1 step 1 now states that the panel *is* the step and a single-agent review is additive and never substitutive; that at close the panel reviews shipped code rather than plans, to a distinct `<phase>-CLOSE-REVIEWS.md`; that the panel is handed known findings with instructions not to re-derive them; that every finding is verified against the tree before being acted on and rejections are recorded as rejections; and that the ordering is execute → panel → single-agent → verify → fix blocking in-phase → present. Directive from Dave, 2026-08-09.

**D-GATE-15.4 (addendum correction, 2026-08-09) — finding F2 is `DI-15.4-06`, not `DI-15.4-05`.** Appended rather than edited, per the Tier C append-only rule the `D-IMPL-92` corrections established. The addendum above closes by saying finding F2 — `substrate_vocabulary` deriving only from in-tree adapters, so `cinv04-substrate-name` enforces "no *built-in* substrate name in core" — is "carried to a design cycle with `DI-15.4-05`". That cross-reference is wrong, and the phase security audit caught it as unregistered flag UF-4. **`DI-15.4-05` is the private-surface gap: which names an adapter *author* must reach for** (`materialize_view`, `_ordered_view`, `_entity_records`, `_relationship_records`, `_LineageRowsFor`, `_GraphTxn`). **F2 is the lint-vocabulary gap: which names the *rule* can see.** Both are questions about the same seam and neither is the other; conflating them left F2 tracked nowhere, since `deferred-items.md` had no entry for it. It is now **`DI-15.4-06`**, with the three options a ruling has to choose between recorded there — accept the bound and state it wherever the property is claimed; derive the vocabulary from installed entry points and accept that a structural invariant becomes environment-dependent, green or red by virtualenv; or a separate non-lint check that runs where the installed set is known. Meanwhile the bound is not silent: `15.4-SECURITY.md` records `T-15.4-48` as accepted risk **R-15.4-C** rather than unqualified CLOSED, so no threat-register row rests on a guarantee wider than the lint gives. **Also recommended by that audit and not yet actioned:** a new register row `T-15.4-50` (Information disclosure, mitigate) for the third-party import-exception disclosure that finding F1 fixed — `T-15.4-05` covers Pydantic `ValidationError` echo only and does not reach it, and this phase is what opened the seam by which an arbitrary installed distribution's exception text could reach an unauthenticated `503` body.

**D-IMPL-94 — Mode 2 packaging attaches above `serve()`; Phase 16's scope carve-outs.** Date: 2026-08-10. Decided by: Dave. Provenance of the parts, stated because they did not all arrive the same way: `D-02`, `D-13`, `D-14` and `D-15` are Dave's locked planning-time rulings recorded in `.planning/phases/16-documentation-rewrite-the-deterministic-path-the-adoption-ar/16-CONTEXT.md`; `Q-2`, `Q-6`, `Q-7` and `Q-8` are dispositions this entry makes and which plan 16-01's blocking `checkpoint:human-verify` puts to Dave before any downstream plan proceeds. Every claim below was read against the tree at `3cbaa0c` on 2026-08-10, not taken from the plan text. **The ruling.** Mode 2 (`llm_packaged`) packaging attaches **above** `serve()`, consuming the `ServedProduct` it returns, and never inside `serve()`. The argument is structural rather than a preference. `serve()`'s inputs are an already-gated `ServableProduct` — only `ccf.products.gates` can mint one, because its constructor takes a module-private token — and a fully-built `DecisionReplayRecord`. It performs exactly two acts: persist the executed queries' canonical bytes through the `QueryPinWriter`, then append the record through `ReplayWriter.write_replay_record`, whose success *returns* the `DeliveryCapability` that delivers. It contains no rendering and no transformation of the product payload; its only branches are the `QueryPinMismatch` refusals raised before any store write, so exactly one path reaches a return and that path runs through the append (`src/ccf/replay/serve.py:214-266`). A packaging stage inside it would introduce the branch the Phase 04 decision exists to forbid — *"serve() is one expression because the replay append returns the delivery capability — there is no branch that reaches delivery without a recorded decision"* — and would place a model call inside the function that mints the audit record, in a module that declares `__ccf_mode__ = "algorithmic"` and is therefore the `@algorithmic` lint's business (CINV-03, CLAUDE.md §11). Attaching above keeps the algorithmic substrate the artifact a supervisor inspects and makes the LLM rendering a presentation layer over it, which is what whitepaper Ch 7 §7.6 *Context Products — System of Delivery* specifies — cited by section number and title only, never body text (CLAUDE.md §11; Phase 14 D-18; `pub01-whitepaper-shingle`). **What `assembly_mode` describes (Q-2).** `ProductClassDeclaration.assembly_mode` (`src/ccf/controlplane/artifacts.py:602`, typed by `AssemblyMode` at `src/ccf/products/contract.py:71`) describes the **substrate** — how the *Context Product* was assembled — and not the delivered artifact. It is therefore `algorithmic` for a product whose packaging sits above `serve()`, and it stays `algorithmic` when Phase 17 lands that packaging, because a presentation layer above `serve()` does not change how the product was assembled. The delivered artifact's mode is recorded where the models invoked are recorded: `ModelVersionRecord.assembly_mode` (`src/ccf/replay/record.py:356`), replay dimension 3, whose docstring already commits that Mode 2 supplies the real version alongside the other pinned rendering primitives when the packaging stage lands. This sub-question is answered here rather than deferred because Section 1's prose cannot be written honestly without it, and the consequence for that prose is direct: `assembly_mode: algorithmic` in a product-class declaration is a **real governance choice with two siblings arriving in Phase 17** — not boilerplate, and not a constant — and Section 1 must teach it that way. **No code accompanies this ruling.** It is a paper act, verified by `git diff --stat -- src/ runtime/` showing nothing for plan 16-01, and nothing across the phase but the single enumerated exception below. **Criterion 10's one enumerated exception (D-13).** Correcting the docstring of `ProductClassDeclaration` in `src/ccf/controlplane/artifacts.py` is **not** "the fabric changed": no behaviour, no signature and no contract change — the class's fields, defaults and validation are untouched — and mkdocstrings publishes that docstring into the site, so the edit is squarely documentation. The occasion is a failure that predates this phase: `tools/publish_scan_tests/test_docstring_leaks.py::test_no_docstring_reproduces_whitepaper_body_text` reports **20 shingle hits** against `src/ccf/controlplane/artifacts.py::ProductClassDeclaration`, re-measured at `3cbaa0c` on 2026-08-10, with every other docstring in the tree clean. ROADMAP criterion 10's `git diff --stat -- src/ runtime/` assertion therefore carries **this single enumerated exception** — one symbol, `ProductClassDeclaration`, in one file, `src/ccf/controlplane/artifacts.py` — rather than requiring zero. The enumeration is literal, not a category: plan 16-04 makes the edit, plan 16-19's `tools/gates/gate_16.py` allowlists exactly that path-and-symbol pair mechanically rather than trusting this prose, and nothing else anywhere under `src/` or `runtime/` is authorised by this carve-out. **The four Gate-2 tripwire residuals are NOT in Phase 16 (D-14).** `.planning/phases/15.1-ergonomics-gap-closure/deferred-items.md` — the authoritative inheritance list, explicitly not `docs/SURFACE-AUDIT.md` §6.4 — places four Gate-2 tripwire residuals here, and none of them is documentation work. Named by their ids in that file: **R4-WR-03**, a loop-carried rebinding is out of both tripwires' reach because they resolve a name to the binding textually last before the read and a loop executes lines out of source order; **R5-WR-01**, `_Binding.until` covers the comprehension's outermost iterable, which Python evaluates in the enclosing scope, measured as a regression in both directions against `6cf00d3` and whose outermost-iterable **walrus** case has never been measured by anyone; the **augmented-assignment mutable-set opacity**, where `ops |= x` records an unreadable binding so a body that mutates the lineage's own set in place and hands it to the gate is admitted; and the **attribute/subscript `global`/`nonlocal` exception**, where attribute and subscript targets are unfollowed rather than misfollowed except when the redirected assignment lives in a nested body, in which case the enclosing body's stale binding stays in force. Each is a change to what `tests/products/test_gate_two_call_shape.py` **detects** — mechanism, not prose. They **carry forward unclosed to Phase 17**, which supersedes the "Where it belongs: Phase 16" lines in `deferred-items.md`; if Phase 17's planning finds its Mode 2/3 scope the wrong home, they move by an appended dated entry, never by silence. The carry is explicit rather than tacit for one reason worth stating: a phase claiming "no fabric changed" that quietly takes on mechanism work invites exactly the criterion-10 argument `D-13` settles. The carry is then **bounded on two limbs**, because without the bound this ruling blocks the phase it exists to protect — the first limb was found by round-1 review of the plans and the second by round-2 re-verification, and each would stop a different wave on its own. **Limb 1 — re-counting a non-vacuity floor.** Several non-vacuity floors in `tests/products/` are counts observed in the tree **with no headroom, on purpose**: `MINIMUM_GATE_CALL_SITES` (9), `MINIMUM_PROJECTING_BODIES` (11) and `MINIMUM_RESOLVED_BINDER_SITES` (11) in `tests/products/test_gate_two_call_shape.py`, and `MINIMUM_IMPLEMENTATIONS` (9) in `tests/products/test_required_operators_guard.py`. They are fed almost entirely by the examples `DOCS-17` retires. **Re-counting such a floor after a deletion this phase's own ruling authorises is bookkeeping forced by the deletion — not one of the four carried residuals, and not Gate-2 mechanism work.** Plan 16-18 relies on this limb; without it that plan meets a red `make ci` it would have no authority to fix. **Limb 2 — retiring an assertion whose sole in-tree witness a ruled deletion removes.** Not every count coupling has a constant to lower. **An assertion in `tests/products/` whose evidence base is a single in-tree witness, where that witness is a path this phase's own ruling deletes, may be retired deliberately in the same commit as the deletion** — on the same footing as re-flooring, and for the same reason. The limb is stated generally rather than as a single named permission, because the phase deletes ten examples and a second witness of this shape would otherwise stop a later wave with no authority to proceed. The measured instance is `tests/products/test_gate_two_call_shape.py::test_the_plan_recognition_refuses_an_unrelated_required_keyword`, whose docstring states its own evidence base as **n = 1** and names the witness — `examples/03-harness-portability/run.py:693`, the `add_argument` call whose `required=True` keyword sits on the following line, verified at `3cbaa0c` as the only `required=True` anywhere under `examples/` — and whose docstring already sanctions the disposition: *"If the last such call ever leaves `examples/`, this fails loudly and can be retired deliberately — which is the right way round."* Plan 16-15 owns it. Both limbs turn on one distinction: **the four carried residuals are changes to what the tripwire *detects*; both limbs of the bound are changes to what there is left to detect over, forced by a smaller tree.** Neither limb permits any change to detection logic, and weakening, skipping or `xfail`ing an assertion remains forbidden under both. The discipline that applies to both is the one `test_gate_two_call_shape.py` already carries in `FLOOR_REMOVAL_INSTRUCTION`: a deliberate removal and an accidental one need the identical edit, so make the edit **in the same commit as the deletion** and **name every removed site or witness in the commit message**, because `git log` is then the only thing that tells the two apart afterwards. Limb 2 additionally requires one thing `FLOOR_REMOVAL_INSTRUCTION` does not cover, because a retired assertion leaves no failure site to carry the instruction: **the retired assertion's measurement must be preserved in the module** — a docstring or a comment carrying the counts and the identity of the removed witness — since a rule whose justification is deleted alongside its witness invites the next reviewer to propose the rejected design again. **ERGO-03's measurement is frozen; the running test retires (D-15).** `tests/examples/test_surface_audit.py` compares `examples/` against `docs/surface/examples/before/`, a baseline frozen at Phase 15's start, and `tools/measure_examples.py` warns in its own comment that re-freezing mid-phase destroys the comparison; the greenfield rewrite makes the comparison meaningless from the first new example directory onward. ERGO-03 is a **Complete** requirement, so its shipped evidence is preserved rather than overwritten and rather than silently deleted: the measured Phase 15 before/after figures are captured as scripted output, with the date and the tree SHA they were measured at, into `docs/surface/examples/ERGO-03-FROZEN.md` **before** any example changes, and only then does the running test retire. The retirement carries a rationale citing this entry in `docs/SURFACE-AUDIT.md`'s ERGO-03 section, so a later reader finds the measurement rather than an absence. **The three open questions this phase answers by disposition rather than by ruling.** **Q-6** — whether `docs/SURFACE-CHANGES.md` publishes into the site (the open editorial question at `docs/SURFACE-AUDIT.md` §5.4): it **does not**. The A-2 maintainer page describes the surface toolchain — `tools/dump_surface.py`, `make surface`, baseline immutability — and links nothing that would drag a `.planning/` path past `pub02-internal-path`. **Q-7** — `DI-15.4-05` and the addendum-corrected `DI-15.4-06` (the public-versus-private adapter surface, and `substrate_vocabulary` deriving from in-tree adapters only) are **not ruled here**. `how-to/write-a-graph-backend-adapter.md` is rewritten against the surface that is settled — a `BackendRegistration(name, config_model, secret_fields, open_store, make_writer)` declared as `REGISTRATION` in a `*_write.py` module, discovered either by reading the adapter package on disk or through the `ccf.graph_backends` entry-point group — and the open question is recorded in the phase record, **never in the published page** (plan 16-06). **Q-8** — `make ci` **does** gain the three gate-checker suites (`tools/lints/tests`, `tools/publish_scan_tests`, `tools/docs_tests`), so that its documented claim to be the full local reproduction of the always-on pipeline becomes true rather than aspirational; plan 16-04 makes the three pre-existing failures green and plan 16-12 owns the `Makefile` composition. Originates: `16-CONTEXT.md` `D-02`, `D-13`, `D-14` and `D-15`; `16-RESEARCH.md` § D.4 and its Open Questions Q-1..Q-8; `src/ccf/replay/serve.py`; `src/ccf/products/contract.py`; `src/ccf/controlplane/artifacts.py`; `src/ccf/replay/record.py`; `src/ccf/graph/registry.py`; `tests/products/test_gate_two_call_shape.py`; `tests/products/test_required_operators_guard.py`; `tools/publish_scan_tests/test_docstring_leaks.py`; `.planning/phases/15.1-ergonomics-gap-closure/deferred-items.md`; `.planning/ROADMAP.md` Phase 16 criterion 10; `.planning/STATE.md` (the Phase 04 decision); whitepaper Ch 7 §7.6.

**D-IMPL-95 — Systems of record are read-only; CCF's Context Graph gets its own store on the same substrate.** Date: 2026-08-10. Decided by: Dave, verbatim: *"create your own db so there is no collision - I want the existing stores to provide the proxy for the enterprise data stores. This is what the CCF reads (never updates or modifies). The CCF can use the same backend to store its own KG for the Context Product. Lets make sure we do not contaminate the source data."* **The ruling.** A deployment binds two distinct stores per substrate, and the boundary between them is structural rather than conventional. The **system of record** — on the four-database FIBO harness, Fluree ledger `fibo:main` and the `fibo` databases on MongoDB, Neo4j and PostgreSQL — is an enterprise data store that CCF's L1 Signal Layer **reads and never writes**. The **Context Graph** (L3) is CCF-owned, lives in a separate ledger or database *on the same substrate*, and is written only through L2. No CCF write path may target a system of record, and no example, test, matrix leg or adapter configuration may name a source store as its graph target. **Why it is structural and not hygiene.** CLAUDE.md §10 makes single-writer an architectural invariant on one side of the fabric: only L2 holds a write handle to L3. This entry states the complementary constraint on the other side: L1 holds only a *read* handle to the systems of record. Together they make the fabric non-destructive to the enterprise **by construction**, which is the property that makes CCF adoptable where it matters — no institution grants a new platform write access to its book of record, and an SDK that requires one is unadoptable regardless of what else it proves. The whitepaper assumes ingestion is non-destructive (Ch 8 §8.3 Pattern 1, CDC→G) without stating the prohibition; this entry states it. **What it changes in Phase 16.** Plan 16-07's opt-in DGX legs resolve their graph target from environment-supplied, CCF-owned names — never `fibo:main` and never a `fibo` database. The existing no-host-literal rule already keeps endpoints out of the tree; this adds that the *target name* is likewise configuration, and that a source-store name appearing as a graph target is a defect, not a convenience. The always-on `sqlite` and `memory` legs are unaffected: both are CCF-owned by construction. **The measured state of the source estate at this date.** Plan 16-02 extended the KYC/UBO estate so the adoption argument's own question becomes answerable — at HEAD, `ownershipPercent` was an attribute of the owning **Party entity** and every `hasOwnershipInterestIn` ran Party→Corporation in exactly one hop, so *"who ultimately owns Acme?"* had no chain to traverse. The extension is a strict superset of the shipped FIBO data, verified rather than asserted: 0 entities lost, 0 relationships lost, 5 corp→corp edges added, and `ownershipPercent` moved onto **9 edges** where the originals carried it on none. Dave loaded it into the source stores on 2026-08-10, and the per-edge percentage is now present in **MongoDB, Neo4j and PostgreSQL** — 16 relationships, 9 carrying the property, confirmed by direct query. **Fluree is the exception, and the reason is modelling rather than tooling.** Fluree holds 4 of the 9 ownership pairs and no per-edge percentage, because there is no Fluree loader in the harness — `fluree_to_mongo.py` runs export-only, out of Fluree into the mirrors — and because RDF has nowhere to hang a qualifier on a triple. That absence is precisely *why* the source modelled `ownershipPercent` as a Party attribute and why every chain was one hop; it is not an oversight in the loaders. CCF's own Fluree adapter already solves this with the **interposed-edge layout** (`src/ccf/graph/backends/fluree_write.py`, 15.3-RESEARCH.md §R5): a CCF relationship is not a Cypher relationship there, because a property-reading relationship variable sees only reifier-bundled edges, so both the edge properties and the CCF type move onto node properties of an interposed node. Bringing the *source* estate to parity needs the same reification decided deliberately — FIBO's own `OwnershipInterest` modelling is the obvious candidate — and it writes to a live ledger, so it is proposed before it is run and is **not** carried out as a side effect of this entry. Until it lands, Fluree is a knowingly incomplete system of record and any claim of cross-substrate source parity must say so. **What was changed outside the repository.** `load_neo4j.py` and `load_postgres.py` in `/home/parkave/fluree/examples/fibo/` — Dave's out-of-tree harness, not SDK code — were patched to carry the per-edge percentage: one `SET rel.ownershipPercent = $pct` clause on the MERGE, and one nullable `ownership_percent NUMERIC` column with its INSERT binding. Both drop-and-reload, so both are idempotent; originals are preserved under `.backups/`. Recorded here because the phase's evidence depends on the source estate's shape, and a later reader re-running the harness needs to know the shipped scripts were amended and why. **What this entry does not rule.** Whether `compare_backends.py`'s own presence matrix should be extended to report percentages rather than owner/company pairs alone (it currently cannot distinguish a faithful mirror from a lossy one — the defect that hid this for a full load cycle); and whether a later phase promotes the four-substrate source estate into a fixture the SDK ships. Originates: Dave's ruling of 2026-08-10 during Phase 16 wave 3; CLAUDE.md §10 (single-writer, L1-L6) and §13 (out of scope: distributed multi-writer coordination); `16-RESEARCH.md` Assumption A3, which asked whether the DGX stores match the JSONL files and is hereby answered — they diverged, and the divergence was measured rather than assumed; `src/ccf/graph/backends/fluree_write.py`; `.planning/phases/16-.../16-02-SUMMARY.md`; whitepaper Ch 8 §8.3 Pattern 1.

**D-IMPL-96 — Criterion 10's "no fabric changed" is revoked; Context Debt must reach the consuming agent.** Date: 2026-08-10. Decided by: Dave, verbatim: *"The condition 'no fabric changed' needs to be revoked. It was established to prevent external adapters leaking into the core CCF product. In this case this is a true defect that was not implemented or caught during testing. And yes the fabric MUST change to do this the right way… Going forward I don't want to use the 'not fabric change' because it has been used out of context."* **What is revoked.** ROADMAP Phase 16 criterion 10 — the `git diff --stat -- src/ runtime/` assertion — is withdrawn, and with it the elaborate machinery built to work around it: `D-13`'s single enumerated exception for `ProductClassDeclaration`'s docstring, plan 16-19's `gate_16.py` allowlist of exactly that path-and-symbol pair, and `D-14`'s two-limb bound on what counts as bookkeeping versus carried Gate-2 residual. Those constructs were sound reasoning *inside* a premise that no longer holds; none of them survives the premise's withdrawal, and re-litigating them is wasted motion. Work already committed under them stands — the docstring correction was a real fix and the retirement-coupling sweep is real machinery. **Why it was there, and why that is not what it was doing.** The rule's purpose was **anti-leakage**: keep substrate adapters, harness bindings and domain values out of the core, which is the standing commitment CLAUDE.md §1 makes as harness neutrality and general-purposeness and which CI already enforces through the import lint, the `@algorithmic` lint and the No-Hardcoding lint. Those enforcements are unaffected and remain in force. What criterion 10 actually did was something else: it converted "do not let the wrong things in" into "do not change anything," and a documentation phase that cannot change the thing it documents will document a defect rather than fix it. That is what happened here. The rule is withdrawn rather than narrowed because a narrower wording invites the same substitution the next time a phase is scoped. **The defect it was concealing.** `DebtAssessment` — `debt`, `signal`, and `factors` carrying `freshness`, `completeness` and `semantic_consistency` — is computed by `ccf.halflife.debt`, exercised in five shipped examples, and appears on **neither** `ContextProductContract` **nor** `DecisionReplayRecord`. It is calculated and discarded. The whitepaper makes `D_c` load-bearing (§7.7.4–§7.7.8: debt gates Forced LLM and Fail-Closed), so a delivered product that carries no debt is a fidelity defect against §7.7 and not merely an ergonomic gap — measured at `6e9fd2f` by reading both contracts field by field. It was not caught because every gate the phase inherited tests what the fabric *does* rather than what the delivered product *carries*, and no test asserts the absence. **Why it matters more than a missing field.** The question that surfaced it is the adoption argument itself. Given a Context Product answering *"who ultimately owns Acme?"*, a consuming agent that cannot tell whether the ownership data was refreshed this morning or eighteen months ago has been handed a database query with extra ceremony. Five of the six axes reach the wire today — `E`/`R` as payload, `T` as `temporal_boundary`/`expires_at`/`ttl_anchor`, `P` as `policy_bundle_versions`, `Π` as `plan_id`, `L` as `operators_invoked`/`graph_snapshot_at` — and the sixth signal, the one that says how much to trust the other five, does not. Dave's stated shape for it is **richer than a scalar**: *"the response for ACME could return something that stated the last time ACME key investor data was updated - or if an event had transpired with one of the key shareholders that should be considered by the consuming agent."* That is per-subject recency **and** material-event salience, not a single product-level number — so the design question is what shape `D_c` takes on the wire, not merely where to put the existing `Decimal`. **Consequences, recorded rather than resolved here.** This entry withdraws a constraint and names a defect; it does not design the remedy. The remedy is a Phase D mini-cycle per CLAUDE.md §3, carrying at least: the on-the-wire shape of debt (scalar, per-factor, per-subject, or per-claim); whether it lands on `ContextProductContract`, on `DecisionReplayRecord`, or on both, and what replay determinism requires of each; how per-entity last-observed timestamps reach it, given `λ_c` already lives in Control Plane configuration; what "a material event transpired" means mechanically without encoding domain values in the core; and whether the three validation gates gain a fourth. Also open: whether the Section 1 example's fixed subject and fixed temporal boundary — both currently literals in artifacts — become runtime arguments in the same cycle, since an agent asking about any company at any time is the use case that exposed all of this. Originates: Dave's ruling of 2026-08-10 during Phase 16 wave 3, on reading `examples/s1-01-hello-context-product`; supersedes ROADMAP Phase 16 criterion 10; withdraws the premise of `D-13` and `D-14` in `D-IMPL-94`; whitepaper Ch 7 §7.7.4–§7.7.8 (`D_c` gates Forced LLM and Fail-Closed) and §5.2 (the six axes); `src/ccf/halflife/debt.py`; `src/ccf/products/contract.py`; `src/ccf/replay/record.py`; CLAUDE.md §1, §3 and §11.

**D-IMPL-97 — A Context Product is one standardised vessel; the assembler is a Protocol whose implementation is a declared agent.** Date: 2026-08-10. Decided by: Dave, across the Phase 16 wave-3 review of `examples/s1-01-hello-context-product`. Two rulings, recorded together because the second only makes sense given the first. **Ruling 1 — the vessel.** Verbatim: *"The true ContextProduct is nothing more than a vessel that has been assembled to represent a unique capability for the calling agent to consume. Kind of like MCP — a standard ContextProduct doesn't really need to subclass, it just needs to have certain operations and payloads which we have already determined via the whitepaper."* A Context Product is **one standardised vessel**, not a class hierarchy. What makes a vessel a `KYC_ContextProduct` is **how it was assembled**, not its Python type. Class identity stays data — a `schema_name` resolved through the Control Plane registry — and the domain payload is disciplined by a **declared payload schema** the class registers, in the shape MCP tools declare `inputSchema`. **This is a wording correction to whitepaper §7.6, not a reversal of it** (Dave, explicitly: *"Vessel wording is not reversal"*). §7.6 currently reads *"the typed scaffold every per-product subclass inherits and extends; the per-product subclasses carry the domain-specific payload"*; its **intent** — a typed payload with cross-cutting invariants living once on the base — is fully preserved by a declared schema, which delivers both without Python inheritance. Four arguments carried the ruling, in the order they matter: **debt by construction**, since debt on the base vessel means every product carries it while every subclass is a place to forget, and *debt computed and discarded* is exactly the defect this review found; **language neutrality**, since a subclass is a Python artifact and a product crossing A2A to a Java or TypeScript consumer cannot inherit a Pydantic class, so a Python-shaped type does not belong at a seam CCF commits to federating across organizational boundaries; **the No-Hardcoding Rule** (CLAUDE.md §6.4), since a `KYC_ContextProduct` class in code makes the product taxonomy code while `schema_name` plus a declared schema makes it configuration, and code contains interpreters, never the things interpreted; and **the code already works this way** — `ContextProductContract.schema_name` is documented verbatim as *"Class identity"*, a string resolved through a registry, so subclassing would layer a hierarchy over a registry that already does the job. Bounding the MCP analogy so it is not over-applied: MCP deliberately carries no governance semantics, being a capability and transport standard, whereas a Context Product's reason for existing is that it carries policy versions in force, lineage that must strictly equal what ran, a freshness SLA, debt, and a replay record that must exist before delivery. The accurate statement is **MCP-shaped in its typing discipline, with a governance envelope MCP has no equivalent for**. **Ruling 2 — the agent is the assembler implementation.** Verbatim: *"The assembler, which I agree should be a Protocol, is what makes the ContextProduct a KYC_ContextProduct"*, and then *"Agent becomes the implementation protocol."* The split is **Protocol for the producer, standardised vessel for the produced**, and the Protocol's implementation is a **declared agent** — a Control Plane artifact produced by the Agent Factory from the `context_assembly` archetype, not SDK code and not a subclass. What the assembler owns, per Dave: it knows where its data lives and how to get it; it talks to the six backends directly, so the adapter work of 15.2/15.3/15.4 is not lost; it creates or uses tools to gather what else it needs; it may enlist other agents to produce content for inclusion, or stay wholly deterministic; it may keep its own KG in whichever of the six stores is simplest; and the calling agent invokes the product rather than an MCP server in front of a database, so the orchestration mess is handled inside. **A correction to a finding recorded earlier in this review, made here so the record is not wrong.** An earlier pass asserted that `ContextAssemblyAgent` "does not exist in `src/`" and that "the producer side has a designated home and the home is empty." **That is false, and the grep behind it searched for the wrong symbol.** All six archetypes ship: `src/ccf/agents/archetypes.py` registers `workflow`, `document_intelligence`, `analytics_insights`, `monitoring_surveillance`, `content_generation` and `context_assembly` in `ARCHETYPES`, the last as `_ContextAssemblyComponent` declaring `fabric_interaction_pattern() == "typed-product-emission"` and two replay obligations — an emitted-product replay envelope, and policy composing before similarity at every assembly step. What is *actually* missing is narrower and more useful to know: `ArchetypeComponent` is a **declarative** Protocol — `archetype_id`, `fabric_interaction_pattern`, `replay_obligations`, `configuration_defaults` — so the archetype declares what the fabric requires of this class of agent and carries **no assembly behaviour**: no source binding, no tool or agent enlistment, no product emission.
