Its own docstring says so — *"The emission pattern is declared here… the transports (in-process, MCP egress, A2A) bind in I.3."* The assembler Protocol is therefore a **new behavioural surface** rather than a fill-in-the-blank, and the archetype is the seam it attaches to. **Why the agent is the right implementation, and this is confirmation rather than convenience.** The six specialization axes of CLAUDE.md §6.2 already describe what an assembler needs, and one of them is decisive: **axis 5, the half-life and debt profile**, is already a **required** field on agent definitions — `halflife_debt_profile`, a pinned `ccf-artifact://` reference, with `DebtProfile` a shipped `VersionedArtifact` at `src/ccf/controlplane/artifacts.py:225` and a published schema at `docs/schemas/ccf.debt-profile.json`. So the home for a product's debt configuration already exists, on the agent, declared and version-pinned. Axis 2 binds which product schemas it emits, axis 3 its perspective, axis 4 the policy regime bounding what it may consult, axis 6 its instruction artifacts; and channel 2 — skilling — governs which tools and peer agents it may enlist, with every grant and revocation already landing in replay. A product's identity therefore becomes **auditable**: replay answers not only what it returned but what it was permitted to consult. **What this entry does not settle.** The on-the-wire shape of debt (scalar, per-factor, per-subject, per-claim) and whether recency and salience are one field or two; whether debt lands on the product, the replay record, or both; what "a material event transpired" means mechanically without domain values in the core; whether payload validation becomes a fourth gate or binds at class registration; whether the request surface is a typed request contract per class — Dave's *"company name and top investors above 25%"* — which is what makes a product **callable** rather than configurable and would collapse the example's fixed-subject and fixed-instant defects into one design; and debt composition weighting for aggregate products. These are enumerated with their evidence in `docs/CONTEXT_PRODUCT_REVISION.md` §9 and are the agenda for the Phase D mini-cycle this ruling implies. Originates: Dave's rulings of 2026-08-10 during Phase 16 wave 3; corrects whitepaper §7.6's wording without reversing its intent; `docs/CONTEXT_PRODUCT_REVISION.md`; `src/ccf/products/contract.py`; `src/ccf/agents/archetypes.py`; `src/ccf/controlplane/artifacts.py`; `docs/schemas/ccf.agent-definition.json`; CLAUDE.md §6.1, §6.2, §6.3 and §6.4; whitepaper Ch 6 §6.3, Ch 7 §7.6 and §7.7.

**D-IMPL-98 — The request surface is a typed request contract per product class.** RESOLVED — option (a). Date: 2026-08-11. Approver: Dave. **The ruling.** Every product class declares a **typed request contract as a Control Plane artifact**, in the `SkillArgumentSchema` shape. Subject and instant become arguments of that request rather than literals in an artifact, which is what makes a Context Product **callable** rather than configurable, and which collapses the fixed-subject and fixed-instant defects into one design instead of two patches. **Measured cost the ruling was made against** (`docs/CONTEXT_PRODUCT_RULING_EVIDENCE.md` § Q1, option (a), measured at `3df4e91` on 2026-08-11 by `tests/spikes/test_contract_field_addition_cost.py`): **one** new artifact class plus **one** new `docs/schemas/*.json` under the bytes-equality drift gate (`Makefile:253`); the declaration threads through **29** `register_product_class` call sites across 26 files and **18** governed `serve(` call sites across 12 files; door-side validation lands on the three delivery paths enumerated under `D-IMPL-100`; and **0** `ContextProductContract` call-site repairs, because the request surface stays off the vessel. **What the alternatives were measured to cost, and why they were not taken.** Option (b) — no contract, subject and instant as explicit parameters of the existing serve path — is **18** `serve(` sites across 12 files, of which 9 are in `examples/` across 8 files, with zero new artifact classes; its measured consequence is that whitepaper §6.3 **Discovery** (`docs/CCF_Whitepaper_1-2.md:1463`) and **Addressability** (`:1465`) remain unrepresented in code, which is **2 of the 3** elements `docs/CONTEXT_PRODUCT_REVISION.md` §1.2 measured as absent. Option (c) — defer the contract, add the parameters now — is (b)'s 18 sites now plus (a)'s full population later, with the two populations overlapping at the 12 `serve(` files, so those 12 files are edited twice. The ruling therefore buys two of the three absent §6.3 elements for a cost that lands entirely on registration and serve, and not at all on the vessel. **Scope consequence, ruled explicitly: discovery and addressability are NOT declaration-only this phase.** `15.5-CONTEXT.md`'s `<deferred>` block permitted a declaration-only landing; under option (a) that permission is moot, because addressability and the request contract are the same commitment. Both land for real. **What this does not re-open.** The slot this contract fills is already reserved — `D-IMPL-49`'s canonical selection signature carries an optional typed per-class block declared in the Control Plane, and this entry supplies that block's shape for product requests rather than altering the signature. Cross-referenced, not restated. **Governs:** plan `15.5-08` (built entirely against this ruling), and the request-path work in `15.5-10` and `15.5-13`; requirement `CPS-07`; ROADMAP Phase 15.5 criterion 7. Originates: `docs/CONTEXT_PRODUCT_REVISION.md` §9 Q1 and `D-IMPL-97`'s closing enumeration of what it did not settle; whitepaper Ch 6 §6.3 *Addressability* at `docs/CCF_Whitepaper_1-2.md:1465`, worked there as `/context-products/credit-risk/counterparty/{counterparty-id}?intent=…&freshness=…`; Dave's stated shape, *"maybe it's just the company name and 'top investors above 25%'."*

**D-IMPL-99 — Debt's shape on the wire is per-factor, using the shipped `DebtAssessment`.** RESOLVED — per-factor. Date: 2026-08-11. Approver: Dave. **The ruling.** Debt travels as the shipped `DebtAssessment` — `debt`, `signal`, and the `factors` carrying freshness, completeness and semantic consistency — not as a scalar. **Measured cost** (`docs/CONTEXT_PRODUCT_RULING_EVIDENCE.md` § Q2): **0 new type work.** The type already ships at `src/ccf/halflife/debt.py:149-165`, is already `Decimal`-safe for replay, and has **6** construction sites in 6 files with **9** `assess_debt` sites in 3 files; adding it to a contract costs `D-IMPL-100`'s placement cost and nothing else. The scalar option was measured as one `Decimal` field wherever the placement ruling puts it — cheaper by nothing, since the placement cost is the whole cost either way — and loses which of F/C/S collapsed, which is the case `DebtAssessment`'s own docstring makes. **Per-subject and per-claim were ruled out as NOT MEASURABLE, and the reason is a missing key rather than a missing budget.** `Observation` (`src/ccf/halflife/decay.py::Observation`) carries exactly `("observation_type", "observed_at")` — no claim id, no entity URI, no join key — so the per-subject key does not exist anywhere on the debt path, and there is no structure in this tree to count a per-subject cost against. That is the same missing link `D-IMPL-101` discharges; building it is a precondition, not a field addition. **Sub-question, ruled: recency and salience are not one field, because only one of them exists.** Recency is computable today by `FreshnessScorer.score(self, observations: Sequence[Observation], *, now: datetime) -> Decimal` (`src/ccf/halflife/debt.py:233-262`, signature read rather than assumed); salience has **no producer anywhere in `src/` or `runtime/`**. Recency ships in the per-factor payload; salience defers under `D-IMPL-101`. **What this does not re-open.** The computable definitions of F, C and S, and the scorer-version pinning that makes a per-factor payload replayable at all, are `D-IMPL-47`'s and stand unaltered — cross-referenced, not restated. **Governs:** plan `15.5-11`; requirements `CPS-01` and `CPS-02`. Originates: `docs/CONTEXT_PRODUCT_REVISION.md` §9 Q2; `D-IMPL-96`, which named debt computed-and-discarded as a fidelity defect against whitepaper Ch 7 §7.7.4–§7.7.8 and left the wire shape open.

**D-IMPL-100 — Debt lands on both the product and the replay record.** RESOLVED — both. Date: 2026-08-11. Approver: Dave. **The ruling.** `DebtAssessment` is carried on `ContextProductContract` **and** on `DecisionReplayRecord`. **Measured cost** (`docs/CONTEXT_PRODUCT_RULING_EVIDENCE.md` § Q3, the four-placement table): the content address of the spike's stand-in vessel **moves** from the measured baseline `14217148e333aee2004640a66f5bdcfff609cbd808753c49ce9fa94af6c50372` to `012f542e…f4f4904`, **and is reconstructible** — a rehydrator reading debt back off the record reproduces the identical bytes, an **observed byte-equality**, not an inference. The cost is therefore product-only's address move **plus** record-only's record work **plus** a signature change to `ReplayEnvironment.rehydrate_product`, a **public Protocol with 7 implementations across 6 files** (`src/ccf/replay/engine.py`, `tests/replay/test_engine_rehydration.py`, `tests/replay/test_query_sequence_pin.py`, `perf/suites/replay/conftest.py`, `examples/06-decision-replay/run.py`, `examples/external/00-hello-context/run.py`), plus a `docs/surface-changes/` fragment. **Product-only is NOT VIABLE, by observed measurement rather than by argument.** The same field added to the vessel with nothing on the record to reconstruct it from makes all **7** `rehydrate_product` implementations return a product the engine's hash check at `src/ccf/replay/engine.py:449-455` rejects with `ReplayDivergence`, because `canonical_bytes` walks a model field-by-field over `type(value).model_fields` (`src/ccf/contracts/canonical.py:102-111`) and the field is therefore in the address. Record-only is deterministic — address unchanged, **0** of the 42 `ContextProductContract` sites move, the tripwire stays green — and reaches no consumer at all, failing ROADMAP criterion 1 and whitepaper `:2696` (*"a runtime signal the agent must consume"*). The fourth placement derived during research, debt on `ServedProduct` beside the product, is one type and one line at its single construction site in `src/ccf/replay/serve.py` and moves no address — and reaches **0 of 3** wire consumers, which is the measured cost of what it does not do. **The three doors, named because criterion 1 says no delivery path drops it, and because a ruling priced against one door understates the cost by two.** All three serialize `servable.product` and **none** touches `ServedProduct.replay_ref`: (1) fabric-internal MCP egress at `runtime/ccf_runtime/mcp/egress.py:152-163`, serializing `canonical_bytes(servable.product)`; (2) the HTTP API `retrieve` route at `runtime/ccf_runtime/http_api/routes/product.py:35-42`, serializing `canonical_response(servable.product)`; (3) the **external front door**, the Experience server's `context_product` resource at `runtime/ccf_runtime/mcp/experience.py:560-570`, serializing `canonical_bytes(servable.product)` — paths 1 and 3 carry the identical three-string signature `context_product(tenant, product_class, version)` and the identical body, which is why pricing against path 1 alone is the specific error the enumeration exists to prevent. **Under this ruling debt reaches all three doors plus in-process callers, and reaches them without a code change at any door**, because each already serializes the vessel and the vessel now carries debt. **The tripwire the implementing plan re-runs:** `pytest tests/replay/test_engine_rehydration.py -q` → **13 passed in 1.07s**, exit 0, measured at `3df4e91` before any change. Plan `15.5-11` runs it before its first edit and after every commit that changes a hashed contract. **Governs:** plan `15.5-11`, which implements against three doors rather than one; requirements `CPS-01` and `CPS-11`; ROADMAP Phase 15.5 criterion 1. Originates: `docs/CONTEXT_PRODUCT_REVISION.md` §9 Q3, whose enumeration offered only product / record / both; the fourth option was derived from the tree during `15.5-RESEARCH.md` and is costed in the evidence rather than adopted. Realizes the remedy `D-IMPL-96` named and deliberately declined to design.

**D-IMPL-101 — "A material event transpired": salience defers to Phase 17; recency ships now.** RESOLVED — option (d). Date: 2026-08-11. Approver: Dave. **The ruling.** The phase ships **recency only**. Salience — the *"an event had transpired with one of the key shareholders"* limb of Dave's Acme case — is deferred, and no mechanism for it lands in this phase. **Measured cost** (`docs/CONTEXT_PRODUCT_RULING_EVIDENCE.md` § Q4): **0**, because recency is already computable by `FreshnessScorer.score(self, observations: Sequence[Observation], *, now: datetime) -> Decimal` (`src/ccf/halflife/debt.py:233-262`), and salience has **no producer anywhere in `src/` or `runtime/`** to defer. **Option (c), supersession-as-Policy-event — the most whitepaper-faithful of the four — was ruled out on measurement as a build rather than a wiring task, and assumption A3 is discharged in the negative with it.** Traced: `supersed` occurs **0** times in `src/ccf/halflife/`, **0** in `src/ccf/projection/` and **0** in `src/ccf/products/`, against **348** in `src/ccf/graph/`; supersession lives at `src/ccf/graph/protocols.py:132-133` as `ClaimRecord.superseded_by` / `superseded_at`; the consumer that would need it is `FreshnessScorer.score`, whose only input is a `Sequence[Observation]`; and `Observation` carries no join key by which the two could meet. Decisively, **`Observation(...)` has 0 constructor call sites in `src/` or `runtime/` — no SDK code constructs one at all.** Making the link exist requires an identity field on `Observation` (or a parallel event sequence beside it), **plus** a producer reading `superseded_by`/`superseded_at` off the graph and joining the two, **plus** a path from that producer to the debt computation. None of the three exists. **Consequence for the phase, stated because a plan depends on it:** plan `15.5-11` edits `src/ccf/halflife/debt.py` under this ruling and **does not** make the A3 link exist, so the evidence module's reported-not-asserted A3 verdict stays true for the phase's life rather than being turned red by correct work. Options (a) opaque per-class salience identifiers compared structurally and never interpreted (the shipped `SkillRequirements` precedent, `D-IMPL-29`), and (b) a `SalienceScorerProtocol` beside the shipped `DebtFactorScorerProtocol`, are **not** ruled in and **not** ruled out — they remain the two shapes a Phase 17 salience cycle chooses between, with their costs already measured here (option (a): 29 `register_product_class` sites or 0 under a default, no new producer needed; option (b): one new Protocol, 0 existing `assess_debt` sites touched if the factor is optional). **Governs:** plan `15.5-11`; requirement `CPS-01`. Originates: `docs/CONTEXT_PRODUCT_REVISION.md` §9 Q4; `15.5-RESEARCH.md` assumption A3, rated MEDIUM-HIGH and explicitly untraced there, traced and discharged in the evidence; whitepaper Ch 7 §7.7.

**D-IMPL-102 — Payload validation is schema validity at registration and payload conformity at bind; no fourth gate.** RESOLVED. Date: 2026-08-11. Approver: Dave. **Dave's ruling, verbatim:** *"Q5 payload validation → Schema validity at registration + payload conformity at Bind (request time). Gate scripts remain structural only."* **The ruling, in its three parts, because the third is as load-bearing as the first two.** (1) **Schema validity is checked at registration**: when a product class is registered, its **declared** payload schema is checked for well-formedness — a malformed declaration is refused at wiring time, before any request exists. (2) **Payload conformity is checked at bind, which is request time**: when an actual request is bound to a product class, the actual payload is checked against that class's declared schema. This limb **couples directly to `D-IMPL-98`** — the typed request contract per product class is what a request binds to, so bind is precisely where a payload meets its declared schema, and the two rulings describe one surface rather than two. (3) **No fourth gate is added to `run_gates`.** The three shipped validation gates — the empty-operator guard, lineage strict equality, TTL bounds — **stay structural and are not joined by a payload gate**, which keeps the whitepaper's three-validation-gate commitment intact rather than widening it. **Measured cost** (`docs/CONTEXT_PRODUCT_RULING_EVIDENCE.md` § Q5). Limb 1, registration, is option (b)'s measurement: **29** `register_product_class` call sites across 26 files if required, or **0** under a default on the `accepted_policy_binding_modes` precedent (`src/ccf/products/contract.py:193-198`), and **0** serve-time benchmark cost. Limb 2, bind-time conformity, lands on the request surface `D-IMPL-98` creates rather than on the gate path — that surface's own measured population is § Q1 option (a)'s **18** governed `serve(` call sites across 12 files and the three delivery paths enumerated under `D-IMPL-100`; conformity checking adds no separate call-site population of its own, because it rides the bind the request contract already introduces. Limb 3 is a cost **not** incurred: option (a)'s **43** `run_gates` call sites across 22 files are untouched, and none of the `source_attestation` keyword-only-defaulted machinery (`src/ccf/products/gates.py:306`) is needed, because nothing is added there. **A measurement that is deliberately NOT cited as support for this ruling.** The evidence's § A8 headroom figures — 80 ns, 230 ns and 507 ns of absolute margin on `perf/suites/micro/test_gates.py`'s three benchmarks — were measured because a *fourth gate* would have landed on the canonical `run_gates` sequence at serve time. **No gate lands, so those figures are not the relevant measurement for this ruling** and must not be quoted as though one were. They remain the correct figures for what they measure, and `D-IMPL-107` still governs how the battery around them is read. **Consequence for the phase, recorded because it changes a plan's premise rather than its detail.** Plan **`15.5-09` is written on the premise of a payload gate in `run_gates` and must be re-scoped**: it ships **bind-time conformity**, not a fourth gate. Its serve-time perf argument goes with the gate — there is no new gate to benchmark — and its verification narrows accordingly. This entry records the consequence and does not make the edit; the re-scope is the planning owner's, not this plan's. Plan `15.5-07` is unaffected in kind: it still declares the payload schema and binds it at registration, which is limb 1 exactly. **Governs:** plans `15.5-07` (schema declaration and registration-time validity) and `15.5-09` (re-scoped to bind-time conformity); requirement `CPS-03`. Originates: `docs/CONTEXT_PRODUCT_REVISION.md` §9 Q5; whitepaper Ch 6 §6.3 and Ch 7 §7.6, whose three validation gates this ruling deliberately leaves at three.

**D-IMPL-103 — `SkillArgumentSchema` is extended in place to one level of nesting.** RESOLVED — branch (i), extend in place. Date: 2026-08-11. Approver: Dave. **Dave's ruling, verbatim, because its scope is the whole of it:** *"Extend SkillArgumentSchema in place to support one level of nested objects and lists-of-objects (sufficient for the UBO owner list). The three typed facts previously lost under flattening become expressible. Existing depth-1 usages remain valid; the change is a widening, not a replacement. Record the schema change in the same surface-change fragment that plan 15.5-11 already needs for the rehydrate_product Protocol update."* **Two bounds, stated plainly because both bind and either one read loosely turns this into a different ruling. First: ONE level of nesting — nested objects and lists-of-objects, one deep — not arbitrary depth.** Depth 2 is the ceiling. **Second: a WIDENING, not a replacement.** Every existing depth-1 usage remains valid, no shipped declaration is rewritten, and no consumer of the current shape breaks. **This supersedes the standing depth-1 ruling on `SkillArgumentSchema` (`src/ccf/skills/catalog.py:194-206`) *only to the extent of that one level*** — the ruling is otherwise intact and continues to govern. **Measured cost** (`docs/CONTEXT_PRODUCT_RULING_EVIDENCE.md` § Q5 sub-question, branch (i)): **3** `to_input_schema()` call sites move (`src/ccf/skills/toolsurface.py`, `tests/skills/test_catalog.py`, `tests/skills/test_tool_surface.py`); **9** files mention `argument_schema` and are in the blast radius — including **all three harness adapters** (`adk`, `claude_agent`, `langgraph`), `cli/commands/skill_import.py`, `src/ccf/skills/catalog.py`, `src/ccf/skills/toolsurface.py` and 3 test modules; **1** committed schema regenerates, `docs/schemas/ccf.skill-catalog-entry.json`, under the bytes-equality gate; and **1** `docs/surface-changes/` fragment is required — which per the ruling is **the same fragment plan `15.5-11` already writes** for the `rehydrate_product` Protocol change, not a second one. **The two alternatives and their measured costs.** Branch (ii), a nesting-capable sibling `ProductPayloadSchema`, duplicates **5** symbols (`JsonSchemaType`, `SkillArgumentProperty`, `SkillArgumentSchema`, `to_input_schema`, and the required-names-exist validator) and moves **0** existing call sites — a second machinery where `D-IMPL-97` asked for reuse of the one already proven. Branch (iii), flattening v0 payloads to one level, moves **0** files and regenerates **0** schemas and **does not survive without loss**: measured against `examples/s1-01-hello-context-product/artifacts/expected.yaml`, **3** declared fields lose a typed fact — `ownership_percent_by_party` (object keyed by party URI), `beneficial_owners` (array of two-field objects) and `entity_count_by_class` (object keyed by entity class) — and flattening `beneficial_owners` into parallel arrays preserves the values while degrading the pairing to an index convention the schema cannot state. Those are the three facts the ruling's first sentence restores. **Assumption A1 is confirmed rather than corrected:** `SkillArgumentSchema` — a schema declared **as data** — is what `D-IMPL-97` meant by reusing the machinery already proven on eight artifact classes, as against the `docs/schemas/` export path, which generates schemas **from Pydantic models** and would therefore require one model per product class, i.e. exactly the subclassing `D-IMPL-97` rules out. **Governs:** plans `15.5-07` (the payload schema declaration) and `15.5-08` (the request contract, which sits in the same shape); requirement `CPS-03`. Originates: `docs/CONTEXT_PRODUCT_REVISION.md` §9 Q5's unavoidable sub-question; `15.5-RESEARCH.md` assumption A1; `D-IMPL-97` ruling 1 (the declared payload schema in the shape MCP tools declare `inputSchema`).

**D-IMPL-104 — Aggregate debt composes worst-factor-wins; the rule is stated now and implemented later.** RESOLVED as to meaning; **implementation DEFERRED**. Date: 2026-08-11. Approver: Dave. **Dave's direction, on why an entry exists for a surface that does not:** *"Record a D-IMPL that states the aggregation rule ... even while the aggregate-product surface does not yet exist. Implementation can wait until the surface appears; the meaning cannot."* **The rule.** An aggregate product's debt is the **per-factor maximum across its constituents** — worst factor wins, freshness against freshness, completeness against completeness, semantic consistency against semantic consistency. **Recorded as consistent with `D-IMPL-31` rather than as a new axiom:** that ruling already composes validity by taking the **most constraining** constituent, and worst-factor-wins is the same discipline expressed on a metric where higher means worse. An aggregate is no fresher than its stalest load-bearing constituent and no more complete than its emptiest; stating that on one axis and its opposite on another would be two rules where one will do. Cross-referenced, not restated. **Measured cost** (`docs/CONTEXT_PRODUCT_RULING_EVIDENCE.md` § Q6): all three weighting alternatives — class-declared decision-critical components, conjunctive-over-critical, and multiplicative-over-all — are **NOT MEASURABLE from this tree**, and the cell says why rather than estimating. An aggregate product — a `ContextProductContract` composed from constituent products — **has no representation in this tree at all**, so there is nothing to count against; and `union_lineage` (**10** call sites across 3 files, already property-tested) composes a **producer/consumer pair** across the peering boundary rather than an N-way sibling set, its own docstring recording order-independence as *"a law about the two set axes"* only, which leaves *whose* `graph_snapshot_at` undefined for N siblings. Option (d), deferring the debt limb, costs **0**. **Implementation is deferred until the aggregate-product surface exists.** Plan `15.5-12` ships criterion 8's three named limbs — lineage union, the TTL floor, and the SLA refusal — **without the debt limb**, and cites this entry for the meaning rather than inventing a rule at the moment it first needs one. When the aggregate surface arrives, the combinator is one pure function beside `context_debt`, adds **0** new weights, and therefore does not touch the additive-form foreclosure at `src/ccf/halflife/debt.py:16-22`. **Governs:** plan `15.5-12`; requirement `CPS-08`; ROADMAP Phase 15.5 criterion 8. Originates: `docs/CONTEXT_PRODUCT_REVISION.md` §9 Q6; `D-IMPL-31`, whose mixing-refusal rule criterion 8 realizes and whose composition discipline this rule extends; whitepaper Ch 7 §7.7 (INV-CH7-62/63, the multiplicative-conjunctive F·C·S commitment).

**D-IMPL-105 — Debt is not per-principal; the coupling dissolves.** RESOLVED — option (b). Date: 2026-08-11. Approver: Dave. **The ruling.** A refused consultation is **simply not part of `C_required` for that principal**. If a principal is not entitled to a source, that source is not among the things their decision requires, so its absence is not a shortfall and does not register as debt. Debt stays **one computation per assembled product**, identical for every principal who receives that product. **Measured cost** (`docs/CONTEXT_PRODUCT_RULING_EVIDENCE.md` § Q7, option (b)): **0**. `assess_debt` remains pure over `(factors, profile)`, its signature is unchanged, and all **9** call sites across 3 files are unchanged. Option (a) — debt computed against the effective entitlement set — is **NOT MEASURABLE from this tree**, because no per-principal entitlement surface exists at any door yet: `build_egress_server` binds **no** authorizer and has **2** call sites in 2 files, and the `TenantAuthorizerProtocol` action tuple is `(grant, tenant, action_id)` with **no product-class dimension**; its cost is bounded below by criterion 5's own work and cannot be separated from it until that lands. Option (c), carrying both figures, is (a)'s cost plus one more field on a surface that does not exist. **The threat this ruling forecloses, recorded because it was named for the first time in the ruling package and is the substantive reason to prefer dissolution over resolution.** Under options (a) and (c), completeness is computed against what the caller was permitted to consult, so the completeness figure **moves when a source is withheld** — and a caller can therefore **infer what they are not allowed to see from the debt they are shown**. That is an oracle over the entitlement configuration, reachable by any principal with ordinary delivery access and no privileged read, and it is worse than a plain refusal because it is quiet: nothing in the response says a consultation was refused, only that completeness is lower than it might be, and a caller who can vary the question can map the boundary. Dissolving the coupling closes the channel **by construction rather than by redaction** — there is no per-principal figure to differ, so there is nothing to difference. **What this does not move.** Entitlement-formative construction stands exactly as the whitepaper commits it (`docs/CCF_Whitepaper_1-2.md:1529`, `:1342`): different principals still legitimately receive different products, assembled from what each may consult. What is ruled is only that **debt is not one of the things that differs**. **Governs:** plan `15.5-11` (debt), and the entitlement track behind ROADMAP Phase 15.5 criterion 5; requirements `CPS-01` and `CPS-05`. Originates: `docs/CONTEXT_PRODUCT_REVISION.md` §9 Q7 and §10; `15.5-RESEARCH.md` § Open Questions, which put forward the dissolving reading; whitepaper Ch 6 §6.3 and Ch 7 §7.7.

**D-IMPL-106 — `freshness_sla` is class-level on `RegisteredProductClass`.** RESOLVED — option (b). Date: 2026-08-11. Approver: Dave. **The whitepaper inconsistency this resolves, quoted per CLAUDE.md §15's ambiguous-passage discipline rather than paraphrased.** The sidebar enumeration at `docs/CCF_Whitepaper_1-2.md:2381`: *"The seven contract fields enumerated below are the same seven fields Fig-CH07-13 renders as the Context Product Spec sidebar (schema, **freshness SLA**, TTL policy, policy binding, lineage graph, semantic version, payload) — the prose specification and the visual sidebar commit to the same contract surface."* The prose enumeration immediately following at `:2381-2405`, unpacked field by field: `schema_name`, `schema_version` (`:2385-2387`), **`plan_id`** (`:2391`), `expires_at` (`:2393`), `ttl_anchor` (`:2395`), `lineage` (`:2401`), `payload` (`:2405`). **The two enumerations of "the same seven fields" differ by exactly one element:** the sidebar names freshness SLA where the prose names `plan_id`. **The two readings and their consequences.** Reading 1 — the sidebar is authoritative, the instance carries an SLA field, and the base therefore has eight fields rather than seven, or else `plan_id` is not one of them. Reading 2 — the prose is authoritative, and the sidebar's "freshness SLA" names a **class-level promise the product was assembled under** rather than an instance field, in which case both enumerations are consistent, nothing is missing, and `plan_id` keeps its place on the vessel. **Reading 2 is ruled.** The SLA is a property of the product class, declared once at registration and pinned by version. **Measured cost** (`docs/CONTEXT_PRODUCT_RULING_EVIDENCE.md` § Q8, option (b)): **29** `register_product_class` call sites across 26 files if required-with-no-default, or **0** under a default on the `accepted_policy_binding_modes` precedent — and **0 content addresses move.** It is the **cheapest measured option of the three**, and the only one that moves no address. Option (a), an instance field on `ContextProductContract`, is **42** call sites across 38 files if required or **0** under a positive sentinel, and **moves the address** exactly as the `D-IMPL-100` product-only measurement shows for any added hashed field — replay-safe, unlike debt, because an SLA *is* reconstructible from the registered product class, but an address move nonetheless. Option (c), both, is (a) plus (b) plus a comparison function. **Two further things the ruling settles.** It **makes the currently-false `src/ccf/products/contract.py` docstring true**, which is why the placement was worth ruling rather than defaulting. And §6.3's *"Freshness SLA varies by signal and by decision"* (`:1453`) suggests a per-signal structure rather than a scalar; that structure's own cost is not separable from the placement until its shape is declared, so **the placement is ruled here and the internal shape is the implementing plan's to declare** — no scalar is ruled in by silence. **What this does not re-open.** `D-IMPL-30` already rules the §6.3 specification element count as nine and already rules the Fig-CH07-13 seven-field typed base a *different artifact* from those elements — which is precisely the distinction reading 2 rests on. ROADMAP Phase 15.5 criterion 2 already reads nine and already cites it at `.planning/ROADMAP.md:930`; **there is no eight-to-nine correction to make**, and none was made. Cross-referenced, not restated. **Governs:** plan `15.5-07`; requirement `CPS-02`. Originates: `15.5-RESEARCH.md` § Open Questions Q8 — surfaced by research, not present in `docs/CONTEXT_PRODUCT_REVISION.md` §9; whitepaper Ch 6 §6.3 at `:1453` and Ch 7 §7.6 at `:2381-2405`; Fig-CH07-13.

**D-IMPL-107 — The `test_composed_chain` perf regression is investigated and fixed inside Phase 15.5.** RESOLVED — option (c), fix in-phase, overriding the recommendation put to Dave. Date: 2026-08-11. Approver: Dave. **The measured state.** `make perf-micro` **already exits 1 at this phase's base commit**, on two benchmarks that are not on the Context Product surface (`docs/CONTEXT_PRODUCT_RULING_EVIDENCE.md` § A8): `perf/suites/micro/test_dispatch.py::test_run_coroutine_sync_overhead` at 3.264e-05 s against a 2.928e-05 s baseline, **+11.48%**; and `perf/suites/micro/test_operators.py::test_composed_chain` at 1.22429 s against a 0.368903 s baseline, **+231.87%** — the ruling was taken against a re-run of the same benchmark reading 1.21884 s, a +230% regression either way. Both predate this phase and neither was caused by it: plan `15.5-01` changed no `src/`, `runtime/`, `examples/` or `perf/` file. Recorded as `DI-15.5-01` in the phase's `deferred-items.md`. **The ruling.** The recommendation presented was option (a) — narrow the affected plans' `<automated>` blocks so they measure the per-benchmark `test_gates.py::*` verdicts rather than the battery's exit code, and carry the regression. **Dave overrode it: `test_composed_chain` is to be investigated and fixed within Phase 15.5.** Not deferred, and **not re-baselined** — a 230% regression absorbed by moving the baseline is a regression no later reader can see, and the baseline chain is the only thing that makes `CPS-13`'s 10% band mean anything. **This is new scope the phase carries, and it is recorded with an owner so that a plan takes it rather than every plan assuming another will.** **Two consequences that travel with the ruling.** First, plans `15.5-07` and `15.5-09` **keep `make perf-micro` in their `<automated>` blocks**, on the expectation that the gate goes green; if the fix has not landed when either runs, the per-benchmark `test_gates.py::*` margins — **80 ns**, **230 ns** and **507 ns** of absolute headroom to the 10% ceiling, § A8 — are the figures that carry the verdict for this phase's work, and the battery's exit code is evidence about neither direction — noting that under `D-IMPL-102` **no fourth gate lands**, so those three margins are headroom this phase does not spend rather than a budget it draws against. Second, **a separate defect worth correcting whatever happens to the gate**: in `15.5-09` `make perf-micro` is the **first** term of an `&&` chain (`15.5-09-PLAN.md:197`), so while it exits 1 it **short-circuits the entire remainder of that verification** — `tests/test_surface_drift.py`, `tests/perf_smoke` and `tests/perf_gate` never run at all, and the plan reports red for a reason unrelated to the three suites it silently skipped. `15.5-07` places it **last** in its chain (`15.5-07-PLAN.md:345`) and therefore skips nothing; the asymmetry is accidental rather than designed, and `15.5-09`'s ordering should be corrected independently of the fix. **Not in scope of this entry:** `test_run_coroutine_sync_overhead`'s +11.48% stays carried as `DI-15.5-01`; only `test_composed_chain` is ruled in. **Governs:** the phase; requirement `CPS-13`; the `<automated>` blocks of plans `15.5-07` and `15.5-09`. Originates: `docs/CONTEXT_PRODUCT_RULING_EVIDENCE.md` § A8 and its Provenance note that the perf figures required one deliberate `make perf-micro` run because that battery is outside `make ci` by design; `.planning/phases/15.5-…/deferred-items.md` `DI-15.5-01`.

**D-IMPL-108 — The product resolver seam is widened once, by a new plan `15.5-15`, carrying both the typed request and the authorized perspective.** RESOLVED — a new wave-5 plan. Date: 2026-08-11. Approver: Dave. **The gap this closes, which was two gaps that turned out to be one.** `DI-15.5-03` recorded that all three exposed doors resolve and enforce an entitled `Perspective` but do not *forward* it into the assemble-and-gate path, so a door guarantees the **refusal** leg while leaving unproven that the product it served was shaped under the perspective it authorized. Separately, plan `15.5-08` declares the must-have truth *"The request surface is validated where it crosses the door, not downstream"* while its `files_modified` names **no door file** — only `src/ccf/products/`, `src/ccf/controlplane/artifacts.py` and `docs/schemas/`. Both are blocked on the same structural change: `ProductResolverProtocol` (`runtime/ccf_runtime/mcp/egress.py:136`) and `ExperienceProductResolverProtocol` (`runtime/ccf_runtime/mcp/experience.py:215`) are **three-string seams** (`tenant, product_class, version`) through which neither a `Perspective` nor a typed request can pass. **Measured blast radius**, counted at `db4fdce` rather than estimated: **2** Protocol definitions; **3** invocation sites (`mcp/egress.py:267`, `http_api/routes/product.py:107`, `mcp/experience.py:629`); **1** dependency field (`http_api/dependencies.py:71`); and **24** files across `runtime/`, `examples/` and `tests/` that supply or reference a resolver. `DI-15.5-03`'s planning-time estimate of "roughly twenty call sites" is confirmed accurate. **The ruling.** Plan `15.5-08` proceeds in wave 4 as scoped — it builds the request contract as a Control Plane artifact with its committed schema, and does **not** wire it. A **new plan `15.5-15`** lands in wave 5 and reshapes the seam **exactly once**, carrying the typed request and the authorized `Perspective` through the same widening. **The reason it is one plan and not two is the reason Q1 option (c) was rejected**: reshaping this seam twice means editing the same 24 files twice, and `D-IMPL-98` declined that cost for the request surface on identical grounds. Doing it once for both is the same argument applied consistently. **Why wave 5 and not wave 4.** Wave 4 already serializes on `src/ccf/products/__init__.py` and `docs/SURFACE-CHANGES.md` across plans `15.5-08`, `09`, `11` and `12`; adding the largest plan in the phase to it would serialize the whole wave behind it. Wave 5 currently holds only `15.5-10`, which touches `src/ccf/agents/` and does not collide with `runtime/`. `15.5-15` therefore runs parallel to `15.5-10` and depends on `15.5-08`. **Consequence for the phase:** the phase is **15 plans, not 14**, and `DI-15.5-03` acquires an owner rather than reaching the gate unowned — Dave named it *"the highest-priority structural debt for the later waves"* on 2026-08-11. **Governs:** new plan `15.5-15`; the door-validation limb of plan `15.5-08`'s must-haves; requirements `CPS-07` and `CPS-12`. Originates: `DI-15.5-03` in the phase's `deferred-items.md`, recorded by plan `15.5-06`; plan `15.5-08`'s unmet must-have truth, found by the orchestrator at wave 3; `D-IMPL-98`'s double-edit argument; `D-IMPL-102` limb 2, whose bind-time conformity check needs the same widened seam to run at the door at all.

**D-IMPL-109 — The ungoverned HTTP product door refuses rather than serving a product no perspective authorized.** RESOLVED — accept the refusal. Date: 2026-08-11. Approver: Dave. **What changed and why it was forced rather than chosen.** Plan `15.5-15` widened `ProductResolverProtocol` and `ExperienceProductResolverProtocol` so the `Perspective` a door authorizes reaches the assembler (`D-IMPL-108`, closing `DI-15.5-03`). The widened seam **requires** the perspective — deliberately not defaulted, because a resolver handed nothing and constructing its own is indistinguishable from one honouring the door's, which is the exact ambiguity the deferred item existed to close. The HTTP door in the **ungoverned** posture can name no perspective, so it now returns **503 `product-door-ungoverned`** where it previously served. The plan's own must-have anticipated this: *"A door that cannot pass a perspective refuses rather than silently constructing one."* **The ruling.** The refusal stands. A door that cannot name the perspective it authorized should not serve, and manufacturing a perspective to preserve availability reintroduces precisely the defect this phase closed. **What it costs, stated rather than buried.** This **supersedes `T-07-24`**, which preserved that posture, and **narrows `D-IMPL-73`'s acknowledged-insecure posture by one route**; `ccf product get` reports the refusal as well. Three shipped tests were **rewritten, not deleted**, to assert the new posture, and the canonical-bytes and 404 claims moved to the governed app where they still assert something. **Restoration is available and documented:** two `create_app` arguments, with code, in `docs/surface-changes/15.5-15-resolver-seam.md` — the ungoverned posture is narrowed, not removed, and a deployment that needs the old behaviour can have it explicitly rather than by default. Removing the ungoverned posture entirely was considered and NOT taken: `D-IMPL-73` exists deliberately for local development and withdrawing it needs its own ruling. **Governs:** `runtime/ccf_runtime/http_api/`; the availability posture of the ungoverned product route; supersedes `T-07-24`. Originates: plan `15.5-15`, flagged by its executor as `threat_flag: availability-posture-change` and escalated to the phase gate rather than resolved in-plan.

**D-IMPL-110 — The surface-drift gate's Protocol blind spot is recorded as debt and fixed in Phase 17.** RESOLVED — defer, with the inventory recorded. Date: 2026-08-11. Approver: Dave. **The defect, verified independently by the orchestrator rather than taken on report.** `tools/dump_surface.py` resolves a class's shape with `inspect.signature` on the **class**, which for a `Protocol` returns `__init__` — universally `(*args, **kwargs)`. It never reads `__call__`. Measured at `4d16cb9`: **56** manifest entries carry the information-free shape `"(*args, **kwargs)"`, of which **30 are named `*Protocol`**, against **54** total Protocol classes across `src/` and `runtime/`. The affected set includes `ArtifactStoreProtocol`, `ControlPlaneProtocol`, `HarnessAdapterProtocol`, `OrchestrationProtocol`, `HITLTransportProtocol`, `LLMClientProtocol` and `DebtFactorScorerProtocol` — that is, most of the pluggable substrate CLAUDE.md §11 mandates Protocols for. **The demonstration.** Plan `15.5-15` took both resolver seams from **three arguments to five** — breaking for every adopter implementing them — and `docs/surface/v0.2-surface.json` is **byte-identical across that change**. Two plans hit the wall independently: `15.5-11` for `ReplayEnvironment` and `15.5-15` for the two resolver Protocols. Both were forced to classify their change as `behavior` rather than `reshaped`, because `reshaped` fails `test_surface_completeness` when the manifest shows no diff to point at. **The ruling.** Recorded as `DI-15.5-06` and carried to **Phase 17**, not fixed in 15.5. The blind spot predates this phase and was not caused by it. **The two consequences of deferring, recorded so the next reader does not have to rediscover them:** (1) Phase 15.5 ships a **breaking Protocol change that its own drift gate cannot see**, so the surface manifest is not evidence of surface stability for this phase and must not be cited as such at the gate; and (2) the `behavior`-instead-of-`reshaped` classification stands as **precedent**, so a later author reading those two fragments will reasonably copy it. Phase 17 should expect to reclassify both entries when the tooling can see them. The orchestrator recommended fixing in-phase via `15.5-14`; Dave ruled otherwise, and the recommendation is recorded here rather than silently dropped. **Governs:** `tools/dump_surface.py`; `docs/surface/v0.2-surface.json`; the `reshaped`/`behavior` classification of the `15.5-11` and `15.5-15` fragments; Phase 17 scope. Originates: plan `15.5-15`'s `DI-15.5-06`, independently verified by the orchestrator at `4d16cb9`.

**D-IMPL-111 — The request seeds the traversal AND lands in the replay record; both halves, because they are not substitutes.** RESOLVED — option (c), both. Date: 2026-08-11. Approver: Dave. **The gap, found at plan `15.5-13`'s checkpoint and verified by the orchestrator rather than taken on report.** `D-IMPL-98` made a Context Product **callable** — subject and instant became request arguments. Nothing carried the question into replay behind it. Two consequences, measured at `d85faf8`: (1) two different *subjects* pin **identically** in `graph_query_sequence`, because the subject enters at the projection and `src/ccf/projection/engine.py:527` calls `compile_segment_query(..., seeds=None)` with a hardcoded literal; two different *instants* do pin differently, because the boundary compiles into the query. (2) `ProductRequest` appears **nowhere in `src/ccf/replay/`** — confirmed by grep over the package — so `intent` and `freshness` are recorded in none of the five dimensions. `graph_query_sequence` therefore answers *"what did this decision ask the graph?"* and not *"which question did this decision answer?"* **Why both, and why they are not alternatives.** Limb **(a)** makes the *graph query* distinguish subjects; it can never record `intent` or `freshness`, which are not graph concepts. Limb **(b)** makes the *record* carry the question; it leaves two subjects pinning identically, with a test asserting that as correct. Each alone leaves half the gap open, so the ruling takes both. **Measured cost of (a):** ONE hardcoded literal. The seeds plumbing already exists end to end — `compile_segment_query` accepts `seeds` and passes it through (`src/ccf/projection/segmentation.py:271`) and `src/ccf/graph/fallback.py:408` already reads `query.seeds`. An earlier framing of this limb as "an operator and query-compilation change" **overstated it** and is corrected here. The real risk is not the edit but its consequence: composition-preservation `(A∘B)(G) = A(B(G))` must still hold with a seeded traversal, gated by four property suites (`tests/properties/test_composition.py`, `test_staged_vs_direct.py`, `test_lineage_union.py`, `test_aggregate_composition_laws.py`). **Measured cost of (b):** **32** `build_replay_record` call sites across **28** files, or **0** forced repairs under a sentinel default — the positive-sentinel pattern this phase has now used five times and which `docs/CONTEXT_PRODUCT_RULING_EVIDENCE.md` § Q1 priced at 0 against 42 vessel sites. The record shape is whitepaper §7.10.1 surface, so this is a fidelity-facing change and not merely an additive field; expect a record content-address move, which must be traced and shown reconstructible exactly as `D-IMPL-100` required of the product address. **What this ruling makes true that was not true before:** plan `15.5-13`'s failure criterion 4 — *"rows 1 and 3 show the same PINS"* — becomes a meaningful test rather than a vacuous one, and criterion 1's promise that a different subject yields a different answer is matched by a different pinned question. **A note on how this was found, worth preserving.** The plan asserted that two different subjects produce two different pinned sequences. They did not. The executor **reported the plan false rather than producing output that looked right**, shipped a test asserting the true property, and added a guard that fails loudly if rows 1 and 2 ever stop pinning alike. That is the behaviour the checkpoint exists to elicit. **Governs:** `src/ccf/projection/engine.py`; `src/ccf/replay/record.py` and the five-dimension shape; the continuation of plan `15.5-13`; requirements `CPS-07` and `CPS-12`. Originates: `DI-15.5-07` in the phase's `deferred-items.md`; `D-IMPL-98`, whose callable-product ruling opened the gap; CLAUDE.md §11 *"Decision Replay is enforced at write time, not retrofitted."*

**D-IMPL-112 — The `s1-01` demonstration is approved, and debt is informative but not yet decision-complete.** RESOLVED — approved with the limitation recorded. Date: 2026-08-11. Approver: Dave. **What was demonstrated, and verified by the orchestrator running it rather than relaying an agent's report.** One product class answered three questions: two companies at one instant, and one company at two instants. A different subject returns a different beneficial-owner list (Jane 30%/John 45% for Acme; Jane 60% alone for Beta). A later instant surfaces Marcus Vale at 30% with **nothing re-ingested** — the transfer was already in the graph on 1 August and became legally effective in September, and the temporal boundary found it. Debt is measured rather than decorative: six weeks of decay moves `D_c` 0.035 → 0.221 and `signal` `ok` → `forced_llm`, driven entirely by freshness (0.995 → 0.803) while completeness and semantic consistency hold. Under `D-IMPL-111` the pins now distinguish both the subject and the instant, and dimension 5 carries the request verbatim. **Dave's verdict, recorded in his own terms because the limitation is the useful part.** A KYC analyst would **not act on `D_c=0.221 / forced_llm / F=0.803` alone**. They would accept it as a correctly computed warning that freshness has decayed into the Forced-LLM band, and would then immediately need three things the product does not carry: (1) **which specific observations contributed the decay**; (2) **when the underlying source filings were last refreshed**; (3) **what operational obligation `forced_llm` actually imposes** — re-pull, accept supervised synthesis, escalate, or something else. His summary: *"The signal is informative and correctly attributed. It is not yet decision-complete."* **Why this is an approval and not a gap.** Phase 15.5 set out to prove that the measurement **exists**, is **attached to both the product and the replay record**, and **correctly names the weakened factor**. All three hold, demonstrably. Decision-completeness — evidence pointers and a prescribed action — is a **different commitment** that this phase did not scope and does not claim. Recording the boundary explicitly is what keeps the phase record honest: a later reader must not infer from "debt is delivered" that an analyst can act on it unaided. **What this makes future work, stated so it can be planned rather than rediscovered:** closing the gap means (a) debt carrying evidence pointers back to the observations whose decay it summarises — which is `D-IMPL-99`'s per-subject and per-claim options, both ruled NOT MEASURABLE because `Observation` carries no entity or claim identity, so the join key must be built first; (b) source-refresh recency as a distinct fact from computed freshness; and (c) a declared obligation vocabulary attached to each debt signal, which is Control Plane configuration and therefore user values, not SDK mechanism (CLAUDE.md §11). Item (a) is the same missing link `D-IMPL-101` discharged when it deferred salience. **Governs:** the phase record's claim about what debt delivery achieves; ROADMAP Phase 15.5 criterion 1; requirements `CPS-01` and `CPS-02`; the scope of any future debt-completeness work. Originates: plan `15.5-13`'s blocking human-verify checkpoint, the demonstration Phase 15.5 was built to make possible, answering Dave's 2026-08-10 question *"What is the benefit of using CCF?"*

**D-IMPL-113 — Bind-time payload conformity is an adopter obligation the SDK documents, not a door the SDK enforces.** RESOLVED — adopter obligation. Date: 2026-08-11. Approver: Dave. **The question.** `refuse_bound_payload` and `refuse_request` (`src/ccf/products/bind.py`, `src/ccf/products/request.py`) ship complete and tested, and **no shipped door calls either** — mechanism without a call site, raised as residual **R1** by the close-time panel and verified against the tree. **The ruling.** The SDK ships the mechanism; the deployment's own resolver calls it. This follows plan `15.5-09`'s own reasoning, which the panel confirmed rather than contradicted: the bind's precondition is that a request has already resolved to a `RegisteredProductClass` **and** the payload for that bind exists, and **the door never holds the registered class** — `ProductResolverProtocol` hands the deployment three strings plus a perspective and a request, and the class is resolved behind that seam. Wiring a call at the door would require the door to acquire the registered class, which is a different seam than the one `D-IMPL-108` widened. **Why not enforce it.** Placing the call is `D-IMPL-102`'s own question, and the natural enforcement point sits adjacent to limb 3's *"gate scripts remain structural only"* line. An SDK-enforced payload check on the serve path is the shape limb 3 declined; enforcing it one layer out would re-litigate that ruling by implementation rather than by decision. The executor declined to place it for exactly this reason and escalated instead, which was correct. **The obligation this creates, and it is the SDK's to discharge.** *Documented* is not the same as *mentioned*: the adopter has to be told, at the seam, that a resolver which does not call `refuse_bound_payload` serves an unvalidated payload against a schema its class declared. **`CPS-03` therefore stays Pending by design, not by omission** — and the requirement row must say which, or a later reader will read an unmet requirement as an oversight. **What this does not license.** It is the second instance of convention-not-enforcement in this phase (`DI-15.5-05` records the first, the binder refusals with no calling path) and the class already travels to Phase 16. Two instances is a pattern worth watching: a Protocol the SDK ships and never calls is indistinguishable, from the tree, from one an adopter forgot. **Governs:** `src/ccf/products/bind.py`; `src/ccf/products/request.py`; requirement `CPS-03`'s disposition. Originates: close-time panel residual R1, verified; plan `15.5-09`'s recorded join point; `D-IMPL-102` limb 3.

**D-IMPL-114 — Prose cites `path::Symbol`, not a line range.** RESOLVED. Date: 2026-08-11. Approver: Dave. **The defect, second occurrence.** Three citations of `src/ccf/replay/engine.py:449-455` — in `docs/DECISIONS.md`, `docs/SURFACE-CHANGES.md` and plan `15.5-11`'s landed fragment — drifted by 11 lines when `D-IMPL-111`'s edit moved the code, raised as residual **R3**. They were deliberately not rewritten in place: an append-only ruling record, a whole-tree-assembled artifact, and a landed plan's fragment are all things a later editor should not silently revise. `DI-15.4-02` already recorded the same class at larger scale — **45 drifted anchors**. **The ruling.** A line-anchored citation is wrong the moment code moves, and code moves every phase; the anchor therefore encodes a fact with a shorter half-life than the claim it supports. **Prose cites `path::Symbol`** — a module path and a symbol name, which survives an edit that moves the symbol and *correctly* breaks when the symbol is renamed or deleted, which is the case a reader actually needs flagged. **Consequences, stated so the work is plannable rather than assumed.** (1) `cite02-anchor` must accept and prefer the symbol form. (2) Existing line-anchored citations need a sweep; this phase's three and `DI-15.4-02`'s 45 are the known population, and neither was measured tree-wide. (3) The sweep is **not** Phase 15.5 work — the gate is ruled approved with the three stale citations extant, and they are recorded as such. **The rejected alternative and why.** Widening `cite02-anchor` to accept bare ranges was considered: it is cheaper, and it is what a checker-shaped fix looks like. It was not taken because it makes the checker stop catching citations that are *genuinely* wrong in order to tolerate ones that merely drifted — trading a real signal for a maintenance saving. **Governs:** `tools/lints` `cite02-anchor`; the citation convention in `docs/`, `.planning/` prose and source docstrings; a future anchor sweep. Originates: close-time panel residual R3, verified; `DI-15.4-02`.

**D-IMPL-115 — `D-IMPL-31`'s expiry formula gains a third term; the strengthening is adopted as an amendment.** RESOLVED — accept as a strengthening. Date: 2026-08-11. Approver: Dave. **What plan `15.5-12` shipped, and flagged rather than slipped.** `D-IMPL-31` ruled `expires_at = min(class MAX_TTL ceiling, derivation over constituent signal validities)`. The executor implemented `compose_expiry` with a **third term, `min(validities)`**, so that *"no aggregate is fresher-labelled than its stalest load-bearing constituent"* is a property **of the function** rather than a property of whichever derivation a class happened to configure. Under the ruled reference default (`derivation = min`) the third term is an **identity** and changes nothing; for any derivation returning later than `min(validities)` it clamps where the two-term formula would not. The executor reported this as a deviation from a ruled formula rather than presenting it as the formula. **The ruling.** Adopted, and recorded **as an amendment to `D-IMPL-31`** rather than as an executor deviation tolerated after the fact — the formula now has three terms and `D-IMPL-31` should be read through this entry. The reasoning: `D-IMPL-31` is a mixing-**refusal** rule, so erring conservative is its intent rather than a departure from it, and a safety property that holds structurally beats one that holds only for well-behaved configuration. A deployment cannot now configure its way past the floor. **The alternative considered and not taken:** constraining the derivation at declaration time instead — forbidding a derivation that returns later than `min(validities)` — reaches the same guarantee but enforces it where a class is declared rather than where an aggregate is composed, leaving the composition function correct only by precondition. The third term needs no precondition. **Governs:** `src/ccf/products/composition.py::compose_expiry`; amends `D-IMPL-31`. Originates: plan `15.5-12`, flagged by its executor; carried to the gate by the orchestrator.

**D-GATE-15.5 — Phase 15.5 approved.** Date: 2026-08-11. Approver: Dave. **15 of 15 plans complete** (14 planned, plus `15.5-15` created mid-phase by `D-IMPL-108`). `make ci` **exit 0** at the gate HEAD, verified by the orchestrator: **2858 passed / 81 skipped**, all eleven examples green including the new `example-s1-01`, `docs-strict` clean with **0** publish-safety violations, `make perf-micro` 12/12 PASS with `perf/baselines/local.json` **never modified** across the whole phase. **Eighteen rulings** — `D-IMPL-98` through `D-IMPL-115` — each recorded with the measured cost it was ruled against. **The close-time protocol ran in the CLAUDE.md §3.1 order**: execute → cross-AI panel against shipped code → single-agent review → verify every finding → fix blocking findings in-phase → present. **Two blocking findings were found and fixed inside the phase**: a cross-tenant authorization hole at the entitlement door, where a policy artifact declaring one tenant and registered under another's key would have authorized (`7453e87`); and an `AGNT-01` capability check blind to `ast.Attribute` and `getattr`, found *independently by two reviewers* (`c6693f4`). **Five findings were rejected as fabricated**, each verified against the tree before rejection — `DebtAssessment.timestamp` (the type has four fields, none a datetime), `VesselAssembler`, `validate_vessel_conformity`, `tests/invariants/test_import_invariants.py`. This is the **second** fabricated-citation output on this project and the rejection is recorded with its evidence. **Accepted as known-stale or known-blind, explicitly rather than by silence:** the regenerated surface manifest is **not** evidence of surface stability for this phase (`D-IMPL-110`) — neither resolver Protocol appears among the 21 reshaped symbols despite going from three arguments to five, the phase's largest adopter break; `docs/schemas/` and `openapi.json` sit outside the D-20 scanner and were swept **manually** (27 files, 638 description strings, 0 shingles), which is clean but is not a gate; and `CPS-03` remains Pending **by design** under `D-IMPL-113`. **Carried to Phase 17:** `DI-15.5-01` and `DI-15.5-04` (a perf pass), `DI-15.5-05` (convention-not-enforcement, now with a second instance), `DI-15.5-06` (the Protocol drift blind spot, with its measured 56/30/54 inventory), `DI-15.5-08` and `D-IMPL-114`'s citation sweep, and the debt-completeness work `D-IMPL-112` scoped. **What the phase answered.** Dave's question of 2026-08-10 — *"today I would put an MCP server in front of the fluree database and run a query and return the results. What is the benefit of using CCF?"* — is answered by `examples/s1-01-hello-context-product`, demonstrated at the `15.5-13` checkpoint and approved under `D-IMPL-112` with its limitation recorded: one product class answers for two companies at two instants, a later instant surfaces an ownership change with **nothing re-ingested**, debt is measured and names *freshness* as what weakened, and the replay record pins both the graph question and the product question. Debt is *informative and correctly attributed*, and **not yet decision-complete** — that boundary is on the record, not papered over.

**D-IMPL-116 — Phase 15.6 runs before Phase 16 resumes, as two explicit tracks.** RESOLVED. Date: 2026-08-11. Approver: Dave. **The question Dave asked, which is the right one:** *"Will fixing the known defects affect the programming interface?"* **Measured answer: the two halves of the outstanding work have opposite API profiles, and that is what decides the sequencing.** **Track A — the five carried defects — is API-neutral.** `DI-15.5-01` (a perf flake) touches nothing. `DI-15.5-04` (`IntermediateCache`'s unreachable-cache key) is internal to `.run()` and behaviour-identical. `DI-15.5-05` (binder refusals with no calling path) needs a ruling, not a signature. `DI-15.5-06` is the **opposite** of a surface change — teaching `tools/dump_surface.py` to read a Protocol's `__call__` makes the *already-shipped* 3-arg → 5-arg resolver break newly visible; it is an audit fix. `DI-15.5-08`/`D-IMPL-114` is a citation sweep. Every one could be carried to Phase 17 without touching an adopter. **Track B — `D-IMPL-112`'s three named gaps — carries every API change there is.** `Observation` is **public surface** (present in `docs/surface/v0.2-surface.json`, referenced across 10 files) and gap 1 requires it to gain an identity field; `DebtAssessment` then gains evidence pointers; and because `debt: DebtAssessment | None` is a **field on `ContextProductContract`** (`src/ccf/products/contract.py::ContextProductContract`), the **product content address moves a third time**. Gap 2 (source-refresh recency, distinct from computed freshness) is new surface. Gap 3 (an obligation vocabulary per debt signal) is a new Control Plane artifact class. **Why 15.6 precedes Phase 16, and the reason is Track B rather than Track A.** The defects are cheap to carry; the API changes are expensive to carry. Two measurements decide it: (1) **Phase 16 has already been invalidated once by exactly this** — 6 of 21 plans landed before 15.5 moved the surface underneath, and **4** of the remaining 15 name the product or door surface directly; (2) **exactly one example artifact currently holds content addresses**, and Phase 16's entire output is examples that encode expected outputs. Moving the address now regenerates **one**; moving it after Phase 16 regenerates ten. And substantively: *"would someone act on this?"* is the question examples exist to answer, so an example built on debt an analyst cannot act on teaches the wrong lesson while Phase 16's adoption argument rests on it. **Two tracks, planned separately, because they differ in risk.** Track A is bounded and mostly mechanical. Track B contains one genuine build. **Scope Track B to the three named gaps, NOT to the open question** — *"answer would someone act on this"* is unbounded and would swallow the phase; `D-IMPL-112` already enumerates exactly three things an analyst asked for. **Gap 1 gets a research spike first (Dave, 2026-08-11), in the `15.5-01` shape.** That spike's precedent is the argument: it cost one plan and *deleted an option from the decision space for free* by measuring Q3's product-only placement dead rather than debating it. The 15.6 spike measures what an `Observation` identity costs across its 10 files and the manifest, what it does to the content address, and — the question most likely to change the design — **whether evidence pointers can ride the replay record rather than the product**, which would avoid the third address move entirely. `D-IMPL-99` ruled per-subject and per-claim NOT MEASURABLE precisely because the join key does not exist; the spike is how that stops being true. **Governs:** Phase 15.6's scope and shape; Phase 16's resumption, which waits on it. Originates: Dave's question of 2026-08-11 after `D-GATE-15.5`; `D-IMPL-112`'s three named gaps; the measured API-impact split above.

**D-IMPL-117 — Track B ships carrying mechanism, not judgement; surface impact is accepted.** RESOLVED. Date: 2026-08-11. Approver: Dave. **Dave's scoping, which materially narrows `D-IMPL-116`'s Track B:** *"I'm ok with impacts to the surface in order to increase the value of the SDK at this point. Regarding judgement, the context product creator will have an idea of what is needed by the client and can provide that as part of the evolutionary capability of the context product. What matters now is having the data structure and api in place to populate and extract this content."* **What this settles.** (1) **Surface impact is accepted.** The `Observation` identity field, the `DebtAssessment` evidence pointers and the third product-content-address move are authorized costs, not risks to be designed around. `D-IMPL-116`'s framing of the spike as a way to *avoid* the address move is superseded: the spike's purpose is to get the **shape** right, not to dodge the cost. (2) **The three gaps are carrying mechanisms, not judgement calls.** `D-IMPL-112` recorded three things a KYC analyst would ask for; it is now explicit that the SDK owes the **structure and the API to populate and extract** each, and owes **none of the content**. Concretely — the SDK ships an `Observation` identity and a way for debt to point at what it summarises, and owes nothing about *which* observations matter; a slot for source-refresh recency distinct from computed freshness, and owes no definition of a source or a refresh cadence; and a signal-to-obligation binding that survives replay, and owes **no obligation vocabulary** (that is user configuration — CLAUDE.md §11, and the same posture `D-IMPL-101` took on salience). (3) **The judgement belongs to the context product creator**, who knows the client, and is expressed through the product's *evolutionary capability* rather than through SDK code. **Why this makes Track B more clearly SDK work rather than less.** *"Answer whether someone would act on this"* is unbounded and would swallow a phase. *"Ship three carrying structures a creator populates"* is bounded, testable, and is mechanism-not-values by construction. **The design fork this raises, and it is the spike's primary question now.** `D-IMPL-97` ruled the vessel carries cross-cutting governance as **fixed structure** and domain content as a **declared schema**. Debt evidence sits across that line: the *pointer mechanism* is governance and belongs in fixed structure, but *what evidence is worth carrying* is precisely the creator judgement this entry assigns away from the SDK. Fixed in the wrong direction, every deployment carries a shape someone else chose; declared in the wrong direction, debt evidence becomes unvalidated free-form and replay cannot reason over it. The spike measures which, against the vessel model and against the replay-reconstruction constraint `D-IMPL-100` established — every field added must be reconstructible from the record, the class, or the projection output, or `src/ccf/replay/engine.py::ReplayEnvironment` raises. **What does NOT change.** Track A stays API-neutral and bounded. Track B stays scoped to `D-IMPL-112`'s three named gaps, not to the open question. Gap 1 still gets the research spike first, in the `15.5-01` shape. **Governs:** Phase 15.6 Track B's scope and the spike's charter; supersedes `D-IMPL-116`'s address-avoidance framing of the spike. Originates: Dave, 2026-08-11, after `D-IMPL-116`.

**D-IMPL-118 — The three Track B carrying structures are new top-level fixed fields on `ContextProductContract`, and the record-side reconstruction they cost is an authorized expense, not a reason to place them elsewhere.** RESOLVED — `qa-toplevel`. Date: 2026-08-12. Approver: Dave. **Dave's selection, verbatim:** *"Q-A (D-IMPL-118) : qa-toplevel."* One clean selection of one presented option id; no hybrid was given and no limb was split. **What the ruling reaches.** `D-IMPL-117` made the fixed-versus-declared fork the spike's primary question, and `docs/DEBT_COMPLETENESS_RULING_EVIDENCE.md` § Q-A prices the four placements as properties of the placement **shape** applied to a member of `ContextProductContract` — *"These four rows are properties of the placement _shape_ applied to a member of `ContextProductContract`, not of any one gap's field. They therefore price all three Track B vessel-carried members identically."* This ruling therefore governs all three: plan `15.6-06`'s debt-evidence carrier, plan `15.6-07`'s source-refresh recency slot, and plan `15.6-08`'s assessment-side obligation member, which rides `ContextProductContract.debt` exactly as the other two do. **Not** in scope: `Observation`'s identity, which is an input to debt computation rather than a member of the vessel; its fixed-field placement was already authorized by `D-IMPL-117` and only its required-versus-optional question was open — that is `D-IMPL-120`. **The measured figure this was ruled against**, quoted from § Q-A row 2 rather than paraphrased: the content address **moves**, `f4cd0517…` → `e358be39…`, and the value is **NOT** reconstructible from `(record, projection)` — *"Rebuild hashes to `84799e67…`, which is neither the delivered address nor the baseline"*, and *"The token is **absent** from the canonical bytes of both inputs. `ProjectionOutput` carries exactly `(plan_id, payload)` and dimension 5 carries no member naming the value, so no implementation of the `(record, projection)` signature can produce it. That is the `ReplayDivergence` raise path."* **This is the not-replay-reconstructible branch, and the ruling therefore carries an obligation rather than a preference.** Every one of the three structures obliges **a matching member on `ContentGenerationPath`** *plus* **an edit to all 7 shipped `rehydrate_product` implementations across 6 files** — the public-Protocol population `D-IMPL-100` enumerated (`src/ccf/replay/engine.py`, `tests/replay/test_engine_rehydration.py`, `tests/replay/test_query_sequence_pin.py`, `perf/suites/replay/conftest.py`, `examples/06-decision-replay/run.py`, `examples/external/00-hello-context/run.py`). Without **both**, every replay of every product raises `ReplayDivergence`, because `canonical_bytes` walks a model field-by-field over `type(value).model_fields` and the new field is therefore inside the address. That obligation is inherited by plans `15.6-06`, `15.6-07` and `15.6-08` and is recorded here as an **authorized cost under `D-IMPL-117`, not a defect**: *"surface impact is accepted … the third product-content-address move [is an] authorized cost, not [a] risk to be designed around."* An executor of any of the three plans that ships the vessel field without the record-side member and the seven rehydrator edits has not implemented this ruling. **The alternative considered and not taken, with its measured cost.** `qa-nested` — the carrier as an optional member of the `DebtAssessment` that already rides `ContentGenerationPath.debt` — was the **cheapest** measured option and was declined: address moves `f4cd0517…` → `7c32baeb…`, reconstructible **YES** with *"Rebuilt bytes are byte-identical to the delivered bytes"* and **0** rehydrator implementations changing, because *"the shipped `debt=record.content_generation_path.debt` carries a reshaped assessment verbatim."* The ruling forgoes that free ride deliberately, buying the field its own identity on the vessel rather than making it a rider on debt. Also declined: `qa-declared` (address moves → `fecd33a2…`, round-trips because *"`payload` comes back verbatim on `ProjectionOutput.payload`"*, but *"replay never compares the evidence against the declared schema"* and `refuse_bound_payload` is called by no shipped door under `D-IMPL-113`, so a declared schema would constrain nothing on the serve path today); and `qa-record` (address does not move, and reaches **0** wire consumers across all three shipped doors — `egress.py::build_egress_server`, `routes/product.py::build_router`, `experience.py::build_experience_server` all serialize `servable.product` and **none** reads `ServedProduct.replay_ref` — deleted by measurement in § Q-B, and independently emptied of purpose by `D-IMPL-117`'s acceptance of the address move). **Governs:** plans `15.6-06`, `15.6-07` and `15.6-08` — the three structures carried on the product vessel — and requirements `DCD-02`, `DCD-03`, `DCD-04`, `DCD-05`; `src/ccf/products/contract.py::ContextProductContract`, `src/ccf/replay/record.py::ContentGenerationPath`, and every implementation of `ReplayEnvironment.rehydrate_product`. **Implementing plans and tasks:** plan `15.6-06` **task 1** (the debt-evidence carrier), plan `15.6-07` **task 1** (the source-refresh recency slot), plan `15.6-08` **task 2** (the assessment-side obligation member); each is written to implement the same branch unless this entry distinguishes them, and it does not. Originates: plan `15.6-01` (the measurement); `D-IMPL-117` (which assigned the fork to Dave and accepted the surface impact); `D-IMPL-97` (where the fixed/declared line is drawn); `D-IMPL-100` (the reconstructibility constraint and the seven-implementation count).

**D-IMPL-119 — Evidence pointers are entitlement-sensitive, and until the assembly path is principal-scoped an evidence pointer is an opaque handle or it is not emitted at all.** RESOLVED — `qb2-scoped`, clarified in four limbs. Date: 2026-08-12. Approver: Dave. **This is the first ruling on evidence pointers.** `D-IMPL-105` ruled the analogous question for **completeness** — debt is pure over `(factors, profile)` and not per principal — and ruled it precisely because of an inference channel: *"a caller can therefore infer what they are not allowed to see from the debt they are shown — an oracle over the entitlement configuration, quieter than a refusal because nothing in the response says a consultation was declined."* That argument transfers to evidence pointers and **no shipped ruling covered it**; plan `15.6-01` task 2 measured it, offered no mitigation and reached no verdict, exactly as `D-IMPL-117` required. **Dave's selection, verbatim:** *"Q-B2 (D-IMPL-119) : qb2-scoped."* **The measured finding the ruling was then clarified against**, quoted from `docs/DEBT_COMPLETENESS_RULING_EVIDENCE.md` § Q-B2 measurement 3: *"**On the shipped path the two are the same set, so the delta is empty — and that is the finding.** There is no perspective-scoped view to derive a narrower pointer from."* Three hops establish it, read off shipped parameter lists rather than described: `src/ccf/deployment/operators.py::build_operator_registry` takes `(parameters, view, pins, policy_bundle_key, similarity_index_version)` — **no perspective, no principal**; `src/ccf/operators/categories.py::PolicyFilter.__init__` takes `(self, parameters)` — **no principal**; `examples/s1-01-hello-context-product/spine.py::assemble_and_serve` reads the graph as `reader.as_of(tenant_id=…, at=…)` — **tenant and instant only**. Measurement 2 completes it: two differently-entitled principals **enumerate the same `entity_uri` set today**, because paths converge at `src/ccf/projection/engine.py::ProjectionEngine` and diverge only at `src/ccf/projection/plan.py::ProjectionPlan`; entitlement is decided **before** assembly at `runtime/ccf_runtime/authz/product_entitlement.py::entitled_perspective`, on the `(tenant, product_class, role)` triple, which is a per-class decision and not a per-entity one. **Dave accepted that finding and clarified the ruling rather than reversing it. Four limbs, in Dave's terms:** **(1)** Evidence pointers **are entitlement-sensitive**; emitting an unfiltered pointer set **is a leak**. This is the normative claim and it stands — it is `qb2-scoped`'s safety property, and it is the SDK's, not the adopter's. **(2)** **This phase does not introduce a perspective-scoped view into the assembly path.** That work is explicitly out of scope for Phase 15.6. **(3)** Consequently plan `15.6-06` must take **one of exactly two paths**: **(a)** ship **opaque / non-revealing handles** that carry **no** entity or claim identity the principal can interpret; or **(b)** **omit evidence pointers entirely** until a later phase adds principal scoping to the assembly path. The choice between (a) and (b) falls to plan `15.6-06`. **(4)** **Raw, interpretable pointers are FORBIDDEN until the scoping gap is closed.** This is a **standing prohibition, not advice**: a pointer naming any of `EntityClaim`'s eight fields (`tenant_id`, `observed_at`, `valid_from`, `valid_to`, `lineage`, `entity_uri`, `entity_class`, `attributes`) in a form the reading principal can interpret is refused by this entry, whatever the deployment's judgement, until the precondition below is met. **The named precondition, recorded as a precondition rather than filed as an open defect.** The absence of a perspective-scoped view in the assembly path is a **precondition for any future "real" evidence-pointer feature** — the three hops above and the same-`entity_uri`-set finding are its evidence. A later phase that wants interpretable pointers closes the scoping gap first; nothing in this phase is blocked on it, and nothing in this phase may work around it. **The recorded interaction with `D-IMPL-118`.** `D-IMPL-118` places the carrier as a top-level field on `ContextProductContract`, which puts it **inside the content address**. Principal-dependent pointer *contents* inside a content address would give two differently-entitled principals **different content addresses for the same assembly** — precisely the coupling `D-IMPL-105` dissolved when it kept debt pure over `(factors, profile)`. **Limbs 3 and 4 are what keep that from arising in this phase**, since neither an opaque handle nor an omitted pointer varies by principal. Recorded as an interaction, not as a new question. **What this entry does not license**, in the voice `D-IMPL-113` used for its own pattern warning: it does not license a deployment reading limb 3(a) as permission to mint a handle that is merely *encoded* rather than genuinely non-revealing — an obfuscated `entity_uri` is an interpretable pointer with an extra step, and limb 4 refuses it;
