it does not license treating the disclosure decision as the adopter's, which is what `qb2-opaque` would have done and this ruling does not; and it does not license a later plan inferring, from limb 2, that scoped derivation is unwanted — limb 1 says the opposite. **The alternatives considered and not taken, with their measured costs.** `qb2-pure` — evidence stays pure over the assembly as debt does, with entitlement enforced elsewhere — keeps one value for one assembly and is consistent with `D-IMPL-105`'s purity, and was declined because *"a principal could infer the existence of withheld sources from pointers they can read"*, the precise inference `D-IMPL-105` cited as its own reason; on the shipped path, where the enumerated set is identical for every principal of a class, that inference is not hypothetical. `qb2-opaque` — the pointer is opaque and the deployment owns whether it discloses anything — reaches limb 3(a)'s *shape* but was declined as a *ruling*, because it *"moves a real disclosure question onto every adopter, and the SDK cannot then say the surface is safe by construction"*; under this entry the opaque handle is the SDK's obligation, not the adopter's discretion. **Governs:** plan `15.6-06` **task 2**, which is where the derivation it constrains actually happens; requirements `DCD-02` and `DCD-05`; `examples/s1-01-hello-context-product/deployment.py::KycScenario` as the population site. **Implementing plan and task: plan `15.6-06` task 2** — the population of the evidence carrier at the `s1-01` assembly, the only place in the phase where the constrained derivation is written. That plan's task-2 acceptance criterion *"if `D-IMPL-119` constrained derivation to the perspective-scoped assembly, the constraint is implemented at the population site rather than assumed, and the summary names the symbol the scoped set is read from"* is satisfied under this entry by limb 3: there is no such symbol, so the task names the limb it took — (a) or (b) — and shows the handle carries nothing interpretable, or that no pointer is emitted. Originates: plan `15.6-01` task 2's three measurements; `D-IMPL-117`; `D-IMPL-105` (the analogous ruling for completeness).

**D-IMPL-120 — `Observation`'s subject identity is optional, carried behind a named positive sentinel rather than required.** RESOLVED — `qc-optional`. Date: 2026-08-12. Approver: Dave. **Dave's selection, verbatim:** *"Q-C (D-IMPL-120) : qc-optional."* **Scope.** This entry decides **required-versus-optional only**. The field's placement on `src/ccf/halflife/decay.py::Observation` is not in question — `D-IMPL-117` already authorized it — and `Observation` is not a member of `ContextProductContract`, so it sits outside `D-IMPL-118`'s Q-A fork entirely. **The measured figure this was ruled against**, quoted from `docs/DEBT_COMPLETENESS_RULING_EVIDENCE.md` § Q-C, which was **executed rather than inferred** — each of the three named artifacts' *actual* observation mapping was `model_validate`d against a required stand-in and against an optional one, and what each raised was recorded: the optional shape costs *"**3 code files** (the 10 construction sites, which populate it) and **0 artifacts** — every artifact validates and the sentinel is carried."* **The alternative considered and not taken, with its measured cost.** `qc-required` costs *"**6 code files** (10 `Observation(…)` call sites across 3 files — 9 in `tests/`, 1 in `examples/` — plus 3 `Observation.model_validate` sites in 3 example `run.py` files) **plus all 3 YAML artifacts**, each of which **raises**"*: `examples/00-quickstart/artifacts/decision.yaml` (1 mapping) — `ValidationError`, 1 error, type `missing`, loc `subject_uri`; `examples/01-agent-basics/artifacts/debt-evaluation.yaml` (2 mappings) — `ValidationError` on **both**, type `missing`, loc `subject_uri`; `examples/08-e2e-demo/artifacts/decision.yaml` (1 mapping) — `ValidationError`, 1 error, type `missing`, loc `subject_uri`. This discharges `15.6-RESEARCH.md` assumption A7 by execution. **The `loc` in those three quoted `ValidationError`s is `subject_uri`, which is the name the spike's stand-in field carried; the field this ruling governs ships as `subject` on `src/ccf/halflife/decay.py::Observation`** — the quotes are reproduced verbatim because they are what the measurement raised, so the difference is the stand-in's name and not a rename of shipped surface. (The Wave-2 presentation's option cells quoted the research-era *"10 files against 13"* split; the measured split above is the figure the ruling was made against and supersedes it.) **The governance cost this ruling accepts, stated rather than elided.** `D-IMPL-97`'s decisive argument for fixed structure — *"every subclass is a place to forget"* — cuts toward **required**, and the optional shape accepts its consequence: **an assembler that could populate the identity and does not is indistinguishable, from the type, from one that has none.** What makes the cost bearable is `D-IMPL-38`'s no-silent-defaults discipline together with the shipped positive-sentinel precedent (`NO_SCORER_PINS`, `NO_GRAPH_QUERIES`, `NO_PAYLOAD_SCHEMA`): the absent value is **a named positive assertion, not `None`-as-unknown**, so an omitted identity and a dropped one are not the same artifact. An implementation that expresses the absence as `None` has not implemented this ruling. **Governs:** plan `15.6-05` and requirement `DCD-02`; `src/ccf/halflife/decay.py::Observation`; the three example artifacts named above, which under this ruling **do not change**. **Implementing plan and task: plan `15.6-05` task 1** — *"`Observation` gains the identity, in the shape Dave ruled"* — with its task 2 populating the identity at `examples/s1-01-hello-context-product/deployment.py::KycScenario`, where `docs/DEBT_COMPLETENESS_RULING_EVIDENCE.md` § Q-D measured **7** fields in hand and discarded. Originates: plan `15.6-01` task 2 (the executed `model_validate` measurements); `D-IMPL-117`; `D-IMPL-97`; `D-IMPL-38`.

**D-IMPL-121 — `D-IMPL-113` extends to `refuse_request`: both request-time refusals are adopter obligations the SDK states at the seam, not doors the SDK enforces.** RESOLVED — `q1-extend`. Date: 2026-08-12. Approver: Dave. **Dave's selection, verbatim:** *"Q-1 : q1-extend."* **The question.** `D-IMPL-113` ruled the *bind* refusal an adopter obligation on reasoning that is structural rather than case-specific — *"the door never holds the registered class — `ProductResolverProtocol` hands the deployment three strings plus a perspective and a request, and the class is resolved behind that seam"* — and `DI-15.5-05` left open whether that reasoning transfers to `src/ccf/products/request.py::refuse_request` or whether the SDK wires it. It transfers, and the SDK does not wire it. **The re-measured population this was ruled against, taken at `4b407e0` rather than quoted.** `git grep` across `src runtime tests examples tools perf`, hand-classified into invocation versus mention: `src/ccf/products/bind.py::refuse_bound_payload` has **one** invocation outside its own tests — `examples/04-cross-process/producer_child.py::producer_child` — and **zero** in `runtime/`; `src/ccf/products/request.py::refuse_request` has **one** — `examples/s1-01-hello-context-product/spine.py::assemble_and_serve` — and **zero** in `runtime/`. The correct statement is narrower than either prior record: **no `runtime/` door calls either function; one shipped example calls each.** **Correction to the record, and it is a process finding rather than drift.** The `DI-15.5-05` entry in `.planning/phases/15.5-the-context-product-surface-vessel-assembler-debt-entitlem/deferred-items.md` states `refuse_request` has **zero** callers. Git says otherwise, and says it about the same day: the call site landed in commit `e4db82c` at **2026-08-11 11:46:21 -0700**, and the entry claiming zero landed in commit `9fd6c12` at **2026-08-11 20:54:59 -0700** — a gap of **9 h 08 m 38 s, inside the same phase**. The entry was **wrong when written, not stale by drift**. The distinction is preserved deliberately: drift is normal and is what `D-IMPL-114` addresses, whereas a figure that was false at the moment of authorship is a process defect, and a phase built on it re-commits the error. **A second correction, in the same entry, of `DI-15.5-08`'s own defect class.** Two of that entry's line anchors have rotted: it cites the payload refusal at `bind.py:145` where `src/ccf/products/bind.py::refuse_bound_payload` now begins at line 164, and the wired registration-time sibling at `contract.py:662-664` where the call in `src/ccf/products/contract.py::register_product_class` now sits at lines 753 to 755. Two anchors in the same entry are still accurate. Recorded because `DI-15.5-05`'s own text demonstrating `DI-15.5-08` is the tightest available evidence for `D-IMPL-114`'s premise. **Why not wire it, restated at this commit rather than inherited.** Both seams were re-read: `runtime/ccf_runtime/mcp/egress.py::ProductResolverProtocol` and `runtime/ccf_runtime/mcp/experience.py::ExperienceProductResolverProtocol` each take five keyword-only arguments — `tenant`, `product_class`, `version`, `perspective`, `request` — and **neither holds the `RegisteredProductClass`**. `D-IMPL-113`'s structural premise is therefore verified against the shipped signatures, not taken on report. Wiring would require the door to acquire the registered class, which is a different seam than the one `D-IMPL-108` widened, and would put an SDK-owned verdict on the serve path that `D-IMPL-102` limb 3 declined — re-litigating that ruling by implementation rather than by decision. **The obligation this discharges, and it was measurably undischarged.** `D-IMPL-113` created it — *"Documented is not the same as mentioned: the adopter has to be told, at the seam, that a resolver which does not call `refuse_bound_payload` serves an unvalidated payload against a schema its class declared"* — and never discharged it: at `4b407e0`, `git grep` for either refusal name across all of `runtime/` returned **zero hits of any kind**, not an import, not a call, not a mention. Under this ruling both resolver Protocol docstrings state what a resolver omitting each refusal serves, why the SDK cannot call it for them, and where a working example call site is, cited as `path::Symbol`; `tools/docs_tests/test_resolver_seam_obligations.py` asserts the statement remains, because a docstring nobody checks is a docstring that rots and `DI-15.5-08` is this phase's own evidence of it. **A false docstring, corrected.** `src/ccf/products/request.py::refuse_request` opened *"Called by the door, once the seam that can carry a typed request exists."* The seam now exists — plan `15.5-15` landed it under `D-IMPL-108` — and no door calls the function, so the sentence asserted something false at this commit. It now says what is true: the deployment's own resolver calls it, at the join point the seam docstrings name. **The alternative considered and not taken.** `q1-wire` — wire `refuse_request` into the shipped doors — was declined. Its argument is real and is recorded rather than dismissed: *mechanism the SDK ships is mechanism the SDK calls*, and no adopter can forget a call the SDK makes. It was not taken because the door does not hold what the refusal needs, because reaching it widens a seam `D-IMPL-108` settled one phase ago, and because it would re-open `D-IMPL-102` limb 3 by implementation. **What this does not license, in `D-IMPL-113`'s own voice.** This is the **third** instance of convention-not-enforcement, and `D-IMPL-113` flagged two as *"a pattern worth watching: a Protocol the SDK ships and never calls is indistinguishable, from the tree, from one an adopter forgot."* Three is not a smaller pattern. What changes with this ruling is that the tree can now tell the two apart — the seam says the join point exists and names a working example — but the watch stands, and a fourth instance should be read as a signal about the seam rather than as another documentation task. **`CPS-03` stays Pending by design, and the requirement row says so, naming both this entry and `D-IMPL-113`** — `D-IMPL-113`'s own instruction, *"the requirement row must say which, or a later reader will read an unmet requirement as an oversight."* **Governs:** `src/ccf/products/request.py::refuse_request`; `src/ccf/products/bind.py::refuse_bound_payload`; `runtime/ccf_runtime/mcp/egress.py::ProductResolverProtocol` and `runtime/ccf_runtime/mcp/experience.py::ExperienceProductResolverProtocol` docstrings; `tools/docs_tests/test_resolver_seam_obligations.py`; requirements `CPS-03` and `DCD-08`. Extends `D-IMPL-113` rather than amending it. **Implementing plan and tasks: plan `15.6-09` tasks 2 and 3.** Originates: `DI-15.5-05`; `D-IMPL-113`; `D-IMPL-102` limb 3; plan `15.6-09`'s re-measurement at `4b407e0`.

**D-IMPL-122 — `DI-15.5-06` is fixed by reconciling the diff against a baseline that stays frozen, with `__call__` capture and the type aliases in scope and the 39 multi-method Protocols descoped on a measured cost.** RESOLVED — `q2a-reconcile`, `__call__`-only fix scope, `q2b-in`. Date: 2026-08-12. Approver: Dave. **Dave's selections, verbatim:** *"Q-2a : reconcile (frozen baseline stays frozen; the diff is reconciled)"*; *"Q-2 fix scope : `__call__`-ONLY (12 of 51 Protocols, 11 new reshaped diff lines)"*; *"Q-2b : q2b-in (the type aliases ARE in scope — 18 distinct aliases / 20 entries)."* Three limbs of one question, ruled together. **Limb 1 — the frozen baseline stays frozen and the diff is reconciled.** `docs/surface/v0.2-surface-baseline.json` is not regenerated. It is pinned by **two** assertions, both named here because naming them is what makes this a ruling rather than a repair: `tests/test_surface_completeness.py::test_the_frozen_baseline_is_byte_identical_to_what_15_01_committed`, which pins `sha256` against the constant `_BASELINE_DIGEST` (`cef0d469db249947f8392331a50bc94bf92d46a4266e4945e2b59944dac043cc`), and `tests/test_surface_completeness.py::test_the_frozen_baseline_was_written_only_by_plan_15_01`, which pins the file's provenance against its git history. An executor cannot regenerate the baseline without failing both, which is exactly what they exist for. **A measured caveat on the second, recorded because it changes how much weight it carries:** its own docstring states it is *"corroboration for the digest, not an independently unfakeable provenance proof"* — it keys on commit-subject text rather than a pinned hash, and it returns without asserting where git is absent or the history is shallow. The digest is the load-bearing pin. **Limb 2 — the fix captures `__call__` and nothing wider.** `tools/dump_surface.py` resolves a class's shape with `inspect.signature` on the class, which for a `Protocol` returns `__init__` and therefore `"(*args, **kwargs)"`. It will be taught to read `__call__`. It will **not** be taught to render a multi-method Protocol's method set. **Limb 3 — the information-free type aliases are in scope.** All **18 distinct aliases / 20 manifest entries** move with the same commit. **The re-measured figures this was ruled against, taken at `4b407e0` and reproducing the manifest rather than quoting a prior count.** Of **80** manifest entries recording `"(*args, **kwargs)"`: **60 are class-kind** (51 distinct classes) and **20 are alias-kind** (18 distinct aliases). Every one of the 51 distinct classes was imported and confirmed `_is_protocol` — there are no false positives in that set. Of those 51, **exactly 12 define `__call__`**: `ccf_runtime.mcp.egress.ProductResolverProtocol`, `ccf_runtime.mcp.experience.ExperienceProductResolverProtocol`, `.AgentDirectoryProtocol`, `.AgentInvokerProtocol`, `.ContextProviderProtocol`, `.LineageProviderProtocol`, `.ReplayRecordReaderProtocol`, `ccf_runtime.mcp.hitl_server.HitlActionRouteProtocol`, `.HitlQueueViewProtocol`, `ccf_runtime.authz.product_entitlement.PerspectiveResolverProtocol`, `ccf_runtime.peering.lf_a2a.PeerCardResolver`, `ccf_runtime.peering.typed_http.PeerEndpointResolver` — **all twelve in `runtime/`, none in `src/`**. **Cost, measured by diffing the frozen baseline against the live manifest.** The current diff is 219 added, 0 removed, 29 reshaped. `tests/test_surface_completeness.py::test_every_diff_line_has_a_matching_public_log_entry` requires every diff line to carry a fragment entry, so a fix's cost is counted in new `reshaped` lines: the `__call__`-only fix moves **12** entries of which **11** are already in the baseline, therefore **11 new `reshaped` diff lines**; a full method-set fix would move **80** entries of which **65** are already in the baseline, therefore **65**. Dave chose 11 over 65 knowingly. **THE DESCOPE, RECORDED UNSOFTENED, BECAUSE THIS PHASE MUST NOT READ AS "THE BLIND SPOT IS FIXED."** `__call__` capture fixes **12 of 51** information-free Protocols. `q2b-in` closes all **18** aliases. That leaves **39 multi-method Protocols still information-free after this fix lands** — their entire public contract is a method set, `__call__` capture reaches none of them, and a signature change to any of their methods will still produce no manifest diff line. **Every one of the seven Protocols `D-IMPL-110` named by name is in the descoped 39:** `ccf.artifacts.protocol.ArtifactStoreProtocol` (4 methods), `ccf.controlplane.registry.ControlPlaneProtocol` (6), `ccf.harness.HarnessAdapterProtocol` (5), `ccf.orchestration.OrchestrationProtocol` (2), `ccf.hitl.HITLTransportProtocol` (6), `ccf.llm.LLMClientProtocol` (1), `ccf.halflife.debt.DebtFactorScorerProtocol` (3) — that is, most of the pluggable substrate CLAUDE.md §11 mandates Protocols for. Also descoped: `ccf.graph.protocols.GraphReader` (5) and `.GraphWriter` (3), `ccf.replay.engine.ReplayEnvironment` (3), `ccf.operators.base.Operator` (4), `ccf.agents.ArchetypeComponent` (4), `ccf.peering.protocol.PeeringProtocol` (2), and 26 more. `ccf.graph.reader.Row` has **zero** members and has nothing to show under any fix. **This is `q2b-out`'s record-honesty argument landing on the Protocol side rather than the alias side, and it is stated here rather than discovered later:** after this fix, the surface manifest remains *not* evidence of surface stability for a multi-method Protocol, and must not be cited as such at any gate. `D-IMPL-110`'s consequence (1) survives the fix for the 39. **The alternatives considered and not taken, with their measured costs.** `q2a-rewrite` — regenerate the baseline and re-pin `_BASELINE_DIGEST` — buys a smaller fragment and a baseline reflecting the tool's corrected view, and was declined because it requires defeating a provenance test whose whole purpose is to make that impossible, and because it would make the already-shipped resolver break invisible again, which is the opposite of the audit `DI-15.5-06` exists to produce. **Full method-set capture** — 51 of 51 Protocols, **65** new `reshaped` diff lines — was declined on that measured cost; it remains the shape that actually closes the blind spot, and a later phase taking it should expect 65 claimed entries plus the reclassification of the `15.5-11` and `15.5-15` fragments `D-IMPL-110` already anticipated. `q2b-out` — descope the aliases — was declined; its own argument is the one this entry applies to the 39 Protocols instead. **Corrections to the record, both measured at `4b407e0` and both given with the prior value.** (1) **`D-IMPL-110`'s inventory has grown and its per-name framing is superseded.** It measured at `4d16cb9`: 56 information-free entries, 30 named `*Protocol`, 54 Protocol classes across `src/` + `runtime/`. Measured now: **80** information-free entries, **34** named `*Protocol`, **56** Protocol definition sites. More consequentially, `DI-15.5-06` states that teaching the tool to record `__call__` *"would move **every** Protocol entry"*; measured, it moves **12 of 51**, because 39 of the 51 have no `__call__` to read. (2) **The "five `Literal` type aliases" figure is wrong and inverted.** Measured, the 18 distinct information-free aliases are **13 `Literal`** — `ArtifactRole`, `AssemblyMode`, `AuditAction`, `DebtFactorName`, `EffectCapability`, `GrantState`, `LineageMode`, `OperatorCategory`, `PayloadViolationReason`, `PolicyBindingMode`, `ProductRequestViolationReason`, `RegistrationMode`, `TTLAnchor` — and **5 not `Literal`**: `ExpiryDerivation` and `HttpClientFactory` and `SkillCallable` (`Callable`), `JsonWebKey` (`Mapping`), `WorkflowOutcome` (`Union`). "Five" is exactly the count of the **non**-`Literal` aliases, so the prior scoping sentence named the wrong five. `q2b-in` covers all 18. **Governs:** `tools/dump_surface.py`; `docs/surface/v0.2-surface.json`; `docs/surface/v0.2-surface-baseline.json`, which does **not** change; `tests/test_surface_completeness.py`; the scope of `DI-15.5-06`; the 39 descoped Protocols, which remain open debt with no owner assigned by this entry. **Implementing plan: plan `15.6-10`** — the manifest fix and this phase's single manifest regeneration. Originates: `DI-15.5-06`; `D-IMPL-110`; plan `15.6-09`'s re-measurement at `4b407e0`.

**D-IMPL-123 — `DI-15.5-08`'s sweep covers every unanchored `.py`-target citation except the 13 in the records `D-IMPL-114` says a later editor should not silently revise: 101 of 114.** RESOLVED — the measured fourth shape. Date: 2026-08-12. Approver: Dave. **Dave's selection, verbatim:** *"Q-3 : the measured FOURTH shape — everything EXCEPT the 13 `D-IMPL-114` names = 101 citations."* This option did not exist in the plan's list; it exists because the measurement produced it. **The framing that must travel with this entry, because a later reader will otherwise assume the opposite.** **The citation gate is GREEN.** At `4b407e0`, `python3 -m tools.lints --report` reports **762 citations matched across 948 files and 0 findings**. The unanchored population is a **coverage** statement — citations the checker declines to check because they carry no anchor to check against — not a set of failures. This sweep raises coverage; it does not repair a red gate. **And `D-IMPL-114`'s consequence (1) is already discharged**: `tools/lints/citations.py::RULE_NODE_ID` implements `cite03-node-id`, and **150** `path::Symbol` citations are already in gated scope with **144 checked and passing**. This ruling is about the sweep, not about the checker. **The measured population, derived by re-using the checker's own scanner rather than counted by hand.** `tools.lints.citations.iter_scanned_files` plus its own exemption, historicity, resolution and anchor predicates reproduce the tool's `no_anchor` bucket **exactly — 435 of 435** — and that bucket filtered to citations whose *target* is a `.py` file gives **114 citations across 27 host files**: `.planning/` **47** in 6 files, `docs/` excluding `surface-changes/` **24** in 4, `src/` **16** in 3, `tests/` **13** in 6, `docs/surface-changes/` **11** in 5, `tools/` **3** in 3. `runtime/`, `examples/`, `perf/` and `public/` host **zero**. **The 13 held back, named by file so the boundary is checkable rather than asserted:** `docs/surface-changes/15.5-11-debt-delivery.md` (**5**), `docs/surface-changes/15-03-audit-baseline.md` (**3**), `docs/surface-changes/15-11-operator-builder.md` (**1**), `docs/surface-changes/15.5-08-request-contract.md` (**1**), `docs/surface-changes/15.5-13-request-in-replay.md` (**1**) — landed plan fragments — plus `docs/SURFACE-CHANGES.md` (**2**), the whole-tree-assembled artifact, which `make surface-changes` regenerates from those fragments in any case, so editing it directly is overwritten rather than preserved. `docs/DECISIONS.md`, the third record `D-IMPL-114` names, contributes **zero** to the 114 because the citation gate already exempts it wholesale. **101 = 114 − 13.** **Correction to the record, with both values.** The population was carried as **125 across 31 files**, with **49** in `.planning/`, **5** in `docs/surface-changes/`, and a remediable floor of **38** (`src/` 17 + `tests/` 17 + `tools/` 4). Measured at `4b407e0`: **114 across 27**, `.planning/` **47**, `docs/surface-changes/` **11**, and the code-trees floor **32** (`src/` 16 + `tests/` 13 + `tools/` 3). **A premise also fails, and it is the one that shaped the options.** The carried framing held that the `.planning/` citations live in files *"`D-IMPL-114` itself says a later editor should not silently revise."* Measured, **none of them does**: `tools/lints/citations.py` already exempts dated records through `HISTORICAL_RECORDS` — `docs/DECISIONS.md`, the whitepaper, `docs/PHASE_*.md`, and the whole of `.planning/phases/*` — so a dated planning record never enters this count at all. The 47 are entirely in living or explicitly maintained files: `.planning/phases/15.1-ergonomics-gap-closure/deferred-items.md` **24**, `.planning/REQUIREMENTS.md` **8**, `.planning/phases/15.5-the-context-product-surface-vessel-assembler-debt-entitlem/deferred-items.md` **8**, `.planning/STATE.md` **5**, `.planning/ROADMAP.md` **1**, `.planning/phases/15.4-backend-registration-seam-the-substrate-stops-naming-its-ada/deferred-items.md` **1** — the first, third and sixth of which are in `MAINTAINED_RECORDS` by name. That is why a shape covering 101 rather than 32 or 56 is available at all. **The alternatives considered and not taken, with their re-measured costs.** `q3-code-trees` — `src/` + `tests/` + `tools/` only — is **32**, not the 38 it was offered as; it touches only living code where a citation's claim is load-bearing, and was declined because it leaves **82** citations line-anchored and rotting, including the 24 in `15.1`'s maintained deferred-items file. `q3-code-plus-docs` is **56**, not roughly 76; it adds `docs/CONTEXT_PRODUCT_RULING_EVIDENCE.md` (15) and `docs/SURFACE-AUDIT.md` (6), both actively maintained and actively cited, and was declined for stopping short of the maintained planning records. `q3-all` — **114** — was declined because it rewrites the 13 landed-fragment and assembled-artifact citations `D-IMPL-114` names by class. **Worth knowing, and recorded so the sweep is planned rather than discovered.** **Five** of the 114 target the exact stale range `D-IMPL-114` was written about — `src/ccf/replay/engine.py` lines 449 to 455 — and **two of those five sit inside the held-back 13**, in `docs/surface-changes/15.5-11-debt-delivery.md`. The sweep therefore leaves the originating defect visibly extant in two places by ruling, which is the intended outcome and not an oversight. **Governs:** the `DI-15.5-08` sweep's scope; `tools/lints/citations.py` and its two-direction ratchet; the citation convention in `docs/`, `.planning/` prose and source docstrings. Does **not** amend `D-IMPL-114`, whose convention and whose leave-alone classes stand unchanged. **Implementing plan: plan `15.6-11`** — the citation sweep and its ratchet. Originates: `DI-15.5-08`; `D-IMPL-114`; plan `15.6-09`'s re-measurement at `4b407e0`.

**D-IMPL-124 — Source-refresh recency publishes a freshness signal, not a source enumeration: the human-interpretable source identities come off the vessel and the inference channel is CLOSED rather than accepted as residual.** RESOLVED — Shape B. Date: 2026-08-12. Approver: Dave. **Dave's ruling, verbatim and in full:** *"Source-set / recency: the field's purpose is freshness, not source enumeration. Publish the freshness signal without human-interpretable source identities (Shape B). The inference channel is closed rather than accepted as residual."* **Where the question came from, and it was named rather than ruled by the plan that shipped the field.** Plan `15.6-07` shipped `ContextProductContract.source_refresh_recency` **populated**, correctly: it was instructed that `D-IMPL-119` governs evidence pointers rather than refresh timestamps and that limbs 3 and 4 must not be applied by analogy, and it did not apply them. Its executor then named a new question instead of ruling it — `.planning/phases/15.6-decision-completeness-and-defect-closure/15.6-07-SUMMARY.md` Findings § 3, *"a reader could learn that a source **exists** and **when it last published** without being entitled to its contents … This is a new question, not a limb of an existing entry, and it is not ruled here."* Reporting it rather than disposing of it silently is the behaviour the checkpoint exists to elicit, and it is recorded as such. **This is the THIRD instance of one inference-channel shape in this project.** `D-IMPL-105` closed it for **completeness** — *"a caller can therefore infer what they are not allowed to see from the debt they are shown … an oracle over the entitlement configuration, quieter than a refusal because nothing in the response says a consultation was declined."* `D-IMPL-119` closed it for **evidence pointers**, ruling them entitlement-sensitive and forbidding interpretable pointers until the assembly path is principal-scoped. This entry closes it for **source recency**. **The measured finding this was ruled against, taken at `4b407e0`.** `src/ccf/halflife/assessment.py::SourceRefresh` carries exactly two members — `source_ref: str`, documented opaque to the SDK and declared by the deployment, and `refreshed_at: datetime`, timezone-aware. `examples/s1-01-hello-context-product/artifacts/product-governance.yaml` declares **three** entries whose `source_ref` values are **human-interpretable strings** — `corporate-registry`, `kyc-registry`, `name-screening` — which is the exact contrast with `D-IMPL-119` limb 3(a)'s opaque handle and is the substance of the question. **The whole set reaches every principal who can reach the product:** `runtime/ccf_runtime/mcp/egress.py::build_egress_server` returns `canonical_bytes(servable.product)` and `runtime/ccf_runtime/http_api/routes/product.py::build_router` returns `canonical_response(servable.product)` — the whole vessel, serialized verbatim. **And nothing scopes the set:** `D-IMPL-119`'s three hops were re-read off the shipped parameter lists and all three still hold — `src/ccf/deployment/operators.py::build_operator_registry` takes `(parameters, view, pins, policy_bundle_key, similarity_index_version)` with no perspective and no principal; `src/ccf/operators/categories.py::PolicyFilter` takes `(parameters)` with no principal; `runtime/ccf_runtime/authz/product_entitlement.py::entitled_perspective` decides on `(grant, tenant, product_class, requested_role, …)`, a **per-class** decision with no per-source dimension. So a reader entitled to the product learns that `name-screening` and `kyc-registry` exist and when each last published, with no check that they are entitled to those sources' contents. **`D-IMPL-118`'s content-address coupling does not arise, and the reason is worth stating precisely.** The declared set is identical for every principal, so one assembly still yields one content address — the coupling `D-IMPL-105` dissolved does not reappear. But it holds because nothing varies, **not** because anything prevents it from varying, and Shape B keeps it that way by removing the identities rather than by scoping them. **Shape B, as ruled.** Replace, or parallel, the current per-source map with an **opaque, non-identifying freshness summary** — for example `oldest_source_refresh_at` and `newest_source_refresh_at`, or a single `sources_freshest_at` / `sources_stalest_at` pair. **No `source_ref` string leaves the vessel.** Cost: a small shape change plus a content-address move. Gain, in Dave's terms: it *removes the oracle entirely while still delivering the freshness information the field exists for*. The closure is the point of this entry — the channel is **closed**, not accepted as residual, and a later reader must not record it as a tolerated risk. **The alternatives considered and not taken, with Dave's own stated costs.** **Shape A** — keep the identities and accept the residual: zero code change, and the risk is a third open oracle of the same family. **NOT TAKEN.** **Shape C** — opaque tokens on the product, with resolution to a human-interpretable `source_ref` only behind an entitlement-checked door: Dave's own words, *"the right long-term design but is new work."* **NOT TAKEN**, and recorded here as **the named long-term shape**, so a later phase adding an entitlement-checked resolution door is completing a design already on the record rather than re-opening a closed one. **Shape D** — filter the set by entitlement now: **blocked**, because it requires the perspective-scoped view in the assembly path that this plan's own measurement showed does not exist, and which `D-IMPL-119` limb 2 puts explicitly out of scope for Phase 15.6. **NOT TAKEN.** **Scope boundary, stated because this entry revises shipped code and the recording plan did not touch it.** Plan `15.6-09` **records** this ruling and **does not implement it**. Shape B revises what plan `15.6-07` shipped: `src/ccf/halflife/assessment.py::SourceRefresh` and `::SourceRefreshRecency`, `src/ccf/products/contract.py::ContextProductContract`, the `src/ccf/replay/record.py::ContentGenerationPath` twin, **all 7** shipped `rehydrate_product` implementations across 6 files, the `s1-01` artifacts and spine, and a **fourth** content-address move in `examples/08-e2e-demo/artifacts/expected_output.txt`. **Implementing plan: plan `15.6-13`**, dedicated to it. **Sequencing constraint, and it is a constraint rather than a preference: `15.6-13` must land BEFORE plan `15.6-10` regenerates the surface manifest.** `15.6-10` owns this phase's single manifest regeneration; a regeneration taken before Shape B lands would capture the pre-Shape-B vessel and be **stale on arrival**, and the phase would then need a second regeneration it has ruled it will not take. **Governs:** `src/ccf/halflife/assessment.py::SourceRefresh` and `::SourceRefreshRecency`; `src/ccf/products/contract.py::ContextProductContract`'s `source_refresh_recency` member; `src/ccf/replay/record.py::ContentGenerationPath`'s matching member; every implementation of `ReplayEnvironment.rehydrate_product`; `examples/s1-01-hello-context-product/artifacts/product-governance.yaml` as the population site; the ordering of plans `15.6-13` and `15.6-10`. Does **not** amend `D-IMPL-118`, whose `qa-toplevel` placement is unchanged — only the member's *contents* change — and does **not** amend `D-IMPL-119`, whose limbs continue to govern evidence pointers and not this field. **Implementing plan: plan `15.6-13`.** Originates: plan `15.6-07` Findings § 3, raised by its executor and deliberately not ruled there; `D-IMPL-105`; `D-IMPL-119`; `D-IMPL-117`; plan `15.6-09`'s re-measurement at `4b407e0`.

**D-IMPL-125 — "Hello CCF" is plan `16-05` rebuilt in place: a Fluree backend, output a human reads, and the produce and consume paths shown as two separate examples.** RESOLVED. Date: 2026-08-13. Approver: Dave. **Origin: the blocking human-verify checkpoint in plan `16-05` returned a change request rather than an approval — twice.** That checkpoint exists because a structural test can prove markers are present, ordered and closed but cannot prove a reader can *see* what the example teaches; its own text said *"This shape is copied by every other example in the suite. If it is wrong, it is cheapest to say so now."* It was right both times, and neither defect was reachable by any gate in the tree. **First reading, 2026-08-13 morning.** Two defects: the example ran on `sqlite` and it printed **nothing at all** — `grep -rnE "print\(|sys\.stdout\.write" examples/s1-01-hello-context-product/*.py` returned no matches across `run.py`, `spine.py`, `deployment.py` and `source_estate.py`, while `run.py::main` assembled three Context Products inside a `tempfile.TemporaryDirectory` and discarded them. `make example-s1-01` exited 0 having shown the operator nothing. Rulings: **Fluree, not sqlite**; **it prints its work**; **Fluree now and the six-backend matrix later at `16-07`**, so nothing may hard-wire the substrate in a way that blocks parameterisation — the backend comes from `artifacts/backends.yaml` through the Phase 15.4 registration seam. **Phase 16's scope is UNCHANGED by this entry** — 21 plans, all 10 ROADMAP success criteria unedited, Sections 1–3 all still ship; Dave's words, *"I don't want to throw away the scope of phase 16, just insert 'hello ccf' before the existing scope begins."* An earlier reading of the same conversation as a *narrowing* — Phase 16 reduced to one example with the remainder deferred to a new Phase 16.1 — was **wrong and is recorded here as rejected**, because it would have rewritten criteria 1, 3, 4, 5, 7 and 9 that the phase still satisfies at close across waves 4 through 12. **Second reading, 2026-08-13 evening, and it is the substantive one.** The rebuilt example ran on Fluree in 6 seconds and printed 339 lines, and Dave rejected it again: *"what this example does not show me is 1) how do I create a context product and 2) as a client how do I receive a context product. Both of these are lumped into one set of code and I cannot discern how easy or hard it is to consume a context product nor do I know how to make one. ... What I am seeing will frighten anyone away who just wants to access data from an MCP server and send in a query."* **The diagnosis, recorded because the error is instructive:** the plan optimised for governance completeness — proving what CCF guarantees — when the newcomer needs two separable answers, *how do I make one* and *how do I get one*. The `spine.py`/`deployment.py` split the plan was careful about is a **layering** split (CCF's calls versus yours); the split Dave asked for is a **producer/consumer** split, and one process doing both cannot show it. The checkpoint question the orchestrator wrote — *"does this teach what CCF gives over a plain query"* — asked for the **argument** and not the **how-to**, and that is why the plan satisfied it while failing the reader. **Rulings: two examples, two commands**, so neither path can hide inside the other; **the consumer shows the in-process call first and MCP second**, isolating *what is a product* from *how does it travel*; and **the client passes its own parameters**, because — Dave — *"a context product is not a precalculated static object"* and a client fetching a fixed URI looks exactly like fetching a cached blob. The 339-line governance transcript becomes opt-in behind `--explain`; the default output is the question, the answer and the address. **Governs:** plan `16-05` and the plans that will carry the produce and consume examples; `examples/s1-01-hello-context-product/`; `examples/s2-01-external-consumers/`, which today holds two zero-CCF client files with no server, no README and no runner. **Does not amend** any of `D-01`..`D-12` in `16-CONTEXT.md`, which remain locked. Originates: plan `16-05` Task 3, both readings; CLAUDE.md §9 (the examples are the adoption contract) and §3.1.

**D-IMPL-126 — The MCP egress door gains a parameterised tool, and four consumption papercuts are fixed before the examples are written.** RESOLVED. Date: 2026-08-13. Approver: Dave. **Ruled against a measurement, not an argument.** Before planning the two examples `D-IMPL-125` ordered, a throwaway spike wrote and *ran* the smallest honest programs for each path and counted them. The numbers, all reproduced against the live tree at `f0af5de`: **producing costs 91 lines of developer Python and 11 mandatory artifacts** (172 non-comment configuration lines, plus 341 lines of `events.yaml` which is data); **consuming costs 9 lines over MCP and 10 over HTTP, with zero `ccf` imports, both green.** Against Dave's stated baseline — *"put an MCP server in front of the fluree database and run a query"*, roughly five lines — **the client is not the problem and never was.** Two further measurements matter: **6 of the 16 manifest entries are droppable** and still yield a byte-identical product (`cbc6b990517d1f63`), and **109 of the 172 configuration lines sit in two files a beginner cannot plausibly author**, `resolution-spec.yaml` (53) and `manifest.yaml` (56), while the other eight artifacts average 7 lines each. **The finding that forced this entry.** The MCP egress door **cannot carry a single parameter**, and this is ruled design rather than oversight: `PRODUCT_URI_TEMPLATE` is three path segments — `{tenant}/{product_class}/{version}` (`runtime/ccf_runtime/mcp/egress.py:116`); the handler passes `request=None` as a hard-coded literal (`:368`); and the server advertises **zero tools**, confirmed live as `TOOLS: []`. `runtime/ccf_runtime/http_api/routes/product.py:36-37` states the position outright — *"This is the one door with a query surface, so it is the one door that can carry a request"* — citing `D-IMPL-108`. The consequence is the one that decides the matter: **which question gets answered is entirely the server's choice, so the MCP door as shipped cannot refute the reading that a Context Product is a precalculated static object** — which is precisely what `D-IMPL-125` requires the consumer example to refute, and precisely the surface Dave's own mental model reaches for. **Ruled: add a parameterised tool alongside the resource, taking the four §6.3 axes as the HTTP door already does.** `runtime/ccf_runtime/mcp/experience.py:400` already proves a parameterised MCP tool is buildable — `get_context` varies `tenant` and `entity`, though not an instant. This **extends** `D-IMPL-108` rather than contradicting it: the *resource* remains the standing-answer surface at its unchanged URI, and the tool is a second door. The alternatives were priced and not taken — extending `PRODUCT_URI_TEMPLATE` with query segments, and leaving MCP as the standing-answer door while documenting HTTP as the parameterised one; the latter was rejected on the ground that it permanently forecloses any MCP example refuting the cached-blob reading. **Ruled: fix the papercuts before the examples are written, not after.** In the order the spike ranked them by cost to a first-time adopter: (1) `request-contract.yaml` returns a `DeclaredArtifact` where `register_product_class` demands a `ProductRequestContract`, and the remedy — hand-building one from three keys of `.declaration` plus `ProductPayloadSchema.model_validate` — is reachable only by reading `deployment.py:867-880`; a `from_declared` classmethod, or a manifest loader that parses `class_id: *.request-contract` into the typed model, removes it at no structural cost. (2) `ServableProduct.model_construct` fails with *"type object 'ServableProduct' has no attribute 'model_construct'"*, which a beginner reads as a pydantic version problem rather than as `INV-CH7-52` working correctly — the type is deliberately unconstructible and the error must say so. (3) `ApiDependencies` demands eight fields to serve one product; five accept `None` undocumented while the type hints say otherwise. (4) `sha256` of the raw HTTP response body **equals the content address exactly** — verified byte-for-byte against the in-process `content_hash` — yet there is no `ETag`, no `X-CCF-Content-Address` header and nothing in the body: a zero-CCF consumer can verify in one stdlib line what it has no way to learn. **Not ruled and explicitly left open: `build_dev_egress_server`.** `build_egress_server` requires six keyword arguments and the SDK ships a usable default for one and a half; a dev-grade helper would take 43 lines of server ceremony to roughly 8, but it is a governance-bypass seam and would need the named, greppable, warning-emitting treatment `acknowledge_insecure_default` already has. **A finding recorded for the example rather than the fabric:** a deliberately naive projection reading `view.relationships` wholesale produced **identical content addresses for two different subjects** — the instant axis varied for free, the subject axis did not, because seeds narrow only when a segment fragment declares a `traverse` (`src/ccf/projection/engine.py:562-578`) and `s1-01` differentiates only because its plan reads `scenario.request.subject` (`deployment.py:420`). A naive Π therefore yields a subject-invariant answer that *looks* precomputed, so the proof `D-IMPL-125` demands is only as strong as the projection the example ships, and the example must say so. **Permitted by criterion 10** as amended by `D-IMPL-96`, which revoked the "no fabric changed" wording precisely so Phase 16 could repair defects it finds. **Governs:** `runtime/ccf_runtime/mcp/egress.py`; `runtime/ccf_runtime/http_api/`; `src/ccf/products/request.py` and the manifest loader; the plans carrying the produce and consume examples. **Extends** `D-IMPL-108`. Originates: the consumption spike of 2026-08-13, run against `f0af5de` with a live `fluree/server:4.1.4`; `D-IMPL-125`; CLAUDE.md §7.1 (MCP as a bidirectional surface) and §9.

**D-IMPL-127 — "Hello World for CCF" is a three-example progression, each adding exactly one idea, inserted ahead of Phase 16's existing example plans — which then proceed on the new surface.** RESOLVED. Date: 2026-08-13. Approver: Dave. **Amends `D-IMPL-125`'s decomposition and nothing else in it, and it does NOT relax the produce/consume separation.** That entry ruled *two* examples split by **produce versus consume**. This ruling adds a second, orthogonal axis: **three examples divided by what the client does** — the axis a newcomer actually travels — while the produce/consume split is **promoted from a one-time decomposition into a standing structural rule that holds inside every example, without exception.** Dave, ruling it in as many words: *"we always split context product production from client access."* So HW-1 separates its assembler from its client even though both run in one process; HW-2 and HW-3 separate them across processes as well. The two axes compose — an example is identified by what its client does, and is *built* with production and access always on opposite sides of a visible boundary. This is the original defect the checkpoint caught (*"Both of these are lumped into one set of code and I cannot discern how easy or hard it is to consume a context product nor do I know how to make one"*), and it is now a rule rather than a fix applied once. **Note for a later reader: this separation is NOT the `spine.py`/`deployment.py` split.** That one divides CCF's calls from the deployment's code — a *layering* split, and the orchestrator's original confusion of the two is what produced the rejected example. Production-versus-access is a different cut, and both must hold. Everything else `D-IMPL-125` ruled stands — Fluree, printed output a human reads, the client passing its own parameters, and the `--explain` demotion of the 339-line governance transcript. **The progression, in Dave's own construction.** **HW-1, the basic form**: a *simple server assembling a simple Context Product*, consumed by a *simple client in the same process*. Its one idea is **what a Context Product is**, with no wire, no process boundary and no governance argument in the way. **HW-2, remote access**: *a different client accessing the same Context Product* over MCP, and *an agent reaching it via A2A*. Its one idea is **how a product travels, and that the client owns the question** — and the discipline that makes it teach is that **only the client changes**: same assembler, same product, same artifacts, so a reader learns that remoteness costs nothing and buys a process boundary. An example that also changed the product would lose that. **HW-3, the sophisticated form**: *a Context Product assembled across multiple backends*, with a client that sees **Context Debt (`D_c`)** and **Context Half-life (`λ_c`)** doing real work. Its one idea is **why governed context beats a query**, and the multi-backend setting is not decoration — debt is only legible once assembly spans sources that disagree about freshness, because a single-backend product makes `D_c` look like a constant. **A2A is placed in HW-2**, paired with MCP rather than given its own example, so a reader can compare an MCP client against an agent over A2A **on the same product**; A2A is first-class in v0 per CLAUDE.md §7.2 and its Control Plane admission verification belongs beside the MCP door, not apart from it. **What happens after HW-3, ruled explicitly because the orchestrator read the opposite and was corrected:** *"Once we get through these we do go to the original examples on the new surface."* **Phase 16's scope therefore remains UNCHANGED for the third time in one day** — 21 plans, all 10 ROADMAP success criteria unedited, Sections 1–3 all still ship, `examples/00`–`08` still retire at `16-18`. The three Hello Worlds are an **insertion** ahead of the existing example plans in the same pattern `D-IMPL-125` established, not a replacement for them and not a narrowing; no ROADMAP surgery is owed and no criterion is rewritten. **Sequencing, and it is a constraint rather than a preference: the fabric work in `D-IMPL-126` lands first.** HW-2 is unbuildable until the MCP egress door can carry a parameter — today `PRODUCT_URI_TEMPLATE` is three path segments, the handler passes `request=None` as a hard-coded literal, and the server advertises zero tools, so a client cannot ask its own question and the example would demonstrate the precalculated-static-object reading rather than refute it. **Plan `16-07`'s parameterised runner is not discarded**; it becomes HW-3's multi-backend assembler, and criterion 4 is satisfied there. **An open measurement carried into planning rather than resolved here:** the spike measured the current produce floor at **91 lines of developer Python and 11 mandatory artifacts**, which is not yet "simple" in the sense HW-1 requires. `D-IMPL-126`'s papercut fixes reduce it and six of the sixteen manifest entries are droppable with a byte-identical product, but whether HW-1 keeps the KYC/UBO-on-FIBO domain `D-IMPL-125` ruled — which makes the answer worth reading — or needs a smaller subject to reach "basic form" is to be settled against a measurement at planning, not by assertion. **Governs:** the three Hello World plans and their sequencing against `D-IMPL-126`; plan `16-05`, whose paused Fluree and `artifacts/backends.yaml` work carries into HW-1; plan `16-07`. **Amends** `D-IMPL-125` (decomposition only). Originates: Dave, 2026-08-13, and the consumption spike that priced it.

**D-IMPL-128 — `get_context` on the Experience MCP server reaches assembly with no entitlement check, and is fixed in this phase; client-chosen `subject` stays a named residual because the parameterised tool's added exposure measures zero.** RESOLVED, two limbs. Date: 2026-08-13. Approver: Dave. **Limb 1 — the defect, and it is in the SDK's external front door.** Measured at `add11aa`: `runtime/ccf_runtime/mcp/experience.py:775-777` is `def get_context(tenant: str, entity: str) -> str: return context_seam(tenant=tenant, entity=entity)`. **Both arguments are client-supplied, and the handler calls neither `verified_grant()` nor `entitled_perspective(...)`.** The `context_product` **resource on the same server** calls both, at `:686-693`, with the full argument set including `requested_role=None`. So one MCP module ships two doors onto assembly and gates only one of them. **The module documents the exact discipline it violates**, at `:48-58`: *"a fabric whose external door is less governed than its internal one has its threat model backwards. This phase's founding complaint is 'an MCP server in front of a database'; shipping the SDK's own front door ungated would be that complaint, self-inflicted."* `experience.py` **is** the external door — `egress.py` is the fabric-internal one — so the posture that docstring argues for is inverted in practice by the tool sitting beside the resource it describes. `tenant` being client-chosen is the sharper half: the handler takes the tenant as an argument and performs no grant check against it. **Ruled: fix it in this phase**, gating `get_context` with the same `verified_grant()` → `entitled_perspective(...)` sequence the resource uses, not an equivalent-looking reimplementation. Authority is criterion 10 as amended by `D-IMPL-96`, which revoked the "no fabric changed" wording precisely so Phase 16 could repair defects it finds. The alternative — record it and defer — was rejected on the ground that `D-IMPL-127`'s HW-2 would then ship a governed MCP door standing beside an ungated one, in a phase whose founding complaint is ungoverned MCP. **How it was found, recorded because the method generalises:** no audit and no lint found it. Plan `16-23` was required to establish *exactly where each protection is enforced on the resource path* before asserting its new tool traversed the same checks, and reading the cited reference implementation is what exposed that the reference does not do it. A plan that had asserted "the new tool follows the existing pattern" would have copied the gap forward and been correct in its own terms. **`get_lineage` and every other tool on that server are to be checked for the same shape** — one instance of a missing gate on a client-parameterised handler is evidence about a class, not an incident. **Limb 2 — client-chosen `subject` is not entitlement-scoped, and the parameterised tool ships anyway.** `runtime/ccf_runtime/authz/product_entitlement.py::entitled_perspective` decides on `(grant, tenant, product_class, resolved role)` and carries **no subject dimension** (`:386-446`), while `subject` becomes the traversal seed (`request_seeds`, `D-IMPL-111` limb (a)) and therefore selects what is assembled. **Ruled: proceed, because the delta the tool introduces is zero and that is a measurement rather than an argument.** Anything reachable as `call_tool(subject=S)` is already reachable as `GET /product/{tenant}/{class}/{version}?subject=S&…` on the same grant, through the same decision, on a door shipped since `D-IMPL-108`. The tool widens the vocabulary of doors, not the set of reachable state. **Closing the gap needs a perspective-scoped assembly path that does not exist**, and two prior rulings already measured that absence and deferred it: `D-IMPL-119` limb 2 measured the entitled-versus-assembled delta as *empty on the shipped path* and put the scoped view explicitly out of scope, and `D-IMPL-124` Shape D was **blocked** on the same absence. `D-IMPL-126` did not authorise building it. **Recorded as a named residual, unassigned by ruling** — the trigger for opening it is a concrete harm or an authorised scoped-assembly workstream, and until then no plan may cite the tool's shipping as evidence that subject-scoping is unnecessary. The rejected alternative was holding the tool until scoping lands, which stops HW-2 entirely and treats a zero-delta change as though it added exposure. **Governs:** `runtime/ccf_runtime/mcp/experience.py` and every client-parameterised handler on it; the plan carrying the fix; `16-23`'s tool, which deliberately does **not** copy `get_context`'s shape. **Extends** `D-IMPL-126`. Does not amend `D-IMPL-119` or `D-IMPL-124`, whose deferrals stand and are the reason limb 2 is a residual rather than a repair. Originates: plan `16-23`'s security analysis, 2026-08-13; verified independently by the orchestrator against `experience.py:686-693`, `:775-777` and `:48-58`.

**D-IMPL-129 — Three tools on the Experience MCP server cannot be entitled without governance machinery the SDK does not have; they ship authenticated, with the absence stated unhedged and pinned by a test that fails the day it is fixed.** RESOLVED. Date: 2026-08-13. Approver: Dave. **The measurement that opened it, taken while planning `D-IMPL-128` limb 1 and verified independently by the orchestrator at `27b101c`:** across all five tool bodies on `runtime/ccf_runtime/mcp/experience.py` — lines 715 to 805 — the substrings `verified_grant` and `entitled_perspective` appear **zero times**. `EXPERIENCE_TOOLS` (`:176-184`) has exactly five members and **not one of them performs an entitlement check**, while the `context_product` **resource** beside them performs both (`:686-693`). `D-IMPL-128` was ruled against one of the five; the audit it required found the other four, and the class is the finding. **The split, argued rather than assumed.** `get_context` and `get_lineage` **are** gateable on the existing artifact and are closed by plan `16-24`: the first reaches Context Product assembly, which `ProductEntitlementPolicy` decides by construction; the second returns `servable.product.lineage`, a **field of the vessel the resource already serves whole**, so the policy that decides who receives the class decides who receives a field of it. **`list_agents`, `invoke_agent` and `get_replay_record` are not**, and the reason is that the deciding artifact does not contain the question. `ProductEntitlementPolicy`'s grants are `permitted_schema_names` and `permitted_roles` (`runtime/ccf_runtime/authz/product_entitlement.py:198-212`), which say nothing about agents and nothing about replay records. Gating `invoke_agent` on it would assert *entitlement to receive product class C implies permission to invoke any agent in tenant T* — a semantic **the SDK would be inventing**, which CLAUDE.md §11 forbids in those words and which the No-Hardcoding Rule forbids in mechanism. **`invoke_agent` is the sharpest of the three and is arguably sharper than the defect `D-IMPL-128` ruled:** `def invoke_agent(tenant, definition_key, definition_version, skill, arguments)` (`experience.py:744-763`) takes **every argument from the client, including the tenant**, and dispatches an agent's bound skill through the injected invoker. `list_agents` returns a client-chosen tenant's agent population, versions, archetypes and bound skill surfaces. **Ruled: authenticate, state it, and pin it.** Each of the three gains `verified_grant()` as its first statement — authentication only, inventing nothing — each docstring says **unhedged** that the tool is authenticated and *not* entitled, and the gap is pinned by a named test asserting the three **succeed** for an unentitled credential, whose own docstring records that **inverting that assertion is the signature of the fix**. A gap held open by an executable assertion cannot rot the way a paragraph in a deferred-items file can, and this project has watched paragraphs rot. **The machinery that would close it, named so a later phase is completing a design rather than reopening one:** `TenantAuthorizerProtocol` (`runtime/ccf_runtime/authz/protocol.py:78`) plus new action ids beside `ACTION_PRODUCT_RETRIEVE` (`runtime/ccf_runtime/authz/refusals.py:35-48`), plus an adopter-registered policy artifact — exactly the shape the HITL door already carries at `runtime/ccf_runtime/mcp/hitl_server.py::_enforce_tenant_authz`. That is a new **required builder seam** and a new artifact type. Neither `D-IMPL-126` nor `D-IMPL-128` authorised it, and building it would delay `D-IMPL-127`'s HW-2 behind work no example needs. **No owner is assigned.** The trigger for opening this entry is a concrete harm or an authorised authorization workstream; until then no plan may cite these tools' shipping as evidence that agent-scoped entitlement is unnecessary. **A live inconsistency recorded with it, because it shows the cost of the SDK requiring nothing:** `tests/crossproc/servers/mcp_experience_child.py` serves the Decision Replay record to any caller with **no identity check**, while `runtime/ccf_runtime/replay/serve.py:679-694` voluntarily scopes the same record to the verified caller. Two shipped surfaces in one tree already disagree about who may read a replay record, and nothing in the SDK adjudicates. **Rejected alternatives.** *Build the authorizer seam now* — closes the class rather than two of five, but is unauthorised scope that delays HW-2. *Disable the three by default under an `acknowledge_insecure_default`-style opt-in* — no invented semantics and no new artifact, but it changes behaviour for any deployment already exposing them, and the ruling preferred an honest, loudly-recorded gap over a silent behavioural break. **Governs:** `list_agents`, `invoke_agent` and `get_replay_record` on the Experience MCP server, and the open-gap test that pins them. **Extends** `D-IMPL-128`. Originates: plan `16-24`'s tool-by-tool audit, 2026-08-13; verified independently against `experience.py:176-184`, `:715-805` and `:744-763`.

**D-IMPL-130 — Phase 15.6's "no class-shape move" audit claim is superseded for Phase 16; the gate now accepts documented moves through a named allowance, and refuses undocumented ones exactly as before.** RESOLVED. Date: 2026-08-14. Approver: Dave. `tests/test_surface_shape_capture.py` was written in Phase 15.6 to refuse **any** class-shape move. That was correct under the "no fabric changed" wording of ROADMAP criterion 10 — wording `D-IMPL-96` (Dave, 2026-08-10) revoked *"precisely so Phase 16 could repair defects it finds."* The gate was never amended to match, so in plan `16-22` a fix criterion 10 explicitly authorises — giving five `ApiDependencies` handles `None` defaults — was refused by a gate encoding the superseded rule. **Ruled: the relaxation is real, deliberate, and recorded here rather than left as an unexplained exception.** The gate now permits a shape move **only** when a `docs/surface-changes/` fragment explicitly claims it, through the named `_SHAPE_MOVED_BY_FRAGMENT` allowance whose entries must be claimed by the fragment that cites them. **An undocumented shape move is refused exactly as before** — the ceiling is not raised, no `tools/lints/allowlist.yaml` entry was added, and nothing was withdrawn. **Also recorded, minor:** `PYDANTIC_MODEL_FORMS` is exported from `src/ccf/products/gates.py` — one surface entry, buying a non-vacuous parametrized test over both the class and instance refusal channels. Accepted as proportionate (Dave, 2026-08-14); noted so it is not later read as an unacknowledged leak. **Governs:** `tests/test_surface_shape_capture.py`; the `_SHAPE_MOVED_BY_FRAGMENT` allowance. **Extends** `D-IMPL-96`. Originates: plan `16-22` execution, 2026-08-14.

**D-IMPL-131 — A genuinely basic hello world comes first; `s1-01` is kept but is no longer the first example shown; and every example separates the config CCF requires from the material that fabricates the scenario.** RESOLVED. Date: 2026-08-14. Approver: Dave. **Origin: Dave read `examples/s1-01-hello-context-product/artifacts/` on GitHub and reacted to the file count** — *"I am becoming extremely concerned about what I'm seeing … Do I really require this many yaml files to construct my context product?"* — with the architectural question underneath it: *"There are context products that will mix structured and unstructured data, access graph dbs, relational dbs and industry events. They won't follow a single way of assembling data. The code I write for context assembly will vary by product."* **The measurement taken in response, and it explains the reaction.** The directory holds 20 YAML files totalling ~1060 non-comment lines. **743 of those lines — roughly 70% — are data and test fixtures, not configuration**: `events.yaml` (341, the L1 envelopes), `source-estate.yaml` (329, the simulated source systems), `expected.yaml` (73, a test fixture). The actual configuration is ~230 lines across 13 files, of which `manifest.yaml` (86) is an *index of the others* and `resolution-spec.yaml` is 53; the remaining eleven average **7 lines each**. Further, **three of the config files are this example's own invention, not CCF's** — `policy-bundle.yaml`, `product-governance.yaml` and `ubo-question.yaml` carry the `fibo-kyc.` prefix, and under `D-IMPL-84` the SDK reserves exactly one prefix (`ccf.`) and hands every other namespace an open `DeclaredArtifact` envelope it never inspects. And the consumption spike had already measured that **6 of the 16 manifest entries are droppable with a byte-identical product**. So the set is not fixed even for this one product. **None of that makes the reaction wrong.** An example that presents data, fixtures, SDK config and adopter-invented config as one undifferentiated directory makes a reader count the wrong things, and the reader counting them was the author of the SDK. That is a presentation defect in the example, not a defence of it. **Ruled, two limbs.** **(1) A true hello world, "super basic", becomes the first example shown.** `s1-01` — KYC/UBO on FIBO over Fluree — **is kept**, and is no longer first. This supersedes `D-IMPL-125`'s ruling that Hello CCF is `16-05` rebuilt in place with the KYC/UBO subject: the KYC/UBO example survives, but the *first* thing a newcomer runs is smaller than it. `D-IMPL-127`'s progression and its standing rule that production is always split from client access both stand unchanged. **(2) Every example separates CCF's required config from the scenario scaffolding**, structurally rather than by README prose: a **config subdirectory under `artifacts/`** holding what CCF actually requires, and a **separate directory** for the material that fabricates the example — the L1 event data, the simulated source estate, the expected-output fixture, the declared questions. A reader must be able to see at a glance which files they would author for their own product and which exist only to make this demonstration run. **The open question this does not settle, and it is the one that matters most:** whether the SDK's artifact requirement is fixed mechanism or scales with what a product does. `s1-01` invokes all four canonical operator categories and therefore declares four `operator-parameters-*.yaml`; nothing has yet tested a product that uses two, or assembles from a relational source, or mixes unstructured content. If the four are required regardless, CCF has baked in one assembly pattern and that contradicts mission success condition 2 (general-purposeness, CLAUDE.md §1). The basic hello world ruled here is the first measurement of that floor; a deliberately-divergent second product is the test of the general case, and is not yet commissioned. **Governs:** the HW-1 plan; `examples/s1-01-hello-context-product/`; the directory layout of every example in the suite. **Supersedes** `D-IMPL-125`'s subject ruling for the first example only. Originates: Dave, 2026-08-14, reading the shipped example.

**D-IMPL-132 — HW-1 is a very simple Context Product assembled from Postgres with an in-process client; and context assembly is the adopter's to shape, never a single format the SDK imposes.** RESOLVED. Date: 2026-08-14. Approver: Dave. **The principle first, verbatim, because it is the load-bearing part and it settles the question `D-IMPL-131` left open:** *"For the record context assembly is specific to whatever I want it to be. Operators help me assemble it but I should not be forced into a single format. Context assembly is similar to assembling a data product except it has more dimensions."* `D-IMPL-131` recorded as **open** whether the SDK's artifact requirement is fixed mechanism or scales with what a product does — noting that `s1-01` invokes all four canonical operator categories and declares four `operator-parameters-*.yaml`, and that if four were required regardless, CCF would have baked in one assembly pattern against mission success condition 2. **This entry closes that as a requirement rather than a finding.** Operators are an *aid* to assembly, not a mandatory pipeline; a product that assembles two ways declares two, and a product reading a relational source is not obliged to look like one reading a graph. Any measurement showing otherwise is a **defect against this ruling**, not a constraint to design examples around. The "data product with more dimensions" framing is the adopter-facing analogy and should reach the published pages. **HW-1, as ruled.** A **very simple** Context Product whose content is assembled from a **Postgres** database, consumed by an **in-process** client — explicitly *not* MCP, which arrives at HW-2. Subsequent examples get *progressively* more complex **in their context assembly**, which is the axis that varies and therefore the axis the suite should teach along. **This supersedes `D-IMPL-125`'s Fluree ruling for the first example only**; `s1-01` keeps Fluree and keeps KYC/UBO, and under `D-IMPL-131` is no longer the first example shown. `D-IMPL-127`'s standing rule that production is always split from client access, and `D-IMPL-131`'s structural split of CCF config from scenario scaffolding, both apply to HW-1 from birth. **Postgres viability, measured at `0f9f511` before the ruling was recorded, because the environment has no Postgres credentials and none are needed:** there is no local Postgres container and `localhost:5432` is closed, but `tests/conftest.py:269-283` already falls back to `testcontainers.community.postgres.PostgresContainer("postgres:16")` whenever `CCF_TEST_POSTGRES_DSN` is unset; `testcontainers` imports cleanly and the Docker daemon is reachable. So HW-1 is reproducible by any adopter with Docker, with no secret in the tree — the same posture Fluree already has, and the reason Postgres is a viable choice here despite being the one substrate with no standing credentials. **Postgres is also available on the DGX** at `192.168.4.76:5432` (Dave, 2026-08-14), reachable from this environment though no credential for it is present here. The two are not alternatives and the posture is the one `conftest.py` already implements: **the self-provisioned container is the default**, so the example runs for any adopter with Docker and no secret enters the tree; **`CCF_TEST_POSTGRES_DSN` points it at a real server** when one is supplied, which is how the DGX instance is used for development and for an opt-in live leg. What is ruled out is only the inverse — an example whose *default* path requires Dave's lab, since that is not reproducible by a reader. **Scope: one example now.** HW-2 and HW-3 are not commissioned by this entry. **Governs:** the HW-1 plan; the assembly-shape constraint on every subsequent example and on the published pages that describe operators. **Supersedes** `D-IMPL-125`'s substrate ruling for the first example. **Closes** the open question in `D-IMPL-131` by ruling the requirement rather than measuring it. Originates: Dave, 2026-08-14.

**D-IMPL-133 — Both parameterised doors carry client-supplied `parameters`; the recorded claim that this was blocked by the `D-IMPL-113` seam is wrong and is corrected here.** RESOLVED. Date: 2026-08-14. Approver: Dave. **Ruling, in Dave's words:** *"We will need to support parameters in mcp from the Client. That was a miss."* **What was shipped and why it is short.** Plan `16-23` gave the MCP egress door a parameterised tool taking the four §6.3 address axes — `subject`, `intent`, `freshness`, `asked_at` — and hard-codes `parameters={}` at `runtime/ccf_runtime/mcp/egress.py:588`. The HTTP door has carried the identical property since `D-IMPL-108`, at `runtime/ccf_runtime/http_api/routes/product.py:330`. Those are the **only two sites** in `runtime/` that construct a `ProductRequest` for a door. Consequence, measured at `753a21d` and recorded in the phase's `deferred-items.md`: a product class declaring **any required request parameter** is unreachable through either door — a `ProductRequest` carrying the four axes and `parameters={}` against the committed `examples/s1-01` contract is refused with `reason='missing-required-parameter' key='decision_instant'`. **The correction, and it matters because a false blocker was on the record.** That deferred entry states the obvious repair is blocked, on the reasoning that carrying a class's declared parameters *"requires validating them against the `RegisteredProductClass`, which the door never holds — it is resolved behind the resolver seam."* **That reasoning is wrong.** `src/ccf/products/request.py::refuse_request` — *"the one request-conformity comparison — address first, then parameters"* — carries its own docstring saying it is *"**Called by the deployment's own resolver, not by any door** (`D-IMPL-121`, Dave, 2026-08-12, extending `D-IMPL-113`)"*, and further records that an earlier revision of that same docstring claimed the opposite and was corrected because *"no shipped door calls this function, so the claim was false as written."* Verified independently: `refuse_request` appears in `runtime/` only in three docstring cross-references (`egress.py:220`, `:547`, `experience.py:240`) and is **called nowhere**. **A door therefore never needs the class, because a door never validates.** It needs only to carry the mapping through to the resolver, which holds the class and runs the comparison where it belongs. The repair is two lines, not a seam widening. The false claim is left standing in `deferred-items.md` only as a corrected entry, never silently deleted. **Ruled: both doors carry `parameters`.** The MCP tool and the HTTP route both accept a client-supplied mapping and pass it through unvalidated to the resolver. **Both**, not one: the phase already recorded that a tool wider than the HTTP door beside it would be the wrong asymmetry, and the same holds inverted. Validation stays exactly where `D-IMPL-121` put it. **Security posture, stated rather than assumed.** Client-supplied `parameters` reach selection through `selection_params` (`D-IMPL-49` reserved the slot), so they influence what is assembled — the same class of exposure as client-supplied `subject`, which `D-IMPL-128` limb 2 ruled a named residual on a measured zero-delta ground. The delta here is likewise bounded by the declared contract: a parameter the class does not declare is refused by `refuse_request` at the resolver, and a required one that is absent already is. What this does **not** do is scope parameters by entitlement, and `D-IMPL-128` limb 2's standing constraint extends to them unchanged — no plan may cite this ruling as evidence that per-parameter scoping is unnecessary. **What it unblocks.** `D-IMPL-132`'s HW-1 carries a client-supplied revenue threshold; `D-IMPL-127` requires HW-2 to reuse HW-1's product changing **only the client**. Without this ruling HW-2 was unbuildable over MCP, and the constraint would have forced HW-1's product class to declare no required parameters — shaping the first example around a door defect. **Governs:** `runtime/ccf_runtime/mcp/egress.py`; `runtime/ccf_runtime/http_api/routes/product.py`; the `deferred-items.md` entry it corrects. **Extends** `D-IMPL-126`; **corrects** the blocked-repair reasoning recorded against `D-IMPL-113`. Originates: Dave, 2026-08-14; verified against `41befe3`.

**D-IMPL-134 — `role` and `registration` become derivable for `ccf.*` classes only; `D-IMPL-80` and `D-IMPL-85` are narrowed, not revoked.** RESOLVED. Date: 2026-08-14. Approver: Dave. **Origin.** Dave read `examples/s1-01-hello-context-product/artifacts/` and asked *"Do I really require this many yaml files to construct my context product?"* Measuring HW-1's remainder answered it: of its 99 non-comment config lines, **59 are structural** — `manifest.yaml` (46), `backends.yaml` (10), `perspective.yaml` (3) — and only 40 are domain values. `manifest.yaml` alone is **46% of the config surface and is an index of itself**, a table restating what the filenames already say. **The obstacle, and why it fell.** `ManifestEntry.role` and `.registration` are today required with no default by explicit ruling — `D-IMPL-80` (2026-08-04) because `role: register` content-hashes an artifact into replay dimension 2, and `D-IMPL-85` (2026-08-05) because `registration` selects between `register_typed` and `register_versioned` with its D-05 bump check. Both exist to force a deliberate choice where a wrong one is silently expensive. **Ruled: they stay mandatory for every class the SDK does not own, and become derivable only for `ccf.*` classes.** The hazard both rulings guard is an adopter accidentally content-hashing event data into the audit plane — a hazard that arises precisely where the SDK cannot know the answer. For `ccf.half-life-table` there is exactly one correct value; requiring it is transcription, not deliberation. **The narrowing sharpens both rulings rather than weakening them:** mandatory exactly where a real choice exists, silent where none does. `key` and `class_id` become derived-with-override on the same principle and need no ruling — `key` from the section name or filename stem, `class_id` from the stem for SDK classes and from the document for adopter-owned ones. **A legibility consequence worth recording:** after the narrowing, `role` appears **only where it was chosen**. HW-1 has exactly one `role: load` entry — `request-contract` — today camouflaged by seven lines of `role: register` transcription. **Governs:** `src/ccf/controlplane/artifacts.py::ManifestEntry`; the single-file loader in `D-IMPL-136`. **Narrows** `D-IMPL-80` and `D-IMPL-85`; neither is revoked. Originates: the brainstorming session of 2026-08-14, recorded at `docs/superpowers/specs/2026-08-14-ccf-config-consolidation-design.md` §6.1.

**D-IMPL-135 — `ProductDiscovery` gains a fourth axis, `audiences`, and it carries the full surface obligation as its own entry.** RESOLVED. Date: 2026-08-14. Approver: Dave. **The question.** Dave asked whether `perspective` is *"a placeholder to let the context product discovery advertise who the context product is aimed to serve"*, planning a registration layer for CCF. It is not: `src/ccf/projection/plan.py:57` defines `Perspective` as *"the role-conditioning tuple Π interprets under"* — `principal_id` is who is asking and `role` in what capacity — derived at a door from the caller's grant (`runtime/ccf_runtime/mcp/egress.py:451-454`), with a principal's grant carrying a **set** of `permitted_roles` (`src/ccf/products/entitlement.py:136`). It conditions a view; it advertises nothing. **What already exists.** The §6.3 **Discovery** element ships as `src/ccf/products/contract.py:373::ProductDiscovery`, carrying three axes — `domain: str`, `intents: frozenset[str]`, `capabilities: frozenset[str]` — with every value *"a deployment-declared opaque identifier. The SDK matches them structurally and interprets none."* It hangs off `RegisteredProductClass` and defaults to `NOT_DISCOVERABLE` (`:569`). **Ruled: add `audiences: frozenset[str]`**, on the same footing — opaque, deployment-declared, uninterpreted. A product's intended audience (`analyst`, `investment_banker`) maps onto none of the existing three; calling a role an *intent* or a *capability* corrupts both meanings, and the two come apart the moment one role serves several intents. **This entry is deliberately separate from `D-IMPL-134` and must not be folded into it.** Adding a field to a shipped §6.3 element carries the full surface obligation: a `docs/surface-changes/` fragment claiming exactly the names that move (the readers glob `[0-9]*-*.md` since plan `16-22`, so a `16-NN-*.md` fragment is read); a `_SHAPE_MOVED_BY_FRAGMENT` claim per `D-IMPL-130`, since adding a field moves the class shape and `tests/test_surface_shape_capture.py` refuses an unclaimed move; and regeneration of `docs/SURFACE-CHANGES.md`, whose currency gate compares it on bytes. Recorded under a ruling about manifest bookkeeping, a later reader tracing why `ProductDiscovery` moved would find nothing. **Explicitly out of scope: the product-catalog door.** `ProductDiscovery` is the schema and `RegisteredProductClass` the registry entry, but **nothing in `runtime/` serves a product catalog** — the SDK ships an *agent* discovery tool (`runtime/ccf_runtime/mcp/experience.py:218`) and a *skill* catalog (`runtime/ccf_runtime/http_api/routes/skill.py`), and no product one, which is the single thing §6.3 puts a registry in front of.
