Dave deferred it: *"Not yet on product catalog."* When it opens, a catalog filtered by the caller's grant is a different object per principal — the same shape as `entitled_perspective`, and adjacent to `D-IMPL-128` limb 2's open residual on per-principal scoping. **Governs:** `src/ccf/products/contract.py::ProductDiscovery`; the `product_class.discovery` section in `D-IMPL-136`. Originates: Dave, 2026-08-14; design spec §8.2 and §14.2.

**D-IMPL-136 — A single-file deployment authoring form: the SDK owns reserved section names, the adopter owns their schemas, and the index is deleted rather than shrunk.** RESOLVED. Date: 2026-08-14. Approver: Dave. **The form.** One `ccf_config.yaml` (or `deployment.yaml`) at the deployment root, with sections named in adopter language — `product_class`, `operators`, `request_contract`, `resolution`, `half_life`, `debt_profile`, `policy`, and optional `default_perspective`. **The section name *is* the class**, so `path`, `key`, `class_id`, `role` and `registration` stop existing rather than becoming optional. Unknown top-level keys are an error. Target: roughly **30 lines against HW-1's current 99**, every line a decision the adopter made. **Why the index is deleted and not merged.** Today `resolution-spec.yaml` is a resolution spec because the manifest *says so* — the file, the key and the class are three strings that must agree, and an index exists to reconcile them. Removing the second name removes the need for the index, and a typo becomes *"I don't recognise the section `resolutoin`"* rather than a refusal about a missing key three layers down. **Ruled: reserved-section semantics.** The **SDK owns the slot name**; the **adopter owns the schema and contents**. CCF recognises `policy:` and `request_contract:` by name — it knows where to find them and which artifact each becomes — and does **not** validate or interpret what is inside. This is compatible with `D-IMPL-84` in its own terms: the SDK still claims exactly one prefix, `ccf.`, and **a reserved section name is not a claim on an adopter's namespace**. **Replay is unaffected, and this was measured before the form was accepted.** The obvious objection — that merging artifacts collapses their version identities and breaks `D-IMPL-26` — fails against the tree. `src/ccf/deployment/pins.py:66-68` records that `ArtifactPin.version` **is a content hash**, *"so pin identity *is* content identity"*; `src/ccf/contracts/canonical.py:194` addresses the **parsed object**; `src/ccf/deployment/manifest.py:617` content-addresses the parsed contract, not the file bytes. Sections parsed from one file therefore yield **byte-identical content addresses**. `register_typed` carries no D-05 bump check (`src/ccf/controlplane/registry.py:128-135`), so a single top-level `semver` is a coherent label for the deployment's config while per-artifact identity remains the content hash. **The file boundary is a loading convention, not an addressing requirement.** **Three findings resolved in the design rather than left to implementation.** (1) `ManifestEntry.path` is validated even when derived — two validators reject globs and paths leaving the manifest's directory (`src/ccf/controlplane/artifacts.py:983`, `:995`) — so every derived entry's `path` is the literal `ccf_config.yaml`, never a synthetic placeholder. Uniqueness is on **`key` alone**: `_keys_are_unique` (`:1195`) records that *"duplicate **paths** under different keys are permitted, deliberately … The asymmetry is a decision, not an oversight"*, and the single-file form is exactly the case that decision anticipated. A proposed `(path, key)` uniqueness rule was **considered and not taken** — trivially true whenever keys are unique, and it would imply paths must vary. (2) `product_class.required_operator_keys` and the `operators:` keys enumerate the same set twice, which is the condition `refuse_operator_set_divergence` guards and the reason HW-1 ships no `projection-requirements.yaml`; the loader **refuses at parse time** when they disagree, naming both sites, rather than picking one as authoritative. **The general lesson, recorded because it will recur: consolidating *files* does not consolidate *claims*, and a guard justified by "there is only one statement here" needs re-checking whenever the authoring surface changes shape.** (3) The audience advertisement had no home; `D-IMPL-135` gives it one. **Backends stay out.** `backends.yaml` remains a separate document: connection material is deployment environment, not Control Plane state, and a DSN inside a content-hashed artifact is a DSN persisted into the audit plane forever. **Compatibility is absolute:** existing multi-file deployments work unchanged, the single-file form is an *additional authoring surface* rather than a replacement for the typed artifact model, and gates, replay and registration see the same typed objects they see today. **The adoption policy, ruled separately from the format and stated because "compatible" does not by itself say what anyone should do** (Dave, 2026-08-14): *"Let's support a dual model — we [build] everything new [on] the new implementation design. We will convert all examples to the new implementation as we start to build them out. This is new examples only."* So: **both forms are supported indefinitely**, not as a migration window; **new examples are authored single-file**; and **existing examples convert only when they are next rebuilt for their own reasons.** There is **no bulk migration and none is planned** — not for `examples/00`..`08`, not for `s1-01`, not for `ext-00` or `s2-01`, and not for the crossproc children or test fixtures that author manifests today. **Consequence for the implementing plan, and it improves the proof:** the equivalence between the two forms is demonstrated by a **test fixture** expressing HW-1's deployment single-file, asserted to yield identical registered artifacts and identical content addresses — **not** by converting HW-1, which stays committed in the multi-file form. The tree therefore carries a live, running example of *each* shape, which is what a dual model should look like, and a fixture pinned to its test cannot drift the way an example can. **Governs:** the manifest loader; `ccf_config.yaml` as an authoring surface; the reserved section names; the adoption policy above. **Depends on** `D-IMPL-134` (the narrowing) and `D-IMPL-135` (`audiences`). **Compatible with** `D-IMPL-84`, stated rather than assumed. Originates: Dave, 2026-08-14; full design at `docs/superpowers/specs/2026-08-14-ccf-config-consolidation-design.md`.

**D-IMPL-137 — The `refuse_operator_set_divergence` demand is conditioned on the registration of the second document; a one-document deployment is a legitimate shape. `D-IMPL-86` is narrowed, not revoked.** RESOLVED. Date: 2026-08-14. Approver: Dave. **Origin.** HW-1 (`examples/s1-00-companies-over-revenue`) authors ONE operator-set document — its plan's `required=` reads the product class's own `required_operator_keys` — and `tests/products/test_gate_two_call_shape.py` had **no compliant state for it**: the coverage assertion demanded the binder call, and had HW-1 made it, `_binder_collapses` property (1) would have refused the same call as a tautology, because `declared=scenario.product_class` is a prefix of the plan's source path. The only exit property (1)'s own message names — *"Build the plan's `required=` from the projection's OWN document"* — is not a code edit but **authoring a second artifact**. The demand was therefore not *call the binder*; it was *adopt the two-document design*, which is a design mandate wearing a call-site check's clothing and is the rigidity `D-IMPL-132` forbade for operators. **Ruled: the demand fires only where its premise holds.** The premise is that two independently authored operator-set documents exist, which is a fact about the **Control Plane** and is read there — any manifest in the deployment's artifact tree declaring `class_id: ccf.projection-requirements` (any manifest, because `examples/04-cross-process` declares it in `producer-manifest.yaml`). Measured across the tree: every deployment with a projecting body registers it **except HW-1**. **The detector is a conjunction of two independent readings, and every other combination is refused.** Registration absent AND the plan's `required=` sharing a source with the class claim ⇒ **one document, demand does not fire**. Registration present AND sharing ⇒ refused by property (1), unchanged. Registration absent AND **not** sharing ⇒ **refused** — a second operator set was authored somewhere the Control Plane does not know about, and the two readings disagreeing is itself the finding. **The exemption is fail-closed in three places**: no artifact tree, no readable class claim, and no readable plan source all mean the demand applies. The first of the three has its own control, `test_a_body_with_no_artifact_tree_is_subject_to_the_demand`, whose synthesized body *does* assemble a `ProductLineage` and whose plan's `required=` resolves to the identical path — the AST agreement that would otherwise grant the exemption — and which asserts `_one_document` refuses it regardless; the hazard it closes is an adopter tree **outside** `examples/` that assembles a lineage and reads one source, and would otherwise buy an exemption by having no Control Plane at all. It is *not* what keeps `test_the_binder_tripwire_detects_a_projection_that_skips_it` armed — that control calls `_names_the_binder`, `_is_plan_construction` and `_is_plan_execution` directly, passes no path, and never reaches `_one_document`, so the conditioning cannot silence it; nor is it **needed** to hold `test_the_binder_argument_checks_reject_a_binder_that_cannot_fail`'s bodies demanded. Those bodies are checked against `REPO_ROOT / "probe.py"`, which is outside `examples/`, and **both** fail-closed guards are independently sufficient for them: `_deployment_root` answers `None`, and none of them assembles a lineage so `_class_claim` answers `None` too. **The first guard is the one that executes** — the two are spelled `root is None or _registers_requirements(root)`, so the `or` short-circuits and the class claim is never read for these bodies at all. That redundancy is precisely why removing the first guard alone changes **not one** of their verdicts (measured body by body), which is the fact that retired the original attribution. Both of those attributions appeared in this entry as first written on 2026-08-14 and are retracted here rather than deleted. **Where the class claim is read in a body that never calls the binder**: `ProductLineage(operators_invoked=…)`, already the module's anchor for the product's side of Gate 2; a body assembling no lineage — the two replay environments' `resolve_plan` methods — is subject to the demand, and both sit in two-document deployments so nothing about them moves. **Properties (2) and (3) are conditioned together, property (1) is not.** Property (2) is spelled `any(call.handed == execution.plan … for call in calls)` and with zero binder calls `any([])` is `False`, so leaving it unconditional would fire on every execution; property (1) staying unconditional is what makes this an absence of demand rather than a licence, since a one-document body that calls the binder anyway is still refused for the tautology. **The exemption is ceilinged at one** (`MAXIMUM_ONE_DOCUMENT_DEPLOYMENTS`), no headroom, carrying the module's existing `FLOOR_REMOVAL_INSTRUCTION`: the floors stop coverage shrinking silently and this stops the exemption spreading silently, which is the same instrument inverted. **What this does NOT do.** It does not narrow the authorship residual `R-3`, which `D-IMPL-86` establishes as AST-undetectable in principle and which remains open and recorded in `src/ccf/deployment/binder.py`'s module docstring. It does not touch Gate 2 proper: the L6 comparison of `lineage.operators_invoked` against `engine_resolved` still does real work in a one-document deployment, because those two sides are the declaration and the engine's resolution — two sources whatever the upstream document count is, and HW-1 is green on that tripwire today. **Governs:** `tests/products/test_gate_two_call_shape.py`. **Narrows** `D-IMPL-86` (and its correction of 2026-08-05); neither is revoked. **Consistent with** `D-IMPL-132`. Originates: the brainstorming session of 2026-08-14, full design at `docs/superpowers/specs/2026-08-14-property-not-shape-gating-design.md` §4.

**D-IMPL-136 — AMENDMENT, 2026-08-15: `ccf_config.yaml` becomes the sole *documented* authoring surface, and HW-1 converts when the loader lands.** RESOLVED. Date: 2026-08-15. Approver: Dave. **Why this is an amendment and not a reversal.** `D-IMPL-136` ruled a dual model: both forms supported indefinitely, new examples authored single-file, existing examples converting only when next rebuilt, and HW-1 (`examples/s1-00-companies-over-revenue`) staying committed in the multi-file form so the tree carries a live running example of each shape. Two facts appeared after that ruling, neither available at the time because HW-1 had not yet shipped. **First, the cost actually being paid is teaching surface, not loader code.** A loader that exists and passes its tests costs nearly nothing to keep; what costs is every document, every example and every adopter conversation forking two ways — which is the friction `D-IMPL-131` was ruled against in the first place. **Second, HW-1 is the first example a newcomer opens** (`D-IMPL-131`: *"a genuinely basic hello world comes first"*), and leaving the first example as the most verbose one in the suite inverts the adoption story the examples exist to tell. **The amendment, piece by piece.** (1) **Documented authoring surface** — was: multi-file and single-file co-equal; now: **`ccf_config.yaml` is the only documented authoring surface**, and multi-file is documented once, as an advanced/escape hatch. (2) **The multi-file loader** — retained **indefinitely**, unchanged: compatibility is absolute, design spec §6.2 already names it as the documented per-section escape for a reserved-key collision, and the marginal cost of keeping working, tested code is near zero. (3) **HW-1** — was: stays multi-file; now: **converts to single-file when the loader lands.** (4) **The equivalence proof** — unchanged: the hand-authored fixture remains the better proof, on `D-IMPL-136`'s own reasoning that *"a fixture pinned to its test cannot drift the way an example can"*, so converting HW-1 costs nothing on that axis. (5) **Every other existing example** — unchanged: converts only when next rebuilt for its own reasons; **there is still no bulk migration and none is planned.** **What this does not do.** It does not remove the multi-file loader, does not break any existing adopter, does not touch the typed artifact model that the single-file form is a *loading convention over*, and does not revisit §9's backends-stay-out boundary. **Governs:** which authoring form the documentation teaches; HW-1's config form once `16-30` lands. **Amends** `D-IMPL-136` and nothing else. **Consistent with** `D-IMPL-131` and `D-IMPL-134`. Originates: Dave, 2026-08-15.

**D-IMPL-138 — The escalations from plans `16-27`…`16-30`, ruled together so execution can start.** RESOLVED. Date: 2026-08-15. Approver: Dave. **Origin.** The plan set implementing `D-IMPL-136`'s single-file form escalated four questions and answered none of them, which was correct — three touch approved prose and one touches a gate's coverage. A fifth, carried in `16-30`'s consolidated list (six unanchored citations inside the design document the plan set may not edit), is **already closed**: `tests/invariants/test_citation_coverage.py` passes at HEAD, resolved by commit `868aa4e` after the plans were written. It is dropped rather than ruled. **(a) Adopter-owned sections carry three bookkeeping lines, not two.** Design spec §6.1 states two; the plans found three are needed — `class_id`, `role`, `registration` — because deriving an adopter's `class_id` would require the SDK to invent an adopter namespace, which `D-IMPL-84` forbids by reserving exactly one prefix (`ccf.`). `D-IMPL-134` already made `role` and `registration` derivable **for `ccf.*` classes only**, so for adopter-owned classes they are mandatory *by that ruling*. **Ruled: accept three, and correct §6.1's prose from two to three.** This is a divergence from approved prose, not from any approved decision, and the more explicit statement is the correct one. Not load-bearing. **(b) Four fields beyond design spec §4.1.** Measured against the shipped model: `src/ccf/controlplane/artifacts.py::DeploymentManifest` carries exactly `semver`, `artifacts`, `backend`, `backend_secrets`, `resolution_spec_key`, `policy_bundle_key`, `tenant_id`, `survivor_cap` — so the four disputed fields **are the manifest's own fields**. This is field parity, not scope creep; §4.1's list was under-specified. **Ruled: accept all four**, on two distinct grounds. `resolution_spec_key` and `policy_bundle_key` are **required** on `DeploymentManifest` with no default, and HW-1's own manifest sets both, so without them the single-file form cannot construct a valid manifest at all and `16-30`'s HW-1 equivalence fixture is **unwritable** — not a judgement call. `backend_secrets` and `survivor_cap` are defaulted and unused by HW-1, so they are **not** load-bearing for that fixture, but they are load-bearing for §10's *"compatibility is absolute"*: a deployment whose adapter takes a credential, or which has configured a D-10 survivor cap, could not otherwise be expressed in the new form, and §10 would be false for every credentialed adapter. Both carry **names, never values** (`SecretReference`), so §9's backends-stay-out boundary is untouched. **(c) Whether `D-IMPL-135`'s `_SHAPE_MOVED_BY_FRAGMENT` obligation binds a `contract`-kind entry.** **Ruled: keep the plan's measure-then-decide; do not pre-decide either way.** The plan's premise — that the shape test skips every entry whose kind is not `class` or `function`, so `ProductDiscovery`'s `"kind": "contract"` entry never fires — is **doubtful**: `tests/test_surface_shape_capture.py::_legacy_shape_of` explicitly handles `kind == "contract"`. The arbiter is `pytest -k surface` at execution. **The SUMMARY must name which gates actually caught the move**, whichever way the measurement goes; if the obligation does not bind, that is a finding about `D-IMPL-135`'s stated mechanism failing to cover contract-kind entries, not a licence to add nothing and move on. **(d) `depends_on` for `16-14`…`16-19` was never rewired.** Three wave collisions are real and measured — `16-28`/`16-14` at wave 7, `16-29`/`16-15` at wave 8, `16-30`/`16-16` at wave 9 — with none of the six depending on any of the four. The six are documentation plans, and at least two sit squarely on the config surface: `16-14` writes `public/tutorial/hello-ccf.md`, and `16-18` rebuilds `examples/00-quickstart/`, which under this date's `D-IMPL-136` amendment converts to single-file when rebuilt. **Ruled: rewire so the chain follows `16-30`.** The six form a **linear chain** — `16-14` → `16-15` → `16-16` → `16-17` → `16-18` → `16-19` — so adding `16-30` to **`16-14`'s `depends_on` alone** pushes all six, and no other edit is required. **Governs:** plans `16-27` through `16-30`; the wave graph for `16-14` through `16-19`; design spec §6.1's line count. **Depends on** `D-IMPL-136` and its 2026-08-15 amendment. Originates: the four plans' own escalations, assessed against the tree on 2026-08-15.

**D-IMPL-139 — HW-1's conversion is accepted at 48 authored lines, and `D-IMPL-137`'s deployment-root detector is extended to the single-file shape without weakening it.** RESOLVED. Date: 2026-08-15. Approver: Dave. **Two consequences of plan `16-31` landing, ruled narrowly and together because each follows from the conversion and neither reopens the design.** **(a) The line count: 48, against the design spec's stated target of "roughly 30 lines against HW-1's current 99". Accepted as measured, not compressed to fit.** The gap is accounted for line by line rather than absorbed: **six** lines are `D-IMPL-138`(a)'s three-line bookkeeping rule applied to the two adopter-owned sections (`request_contract`, `policy`), **two** are `request_contract`'s schema fields, and **ten** are header and nesting compounding. The ~30 target predates `D-IMPL-138`(a) and the two required pointer fields, so it was unreachable once those were ruled; the alternative — moving content out of the file to hit the number — would have hidden the cost rather than paid it, and was explicitly forbidden by the plan. **The measurement that matters is unchanged and is the one the original complaint was about: 10 authored config files become 2, and 99 non-comment lines become 48, with `manifest.yaml`'s 46-line index of itself deleted outright.** **The conversion is proved faithful by the content address, which did not move**: `5cfee793e5fb686a82becac08cddf2ca187c3ef9ae26bd9a8fd41030656a6d36` before and after, matching the value `README.md` already published; pin identity *is* content identity (`src/ccf/deployment/pins.py::ArtifactPin.version`), so a moved address would have meant the conversion changed the deployment. `make ci-core` **3305 passed / 0 failed**, matching BASE exactly. **(b) `D-IMPL-137`'s `_deployment_root` now recognises two Control Plane shapes, and the extension is additive.** That helper located a deployment's Control Plane tree by the existence of an `artifacts/` subdirectory. Deleting HW-1's `artifacts/` tree broke it, failing six tests that assert HW-1's one-document exemption. It now also recognises a `ccf_config.yaml` at the deployment root — the second shape `D-IMPL-136`'s dual model permits — and **scans that document for a `ccf.projection-requirements` registration rather than assuming its absence**, on the same discipline every other reading in that module follows. (It can never carry one: `ccf.projection-requirements` is not among `D-IMPL-136`'s reserved section names, which is a decision rather than a gap — but the module reads rather than assumes, and that property is the point.) **Nothing about `D-IMPL-137` is weakened, and this was verified rather than argued**: the exempt-deployment list is unchanged at exactly `['examples/s1-00-companies-over-revenue']`; `MAXIMUM_ONE_DOCUMENT_DEPLOYMENTS` remains **1** with no headroom; `_deployment_root` still answers `None` for a path with no Control Plane surface, so the fail-closed default that keeps `test_the_binder_argument_checks_reject_a_binder_that_cannot_fail` armed is intact; and the two-reading conjunction still decides every row. 77 tests pass in that module. **What this entry does NOT do.** It does not revisit the `D-IMPL-136` amendment, does not convert any other example, does not touch the multi-file loader, and does not narrow the authorship residual `R-3`. **Two plan defects found by the executor are recorded in `16-31-SUMMARY.md` rather than here, because they are plan errors and not design decisions:** the plan asserted `tenant_id` absent from HW-1's manifest when `tenant_id: hw1-demo` was present at line 21, and it forbade touching `produce.py` when `produce.py::assemble_and_serve` holds the boot call that had to move to `load_config_and_boot`. Both were disputed with the measurement and fixed minimally. **Governs:** `examples/s1-00-companies-over-revenue/ccf_config.yaml`; `tests/products/test_gate_two_call_shape.py::_deployment_root`. **Extends** `D-IMPL-137`; **depends on** the `D-IMPL-136` amendment and `D-IMPL-138`(a). Originates: plan `16-31`'s blocking checkpoint, approved by Dave on 2026-08-15.

**D-GATE-15.6 — Phase 15.6 approved.** Date: 2026-08-13. Approver: Dave. **13 of 13 plans complete** (12 planned, plus `15.6-13` created mid-phase to implement `D-IMPL-124`), across 10 waves, every wave merged with a green post-merge `make ci`. `make ci` **exit 0** at the gate HEAD, observed by the orchestrator rather than relayed by an agent: **3086 passed / 81 skipped**, `mypy --strict` clean, `python -m tools.lints --all` exit 0, eleven examples green, `docs-strict` green with its **four** steps including the `docs/schemas/` scan root this phase added. The product content address moved **four** times, not the three budgeted, each move regenerated by *running* the example: `374f9f4d…` → `06e454f4…` → `36ebd759…` → `7d0a3d60…` → `75365e18…` (final). **Seven rulings** — `D-IMPL-118` through `D-IMPL-124`, each recorded with the measured cost it was ruled against: `-118` `qa-toplevel` (the placement shape priced across all three Track B vessel members, not per field); `-119` `qb2-scoped` in four limbs (the delta between the entitled and the assembled set measured **empty** on the shipped path — there is no perspective-scoped view to derive a narrower pointer from); `-120` `qc-optional` (optional costs 3 code files and 0 artifacts; required costs 6 code files plus all 3 YAML artifacts, each of which raises); `-121` `q1-extend` (re-measured at `4b407e0`: one invocation each outside their own tests, zero in `runtime/`); `-122` `q2a-reconcile` + `__call__`-only + `q2b-in` (12 of 51 Protocols, 11 new reshaped diff lines, 18 aliases / 20 entries, 39 multi-method Protocols descoped on a measured cost); `-123` the measured **fourth** shape, 101 of 114 unanchored `.py`-target citations — an option that did not exist in the plan's list because the measurement produced it; `-124` Shape B, the source identities off the vessel and the inference channel closed rather than accepted as residual. **The close-time protocol ran in the CLAUDE.md §3.1 order**: execute → cross-AI panel `--all` against **shipped code** → single-agent review → verify every finding against the tree → fix every blocking finding in-phase → present. **Three rounds of findings, not two.** The panel (4 reviewers, `claude` excluded as the executing runtime) returned 4 blocking, 5 advisory, 1 false; the single-agent pass — which can execute code — returned 1 blocker, 1 critical, 5 warnings and found what the panel structurally could not, that the panel's own fixes had closed an *instance* rather than the class; and **the observed gate itself found five citation defects in the gate package**, written by the orchestrator after an agent had truthfully reported green. **Blocking findings found and fixed inside the phase, 11 commits, each proved by a test that failed before and passed after:** panel B2 blank evidence handle (`63e276e`), B3 `obligation_in_force` unvalidated on the vessel (`0d66619`), B1 the subject sentinel namespaced and `ccf.` reserved (`3f410af`), B4 the anti-vocabulary guard widened to tuple-shaped tables (`ad2bd57`); then review BL-01, the guard still blind to a **dict** — *the* shape a signal→obligation mapping is written in — closed by enumerating the language's container forms **once**, exhaustively, as the parameter list of the refusal test (`7a37b93`, `tests/invariants/test_no_obligation_vocabulary.py::CONTAINER_FORMS`, 24 forms, zero exemptions, independently re-verified one injection per form with `git diff` clean after each); CR-01 an instant that named no zone and a bare float admitted into the content address (`23c9dc0`); and WR-01 through WR-05 (`ae59168`, `e26c41c`, `4e82254`, `872869e`, and the scoping correction `ddec631`). **One finding rejected as false, with its evidence:** Gemini's *“`_refuse_float` is undefined; instantiation raises `NameError`”* — the helper is `src/ccf/halflife/assessment.py::_refuse_float`, defined and used four times; the reviewer could not see it because the orchestrator truncated the source diff at 1400 lines, which is recorded as **the orchestrator's error, not the reviewer's**. This project has now produced fabricated findings in three separate reviews, and the rejection is on the record with what refuted it. **PERF-04 re-baseline event (D-IMPL-70 ritual step 5).** `perf/baselines/local.json` was **byte-identical across the entire phase** and was re-baselined **only at this gate, on Dave's explicit approval of 2026-08-13** — *“Both medians are now substantially faster than the approved baselines… Leaving them produces permanent speed-up WARNs”* and *“No unauthorized change occurred — the file is still byte-identical to the prior baseline. This is a deliberate, observed update.”* Exactly two ids changed, from four consecutive quiet-machine `make perf-micro CCF_PERF_RUNNER_CLASS=local` runs at commit `69dd415`: `suites/micro/test_operators.py::test_composed_chain` 0.368903 → 0.184344 s and `suites/micro/test_operators.py::test_composed_ab` 0.230928 → 0.079929 s; a fifth verification run after the edit was **13/13 PASS with zero WARNs**. Every other median is the 2026-08-03 approved value, and no threshold override was compensated away — the single `[override]` in `perf/thresholds.toml` names the dispatch benchmark (`DI-15.5-01`) and neither re-baselined id. The rationale uses the **corrected** retention account per Dave: the deferral in `src/ccf/operators/planner.py::IntermediateCache` costs **one additional `GraphView` per deferred operator**, not “views retained where none were before” — `OperatorOutput.view` is a `GraphView`, so the eager scheme already retained one; the panel's advisory overstated it and the single-agent pass corrected it. **Dave's four rulings at the gate, beyond the re-baseline:** (1) `DI-15.6-01`(a), the four inherited `tools/` failures — *“Accept as inherited (verified identical at `9905e8f`). They predate the phase and were not caused by it. Record them as known residual; do not treat them as 15.6 defects”*; (2) `DI-15.6-01`(b), the governance question underneath them, which is *“the real issue”* — *“Treat the disagreement as a real coverage hole, not a false positive on either side”*, with the required follow-up to **either** extend the D-20 / `docs-strict` surface to the schema-description channel **or** make `tools/publish_scan_tests/test_docstring_leaks.py::test_no_docstring_reproduces_whitepaper_body_text` part of the required CI surface, and with the enabling mechanism **named explicitly** as he required: `testpaths = ["tests"]` in `pyproject.toml` keeps the whole `tools/` package out of `make ci-core`; (3) the retired spike's loose end — record the break, *“Leave the evidence document untouched”*, with his dated note carried verbatim in `docs/15.6-GATE-PACKAGE.md` § 5.3 and in the phase's `deferred-items.md`, and his framing that *“retirement converts ‘here is how to reproduce this’ into ‘trust the recorded figure.’ That conversion is now explicit rather than silent”*; and (4) `D-IMPL-99`'s two remaining rotted anchors — *“Do not edit the shipped ruling. Carry the two rotted anchors forward as known residual under `D-IMPL-99`”*. The five recorded dispositions were **accepted as stated** (*“No ruling sought; no objection”*), and the three advisory calls were all ruled: no tightening of Shape B's zero-span residual this phase (*“a measurement edge case, not a new oracle”*), no narrowing of the `ccf.` reservation this phase (*“a ruling with address-move consequences, not a repair”* — a future item must budget the address moves), and the corrected deferred-cache retention delta accepted with no remeasurement for gate close. **Accepted as known-stale or known-blind, explicitly rather than by silence:** the four inherited `tools/` failures, re-observed at the gate HEAD as **4 failed / 540 passed** with unchanged shingle counts; the two scanners that disagree about the same tree, because `tools/dump_schemas.py::emit` copies docstrings verbatim into schema `description` fields and only one of the two scanners blocks; `D-IMPL-99`'s two rotted anchors; `DI-15.5-06` **narrowed, not closed** — 39 multi-method Protocols remain information-free, including all seven `D-IMPL-110` names, and the manifest must **not** be cited as evidence of surface stability for them; `ContextProductContract.debt_evidence` shipping as a paid-for carrier holding a sentinel and no pointers, which is `D-IMPL-118` and `D-IMPL-119` limb 3(b) working as intended together; the citation population left outside `D-IMPL-123`'s ruled scope; and a third micro benchmark, `suites/micro/test_factory.py::test_task_factory_construct`, observed straddling its 10% band across four quiet runs and deliberately **not** re-baselined because the approval named two ids. **A positive result worth keeping, at Dave's request:** `src/ccf/halflife/assessment.py` and `src/ccf/halflife/debt.py` are the two modules Track B rewrote most heavily across four waves, adding many docstrings — and their whitepaper-shingle counts (15 and 14) did not move at all between `9905e8f` and the gate HEAD. **Carried forward, every entry with a named owner** (`.planning/phases/15.6-decision-completeness-and-defect-closure/deferred-items.md`): `DI-15.6-01`(a) and (b) and `DI-15.6-02` and panel advisory A5 to **Phase 16**; `DI-15.6-03` closed by its own recording; `DI-15.6-04` under `D-IMPL-99`; `DI-15.6-05` unassigned by ruling until a concrete harm appears; `DI-15.6-06` to the next PERF-04 event, which is Dave's under `D-IMPL-70`. **What Phase 16 must re-read before it resumes.** Phase 16 is parked at 6 of 21 and was already invalidated once by exactly this. This phase reshaped `src/ccf/halflife/decay.py::Observation`, `src/ccf/halflife/assessment.py::DebtAssessment` and the debt profile, added three top-level members to `src/ccf/products/contract.py::ContextProductContract`, and moved the product content address four times. Counting references to the reshaped surfaces across the fifteen unexecuted plans, the ones that **must be re-read** are **`16-07`** (8 references — it asserts byte-identical served products across substrates), **`16-10`** (8 — in-process then cross-process embedding), **`16-09`** (5 — external MCP consumption over all three harnesses), **`16-17`** (4 — publishes the embedding half), **`16-11`** (2) and **`16-08`** (the λ_c / D_c teaching plan, which now has three carried members and an obligation to teach); `16-14` and `16-18` carry one reference each and want a check rather than a re-read. **The re-read is a precondition of resuming Phase 16, not a courtesy** — an example that encodes a stale content address regenerates the wrong artifact and the failure surfaces as a diff nobody expected. Originates: CLAUDE.md §3.1; plan `15.6-12` tasks 3 and 4; `docs/15.6-GATE-PACKAGE.md`; `.planning/phases/15.6-decision-completeness-and-defect-closure/15.6-CLOSE-REVIEWS.md` and `15.6-REVIEW.md`; `D-IMPL-70` (the PERF-04 re-baseline ritual); `D-IMPL-116` and `D-IMPL-117`, which commissioned the phase.
