**D-IMPL-140 — The `D-IMPL-123` held-back citation population grew by one legitimate landed fragment; the pin moves 13 → 14 and nothing else changes.** RESOLVED. Date: 2026-08-15. Approver: Dave. **What was measured, and why it was invisible until now.** `tests/invariants/test_citation_coverage.py::test_the_held_back_thirteen_are_out_of_scope_by_ruling_not_by_accident` pins the size of the held-back population — citations inside landed plan fragments under `docs/surface-changes/`, plus the assembled `docs/SURFACE-CHANGES.md` — so that a later edit quietly pulling a host into scope, or quietly dropping one out of it, is visible. The pin stood at 13: eleven citations across five landed fragments plus two in the assembled artifact. The population is now **14**, and the additional host is `docs/surface-changes/16-24-experience-door-entitlement.md` — a Phase 16 fragment that landed legitimately and joined a globbed pattern (`docs/surface-changes/*`) whose count was never updated to match. **This is bookkeeping lag, not a design change.** No fragment was edited, no host left scope, and the ceiling's subject did not shrink; a sixth fragment simply exists where five did before. **Why the pin could not be moved without this entry.** The assertion's own failure text states that a legitimately edited or added fragment *"is a ruling-scope change and belongs in a decision entry, not in this constant"* — precisely because a deliberate addition and an accidental regression require the identical edit, so nothing at the failure site distinguishes them and `git log` plus this entry become the only record that does. Three executors and the orchestrator each declined to move it for that reason, which is the intended behaviour. **The two rejected alternatives, and why.** Converting the fragment's citations to `path.py::Symbol` form would edit a *landed* fragment, which `D-IMPL-114` forbids in the same words the held-back exclusion exists to enforce — a later editor must not silently revise a landed fragment to chase a line number. Introducing a new exclusion category for landed fragments would add mechanism to paper over a count that simply needs to reflect reality. Dave's ruling: *"That is a bookkeeping lag, not a design change… both add mechanism to paper over a count that simply needs to reflect reality."* **What changed:** the pinned count 13 → 14 and the two docstring figures that quote it, in one commit naming the fragment. `HELD_BACK_HOSTS` is untouched, `MAXIMUM_UNANCHORED_PY_CITATIONS` is untouched, and no exclusion rule was added. **Context that makes this entry load-bearing rather than clerical:** this failure was hidden for a full execution wave behind a *vacuous* pass — `tools/lints/citations.py::SKIP_DIRS` omitted `.claude`, so six live agent worktrees made every basename resolve to seven paths, and an ambiguous basename is dropped from the sweep rather than checked. The gate reported green in the main checkout while three independent executors correctly measured it red in theirs. `.claude` is now skipped (same session), which is what made this population visible to be ruled on at all. See `DEF-17-04` in Phase 17's `deferred-items.md`.

**D-IMPL-141 — `binding.yaml` is a DATA document, with exactly one named exception: the projection.** RESOLVED. Date: 2026-08-16. Approver: Dave. **Supersedes the entry that stood here**, which proposed `resolver:` and `question:` as `<module-path>:<attribute>` factories the loader imports and calls. **That shape was rejected.** It reintroduces producer Python into `deployment/` — precisely the option declined at the 2026-08-15 brainstorm, whose chosen alternative was *"the SDK ships one generic resolver — the deployment folder stays pure configuration, no Python in it, nothing to discover"* and whose declined alternative carried the recorded cost *"a reader opening that folder sees a hook they think they must understand."* The ruling of record is `docs/superpowers/specs/2026-08-16-reference-plan-factory-and-debt-scorer.md`, Amendment A. **What measurement settled, and what it dissolved.** The proposal's tension was real but only half of it survived contact with the tree. `~ccf.projection.plan.Perspective` is **two plain strings** (`principal_id`, `role`); `~ccf.products.request.ProductRequest` carries `subject`, `intent` and `freshness` as **plain `str`**; `schema_name`/`schema_version` come off the request's own address; `asked_at` is a `datetime` the SDK already owns through `as_of` (`D-04`). **Nothing in the request path requires computation**, so `question:` and `perspective:` never needed factories — they are declared as data, and a factory producing them would have been a factory producing constants. `~ccf.halflife.debt` already ships the debt machinery and `17-11` shipped `~ccf.products.debt_scorer.AssemblyDebtScorer`, the `GraphView` → `DebtAssessment` adapter, so the debt half needed no deployment code either. **The one gap that survived, and why it is principled rather than unfinished (Amendment A.2).** `~ccf.projection.plan.ProjectionPlan` is **itself a Protocol** — `required_operators()` plus `invoke()`, code and not data — and **there is no declarative plan artifact anywhere in the tree**. A product class declares a `payload_schema_key`, so the payload's *shape* is declared, but the **mapping from operator outputs to that shape is code and nothing declares it**. A reference factory would have to invent that mapping: deciding which entities become which field, and what a count counts, is **invention, not mapping** — the No-Hardcoding violation `~ccf.products.resolver.ProjectionPlanFactoryProtocol`'s own docstring warns about, and the docstring is right. Dave's ruling, in substance: *"§11 does not require the impossible. It requires a reference implementation when one can be pure mechanism. Here it cannot."* **The withdrawn alternative, recorded so it is not re-proposed.** A *pass-through* reference plan was put forward during the same exchange and **withdrawn**: it would have had to invent the identical mapping, one layer less visibly. A reference implementation that hides an invention is worse than an absence that names one. **The resolved shape.** `binding.yaml` survives as a **data** document at the deployment root, stating: `config` (the `ccf_config.yaml` path); `question` (`subject`, `intent`, `freshness`, `asked_at`); `perspective` (`principal_id`, `role`); the resolver's plain-data arguments (`product_class_key`, `request_contract_key`, `operator_parameter_keys`, `policy_bundle_key`, `replay_record_class`, `query_pin_class`, `max_ttl_seconds`, `product_ttl_seconds`); `artifact_store`; `debt` (the λ_c and `D_c`-profile Control Plane keys, plus the `C` and `S` this deployment **declares** because the SDK ships no reference measurement for either); and an optional `connection`. The SDK builds `~ccf.products.resolver.GovernedResolver` from that data. **`plan` is the single permitted `<module-path>:<attribute>` reference**, naming the deployment's projection or a factory returning one. **Three keys the proposal had are gone**, each refused by name rather than merely unrecognised: `resolver:` (the SDK builds it), `question:`-as-a-factory (it is data), and `connection:`-as-a-reference (connection material is data under `connection:`, or it is absent). **The mechanism that keeps the exception an exception, which is why this is a ruling and not a fixture edit.** Amendment A.4's load-bearing sentence: *an exception with no mechanism holding it narrow is not an exception; it is a precedent.* `~ccf.client.adapters.binding_document.load_binding_document` is that mechanism and it enforces the narrowing **twice**, because either check alone leaks. `~ccf.client.adapters.binding_document.RULED_OUT_KEYS` refuses a rejected key **by name**, with a message that says the key was *ruled out* and cites this spec — told merely *"not a binding-document key"*, an author of the rejected shape would re-spell it. `~ccf.client.adapters.binding_document.looks_like_a_reference` refuses a `<module-path>:<attribute>`-shaped value **in any data position**, naming the dotted position, because key-name refusal cannot see a second reference smuggled through a key the schema *does* permit — and a schema widened by nobody's decision is the precedent A.4 forbids. The predicate's shape is deliberately *names a `.py` file*, not *contains a colon*, because a declared `asked_at` carries three colons and is data. `~ccf.client.adapters.binding_document.REFERENCE_KEYS` is a **one-element** tuple, so granting a second exception is a visible edit to a named constant rather than a widened condition inside a branch. **What was kept from the proposal, unchanged and deliberately.** (a) `binding.yaml` is **deployment-environment material, never a Control Plane artifact** — not registered, not content-hashed, carrying no `semver`, never reaching Decision Replay dimension 2. That posture is what lets connection material sit here at all: `docs/superpowers/specs/2026-08-15-ccf-client-design.md` §9 keeps connection material out of content-hashed state, and a *Control Plane* document naming a store would put deployment environment inside the audit plane. (b) The **strict loader** in the `~ccf.controlplane.artifacts.DeploymentConfig` posture: an unknown top-level key is refused by name rather than ignored, because wiring that silently selected a default would surface as a wrong answer rather than a failed boot. (c) **Path containment**, the two-check rule `~ccf.controlplane.artifacts.ManifestEntry` already holds a manifest to: the written check refuses an absolute path and a `..` segment, the resolved check refuses a plain name that is a symlink out, and neither covers the other. (d) The **trust posture** (`T-17-19`): loading the one reference executes the deployment's Python, accepted because the deployment root is its own trust boundary and the process already runs that deployment's code; what is *enforced* rather than trusted is containment, and the fact that there is exactly one such reference rather than an open set. **What this costs, stated rather than glossed.** The first example's `deployment/` is **not** pure configuration; Amendment A.3 says so and replaces the original success criterion 3 with 3′. What it still buys is measured: the client fixture's Python went from a 295-line module holding five things — a projection, a plan factory, a debt scorer, a connection factory and a question factory — to a 127-line module holding one projection whose only public name is the attribute `plan:` names. A reader is told to ignore one file that does one thing. **The future work that would close the exception**, named so it is not forgotten: a **declarative plan surface** — an artifact form for the operator-output → payload-field mapping. It is a design effort in its own right, not a side-quest, and until it exists the exception stands. **Governs:** `~ccf.client.adapters.binding_document`; `~ccf.client.adapters.inprocess`; `tests/client/fixtures/deployment/binding.yaml`. **Depends on** `D-IMPL-136` (the closed-world config that makes a second document necessary) and `D-06` (the binding address that names a root). **Related:** `D-IMPL-129` (the v0 not-list this leans on for the absent principal); `D-IMPL-47` (the scorer surface a declared `C` or `S` pins into replay under); `D-IMPL-38` (a default is a value nobody chose — why the declared factors are data rather than a library default).

**D-GATE-17 — Phase 17 (Client Access) approved.** Date: 2026-08-16. Approver: Dave. **What the phase delivered.** The transport-agnostic client surface `~ccf.client` — exactly four exported names — with the `Result`/refusal contract, the in-process adapter, **one decode path** every transport returns through, the client adapter conformance suite as a named CI gate, `~ccf.products.resolver.GovernedResolver` and `~ccf.products.resolver.build_governed_resolver`, the reference `~ccf.products.debt_scorer.AssemblyDebtScorer` with `DeclaredFactor`, a **data-only** `binding.yaml` held narrow by two independent refusal checks, and the first clean client example, `examples/hw1-companies-over-revenue`. That example is the artifact the phase was opened to produce: `consume.py` at **78 lines** importing only stdlib, `~ccf.client` and the adapter, beside an opaque `deployment/` holding **one** Python module and three data files — against Phase 16's rejected 722-line example whose `consume.py` imported the producer's `deployment.MANIFEST`, of which Dave's verdict was *"seasoned developers such as myself would not want to go near this thing with a 10 foot pole."* **Scope: 12 of 14 plans executed.** `17-08` (deletion of the condemned `s1-00`/`s1-01` example trees) and `17-10` (the MCP door and adapter) are **explicitly deferred** under `D-IMPL-146`, both plan files carrying a DEFERRED banner so an execute-phase run cannot pick them up. The reasoning there is a scoping principle rather than a one-off: MCP is a second transport carrying its own open questions, and *"folding it into the same phase risks the accretion pattern we just spent the phase escaping. One solid transport + the full contract + the example is a coherent, shippable increment."* **Both halves of the CLAUDE.md §3.1 close protocol ran, in order.** The cross-AI panel (`.planning/phases/17-*/17-CLOSE-REVIEWS.md`) reviewed **shipped code, not plans**, four reviewers across four model families, `claude` excluded as the executing runtime, and was handed the ten already-known findings with instructions not to re-derive them. The single-agent pass (`17-REVIEW.md`) ran additionally, never as a substitute. **Every finding was verified against the tree before being recorded or acted on**, and the rejected one is on the record as rejected: Codex rated the phase HIGH almost entirely on `examples/hw1-companies-over-revenue/deployment/projection.py` spelling payload field names in Python — a construct `D-IMPL-141` Amendment A.2 authorizes **in writing**, because mapping a `GraphView` onto payload fields is invention and is exactly why the projection is the one permitted module. What survived is a docstring overclaim, carried as advisory. The lesson generalizes and belongs in the next panel's prompt: **a reviewer unaware of a governing ruling reads a ruled exception as a violation**; hand future panels the relevant decision entries alongside the code. **Two BLOCKING findings, both found by EXECUTION rather than by reading, both fixed in-phase and sabotage-tested.** `BL-01`: two coverage floors gained headroom **during this phase** and were not re-raised, while both docstrings insist *"no headroom, on purpose"* — measured by running the module's own helpers, `_gate_scopes()` returned 12 against `MINIMUM_GATE_CALL_SITES = 11`, `_projecting_scopes()` returned 14 against `MINIMUM_PROJECTING_BODIES = 13`. The 12th site is `~ccf.products.resolver.GovernedResolver.__call__`, added when `17-03` moved the composition into the SDK; the 14th body is `build_projection`, added by `17-09`. `MINIMUM_GATE_CALL_SITES` has now been short **twice**, the second time in the phase that documented the failure mode — which is why both comments now carry the rule: **a floor is only a floor while it equals the count, and the plan that adds a witness is the plan that must raise it.** Adding a witness makes the assertion *more* satisfied, so nothing goes red to say so. Raised to 12 and 14, verified at zero headroom across all three floors, sabotage-tested (a floor above its count fails; restored, 96 pass). `BL-02`: `as_of=None` resolved to a literal declared in `binding.yaml` rather than to a clock, so the default path answered as of a fixed past moment — measured, the shipped example printed `answer expires 2026-03-02 12:10:00+00:00` on 2026-08-16, five and a half months expired, while `~ccf.client.client.ProductClient.ask` documents *"the producing deployment answers against its own clock"*, the README promises *"today's answer"*, and `D-04` says the same. `None` now resolves to `datetime.now(UTC)`. **That fix broke six tests and not one was churn**: each had been reproducible only because the declared literal happened to be fixed, so every address-stability leg had been **inheriting determinism from a config value rather than testing it**. The `D-15` served-parity leg was *right* to fail — under a real clock two transports answer milliseconds apart, so a parity leg handed an unpinned question compares clocks rather than answers. `QUESTION` is now split into a **pinned** form for every parity and address leg and an `UNPINNED_QUESTION` for the single leg that must ask with `None` to prove the server resolves an instant; those requirements are opposite and one constant had served both. `REFUSING_QUESTION` is pinned for the same reason — a refusal carries the request identity, `temporal_boundary` included. One adapter assertion read `'forced_llm' == 'ok'`, because the fixture's observations sit near the old literal and asking *now* correctly reports them five months stale. **Nothing was relaxed to pass**, and *"one question, one address"* is now a claim about assembly rather than about a config value. **A third ruling followed from `BL-02`:** `question.asked_at` became **optional** — Dave, *"a required field that no code path reads is a latent defect... keeping it required solely because the schema was closed earlier is the wrong reason."* Recorded as a narrow amendment to `D-IMPL-141`'s **data shape**, reason visible: the field became unread after `BL-02`, therefore the requirement was dropped. It is still validated as a **zoned** ISO 8601 instant when declared — *optional is not unvalidated* — and `tests/client/test_binding_document.py::test_binding_document_accepts_a_question_that_omits_asked_at` asserts the **absence** path specifically, because every fixture in the tree declares the field and a loader that had gone on requiring it would have passed the entire suite. The reshape is claimed in `docs/surface-changes/17-close-asked-at-optional.md`: added 0, removed 0, reshaped 1. **Known consequences recorded rather than papered over.** (1) **The example's headline command prints an empty answer on a clean checkout** — `0 of 0 assembled`, `D_c 1.0000`, `fail_closed` — because `D-32` forbids committing data to `examples/` and nothing in the repository populates a deployment. Verified on the main checkout. The mechanism is **honest**: that is `17-11`'s empty-assembly branch refusing to report a confident number about a graph that said nothing, carrying no scorer pin. No test depends on a populated deployment (464 passed after deleting both generated paths), so CI is unaffected. It is a joint consequence of `D-32` and the deliberately client-only scope, and belongs to the producer example that also closes the `refuse_request` citation gap. (2) **A process item for future phases:** plan `17-09` shipped `examples/hw1-companies-over-revenue` *and* edited the two gates that judge it, same phase, same agent — raised by the executor against itself, *"worth a reviewer who is not me."* Both outside reviewers examined it and both concluded the detectors **genuinely constrain** the shape rather than name-check the example; Cursor's words, *"detector quality is good; independence is not."* The single-agent pass agreed, then located the real shaping elsewhere — in `BL-01`. **The disposition to carry: a plan should not ship an artifact and the gate that judges it.** (3) The resolver-seam collision that blocked `17-08` is recorded concretely in `D-IMPL-146` so a later phase inherits facts rather than a summary. **Advisory findings carried forward; no blocking items remain:** the per-module budget gate, the client exception taxonomy leaking past `TransportError`, `_parse` swallowing `SyntaxError` under a `# pragma: no cover` that admits the branch has never executed, the documentation-only credential posture on `connection:`, the purity gate admitting any `ccf.client.*` submodule, the projection docstring overclaim, the `required[0]` positional assumption, `load_reference` mutating `sys.path`, plus the single-agent pass's seven warnings and eight advisories. **Verification at the gate, measured on the main checkout with exit codes captured directly and BRANCHED ON rather than echoed** — the session made the `make … | tail` mistake twice during this phase and reported a green tree that was red: `make ci-quick-suite` **exit 0**, `3739 passed / 0 failed / 2 skipped / 172 deselected`; `make gate-tools` exit 0; `tools.lints --all` exit 0; `make docs-strict` exit 0; `make typecheck` exit 0 over 209 source files. **GitHub Actions green on all four jobs** at run `31974549984` — the first fully green run this branch has ever had, and the first time the four substrate legs (Postgres, Neo4j, Mongo, Fluree) and the coverage step ever executed, unblocked by `D-IMPL-143`. **Merge authorized on the condition Dave set:** the `--no-ff` merge to `main` proceeds only after a green Actions run on **the commit actually being merged**, not on an earlier one — *"the two blocker fixes and the `asked_at` change are material enough that the final commit should be the one CI has seen."* **The phase's standing lesson, now at eleven instances and unchanged:** every defect found was in **verification machinery or in plans, never in mechanism** — a check that stops checking, or one that keeps reporting green while verifying the wrong thing. Root causes worth carrying: Python's discovery primitives default to LENIENT, so a gate built on one inherits its leniency unless overridden explicitly; and prose drifts out from under code, so a docstring, comment or README asserting a behaviour is not evidence the behaviour holds. Originates: CLAUDE.md §3.1; `.planning/phases/17-*/17-CLOSE-REVIEWS.md`; `17-REVIEW.md`; `D-IMPL-141` through `D-IMPL-146`.

**D-IMPL-147 — Phase 18 is opened on the MCP transport, and `17-10` is lifted into it keeping its plan ID.** RESOLVED. Date: 2026-08-17. Approver: Dave. **The question.** `D-GATE-17` closed, gated and merged Phase 17 at `b32348f`, leaving `main` the approved state and nothing in flight — and `.planning/ROADMAP.md` ended at Phase 17, so the next phase was undefined rather than merely unplanned. Four candidates were put to Dave with their consequences: (1) `17-10`, the MCP door and adapter, deferred out of 17 by `D-IMPL-146`; (2) `17-08`, deleting the condemned `s1` trees, also deferred; (3) Phase 16, stopped at 6/21 and never gated; (4) the producer example. **Ruling: (1).** The reasoning recorded because it is an ordering principle and not a preference — the conformance suite was **already written to take a second subject and says so in its own docstring** (*"Plan 17-10 adds a second module exactly like this one and changes nothing in suite.py"*), so the second transport is the cheapest available falsification of a claim Phase 17 could only assert. A single adapter cannot test a claim about adapters: the suite's cross-adapter comparisons currently degenerate to self-comparisons, and a second real adapter is what makes them comparisons. `17-08` was **not** chosen because it is blocked on real work rather than on sequencing — `refuse_request` would have no surviving demonstration anywhere in `examples/` once those trees go, and the remedy is to write a producer-side example, not to relax a gate (the concrete measurement is in `D-IMPL-146`). **Phase 17 is NOT reopened.** It is gated and merged, and per `CLAUDE.md` §2.2 phase work happens on a phase branch with the merge at the gate; a closed phase does not reabsorb a plan. Branch `phase-18`, cut from `main` rather than from `phase-17`, because Phase 17's work is already merged and there is nothing in-flight to carry. **THE PLAN ID IS RETAINED AS `17-10`, DELIBERATELY.** Renumbering it to `18-01` would have staled five citations: three shipped test docstrings — `tests/examples/test_client_example_purity.py`, and `tests/client/conformance/test_inprocess_conformance.py` twice, once in the module docstring and once on the collection every conformance leg compares over — plus two **immutable** decision entries, `D-IMPL-146` and `D-GATE-17`, which record as history that a plan *named* `17-10` was deferred. Decision entries are the historical record and are not rewritten to accommodate a later move. The rename was unnecessary anyway, and this is the load-bearing measurement: **plan discovery is directory-scoped and prefix-agnostic** — `execute-phase`, `verify-phase`, `secure-phase` and `review` all glob `"$PHASE_DIR"/*-PLAN.md`, so the phase reads the file under its original name. The single prefix-sensitive workflow, `plan-review-convergence.md` (`${padded_phase}-*-PLAN.md`), is not needed for a plan that already passed the checker in Phase 17. This is `D-IMPL-144`'s stable-ID discipline paying off in an unanticipated direction: because citations name an *identifier* rather than a location, moving the artifact costs nothing. **The deferral banner is amended, not deleted** — the same discipline the plan itself applies to the egress docstring: keep the reasoning, change the behaviour. The banner now records both the `D-IMPL-146` deferral and this lift, and states that the ID retention is intentional so a later reader does not "tidy" it. **Four premises re-measured on `main` @ `b32348f` before the lift, so the phase starts on facts rather than on a plan's assumptions:** (a) **`mcp==1.28.1` is already a declared, pinned dependency** and installed — therefore `T-17-SC`'s blocking human checkpoint for an `[ASSUMED]` package does **not** fire and no install is owed; the plan's own instruction was to *stop* if it were not, so this was checked before anything moved. (b) All seven referenced artifacts exist: `tests/client/conformance/suite.py` (759 L), `test_inprocess_conformance.py` (188 L), `~ccf.client.adapters.inprocess` (445 L), `~ccf.client.binding`, `~ccf.client.codec`, `~ccf.products.refusal`, `~ccf_runtime.mcp.egress` (599 L). (c) **Task 3's harness-lint boundary question is answered NEGATIVE, empirically rather than by reading the rule** — `src/` already imports `mcp` in four modules (`~ccf.mcp.client`, `~ccf.artifacts.mcp_store`, `~ccf.signal.mcp_source`, `~ccf.signal.mcp_envelope_map`) and `tools/lints/harness_imports.py` denies a frozenset of *harness* packages that does not contain MCP, because MCP is CCF's own integration surface (`CLAUDE.md` §7.1) and not a harness; the lint is green today with those four imports in place. The plan told its executor to stop and report if the lint refused; it does not, so no ruling is owed and Task 3 cannot stall there. (d) `17-09`, the plan's former `depends_on`, is merged — the dependency is discharged, so `wave: 8, depends_on: ["17-09"]` becomes `wave: 1, depends_on: []`. **Two ROADMAP staleness defects were corrected in passing, and are recorded because neither was mine and both were load-bearing:** Phase 17 **had no bullet in the Phases list at all** — it was planned, executed, gated and merged with a detail section and a progress row but no entry — and its Progress row read `0/10 — Planned 2026-08-15` throughout. The absent-box case is not cosmetic: the Phase 14.1 note records that an unchecked box once made `roadmap.analyze` return `next_phase: "14.1"`, routing auto-advance into a phase that was deliberately never planned. **Governs:** `.planning/phases/18-mcp-transport-the-second-door-and-the-portability-proof/17-10-PLAN.md`; the Phase 18 entry in `.planning/ROADMAP.md`; the `phase-18` branch. **Related:** `D-IMPL-146` (the deferral this discharges, and the `17-08` blocker it does not), `D-GATE-17` (the gate that made the lift possible), `D-IMPL-144` (stable-ID citations, the property that made ID retention free), `D-IMPL-133` and `16-26` (the `parameters={}` absorption the plan carries), `D-15`, `D-24`, `D-28`, `D-32`.

**D-IMPL-133 Addendum A — implemented in plan `17-10`; `16-26` absorbed; the recorded objection is half false and half honoured.** Date: 2026-08-17. Implements the standing ruling of 2026-08-14 (*"both doors carry `parameters`"*); rules nothing new. **`16-26` was recorded "neighboring, not gating" and was gating.** The measurement, not the intention: `17-10`'s deliverable 5 binds a real MCP adapter into the `D-15` conformance suite, and the suite's fixture class declares `minimum_score` **required** (`tests/client/fixtures/deployment/ccf_config.yaml:46`). With `parameters={}` hard-wired, the MCP adapter could not ask the phase's own question, could not pass `D-15(a)` served-parity against the in-process adapter, and could not carry `D-23`'s two-thresholds-two-addresses property — every call would have refused `missing-required-parameter` identically, so the parity legs would have compared two refusals and reported green while proving nothing. Absorbed and planned rather than deferred a second time. **The plan directed that the recorded reason in the egress docstring be a constraint on the fix rather than an obstacle to it, and be amended rather than deleted. It was both, and its two halves resolved differently — which is why the amendment records both rather than declaring the paragraph obsolete.** *The coercion half is FALSE AS MEASURED.* It read: *"a flat tool-argument map cannot carry `D-IMPL-103`'s declared nested types without the door guessing at coercions."* An MCP tool-argument map is **not flat and not string-valued** — it is JSON. Measured against `mcp==1.28.1` by registering a probe tool taking `dict[str, Any]` and calling it: a presented `3` arrived `int`, a presented `"3"` arrived `str`, `true` arrived `bool`, and `{"a": [1, 2]}` arrived a nested `dict`. `D-IMPL-103`'s one level of declared nesting therefore crosses intact with nothing whatever for the door to guess at, and both doors forward the mapping **verbatim** — no coercion, no defaulting, no inspection. *The width half is TRUE AND STILL BINDING.* It read: *"a declared-parameter surface here would make this tool wider than the HTTP door."* Measured: it would have, because the HTTP door hard-wired the identical `parameters={}` at `routes/product.py:330` for the identical recorded reason, and those two were the **only** two sites in `runtime/` constructing a `ProductRequest` for a door. Repairing the MCP tool alone would have produced exactly the asymmetry the objection names. It is honoured by repairing **both**, which is what `D-IMPL-133` ruled in the first place — *"both, not one"* — and is why plan `17-10` edited `routes/product.py` despite that file being absent from its `files_modified` list. **The HTTP door carries the mapping as one JSON object in a single query parameter, not as flattened query keys**, and that shape is load-bearing rather than incidental: a flat, string-valued query genuinely cannot carry declared types or nesting without the door guessing, so flattening would have made the recorded objection true again on the door where it was originally written. JSON makes the two surfaces the same width. Malformed text, or JSON that is not an object, is refused 400 under the new typed code `ERROR_PRODUCT_PARAMETERS_MALFORMED` rather than falling back to the empty mapping — a fallback would answer a question the caller did not ask while reporting success. Supplying `parameters` with none of the four §6.3 axes is refused `ERROR_PRODUCT_REQUEST_INCOMPLETE`: the declared remainder qualifies a question, and there is none to qualify. **`parameters` is OPTIONAL on both doors, and the asymmetry with the four axes is deliberate.** The three address segments and the four §6.3 axes are the *address* and stay required together, so a partial address remains **unrepresentable** on the tool rather than merely refused; the declared remainder is not part of the address, so omitting it is the empty mapping — *this question needs nothing beyond its four axes* — and every deployment that adopted either door before this change is byte-identical in behaviour. **No door validates, and that is why this is a two-line repair.** `refuse_request` is still called by the deployment's resolver and by no door (`D-IMPL-121`); an undeclared parameter is refused `undeclared-parameter` there, a wrong-typed one `type-mismatch`, and `missing-required-parameter` now names a caller who genuinely omitted the parameter rather than every caller alike. **The width ceiling is now mechanized rather than asserted**, which is the part that outlives this entry: `tests/mcp/test_egress_parameters.py::assert_same_verdicts` runs **one shared five-case table** against both real doors and requires identical verdicts, and it **refuses a table in which no case is served** — because a refusing-only table would have passed against the two unrepaired doors, which carried `{}` and therefore agreed perfectly while carrying nothing. That guard is the difference between a width gate and a width gate's shape. **Two sabotage controls (`D-22`) pass divergent subjects into the real assertions** — a deliberately-wider fake door that admits the undeclared parameter, and a coercing fake that reads `"11"` as `11` — and both are shipped tests rather than reverted edits. **Three shipped assertions moved, each because the surface genuinely moved and none to make a failure go away:** the tool's advertised argument set is now eight properties against seven `required` (previously one set asserted by equality against both, which cannot express an optional argument); the handler-signature test's absent-name list drops `parameters` and keeps `role`, `schema_name` and `schema_version`, because those three claim an *address* and `parameters` does not; and `openapi.json` regains one query-parameter entry under the committed-drift gate. **Governs:** `runtime/ccf_runtime/mcp/egress.py`; `runtime/ccf_runtime/http_api/routes/product.py`; `runtime/ccf_runtime/http_api/errors.py`. **Related:** `D-IMPL-133` (the ruling this implements), `D-IMPL-126` (the tool this widens), `D-IMPL-108` (the HTTP door's request surface), `D-IMPL-121` and `D-IMPL-113` (validation stays at the resolver), `D-IMPL-103` (the declared nested types that cross intact), `D-IMPL-147` (the lift that scheduled this), `D-15`, `D-23`.

**D-IMPL-147 Addendum A — the `CLNT-15` traceability remap, and a broader staleness finding it exposed.** Date: 2026-08-17. **`CLNT-15` is remapped from Phase 17 to Phase 18** in `.planning/REQUIREMENTS.md`'s traceability table. Measured, not assumed: `CLNT-15` (*"the MCP product door carries client-supplied parameters and emits the canonical refusal envelope, and the MCP adapter ships on passing the same conformance suite as in-process rather than on demoing"*) is claimed by **exactly one plan**, `17-10`, and by **no Phase 17 SUMMARY** — so Phase 17 gated with a requirement mapped to it that none of its executed plans satisfied. With the implementing plan lifted, leaving the row on Phase 17 would have made the table assert a debt Phase 17 can no longer discharge. `CLNT-12` by contrast is **correctly left on Phase 17**: eight Phase 17 plans claim it and two SUMMARYs record satisfying it, so it is a cross-cutting requirement met there which Phase 18's plan merely re-asserts for its own gates — the ROADMAP's *"each to exactly one phase"* rule is preserved rather than broken by the move. **A BROADER FINDING, surfaced to Dave and DELIBERATELY NOT mass-edited:** every `CLNT-01`..`CLNT-17` row still reads `Pending`, including the fourteen that `D-GATE-17` demonstrably closed. The traceability table's Status column was never advanced during Phase 17's execution, gate or merge — the same class of defect as the Phase 17 progress row reading `0/10 — Planned` throughout, and of the missing Phases-list bullet. Three independent bookkeeping surfaces went stale in one phase while the mechanism they describe was verified green, which is worth noting as a pattern: **the project's verification rigour is applied to code and not to its own status records.** Mass-updating seventeen statuses is a scope decision for Dave rather than a side effect of opening Phase 18, so only the one row whose correctness the lift actually changed was touched. **Governs:** `.planning/REQUIREMENTS.md` traceability table. **Related:** `D-IMPL-147`, `D-GATE-17`.

**D-IMPL-148 — the MCP door carries the replay reference; cross-adapter served parity is not weakened to admit an adapter.** RESOLVED. Date: 2026-08-17. Approver: Dave. **The question, surfaced mid-execution by plan `17-10` and verified against the tree before it was put.** `tests/client/conformance/suite.py::assert_served_parity` compares **every declared field** of `Result` — derived from the class by `result_fields()` rather than written down — and `replay_ref` is among them (`src/ccf/client/result.py`, in `GUARDED_FIELDS`). The in-process adapter supplies it as `outcome.replay_ref.uri`. **The MCP egress door emits `canonical_bytes(servable.product)` and nothing else**, so an `mcp+stdio` adapter would produce `replay_ref=None` and the served-parity leg — the phase's central claim — would fail on that field. Task 4 was therefore unsatisfiable as `17-10` wrote it, and neither Task 2 nor Task 3 scheduled the fix while Task 4 asserted the parity would hold. **Ruling: the door carries the reference. It is folded into Task 2 rather than added as a fifth task.** **Why this and not the exemption.** Exempting `replay_ref` from cross-adapter comparison was the fastest green, and was rejected as *weakening a shipped assertion in order to admit a feature* — which `D-GATE-17` names as this phase-family's signature defect, at eleven recorded instances, all of the same shape: treating absence of a failure signal as presence of a success signal. It would also have hidden a **general** door defect behind an **adapter-specific** exemption. **Why the fix is small in access and real in shape.** The reference is not missing — it is **discarded at the door**. `ServedProduct` already carries `replay_ref: ArtifactRef` (`src/ccf/replay/serve.py`), and the egress door already holds that object at both of its resolver call sites before returning only the product bytes. What the fix actually costs is the **tool's return shape**: the tool today returns the bare product JSON, byte-identical to the MCP resource, and plan `17-10` Task 1 now asserts that byte-identity. **That shape was already changing in Task 2 regardless**, because a refusal cannot be raised — measured against `mcp==1.28.1`, FastMCP wraps a raised tool exception as `ToolError: Error executing tool <name>: <message>` and delivers it as `isError=True` with that prose prefix, so carrying a `RefusalEnvelope` that way would force the client to string-parse a server's wording for a governed answer. The refusal must be the tool's **return value**, the return value therefore becomes a structure, and the replay reference rides that same change instead of forcing a second one. The served and refused shapes stay discriminable **structurally** rather than by sniffing, because `RefusalEnvelope` and `ContextProductContract` are both `extra=forbid` with disjoint required fields and neither can validate as the other. **`wire.py` had already called this absence what it is** — *"No shipped door emits this today, which is a defect the client seam exists to fix rather than a feature request"* — so this ruling executes a stated intent rather than inventing scope. **SCOPE — SUPERSEDED BY ADDENDUM A (Dave, 2026-08-17), and kept rather than deleted because the reasoning is what the amendment overturns. The paragraph as written stands as the record of what was assumed; the behaviour it describes is no longer the behaviour. Read Addendum A at the end of this file before relying on anything below.** The superseded text: **SCOPE, STATED AS AN ASSUMPTION RATHER THAN A RULING, because Dave was offered the sub-question and did not annotate it: the HTTP door is NOT in scope here and its identical gap is DEFERRED, not fixed.** `grep replay_ref runtime/ccf_runtime/http_api/routes/product.py` returns nothing — the HTTP door discards the reference exactly as the MCP door does. It is excluded because **no assertion forces it**: the conformance suite compares *adapters*, there is no HTTP adapter, and so no shared assertion spans both doors. This is the material difference from `D-IMPL-133`, where both doors were required precisely *because* one shared five-case table could not be satisfied while either door carried nothing. **The moment an HTTP adapter is bound into the `D-15` suite, this deferral becomes the same blocker in the same place** — recorded here so it is found rather than rediscovered. **Governs:** `runtime/ccf_runtime/mcp/egress.py`; plan `17-10` Tasks 2 and 4; `tests/client/conformance/suite.py`, which this ruling explicitly protects from edit. **Related:** `D-IMPL-147` (the lift that scheduled the plan), `D-IMPL-133` (the both-doors precedent, and why it does not extend here), `D-GATE-17` (the defect class the exemption would have repeated), `D-15` (the conformance suite), `D-28` (the refusal envelope), `D-12` (the adapter constructs no `Result`).

**D-IMPL-148 Addendum A — both doors carry the replay reference, and Task 4 is gated on both.** RESOLVED. Date: 2026-08-17. Approver: Dave. **What this amends.** The base entry above scoped the replay-reference fix to the MCP door alone and recorded the HTTP door's identical gap as **deferred, not fixed** — explicitly *"stated as an assumption rather than a ruling, because Dave was offered the sub-question and did not annotate it."* Dave has now annotated it. **The scope paragraph is amended rather than deleted**, on this project's standing discipline for a recorded reason that measurement overturns: the paragraph stays as the record of what was assumed, marked superseded, and this addendum carries the behaviour. **THE RULING, in substance.** (1) **Add the replay-reference channel to BOTH doors** — `runtime/ccf_runtime/mcp/egress.py` **and** `runtime/ccf_runtime/http_api/routes/product.py` — so both emit the same canonical field the in-process adapter already supplies and `assert_served_parity` already compares. (2) **Only after both doors emit it** may the `mcp+stdio` adapter be bound into the conformance suite; plan `17-10` Task 4 is gated on that ordering, which is why Task 2's executor stopped before Task 3. (3) **Do not special-case MCP, do not drop `replay_ref` from the parity comparison, and do not weaken the parity suite.** Making a second adapter official while it and the HTTP door silently omit a compared field *"would make the conformance suite lie."* (4) If the HTTP channel had proved genuinely out of scope, **Task 4 would split or defer** — but the assertion would never narrow. Deferring is acceptable; special-casing is not. **WHY BOTH, when the base entry argued only one was forced.** The base entry's reasoning was that *no assertion forces the HTTP door*, because the conformance suite compares adapters and there is no HTTP adapter. That is true and it is the wrong test. `D-IMPL-133` had already ruled the same question for `parameters` and ruled it *"both, not one"* — and the base entry's own closing sentence conceded the consequence: *"The moment an HTTP adapter is bound into the `D-15` suite, this deferral becomes the same blocker in the same place."* A gap knowingly left on one door is a gap the next assertion to span both discovers the hard way, which is this phase-family's recorded defect shape rather than a hypothetical. **HOW IT WAS BUILT, and the one thing that had to move.** The reference was never missing — it was **discarded at the door**. `ServedProduct` carries `replay_ref: ArtifactRef` and every shipped door read `.product` off it and dropped the rest. What the fix costs is the **MCP tool's return shape**, and that shape was already changing regardless: a refusal cannot be raised over FastMCP without a prose prefix (measured, `mcp==1.28.1`), so the refusal had to become the tool's return value, the return therefore became a structure, and the reference rides that same change instead of forcing a second one. The structure is `ccf.products.door.DoorOutcome`, declared in `src/` beside the types it carries for the reason `ccf.products.refusal` gives for its own placement — it is emitted in `runtime/` and decoded in `src/ccf/client`, and `src/` cannot import `runtime/`. **The HTTP door needed no shape change**, because it has a header channel: the reference travels as `X-CCF-Replay-Ref`, an outcome-level sibling of the vessel on the identical argument `X-CCF-Content-Address` already stands on — the body *is* the product, so a reference carried inside it would change the bytes and therefore change the address they claim. `openapi.json` did not move, verified by regeneration under the committed-drift gate. **THE ASYMMETRY IS THE TRANSPORTS, NOT THE DOORS.** Each surface publishes the same values through whatever sibling channel it actually has: HTTP a header, the MCP tool an envelope. **The MCP resource is deliberately excluded and this is the boundary, stated so it is found rather than rediscovered**: it carries no request, so it has no question a refusal envelope could name, and it has neither header nor envelope, so it has no sibling channel — giving it one means making it a different resource, and the `mcp+stdio` adapter calls the *tool* (it must, to carry `parameters`), so conformance is unaffected. **WHAT REMAINS DEFERRED, NAMED.** The HTTP door still has **no refusal channel** (DIV-4). A resolver answering with a `RefusalEnvelope` there reaches `served_answer` and raises `TypeError` naming the shape — an opaque 500, exactly as the pre-existing `AttributeError` produced, but now saying what went wrong. Rendering a fabric refusal over HTTP is a status-code and body-shape decision nobody has ruled, and inventing one on a shipped door would put an unruled contract in front of adopters. **ONE SHIPPED ASSERTION WAS RESTATED, AND THE RESTATEMENT IS NOT A WEAKENING.** Plan `17-10` Task 1 shipped `tool_text == resource_text` — the tool's *whole return* was the vessel byte for byte. With the tool answering in a `DoorOutcome`, the comparison moved to the member carrying the vessel: the bytes are still the resource's and still the canonical serializer's output, character for character, and the text must now additionally validate as a `DoorOutcome`, so a door emitting an ad-hoc shape fails where before it could not. What is no longer claimed is that the return has *no frame around* those bytes — exactly and only what this ruling changed. `tests/crossproc/test_mcp_egress.py::test_two_questions_reach_two_content_addresses` became **stronger** for the same reason: its digests are now taken over the vessel the envelope carries, which is the product's actual content address, where before they were taken over whatever the tool returned. **Governs:** `runtime/ccf_runtime/mcp/egress.py`; `runtime/ccf_runtime/http_api/routes/product.py`; `runtime/ccf_runtime/http_api/routes/__init__.py`; `src/ccf/products/door.py`; plan `17-10` Tasks 2 and 4; `tests/client/conformance/suite.py`, which this ruling continues to protect from edit. **Related:** `D-IMPL-148` (the base entry this amends), `D-IMPL-133` (the both-doors precedent, which *does* extend here — reversing the base entry's reading), `D-IMPL-121` (validation stays at the resolver; this widens what a door carries, never what it checks), `D-IMPL-126` (the content-address header whose argument the replay-reference header reuses), `D-15` (the conformance suite), `D-28` (the refusal envelope), `D-22` (every control passes its broken subject into the shipped assertion).
